normalized in its exact attaching value. This proves the assertion.

The exact lift is also compatible with the nonlinear gauge action.
For $g=\exp\zeta$, the actual normalized difference is
\[
 \bigl(\rho^{-1}(g^{-1}A_Bg-A_B),\
                     g^{-1}\beta g+g^{-1}d_\Sigma g-\beta,0\bigr).
\]
Writing $\gamma=\beta-\alpha$ and $q=g^{-1}d_\alpha g$, its
surface component is $q+g^{-1}\gamma g-\gamma$, with
$d_\alpha q=-[q\wedge q]/2$. The surface algebra therefore gives
quadratic difference bounds with the same bounded coefficient and
no second derivative of $\zeta$. The map
$\zeta\mapsto E_\alpha(\alpha^{\exp\zeta}-\alpha)$ has
invertible derivative $d_\alpha$, so small exact attaching coordinates
can be prescribed. The attaching flat class stays fixed; the harmonic
projection of a finite nonlinear displacement need not vanish.
\end{proof}

The localization estimate at the cap radius does not alone control
the intermediate annuli on which the backgrounds differ. We give the
additional estimate, keeping the surface mass arbitrary.

\begin{lemma}[intermediate flat annuli]\label{lem:gluing-flat-annuli}
Write $r_1=R_1^{-1}$ and use a boundary coordinate $\zeta=s+it$
at infinity, with $t\geq0$. Let $f=f_{R_1}$, so
\[
 f(\zeta)\leq C(r_1^2+|\zeta|^2)^{-1},\qquad
 f(\zeta)=c(\zeta)|\zeta|^{-2}\quad (|\zeta|\geq2r_1),
\]
where $c$ is fixed smooth and positive. For a surface mass $m\geq0$
suppose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-flat-stream-data}
 \begin{gathered}
 a=*d\psi,\quad y-z=m\psi,\quad
 (\Delta+m^2f^2)\psi=fH-mf^2z,\\
 (\Delta+m^2f^2)y=\Delta z+mfH,\qquad
 \psi|_{\partial B}=y|_{\partial B}=z|_{\partial B}=0,\quad x(0)=0,
 \qquad H|_{D_{r_0}}=0.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Assume $x,z\in H^{2+\varepsilon}(D_{r_0/2})$ and set
\[
 B_m=\|H\|_{H^\varepsilon}+\|(x,z,y)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}
                   +r_0^3\|(x,z)\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}(D_{r_0/2})},
 \qquad A_m=C\varepsilon^{-2}r_0^{-8}B_m.
\]
On a fixed half-annulus in the variable $Z=\zeta/r$, for
$64r_1\leq r\leq r_0/64$, uniformly in $m$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-flat-annular-estimate}
 \begin{split}
 \|\psi(r\cdot)\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}}&\leq A_m,\\
 \|(a/f,x,y)(r\cdot)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}&\leq CrA_m,\\
 \|(ma,mfy)(r\cdot)\|_{H^\varepsilon}&\leq CA_m.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
The same estimates hold after square summation with the fixed
surface Sobolev weights.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Put $A_m^0=C\varepsilon^{-1}r_0^{-5}B_m$. The local
$H^{2+\varepsilon}$ estimate, point normalization and zero trace give
$|x(\zeta)|\leq A_m^0|\zeta|$ and
$|z(s,t)|\leq A_m^0t$. Kato comparison in the second equation
of \eqref{eq:gluing-flat-stream-data}, on a fixed smooth subdomain
of $D_{r_0/2}$, gives $|y(s,t)|\leq CA_m^0t$ on a smaller
half-disc. The comparison uses the global $C^0$ bound of $y$
for its artificial boundary and the local $H^{2+\varepsilon}$
norm of $z$ for the forcing. The Sobolev and Poisson constants
are those of Proposition~\ref{prop:small-fractional-exponents};
the displayed $\varepsilon^{-1}r_0^{-5}$ dominates them.
On an enlarged $r$ annulus,
\[
                 \|(x,z)(r\cdot)\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}}
                                                 \leq CrA_m^0.
\]
Indeed the value and first derivative use the $C^1$ estimate;
the second derivative has $L^2$ scaling $r$, and its fractional
part has scaling $r^{1+\varepsilon}$.

First let $m\geq r/16$ and put $N=m/r$. On these annuli
$g_r(Z)=r^2f(rZ)$ and its inverse have uniformly bounded
fixed derivatives. The equation for $y(rZ)$ is
\[
 (\Delta_Z+N^2g_r^2)y(rZ)=r^2\Delta z(rZ).
\]
Its outer $L^2$ norm and right-hand $H^\varepsilon$ norm are
at most $CrA_m^0$. Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-mass-dirichlet} on
interior or Dirichlet-seam patches yields
\[
 \|y(r\cdot)\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}}
 +\|Ny(r\cdot)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}
 +\|N^2y(r\cdot)\|_{H^\varepsilon}\leq CrA_m^0.
\]
For $1/16\leq N\leq1$ the same assertion is ordinary local
regularity with bounded potential. Since $m^{-1}\leq16/r$,
$\psi=(y-z)/m$ has the required strong bound. The identities
\[
 ma(rZ)=r^{-1}*d_Z(y(rZ)-z(rZ)),\qquad
 mf(rZ)y(rZ)=r^{-1}g_r(Z)Ny(rZ)
\]
give the two weak bounds.

Now let $0<m<r/16$. If $m\leq r_1$, Kato comparison in the
first stream equation bounds $|\psi|$ by the Dirichlet Green
potential of $f|H|+mf^2|z|$ on $B$. If $m>r_1$, perform
this comparison on $B\setminus D_{4m}$. On the new semicircle
$|\psi|=|y-z|/m\leq CA_m^0$, so its harmonic contribution is
bounded by $CA_m^0$. Domain monotonicity bounds the punctured
Green function by $G_B$. No regularity at the artificial corners
is needed for this weak scalar comparison.

Locally $G_B$ is bounded by the half-plane kernel plus
$Ct_\zeta t_\eta$, where
\[
 G_{\mathbb H}(\zeta,\eta)=\frac1{4\pi}
          \log\left(1+\frac{4t_\zeta t_\eta}{|\zeta-\eta|^2}\right).
\]
For $|\zeta|\asymp r$ and $|\eta|<r/4$ it is at most
$Ct_\eta/r$. Consequently the inner contribution when $m\leq r_1$
is bounded by
\[
 C\frac mr A_m^0\int_0^{r/4}\frac{s^3}{(r_1^2+s^2)^2}\,ds
 \leq C\frac mr(1+\log(r/r_1))A_m^0\leq CA_m^0.
\]
When $m>r_1$, the lower limit is $4m$ and the bound is
$C(m/r)\log(r/(4m))_+A_m^0$. The comparable-radius part has
an integrable logarithmic singularity and bound $C(m/r)A_m^0$.
For $|\eta|>4r$ in the local half-disc,
$G_{\mathbb H}\leq Crt_\eta/|\eta|^2$, giving
$Cmr\int_{4r}^\infty s^{-3}ds\,A_m^0\leq C(m/r)A_m^0$.
The smooth Green correction contributes at most
$Cmr(1+\log(r_0/r_1))A_m^0$ in the first case and
$Cmr(1+\log(r_0/m))A_m^0$ in the second. These are bounded
because $x\log(1/x)$ is bounded. Finally, outside a fixed
multiple of $D_{r_0}$, the bounds $G_B\leq Crr_0^{-2}$ and
$f\leq Cr_0^{-2}$ give
\[
                      C(rr_0^{-4}+mrr_0^{-6})B_m.
\]
All of $H$ lies in this remote region. The same remote calculation
handles $m=0$. We have proved
$\|\psi(r\cdot)\|_{C^0}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}r_0^{-6}B_m$
on enlarged annuli.

There the equation is
\[
 (\Delta_Z+(m/r)^2g_r^2)\psi(rZ)=-(m/r^2)g_r^2z(rZ).
\]
The potential is uniformly bounded and the right-hand side has
$H^\varepsilon$ norm at most $C(m/r)A_m^0$. Local interior
or Dirichlet regularity gives the asserted $H^{2+\varepsilon}$
bound. The identity $y=z+m\psi$ gives the strong bound for $y$;
$ma=(m/r)*d_Z\psi$ and $mfy=(m/r^2)g_ry$ give the weak bounds.
In both mass ranges $a/f=r g_r^{-1}*d_Z\psi$, proving its
strong estimate. The loose factor in $A_m$ includes all preceding
restrictions.

For a finite set of surface modes, square the inequalities with each
mode's own $B_m$. Parseval bounds the square sum of these data by
the Hilbert-valued input norms. The mode-dependent puncture was only
used for a uniform scalar bound; it is not a common operator domain.
Completeness then permits the full spectral limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[nonflat annular estimate]\label{lem:gluing-cap-annuli}
Let $U=(a,b,\nu)$ solve $\mathcal A_{\rho,f}U=F$ on the cap,
with both attaching values zero and $F=0$ on $D_{r_0}$ on both
sides. Write $a=*d\psi$, with zero Dirichlet trace. For
$64r_1\leq r\leq r_0/64$ put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-cap-annular-norm}
 \begin{split}
 Z_r(U)={}&\|\psi(r\cdot)\|_{H_B^{2+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
  +r^{-1}\|(a/f,b,\nu)(r\cdot)\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}\\
 &+\|(\rho d_\alpha a,\rho f d_\alpha b)(r\cdot)
                                             \|_{H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-cap-annular-bound}
 Z_r(U)\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}r_0^{-8}(\|U\|_X+\|F\|_Y)
          +C\varepsilon^{-2}(\rho+\delta)R_1^6\|U\|_X.
\end{equation}
The norms are on fixed rescaled half-annuli. Fixed enlargements
are understood in applications of local estimates.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use the flat comparison and scalar-only correction $W$ in the proof
of Proposition~\ref{prop:nonflat-cap-localization}. The data $B_m$
of Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-flat-annuli} have square sum at most
$C(\|U\|_X+\|F\|_Y)$: the factor $r_0^3$ cancels that
comparison's local $r_0^{-3}$ bound. Its flat part therefore
satisfies the first term of \eqref{eq:gluing-cap-annular-bound}.
The scalar correction has $x_W=z_W=0$ and
\[
 \|W\|_X\leq CM_1L^{2\varepsilon}(\rho+\delta)\|U\|_X,
 \qquad \|\psi_W\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}}\leq CM_1\|W\|_X.
\]
Its potential has zero trace. The $C^1$ estimate and rescaling give
$\|\psi_W(r\cdot)\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}}
 \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}rM_1\|W\|_X$.
Its strong surface term in $Z_r$ is at most $Cr^{-2}\|W\|_X$,
and each weak term at most $Cr^{-1}\|W\|_X$.
Since $r\geq r_1$, $M_1\leq CR_1^2$ and $L\leq CR_1$,
these fit the second term of the asserted bound.

The restored coexact correction $S$, its harmonic part $h$, and
$\nu$ have zero attaching values and local $H^{2+\varepsilon}$
bounds $Cr_0^{-3}\|U\|_X$. Their strong annular norms are at
most $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}rr_0^{-5}\|U\|_X$.
Here $d_\alpha h=0$, while the surface derivative gain of $S$
bounds its weighted derivative by $C\rho r^{-3}\|U\|_X$.
The remaining surface term is
$T_\Sigma=\rho *_\Sigma[\beta-\alpha,\psi]$. Its global
strong and weak norms are bounded by $C\rho M_1\|U\|_X$
and $C\rho M_1L^\varepsilon\|U\|_X$. Restriction gives
$C\rho r^{-2}M_1\|U\|_X$ and
$C\rho r^{-1}M_1L^\varepsilon\|U\|_X$ in $Z_r$.
These also fit the printed $R_1^6$ loss. The restriction estimate
used here is $\|v(r\cdot)\|_{H^{j+\varepsilon}}
\leq Cr^{-1}\|v\|_{H^{j+\varepsilon}}$, $j=0,1$, on fixed
enlargements. If $v(0)=0$ and $v\in H^{2+\varepsilon}$, its
strong rescaled norm instead has the extra factor
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}r$. These follow from the value, derivative
and fractional scalings used in the preceding proof. All restored
terms have thus been included.
\end{proof}

The annular norm has a direct physical interpretation. At $|\zeta|\asymp r$
the physical base length is $\rho/r$, and the physical base coefficient
is $a/f$. Both its strong $H^{1+\varepsilon}_BH^k_\Sigma$ norm
and the weak exterior surface derivative norms of all negative
coefficients are at most
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-cap-annular-physical}
                            C\rho^{-\varepsilon}r^{1+\varepsilon}Z_r(U).
\end{equation}
For the weak rows the coefficient multiplier is $r^2/\rho$ and
the physical $H^\varepsilon$ scale is $(\rho/r)^{1-\varepsilon}$.
This controls $d_\alpha b$, not an additional full derivative of
its exact component. The stream commutator has the same physical
source bound. In fact the zero trace of $\psi$ and the fixed-annulus
Poincar\'e estimate give
\[
 \|\rho d_\alpha\psi(r\cdot)\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
                         \leq Cr Z_r(U).
\]
Consequently $c=b-\rho *_\Sigma d_\beta\psi$ has rescaled
strong norm at most $CrZ_r(U)$. The mixed commutator is
$(\bar\partial\chi)c$, the positive one is
$(\bar\partial\chi)\nu$, and the scalar one contains
$f^{-1}\Delta\chi\,\psi$ and $f^{-1}d\chi\,d\psi$.
Their rescaled source norms are bounded by $CZ_r(U)$; the extra
base-connection term is $O(\delta r)\psi$. On dyadic annuli the
physical bounds sum because
$\sum r_j^{1+\varepsilon}\leq Cr_{max}^{1+\varepsilon}$.
Smooth annular cutoffs before extension control the fractional
cross terms. The finitely many innermost annuli use
\eqref{eq:small-exponent-localization}.

\subsection{Residual homogeneous fields and the inner cutoff}

\begin{lemma}[residual homogeneous estimate]\label{lem:gluing-residual-homogeneous}
On the fixed Euclidean product collar, let $V=(a,b,\nu)$ solve
both the residual homogeneous geometric equation and full covariant
Coulomb equation on $D_{2\ell}$, with actual linear matching and
$a(n)=0$ on the seam. Here $a$ is a physical coefficient. There
is no condition on the semicircular outer arc. Put
$N_\ell=\ell\Lambda_\alpha$, with $\Lambda_\alpha$ the square
root of the flat surface Laplacian, zero on the harmonic and
positive factors. For each fixed $j,k$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-all-mass}
 \sum_{a+b\leq j}\|N_\ell^a V(\ell\cdot)\|_{H_B^bH^k_\Sigma(D_1)}
                \leq C_{j,k}\|V(\ell\cdot)\|_{L_B^2H^k_\Sigma(D_2)}.
\end{equation}
If $B_\varepsilon=\|V\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma(D_d)}$
on a fixed collar neighborhood, the right-hand side is at most
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}B_\varepsilon$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-residual-background}. After dilation by
$\ell$, the base connection is $O(\ell^2)$ and the surface
connection differs from $\alpha$ by $O(\ell)$. Fold the positive
side in a smooth complex frame based at the attaching value. The
polar unitary for its matching projector and the constant projector
at zero maps the constant seam condition to the actual one. Its
difference from $I$, with fixed rescaled base derivatives, gains
one surface derivative and is $O(\ell)$; this follows from
\eqref{eq:hodge-projection-derivative}. Extend it normally constantly
and transform the mixed target as well as the domain. Its commutation
with the folded complex structure preserves the base principal part.
Leave the base coefficients and the Coulomb and scalar targets unchanged.
The transformed operator is $D_{0,\ell}+K_\ell$. On each
nonharmonic surface mode, $D_{0,\ell}$ is
\[
 (a,x,y)\longmapsto(\delta_Za+mx,-\operatorname{curl}_Za+my,
                          d_Zx+*d_Zy-ma),\qquad m=\ell\lambda,
\]
with $a(n)=0$ and $y=0$. On the finite harmonic and positive
factors it is the constant Cauchy--Riemann transmission operator.

The remainder $K_\ell$ is surface order zero. A base derivative
of the polar map is $O(\ell)$. Both
$\ell d_\alpha(W-I)$ and $\ell(W^{-1}-I)d_\alpha$ are
order zero and $O(\ell^2)$ because $W-I$ gains one derivative.
The changes $d_\beta-d_\alpha$ and the rescaled base connection
are also $O(\ell^2)$; positive frame terms are $O(\ell)$.
The principal symbol of $\Lambda_\alpha$ is scalar, and finite
blocks have smooth surface vectors. Thus, for fixed orders,
\[
 \|\partial_Z^b\operatorname{ad}_{N_\ell}^aK_\ell
                  \|_{H^k_\Sigma\to H^k_\Sigma}\leq C_{a,b,k}\ell.
\]
These bounds also show that the polar maps preserve the norms in
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-all-mass}.

For a field $v$ supported away from the outer arc and satisfying
the constant seam condition, expansion of the flat first-order rows gives
\[
             \|d_Zv\|_2^2+\|N_\ell v\|_2^2
                                      =\|D_{0,\ell}v\|_2^2.
\]
The mass boundary terms vanish by $a(n)=y=0$; the base Hodge
boundary term vanishes on the straight seam. On the harmonic
part the symplectic pairing of the trace and its tangential derivative
vanishes because both lie in the fixed Lagrangian subspace. This
identity also holds with the surface Sobolev weights. A cutoff
on nested half-discs now gives
\[
       \|dv\|_{L^2(D')}+\|N_\ell v\|_{L^2(D')}
                                           \leq C\|v\|_{L^2(D'')}.
\]
Tangential differentiation and $N_\ell$ preserve the constant
boundary domain and commute with $D_{0,\ell}$. Their differentiated
equations have $-K_\ell$ times the differentiated field plus the
lower derivatives multiplied by the bounded commutators above.
Induction on nested discs adds one tangential, normal, or weighted
surface derivative. The coefficient of the normal derivative in
$D_{0,\ell}$ is invertible; solving the equation for that derivative
then recovers every higher normal order. This proves the asserted
integer estimates.

For weak fields, tangential difference quotients and
$(I+\tau N_\ell)^{-1}$ justify the differentiations. This
regularizer preserves the constant seam, and
$[R_\tau,K_\ell]=-\tau R_\tau[N_\ell,K_\ell]R_\tau$
bounds its error. Cutoff energy estimates, weak compactness and the
normal equation give the same induction. No higher-order compatibility
of an arbitrary forcing is used. Finally the fixed-base embedding
$H^{1+\varepsilon}\to C^0$ gives
$\|V(\ell\cdot)\|_{L^2(D_2)}\leq
 C\varepsilon^{-1/2}B_\varepsilon$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[based residual variation and stream cutoff]
\label{lem:gluing-residual-cutoff}
Let $V$ be a residual inverse output homogeneous on $D_{2\ell}$,
where $\ell\asymp\rho R_0$, and put
$\mathfrak b=C\varepsilon^{-1/2}B_\varepsilon$.
There is a compactly supported infinitesimal gauge change giving
$V^G$ with the same geometric source and attaching surface value.
On $D_{\ell/4}$ it satisfies ordinary base divergence zero,
normal trace zero, and $a^G(0)=0$. Write
\[
 e=E_\alpha b(0),\qquad h=H_\alpha b(0),\qquad
 P=P^E_e+P^H_h.
\]
Here $P^E_e$ is an exact geometric gauge field and $P^H_h$ is
a fixed smooth finite-dimensional matching profile with bounded
source. The field $V^0=V^G-P$ has all three attaching values
zero and can be written $a^0=*d\psi$, $\psi|_{\rm seam}=0$.
For $r=\rho R_-$, $q_0=r/\ell$, and $\psi(\ell Z)=\ell\Psi(Z)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-vanishing}
 \begin{split}
 \|V^0(r\cdot)\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
 +\ell\|V^0(r\cdot)\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^{k+1}_\Sigma}
                                                   &\leq Cq_0\mathfrak b,\\
 \|\Psi(q_0\cdot)\|_{H_B^{2+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
 +\ell\|\Psi(q_0\cdot)\|_{H_B^{2+\varepsilon}H^{k+1}_\Sigma}
                                                   &\leq Cq_0^2\mathfrak b.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Let $\chi_-$ vanish on $D_r$ and equal one outside $D_{2r}$,
and set $\mathcal C_-V^0=(*d(\chi_-\psi),\chi_-b^0,\chi_-\nu^0)$.
The commutator
$E_-=D_0^{\rm geo}\mathcal C_-V^0-\chi_-D_0^{\rm geo}V^0$
has cap source norm at most
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-stream-error}
             \|E_-\|_{Y_{\rm cap}}
                      \leq CR_-^\varepsilon(R_-/R_0)\mathfrak b.
\end{equation}
The separate inner profile term obeys
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-profile-source}
       \|(1-\chi_-)D_0^{\rm geo}P^H_h\|_{Y_{\rm cap}}
                        \leq C\rho R_-^{1+\varepsilon}\mathfrak b.
\end{equation}
These source norms include the physical-to-cap source multiplier.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The preceding lemma and the flat spectral gap give, for all fixed
orders needed here,
\[
        \ell^q\|V(\ell\cdot)\|_{H_B^jH^{k+q}_\Sigma(D_1)}
                                                   \leq C_{j,q}\mathfrak b.
\]
Put $B(Z)=\ell A_B(\ell Z)$ and seek a physical gauge potential
$\sigma(z)=\ell\xi(z/\ell)$. The transformed base coefficient
is $a(\ell Z)+d_Z\xi+[B,\xi]$. Linearized flatness on the
seam and the homogeneous scalar ASD row imply
$\partial_Ta_S=0$ there; $B_S$ satisfies the same condition.
Reflect $a_S,B_S$ evenly and $a_T,B_T$ oddly. This has the
$C^{1,\gamma}$ regularity needed for a full-disc Dirichlet solve.
Write $\xi=Sc+h$ with $h=0$ on the unit circle, and solve
\[
 \delta_Z(d_Zh+[B,h])=-\delta_Za(\ell Z)-\delta_Z[B,Sc].
\]
The operator is a small first-order perturbation of $\Delta_D$.
Reflection and uniqueness give $\partial_T\xi=0$. The map
from $c$ to the tangential value of
$d_Z(Sc+h_c)+[B,Sc+h_c]$ at zero is $I+O(\ell^2)$.
Choose $c$ to cancel the attaching base value, then replace
$\xi$ by $\xi-\xi(0)$. Since $\delta B=0$, $B(n)=0$ and
$B(0)=0$, this preserves all the conditions. Subtraction as a
whole surface section also preserves $b(0)$.

One-sided Neumann regularity on the original smooth half-disc,
followed by surface differentiation, gives
$\ell^q\|\xi\|_{H_B^{j+1}H^{k+q}_\Sigma}\leq C_{j,q}\mathfrak b$
on a smaller disc. Cut the potential off radially inside $D_{\ell/2}$,
with cutoff one on $D_{\ell/4}$, and set
$V^G=V+(d_A\sigma,0)$. Its geometric source is unchanged by
gauge covariance, including the perturbations. Normal trace is
preserved. Rescaling the strong coefficients and using $q=1,2$
for their extra surface derivatives gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-gauge-bound}
 \|V^G-V\|_{X^{\rm res}_{\varepsilon,k}}
                                        \leq C\ell^{-\varepsilon}\mathfrak b.
\end{equation}
The support lies where the collar cutoff is one. There the complete
residual norm is controlled by the strong base and full extra surface
norms used in this calculation. In particular the second surface
derivative of the potential is estimated from the homogeneous output;
it is not required of an arbitrary cap field.

With $\zeta=G^0_\alpha d_\alpha^*e$, use
$P^E_e=([A_B,\zeta],d_\beta\zeta,0)$ locally. Extending it
by cutting off the potential gives a global exact gauge field.
For $P^H_h=(0,b_h,\nu_h)$ extend $\ell_\alpha^{-1}h$ with
constant coordinates in a smooth positive tangent frame, set
$b_h=\ell_{\beta(s)}\nu_h$ on the seam, and extend smoothly
into the negative side. A fixed cutoff gives an actual global
matching field with
\[
                  \|D_0^{\rm geo}P^H_h\|_{C_B^jH^k_\Sigma}
                                                    \leq C_{j,k}|h|.
\]
It need not be homogeneous. Both profiles have zero base attaching
value and preserve ordinary base divergence. Thus $V^0$ has all
three values zero and $D_0^{\rm geo}V^0=-D_0^{\rm geo}P^H_h$
on the local homogeneous region. All its fixed rescaled base and
weighted surface norms remain bounded by $C\mathfrak b$.
The stream function exists because ordinary base divergence is zero;
its seam trace is constant, taken to be zero. Its value and first
base derivatives vanish at zero. Taylor's formula with the fixed
higher rescaled estimates gives \eqref{eq:gluing-residual-vanishing},
also after surface differentiation.

The added stream coefficient $\psi*d\chi_-$ has rescaled size
$Cq_0\mathfrak b$. Hence
\[
 \|\mathcal C_-V^0-V^0\|_{X_{\rm phys}}
                                      \leq Cr^{-\varepsilon}q_0\mathfrak b.
\]
The weak exterior derivative terms have an additional factor
$r/\ell$. In the scalar commutator the terms
$\Delta\chi_-\,\psi$ and $d\chi_-\,d\psi$ have rescaled
$H^\varepsilon$ size $C\mathfrak b/\ell$.
The base-connection bracket is smaller. In the mixed row use
$(d\chi_-)(b^0-*_\Sigma d_\beta\psi)$:
$b^0$ is $O(q_0\mathfrak b)$ and
$d_\alpha\psi(r\cdot)=O(q_0^2\mathfrak b)$ by
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-vanishing}. The coefficient
$\beta-\alpha=O(r)$ bounds its remainder. The positive row
has the same estimate. Thus $E_-$ has physical rescaled
$H^\varepsilon$ size $C\mathfrak b/\ell$.
At this annulus $|\zeta|\asymp R_-^{-1}$, the cap source
multiplier is $\rho f\asymp\rho R_-^2$, and the compact-base
$H^\varepsilon$ scale is $R_-^{\varepsilon-1}$. Their product
proves \eqref{eq:gluing-residual-stream-error}.

The bounded source of $P^H_h$ must be retained over the whole
inner disc. On a compact-coordinate annulus of radius $v\geq R_-^{-1}$,
its transformed rescaled coefficient has size
$C\rho v^{-2}\mathfrak b$, with norm
$C\rho v^{-1-\varepsilon}\mathfrak b$. The dyadic sum is
dominated by $v\asymp R_-^{-1}$; the remaining fixed cap
region contributes $C\rho\mathfrak b$. This proves
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-profile-source}. The true capped
region at $R_1^{-1}$ is not involved.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Conversion of complete sources and graph fields}

\begin{lemma}[fractional coordinate conversion]\label{lem:gluing-coordinate-conversion}
Let $z=c+\rho\Phi(\zeta)$ and $f_\infty=|d\Phi|_{g_B}$.
For $R\leq c_1R_1$ with $\rho R^2\leq1$, choose a cutoff
$\chi_R$ zero for $|\zeta|\leq R^{-1}$ and one for
$|\zeta|\geq2R^{-1}$. The source conversions
\[
 S_{\rho,R}F=\chi_R\rho f_\infty O F\circ(c+\rho\Phi),
 \qquad
 T_{\rho,R}G=\left(\frac{\chi_R}{\rho f_\infty}O^{-1}G\right)
                                      \circ\Phi^{-1}((z-c)/\rho)
\]
satisfy, for $0\leq\varepsilon\leq1/4$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-source-conversion}
 \|S_{\rho,R}F\|_{H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}
               \leq CR^\varepsilon\|F\|_{H_{B,\rm phys}^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma},
 \qquad
 \|T_{\rho,R}G\|_{H_{B,\rm phys}^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}
               \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}\|G\|_{H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}.
\end{equation}
For a physical field with strong $H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma$
coefficients and exterior surface derivatives in $H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma$,
its cutoff cap conversion satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-field-conversion}
 \|C_\rho(\chi_RV)\|_{X^{\rm raw}_{\rm cap}}
              \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}R^\varepsilon\|V\|_{X_{\rm phys}}.
\end{equation}
Here cutoffs in the two coordinate systems are identified by pullback.
The surface frame is the product frame and the positive frames are
smooth up to the compactifying point. The source rotations $O$
include the conformal phase. Inverse identities require nested cutoffs
and data supported where those cutoffs are one.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The physical base volume is $\rho^2f_\infty^2$ times the
compact volume, so the uncut source maps are inverse $L^2$
isometries, up to the fixed bounded surface frames. Near infinity,
\[
 f_\infty\asymp|\zeta|^{-2},\qquad
 |d\log f_\infty|+|dO|\leq C|\zeta|^{-1},\qquad
 |df_\infty|/f_\infty^2\leq C.
\]
The forward $H^1$ estimate is
\[
 \|S_{\rho,R}F\|_{H^1}
       \leq C(R\|F\|_2+\rho R^2\|F\|_{H^1})\leq CR\|F\|_{H^1}.
\]
For the reverse derivative, change volume first. The resulting
compact $L^2$ expression is
\[
 \frac1{\rho f_\infty}
       \bigl(d(\chi_RO^{-1}G)-\chi_RO^{-1}G\,d\log f_\infty\bigr).
\]
All coefficient factors divided by $f_\infty$ are bounded,
and $|d\chi_R|/f_\infty\leq C/R$. Hence its $H^1$ norm
is at most $C\rho^{-1}\|G\|_{H^1}$, with no $R$ loss.
Interpolating the same maps with their $L^2$ bounds proves
\eqref{eq:gluing-source-conversion}. Fixed half-domain extensions
are used at the geometric seam; zero extensions cross only the
artificial boundaries where the smooth cutoffs vanish.

For the surface and positive coefficients the compact $L^2$
pullback norm is controlled by the physical $C^0$ norm, with
constant $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}$. Derivatives of the pullback
are the forward source transforms of physical base derivatives.
On a compact annulus of radius $v$, conformal invariance of the
two-dimensional Dirichlet integral gives
\[
 \|V(c+\rho\Phi(v\cdot))\|_{H^1}
       \leq C(\|V\|_{C^0}+\|d_BV\|_2)
       \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|V\|_{X_{\rm phys}}.
\]
For the cutoff derivative, its size $v^{-1}$ cancels the
$L^2$ scale $v$, leaving $v^{-\varepsilon}$.
There is no rotating surface frame. Smooth cap and physical positive
frames have derivative terms whose rescaled $C^1$ bound is
$C(1+\rho/v^2)$, bounded because $v\geq R^{-1}$ and
$\rho R^2\leq1$. Their fractional contributions sum as
$v^{1-\varepsilon}$. Thus no angular phase with derivative
$v^{-1}$ on every annulus is used for these strong coefficients.

The base coefficient is $f_\infty O_a a_{\rm phys}/M_1$.
Its differentiated physical field is a source transform with an
additional factor $f_\infty/M_1$, bounded on the retained support.
The endpoint calculation still gives constants $C$ and $CR$.
The derivatives of $f_\infty$, the cutoff and the base phase
have annular $H^\varepsilon$ bound
\[
          \frac{C\varepsilon^{-1/2}}{M_1v^{2+\varepsilon}}
                                                   \|V\|_{X_{\rm phys}}.
\]
Their geometric sum is at most
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}R^{2+\varepsilon}M_1^{-1}\|V\|$,
which fits \eqref{eq:gluing-field-conversion} since $R^2\leq CM_1$.
Finally the weak graph rows are source transforms of the physical
exterior derivatives. A fixed smooth surface-dependent identification
adds bounded zeroth-order terms with the same estimate. No surface
divergence of $b$ is introduced.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Matching transports and the geometric right inverse}

On an overlap annulus $|\zeta|\asymp r$, $R_1^{-1}\lesssim r
\lesssim R_-^{-1}$, put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-overlap-coefficients}
                    q(r)=r+\rho/r+\delta,\qquad
                    \eta(r)=\delta+\rho^2/r^3.
\end{equation}
In the fixed surface and positive frames, the cap, residual and
preglued surface connections and positive values differ from the
attaching values by $O(q(r))$. Their compact base coefficients
and differences are $O(\eta(r))$. These bounds include every
fixed rescaled base and surface derivative needed below. For the
residual base coefficient this follows from $A_B(z)=O(|z|)$
and $z=c+\rho/\zeta$. The smooth interpolation has the same
bounds by the chain rule, with affine interpolation in the base
connection. For sufficiently small $\rho$,
$\rho R_1^2+\delta R_1\leq c$, so $q(r)\leq Cr$.

\begin{lemma}[paired matching transports]\label{lem:gluing-paired-transports}
There are paired domain and target maps $T^X_{ji},T^Y_{ji}$
between either model background $i$ and the preglued background $j$,
on the supports used below, with the following properties. They
preserve the actual seam and negative normal condition, fix the base
component and scalar target, and satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-transport-graph}
 \|T^X_{ji}U\|_{X^{\rm raw}_{\rm cap}}
                 \leq CR_1^\varepsilon\|U\|_{X^{\rm raw}_{\rm cap}}.
\end{equation}
Their paired error $K_{ji}=\mathcal A_jT^X_{ji}-T^Y_{ji}\mathcal A_i$
is surface order zero and obeys
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-paired-error}
 \|K_{ji}U(r\cdot)\|_{H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}
                       \leq C\|(a/f,b,\nu)(r\cdot)
                                                   \|_{H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}.
\end{equation}
All assertions are on the retained Euclidean annuli. The maps are
identity where the backgrounds agree.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use the smooth local flat retraction of the surface-connection chart
to define $\beta^{\rm flat}$ throughout each annulus. It equals
$\beta$ on the seam. In the fixed complex positive fiber $V$,
extend the matching projector by
\[
 P=\begin{pmatrix}
 E_{\beta^{\rm flat}}+\tfrac12H_{\beta^{\rm flat}}
                         &\tfrac12\ell_{\beta^{\rm flat}}\\
 \tfrac12\ell_{\beta^{\rm flat}}^*&\tfrac12 I
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
It is orthogonal and $JPJ^{-1}=I-P$ for the folded complex
structure $J=(*_\Sigma,-J_V)$. Its interior extension is only
an extension of the tangent projector; no interior matching is
asserted. By \eqref{eq:hodge-projection-derivative}, $P_j-P_i$
gains one surface derivative, with norm $Cq(r)$ and the same
fixed rescaled derivative bounds, also one surface order lower.
The finite blocks have smooth surface vectors. Set
\[
 A_{ji}=P_jP_i+(I-P_j)(I-P_i),\qquad
 W_{ji}=A_{ji}\bigl(I-(P_j-P_i)^2\bigr)^{-1/2}.
\]
The convergent power series and the projector identities show that
$W$ is unitary, commutes with $J$, and maps $\operatorname{ran}P_i$
to $\operatorname{ran}P_j$. Every term of $W-I$ contains
$P_j-P_i$, so it has the same one-derivative gain and $Cq(r)$
bound. Apply $W$ to $(b,\nu)$ in the domain and to the folded
mixed and positive target rows, leaving the other components fixed.
The cap map is used on the localized support $|w|\leq R_1/4$;
its logarithm can be cut to zero nearer infinity, where that field
vanishes. The residual map is used only for $|w|\geq R_-$,
and is similarly extended where its input vanishes. These extensions
remain complex unitary and have the same derivative bounds on their
relevant supports.

The first compact base derivatives of $W$ are bounded by $C$,
and the second by $CR_1$, since $q/r\leq C$ and $q/r^2\leq CR_1$.
Integer multiplier estimates and interpolation give
$\|Wv\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_BH^k_\Sigma}
\leq CR_1^\varepsilon\|v\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_BH^k_\Sigma}$.
On $H^\varepsilon_B$ the multiplier norm is $C$.
The extra weak surface graph term contains
$\rho f d_\alpha\pi_\Sigma(W-I)(b,\nu)$, an order-zero
operator of size $C\rho/r$ and first compact base derivative
$C\rho/r^2$. Both are bounded. This proves
\eqref{eq:gluing-transport-graph} without a surface divergence
of $b$.

The normalized principal geometric rows on these annuli are
\[
 -f^{-1}\operatorname{curl}_B a-\rho f *_\Sigma d_{\beta_i}b,
 \qquad
 \bar\partial_Bb-\rho\bar\partial_{\beta_i}a+[A_{B,i}^{0,1},b],
 \qquad \bar\partial_B\nu+\Omega_i\nu,
\]
with the scalar bracket $f^{-1}*[A_{B,i}\wedge a]$ included.
The positive connection coefficient $\Omega_i$ is $O(q/r)$.
Pairing the domain and target maps cancels derivatives of the input
in the base principal part. In the scalar row the surface difference
has order zero and size $C\rho q/r^2$ on $(b,\nu)$; the
base bracket difference has size $C\eta$ on $a/f$.
The mixed rows contain $\bar\partial W$, of size $Cq/r$,
base brackets of size $C\eta$, and
$\rho(d_{\beta_j}-Wd_{\beta_i})a$.
The last term is surface order zero because $(W-I)d_\alpha$
is order zero, using the gain one order lower; its size on $a/f$
is $C\rho q/r^2$. Thus the right-hand coefficient before
simplification is
$C(q/r+\eta+\rho q/r^2)$, bounded under the stated assumptions.
It and its first rescaled derivative are bounded, which proves
\eqref{eq:gluing-paired-error} by interpolation.
\end{proof}

Two consequences will be used in the construction. For the localized
zero-attaching cap output $V=\mathcal C_1U$, set
\[
 Z_* =C\varepsilon^{-2}R_0^8(\|U\|_X+\|F_{\rm cap}\|_Y)
             +C\varepsilon^{-2}(\rho+\delta)R_1^6\|U\|_X.
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-cap-annuli} gives
$\|(a_V/f,b_V,\nu_V)(r\cdot)\|_{H^\varepsilon}\leq CrZ_*$.
On the finitely many retained annuli adjacent to $r_1$, the
rescaled proof of \eqref{eq:small-exponent-localization} gives
the same bound, with the same restored terms. In particular the
extra stream term is $f^{-1}\psi*d\chi=O(r_1)\psi(r_1\cdot)$.
The cap-to-preglued difference is supported in $r\lesssim R_2^{-1}$.
Using a smooth annular partition and summing $r^{2-\varepsilon}$
in \eqref{eq:gluing-paired-error}, then using
\eqref{eq:gluing-source-conversion}, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-cap-background-source}
                 \|K_{{\rm pre},{\rm cap}}V\|_{Y_\rho}
                         \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}R_2^{-2+\varepsilon}Z_*.
\end{equation}
For a residual field whose physical coefficient triple has rescaled
$H^1_BH^k_\Sigma$ bound $\mathfrak b$ on the relevant annuli,
the corresponding sum is instead $\sum r^{1-\varepsilon}$,
giving
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-background-source}
                 \|K_{{\rm pre},{\rm res}}V\|_{Y_\rho}
                    \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}R_-^{-1+\varepsilon}\mathfrak b.
\end{equation}
Both sums are uniform as $\varepsilon$ decreases. The rescaled
$H^1$ bound for an unmodified residual field follows from its
fixed-base $C^0$ bound and conformal invariance of the Dirichlet
integral, and is at most
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|V\|_{X^{\rm res}_{\varepsilon,k}}$.
Annular cutoffs are applied before extension, so these estimates
are in the complete fractional source spaces.

\begin{proposition}[geometric inverse on the complete source]
\label{prop:gluing-geometric-inverse}
At the radii \eqref{eq:gluing-four-radii}, the actual geometric
linearization $D_\rho:X_\rho\to Y_\rho$ admits a right inverse
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-geometric-inverse}
                         D_\rho Q_\rho=I,\qquad \|Q_\rho\|\leq CT^2.
\end{equation}
The finite residual end parameters are retained. This is an inverse
for geometric sources on the domain without a global Coulomb equation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $\theta_0$ be one on $|z-c|\leq\rho R_0$ and zero
outside $|z-c|\leq2\rho R_0$. For arbitrary $F\in Y_\rho$
put $F_{\rm in}=\theta_0F$ and $F_{\rm out}=(1-\theta_0)F$.
Convert the inner source to $G_{\rm cap}$, treating partition
and conversion as the single cutoff source map of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-coordinate-conversion}. Then
\[
             \|G_{\rm cap}\|_{Y_{\rm cap}}
                                  \leq CR_0^\varepsilon\|F\|_{Y_\rho}.
\]
It vanishes near infinity on a half-disc of radius comparable to
$R_0^{-1}$. The backgrounds agree on its support. Define
$G_{\rm res}=(T^Y_{{\rm pre},{\rm res}})^{-1}F_{\rm out}$,
with the zero Coulomb row inserted. This is a bounded source map.
Indeed the source partition has norm $C(\rho R_0)^{-\varepsilon}$.
On the outer source support the target map's difference from identity
has physical coefficient size $C/R_0$ and first base derivative
$C/(\rho R_0^2)$. Extend that difference by a cutoff inside
the vanishing source region. Interpolation gives
\[
 \|((T^Y)^{-1}-I)(1-\theta_0)F\|_{Y^{\rm res}_{\varepsilon,k}}
             \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}R_0^{-1-\varepsilon}\|F\|_{Y_\rho}.
\]
The integer surface source terms add no base derivative. The local
product norm controls the lower isotropic term, proving
$\|G_{\rm res}\|\leq C\|F\|$. This source is zero on
$D_{\rho R_0}$.

For the cap block take $U_{\rm cap}=Q^0G_{\rm cap}$ with
both attaching values zero, and localize by $\mathcal C_1$.
After conversion and paired transport, call its output $P_{\rm cap}F$.
It is supported where $\theta_1=1$. Set
\[
 L_{\rm cut}=C\left(\varepsilon^{-3}R_0^8R_1^{-1+2\varepsilon}
                 +\varepsilon^{-1}(\rho+\delta)R_1^{6+\varepsilon}\right).
\]
The inverse and localization estimates, together with
\eqref{eq:gluing-cap-background-source}, give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-cap-block-error}
 \|D_\rho P_{\rm cap}F-F_{\rm in}\|_{Y_\rho}
 \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}
       \left(L_{\rm cut}\|G_{\rm cap}\|_Y
                                  +R_2^{-2+\varepsilon}Z_*\right).
\end{equation}
Once the displayed localization error is small, its domain norm is
at most $C\varepsilon^{-1}R_1^{2\varepsilon}R_0^\varepsilon\|F\|$.
This smallness is checked below. No condition on the support of the
new error is imposed on the eventual inverse.

Apply the fixed residual inverse to $G_{\rm res}$ and call the
output $U$. Choose $\ell=c_0\rho R_0$ so that $D_{2\ell}$
is contained in the homogeneous region. Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-residual-cutoff}
applies with $\mathfrak b=C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|F\|$ and gives
$U^G$, the residual profiles $P=P^E_e+P^H_h$, and the stream
function of $U^0=U^G-P$. Let $B=\mathcal B_\rho(e,h)$ be
the true cap kernel field. Write $E=\|e\|_{H^k}+|h|\leq C\mathfrak b$,
$r=\rho R_-$, and $\chi=\chi_-$. On the cutoff annulus put
\[
 V=\chi U^G+(\psi*d\chi,0,0)=\mathcal C_-U^0+\chi P,
 \qquad
 P_{\rm res}F=T^X_{{\rm pre},{\rm res}}V+(1-\chi)B.
\]
Inside $D_r$ define it to be $B$, and outside $D_{2r}$ it
is the transported $U^G$. These definitions agree where the
cutoffs are constant. The residual transport is used only where
its input vanishes towards the inner core. The cap profile is
supported where the pregluing is exactly the cap. Thus the actual
seam, normal condition and all end parameters are preserved.

We record the source identity explicitly. Since
$D_0^{\rm geo}U^0=G_{\rm res}-D_0^{\rm geo}P$ and
$\chi G_{\rm res}=G_{\rm res}$,
\[
 D_0^{\rm geo}V=G_{\rm res}+E_-+[D_0^{\rm geo},\chi]P.
\]
The terms $\chi D_0^{\rm geo}P$ cancel, including the possibly
nonzero source of $P^H_h$. The paired base symbols imply
$T^Y[D_0^{\rm geo},\chi]P=[D_\rho,\chi]T^XP$.
Since $D_\rho B=0$ on the support of $1-\chi$, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-block-identity}
 D_\rho P_{\rm res}F-F_{\rm out}
       =T^YE_-+K_{{\rm pre},{\rm res}}V
                          +[D_\rho,\chi](T^XP-B).
\end{equation}
The first term is the stream commutator of
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-stream-error}, not the difference
relative to the uncut source. The source of the harmonic residual
profile has been canceled, not discarded.

The field $V$ has rescaled physical $H^1_BH^k_\Sigma$ size
$C\mathfrak b$ on all relevant annuli. This holds for $U$
by its $C^0$ bound and Dirichlet integral. For its gauge correction,
the fixed rescaled homogeneous estimates at scale $\ell$,
including one extra surface order, give the same bound on smaller
annuli. The extra stream coefficient on the $r$ annulus has
strong rescaled size $Cq_0\mathfrak b$ by
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-vanishing}. Hence
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-background-source} applies without
another point-evaluation loss.

At the $r$ annulus write $r_-=R_-^{-1}$ for its compact radius.
The cap profile differs from its surface and positive attaching
values by $Cr_-E$, and its physical base coefficient has size
$C(\delta/\rho)r_-^2E$. For the exact part this is the formula
in Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-cap-profiles}; for the harmonic part it
is the finite parameter bound \eqref{eq:cap-kernel-parameter-bounds}.
The residual profiles differ from their attaching values by
$C(\rho/r_-)E$ and have base coefficient of that same size.
The transport adds $Cq(r_-)E$ in the surface and positive fields.
All these bounds hold in rescaled $H^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma$.
The commutator $[D_\rho,\chi]$ is $O(r_-^{-1})$ on the
surface and positive coefficients and on the physical base coefficient.
Compact scaling and source conversion therefore give
\[
 \|[D_\rho,\chi](T^XP-B)\|_{Y_\rho}
 \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}
       \left(R_-^{-1+\varepsilon}+\rho R_-^{1+\varepsilon}
                    +\frac\delta\rho R_-^{-2+\varepsilon}\right)E.
\]
The intermediate $\delta R_-^\varepsilon$ term is absorbed
using $\delta R_1\leq c$. Combining these estimates yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-residual-block-error}
 \begin{split}
 \|D_\rho P_{\rm res}F-F_{\rm out}\|_{Y_\rho}
 \leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{-1/2}
 \left(R_-^\varepsilon\frac{R_-}{R_0}+R_-^{-1+\varepsilon}
       +\rho R_-^{1+\varepsilon}
       +\frac\delta\rho R_-^{-2+\varepsilon}\right)\|F\|_{Y_\rho}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}

We also check the complete domain bound. The outer factor of $X_\rho$
sees only $(1-\theta_1)U$, with norm at most
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}(\rho R_1)^{-\varepsilon}\|F\|$.
On the raw cap factor, convert $\theta_1U^G$ using
\eqref{eq:gluing-field-conversion}, cut by $\chi$, and apply
\eqref{eq:gluing-transport-graph}. A scalar strong cutoff at
radius $R_-^{-1}$ has norm $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_-^\varepsilon$;
its weak multiplier norm is smaller. Together with
\eqref{eq:gluing-residual-gauge-bound}, this contribution is at
most $C\varepsilon^{-3/2}\ell^{-\varepsilon}
R_1^{2\varepsilon}R_-^\varepsilon\|F\|$, plus a lower-order term.
The extra stream coefficient has strong raw norm at most
\[
 C\frac{R_-^2}{M_1}R_-^\varepsilon q_0\mathfrak b
                      \leq CR_-^\varepsilon q_0\mathfrak b,
\]
and weak exterior derivative norm at most
$CR_-^\varepsilon q_0^2\mathfrak b$.
For the latter, multiply its rescaled full surface derivative
$q_0\mathfrak b/\ell$ by $\rho R_-^2$ and by the compact
$H^\varepsilon$ scale $R_-^{\varepsilon-1}$.
The retained cap profile has norm
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_-^\varepsilon E$ and lies entirely
where $\theta_1=1$. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-parametrix-bound}
 \|P_{\rm res}F\|_{X_\rho}
       \leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\rho^{-2\varepsilon}R_1^{4\varepsilon}
                                                       \|F\|_{Y_\rho}.
\end{equation}
Every operation was defined for arbitrary complete sources using the
weak homogeneous estimates; no density assertion for a restricted
source class is needed.

Set $P_\rho=P_{\rm cap}+P_{\rm res}$. To check the powers,
write $R_-=T^a,R_0=T^b,R_2=T^c,R_1=T^d$ temporarily.
The nonexponential cap errors in \eqref{eq:gluing-cap-block-error}
have exponents $3+8b-d$ and $3+8b-2c$.
The residual errors have exponents $1/2+a-b$ and $1/2-a$.
With $(a,b,c,d)=(4,8,40,80)$ these are $-13,-13,-7/2,-7/2$.
All factors $R_j^{C\varepsilon}$ and $\rho^{-C\varepsilon}$
are bounded. The fixed-order reconstruction estimate
\eqref{eq:gluing-cap-background-error} makes every term containing
$\rho$ or $\delta/\rho$ exponentially small times a fixed
power of $T$. It also verifies all inverse criteria and support
separations. Therefore
\[
 \|D_\rho P_\rho-I\|_{Y_\rho\to Y_\rho}
             \leq CT^{-7/2}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}<\tfrac12,
 \qquad \|P_\rho\|\leq CT^2.
\]
The Neumann series on the complete source space defines
$Q_\rho=P_\rho(D_\rho P_\rho)^{-1}$ and proves
\eqref{eq:gluing-geometric-inverse}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The error of the exact-matching pregluing}

\begin{lemma}[pregluing error]\label{lem:gluing-pregluing-error}
Let $\mathcal F$ denote the geometric equation, with the target
identifications defining $Y_\rho$. Its pregluing error satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-pregluing-error}
 \begin{split}
 e_\rho:=\|\mathcal F(x_{\rm pre})\|_{Y_\rho}
 &\leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}
     \left(R_2^{-1+\varepsilon}+\rho R_2^{1+\varepsilon}
                    +\frac\delta\rho R_2^{-2+\varepsilon}\right)\\
 &\leq CT^{-40}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
It is supported on the single interpolation annulus.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Both backgrounds solve the exact same physical equations where they
are used, and all perturbation outputs and dependencies are disjoint
from the modification. Put $v=R_2^{-1}$ and rescale the transition
by $\zeta=vZ$. The surface and positive differences have size
$q=v+\rho/v+\delta\leq Cv$, and the compact base connection
difference has size $\eta=\delta+\rho^2/v^3$.
These are background coefficients $A_B$, not the normalized
variation $a$. Interpolation in the smooth matching chart differs
from affine interpolation of the surface fields by a remainder
$S$ of size $Cq^2$ in all fixed rescaled base and surface orders.
Taylor's integral formula contains two factors of the coordinate
difference; differentiating retains two such factors because their
fixed derivatives also have size $O(q)$. The positive interpolation
has the same quadratic property.

The normalized scalar background equation is
\[
                    (\rho f)^{-1}*_BF_B+\rho f *_\Sigma F_\beta.
\]
Subtract the affine combination of the two exact equations. The
base cutoff curvature has size $C\eta/v$, its quadratic bracket
has size $C\eta^2$, and the surface curvature and chart terms
have size $Cq^2$. Thus the normalized rescaled scalar source is
at most
\[
 C\left(\frac v\rho\eta+\frac{v^2}\rho\eta^2
                         +\frac\rho{v^2}q^2\right)
              \leq C\left(\frac\delta\rho v+\frac\rho{v^2}\right).
\]
For the quadratic base terms this uses
$\delta^2v^2/\rho$, $2\delta\rho/v$ and $\rho^3/v^4$,
absorbed using $\delta v\leq c$ and $\rho/v\leq c$.
The surface term is $O(\rho)$. Compact $H^\varepsilon$ scaling
multiplies by $v^{1-\varepsilon}$ and gives
$C((\delta/\rho)v^{2-\varepsilon}+\rho v^{-1-\varepsilon})$.

The compact mixed background equation is
$\bar\partial_B\beta-\bar\partial_\beta A_B^{0,1}=0$,
with no additional $\rho$ factor. Its affine cutoff term is
$Cq/v$, its bracket difference is $C\eta q$, and the chart
terms are $Cq^2/v+C\eta q^2$. The positive equation in a
fixed smooth coordinate chart has the same leading cutoff term
and remainder $Cq^2/v$. Since $q\leq Cv$ and $\eta$ is
bounded, these rows have rescaled source norm $C$, hence compact
norm $Cv^{1-\varepsilon}$. One rescaled derivative has the same
bounds, so the fractional estimates are uniform by interpolation.
A smooth cutoff before extension includes the fractional cross terms.
Finally \eqref{eq:gluing-source-conversion} contributes only
$C\rho^{-\varepsilon}$. Substitution of the four radii and
\eqref{eq:gluing-cap-background-error} proves the result.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Holonomy at the fractional graph order}

We specify the primary perturbation class before selecting the regular
residual solution. Enumerate DFL's cylinder functions by $h_i$, with
original weights $C_i$. Let $D_i$ dominate the fixed finite-family
constants in the fractional estimates proved below. For each $m$,
let $E_{i,m}$ dominate the defining loop, frame, coarea and
class-function constants needed through order $m$ on $W^{m,6}$,
and through order three in the coarea exponents between three and
six. Smooth-background bounds can be factored out of these constants.
Set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-primary-weights}
 C_i^\infty=\max\left(C_i,D_i,
                     2^i\max_{1\leq m\leq i}E_{i,m}\right),
                 \qquad \sum_i C_i^\infty|a_i|<\infty.
\end{equation}
For every fixed $m$ the tail constants obey
$E_{i,m}\leq2^{-i}C_i^\infty$, and the head is finite.
Thus the original DFL estimates and every fixed required higher
estimate converge. This is an explicit strengthened choice of weights,
not a consequence inferred from unspecified weights in
\cite[Proposition~6.1]{DaeFukLip21a}. Every finite cylinder
direction remains available and their span is dense. DFL's
cokernel-separation argument uses those finite directions, so its
universal surjectivity and Sard--Smale argument apply in this weighted
space as well. Finite cylinder additions preserve the condition.

\begin{lemma}[fractional holonomy estimate]\label{lem:gluing-holonomy-estimate}
For the finite secondary perturbations and primary series
\eqref{eq:gluing-primary-weights}, at a fixed smooth background set
\[
 R(v)=\mathcal H(A_0+v)-\mathcal H(A_0)-D\mathcal H(A_0)v.
\]
For compactly supported or weighted-decaying variations,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-holonomy-remainder}
 \|R(v)-R(w)\|_{Y^{\rm res}_{\varepsilon,k}}
 \leq C\varepsilon^{-1}
       (\|v\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_\omega}+\|w\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_\omega})
                                      \|v-w\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_\omega}.
\end{equation}
For each fixed positive $\varepsilon\leq1/4$, the perturbation
map is $C^1$ from this connection space to the full residual source
space. Constant finite end parameters are incorporated by exact end
families, as in the next subsection.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a secondary family $\gamma:S^1\times D^4\to X$, define
$(Pv)(z)(t)=v_{\gamma(t,z)}(\partial_t\gamma)$, including a
compact auxiliary output cutoff. In local bundle frames its path space
is $\mathcal E=L^1(S^1;\mathfrak{su}(2))$; a smooth gluing
matrix at the endpoints is included in the output function.
H\"older in $t$ and coarea for the full submersion give
\[
       \|Pv\|_{L_z^4\mathcal E}\leq C\|v\|_{L_X^4},\qquad
       \|Pv\|_{W_z^{1,4}\mathcal E}\leq C\|v\|_{W_X^{1,4}}.
\]
Differentiation hits $v$ or fixed smooth loop coefficients. In
particular $P:H^1\to L^4\mathcal E$ and
$P:H^2\to W^{1,4}\mathcal E$. Fubini gives an $L^1$ path
for almost every output point. Coarea also makes the definition
independent of the representative of $v$. No trace on each individual
rough loop is asserted.

For a skew-Hermitian $L^1$ path $a$, parallel transport is the
unique absolutely continuous solution of its integral equation and
is unitary. Ordered Duhamel integrals, first for smooth paths and
then by $L^1$ approximation, give
\[
 \|D^m\operatorname{Hol}(a)[h_1,\ldots,h_m]\|
                                      \leq C_m\prod_j\|h_j\|_{L^1}.
\]
The constants are independent of $a$. Smooth functions of unitary
holonomy have the same fixed-order bounds by compactness of the
unitary group. Write the local output as $f_z(a_0(z)+Pv(z))$.
For $C_z(u)=Df_z(a_0(z)+u)-Df_z(a_0(z))$, the first three
path derivatives give
\[
 \|C_z(u)\|\leq C\|u\|_{\mathcal E},\qquad
 \|C_x(u)-C_y(v)\|\leq C\|u-v\|_{\mathcal E}
                                      +C|x-y|\|v\|_{\mathcal E}.
\]
The last term retains a factor of the variation because it is a
difference of derivatives at the varying and reference paths.

For $\mathcal E$-valued parameter functions use the raw difference
seminorm
\[
 [g]_{\varepsilon,4,2}^2
       =\int_{|h|<1}|h|^{-4-2\varepsilon}\|g(\cdot+h)-g\|_{L_z^4\mathcal E}^2dh.
\]
For four-dimensional input frequency pieces $v_j$, the endpoint
bounds give $\|Pv_j\|_{L^4}\leq C2^j\|v_j\|_2$ and
$\|\nabla Pv_j\|_{L^4}\leq C2^{2j}\|v_j\|_2$.
At $|h|\asymp2^{-l}$, with $b_j=2^{(1+\varepsilon)j}\|v_j\|_2$,
\[
 2^{\varepsilon l}\|\Delta_hPv\|_{L^4}
 \leq C\sum_{j\leq l}2^{-(1-\varepsilon)(l-j)}b_j
                   +C\sum_{j>l}2^{-\varepsilon(j-l)}b_j.
\]
The convolution kernels have $\ell^1$ norms $C$ and
$C\varepsilon^{-1}$. Young's inequality and the input Hilbert
Sobolev norm give
\[
       \|Pv\|_{L^4\mathcal E}+[Pv]_{\varepsilon,4,2}
                              \leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\|v\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}.
\]
This uses only norms and the triangle inequality in the path space.
For the finite-dimensional output, Fourier integration of
$|e^{i\xi\cdot h}-1|^2|h|^{-4-2\varepsilon}$ shows that
$\|r\|_{H^\varepsilon}\leq C(\|r\|_2+[r]_{\varepsilon,2,2})$
uniformly. Indeed adding the $L^2$ term to the lower bound
$c(|\xi|^{2\varepsilon}-1)/(2\varepsilon)$ controls the
Bessel multiplier uniformly; low frequencies are covered by $L^2$.

Taylor's formula writes the remainder difference as
\[
 R(v)-R(w)=\int_0^1 C_z(Pw+tP(v-w))P(v-w)\,dt.
\]
In a translation difference of this expression one factor has a
fractional difference and the other has only its $L^4$ norm.
H\"older with exponents four and four therefore pays the
$\varepsilon^{-1}$ factor once. The explicit coefficient difference
adds $|h|$ times a product, whose difference integral is bounded
because $\varepsilon<1$. This proves the unweighted estimate.

For a primary gradient the actual cylinder functions are smooth
functions of integrated holonomy traces for immersed
$\gamma_i:S^1\times D^2\to Y_0$
\cite[Section~6.1]{DaeFukLip21a}. The gradient contains a local
loop gradient multiplied by coefficients depending on all integrated
traces. At an output point $\gamma_i(t,z)$, use the shifted whole
loop $\tau\mapsto\gamma_i(t+\tau,z)$ for the local path.
An output derivative in $t$ then differentiates the pullback of the
input. Finitely many immersion inverse charts and their Jacobians
account for self-intersections. Include the global families in
\[
 \mathcal E_{\rm cyl}=L^1(S^1)\oplus
                         \bigoplus_i L^4(D^2;L^1(S^1)).
\]
Their output dependence is only in the cylinder flow coordinate,
apart from cutoffs. H\"older and coarea give the same two endpoint
bounds. Products of at most three transverse path variations are
integrable, so the integrated traces and the smooth coefficient
functions have the bounded derivatives used above. Thus the proof
applies to the full primary gradient. An $L^1$ norm in the
transverse variable would not justify those products.

The fixed end weight is comparable between inputs and outputs on
these loop families. Assign one weight to the factor with a difference,
or to the corresponding differentiated factor; keep the other
unweighted. Logarithmic derivatives of the weight contribute the same
$|h|$ term. This proves the weighted estimate. Summation with
\eqref{eq:gluing-primary-weights} is legitimate in each bound.
The perturbation outputs vanish where the principal surface operator
and collar cutoff are supported. In $\Gamma_Y^jR$ the remaining
terms are the scalar identity or smooth finite-rank operators bounded
from $L^2$: after the first finite-rank factor all subsequent surface
derivatives act on smooth vectors. Thus the estimate is in the
complete source norm, as asserted.

For differentiability fix $\varepsilon>0$ and put
\[
 \sigma=\tfrac32\varepsilon,\qquad
 r=\frac4{1+\varepsilon/2},\qquad
 q=\frac4{1-\varepsilon/2},\qquad \frac1r+\frac1q=\frac12.
\]
Use the auxiliary primary path space
$L^1_\tau\oplus\bigoplus_i L^3_{y_i}L^1_\tau$.
Its derivatives through order three are bounded; both $r$ and $q$
exceed three, so the coarea endpoints hold for these two exponents.
With $\kappa(t)=4(1/2-1/t)$, input frequency pieces obey
\[
 \|Pv_j\|_{L^t}\leq C2^{\kappa(t)j}\|v_j\|_2,\qquad
 \|\Delta_hPv_j\|_{L^t}\leq C\min(1,2^j|h|)2^{\kappa(t)j}\|v_j\|_2.
\]
Since $\kappa(r)+\sigma=1+\varepsilon$, the preceding dyadic
argument yields a $\sigma$-difference bound in $L^r$;
$\kappa(q)<1+\varepsilon$ gives the $L^q$ bound. Applying
H\"older to the candidate derivative remainder gives
\[
 \|(Df_z(Pv)-Df_z(0))Ph\|_{H^\sigma}
                    \leq C_\varepsilon\|v\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}
                                             \|h\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}.
\]
Taylor's remainder is $O(\|h\|^2)$ in $L^2$ by the $L^4$
coarea estimate and $O(\|h\|)$ in $H^\sigma$ on a fixed
input ball. Interpolation with $\varepsilon/\sigma=2/3$ gives
$O(\|h\|^{4/3})$ in $H^\varepsilon$. The derivative difference
is $O(\|v-w\|\|h\|)$ in $L^2$ and $O(\|h\|)$ in
$H^\sigma$, hence is operator-norm continuous into $H^\varepsilon$
with modulus $C_{\varepsilon,R}\|v-w\|^{1/3}$.
The same weighted and surface-source arguments apply. The constants
for these coarea exponents were included in
\eqref{eq:gluing-primary-weights}. This proves $C^1$, separately
from the quadratic Lipschitz estimate.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A complete nonlinear chart and the first correction}

\begin{proposition}[nonlinear graph chart]\label{prop:gluing-nonlinear-chart}
There is a chart $\Phi_\rho$, with $\Phi_\rho(0)=x_{\rm pre}$
and derivative $I$, from a ball in $X_\rho$ to configurations
satisfying the exact nonlinear matching, normal and outer Lagrangian
conditions, with exact finite asymptotic representatives. Express the
equation in the fixed target by parallel transport along its positive
coordinate curves, and write
\[
 F_\rho(U)=\mathcal F(\Phi_\rho(U))
              =\mathcal F(x_{\rm pre})+D_\rho U+N_\rho(U).
\]
For a fixed $K_0$ independent of $\varepsilon$ and $\rho$, put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-nonlinear-constant}
 B_\rho=C\varepsilon^{-4}\rho^{-4\varepsilon}R_1^{K_0\varepsilon}
                         (1+\rho M_1^2R_1^{K_0\varepsilon})\leq CT^4.
\end{equation}
The chart and its inverse are defined on a ball of radius $c/B_\rho$,
in the finite-profile coordinates below, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-nonlinear-difference}
 \|N_\rho(U)-N_\rho(V)\|_{Y_\rho}
  \leq B_\rho(\|U\|_{X_\rho}+\|V\|_{X_\rho})\|U-V\|_{X_\rho}.
\end{equation}
The ambient chart remainder satisfies the same quadratic difference
bound. For each fixed positive scale the equation map is $C^1$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use a smooth exponential-type family $e_\kappa(q,\nu)$ on the
positive moduli space, with derivative $I$, adapted to the two
outer Lagrangians and constant mixed ends as in
\cite[Lemma~4.4]{DaeFukLip21a}. The parameter $\kappa$ is
constant on the entire shrinking neighborhood. For a flat irreducible
surface connection $\alpha$, the surface chart of
Proposition~\ref{prop:nonlinear-cap-chart}, with this positive
coordinate, has a quadratic correction $S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b)$
satisfying
\[
 E_\alpha S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b)=0,\qquad
 \alpha+b+S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b)\text{ flat} \Longleftrightarrow\ C_\alpha b=0.
\]
On the flat locus its class is
$e_\kappa([\alpha],\ell_\alpha^{-1}H_\alpha b)$.
The correction depends on the exact and harmonic coordinates; the
coexact coordinate passes through linearly. If $q$ is the flat
shift determined by those coordinates, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-chart-surface-gain}
 S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b)=H_\alpha S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b)
                              -\tfrac12d_\alpha^*G^2_\alpha[q\wedge q].
\end{equation}
It therefore gains one surface derivative, with quadratic difference
bounds $H^k_\Sigma\to H^{k+1}_\Sigma$, $k\geq2$.

For a definite choice of the background flat retraction, solve
$F_{\beta-d_\beta^*\eta}=0$ for the small two-form $\eta$.
At a flat connection the derivative in $\eta$ is
$-d_\beta d_\beta^*:H^{k+1}\Omega^2\to H^{k-1}\Omega^2$,
an isomorphism by irreducibility. The implicit function theorem
and uniqueness of the small solution give a smooth equivariant
retraction $r(\beta)$ whose local definitions agree. Put
$\alpha(z)=r(\beta_{\rm pre}(z))$ on a seam collar. Choose
its cutoff $\psi$ to be one at the seam, using a fixed compact
cap collar near the bubble and a fixed physical residual collar
supported where $\varphi=1$ outside. On the entire outer cap
annulus both cutoffs are one. The same retraction and $e_\kappa$
are used there, so these formulas agree. The background remains
in the fixed retraction neighborhood on those collars. Its first
compact base derivatives are bounded by $C$ and its second by
$CR_1$; outside the cap they have fixed bounds.

For the physical negative variation $\xi$, with surface part $b$,
start with
\[
 \widehat A(U)=A_{\rm pre}+\xi+\psi S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b),
                     \qquad u(U)=e_\kappa(u_{\rm pre},\nu).
\]
The surface chart identities give exactly the nonlinear matching
condition and its local converse. The normal base trace is unchanged;
the adapted positive map gives exactly the outer Lagrangian conditions.

We next specify the ends. For a finite mixed-end tangent $p$, let
$q_p=e_{\pm1}(q,s_p)$ be its prescribed nearby Lagrangian value.
Choose smooth perturbed-flat representatives $B_p$ with derivative
$p$ by the local three-dimensional Coulomb slice, reparameterized
by $q_p$. Prescribe their boundary representatives using the surface
chart above. The original and prescribed boundary families have the
same class and first derivative, so their small unitary boundary
gauge is $I+O(|p|^2)$. Extend its logarithm through a fixed collar,
with the covariant normal jet chosen to preserve zero normal variation,
and then cut it off. Both the logarithm and prescribed jet are
$O(|p|^2)$. Gauge covariance preserves the perturbed-flat equation
and the derivative at zero. This is the end-representative construction
of \cite[Section~4.1]{DaeFukLip21a} with the specified boundary chart.
If $\widehat B_p$ is the limiting connection of $\widehat A$
on a constant tangent profile, then
$C_p=B_p-\widehat B_p$ is $O(|p|^2)$ with zero tangential
boundary value and zero normal variation. With fixed end cutoffs
$\tau_\pm$, define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-exact-end-chart}
          A(U)=\widehat A(U)+\tau_+C_{p_+}+\tau_-C_{p_-}.
\end{equation}
This preserves the seam and normal conditions and has the exact
end configurations $(B_{p_\pm},q_{p_\pm})$.

For ambient configuration norms, retain $p$ as a finite coordinate
and subtract $\tau(B_p-B)$ before taking a weighted norm.
Relative to the linear profile $\tau p$, the remaining chart
correction on each end is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-chart-end-subtraction}
       \psi S_{\alpha,\kappa}(b)
               -\tau\psi_\infty S_{\alpha_\infty,\pm1}(p_\Sigma).
\end{equation}
It decays because the background converges exponentially and
$b-p_\Sigma$ is in the weighted space. These ambient graph
spaces are complete by the closed-distribution argument of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-complete-domain}, omitting the linear
boundary constraints. An unsubtracted constant $B_p-B-p$ is
never assigned a weighted-decay norm.

We prove the estimates for this chart. On the cap collar let
$S(z,b)=S_{\alpha(z),\kappa(z)}(b)$. It is smooth from
$H^k_\Sigma$ to $H^{k+1}_\Sigma$, vanishes quadratically,
and has first explicit base derivatives bounded by $C$ and second
by $CL$, $L\leq CR_1$, also after the fixed derivatives in $b$
used below. A dyadic composition estimate avoids a full $L$ loss.
For low sums $S_j$ and frequency pieces $\Delta_j$, telescope
\[
 S(z,b)=\sum_{j\geq0}m_j(z)\Delta_jb,\qquad
 m_j=\int_0^1D_bS(z,S_jb+t\Delta_jb)\,dt,
\]
including the low-frequency term. On a small fixed $C^0$ ball,
\[
 \|m_j\|_\infty\leq C\|b\|_\infty,\quad
 \|Dm_j\|_\infty\leq C2^j\|b\|_\infty,\quad
 \|D^2m_j\|_\infty\leq C(2^{2j}+L)\|b\|_\infty.
\]
Differences satisfy the same bounds with $\|b-c\|_\infty$.
For output frequency $n>j$, one or two differentiations in its
projection give
\[
 \|\Delta_n(m_j\Delta_jb)\|_2
 \leq C\|b\|_\infty\|\Delta_jb\|_2
       \min\{2^{-(n-j)},(1+L2^{-2j})2^{-2(n-j)}\}.
\]
After the $H^{1+\varepsilon}$ weight, its convolution kernel
is bounded by
$C\min\{2^{\varepsilon(n-j)},L2^{-(1-\varepsilon)(n-j)}\}$,
whose sum is $C\varepsilon^{-1}L^\varepsilon$ by splitting
at $n-j=\lceil\log_2L\rceil$. For $n\leq j$ the kernel
is $C2^{-(1+\varepsilon)(j-n)}$. Hilbert-valued square summation
and the $C^0$ bound $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|b\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}$
give the coarser quadratic difference estimate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-surface-composition}
 \|S(b)-S(c)\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^{k+1}_\Sigma}
 \leq C\varepsilon^{-2}L^{K_1\varepsilon}
      (\|b\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
       +\|c\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma})
                                   \|b-c\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}.
\end{equation}
For a fixed physical patch, $L=1$ and the second-derivative
kernel alone is uniformly summable, leaving only the evaluation
loss. The coarser fixed-patch constant $C\varepsilon^{-1}$
will suffice. Fixed coefficient multiplications are absorbed in
$K_1$. This argument uses Hilbert structure only for the input
and output norms, not for the operator-valued coefficients.

On the cap, the quadratic $a^2$ term has coefficient $\rho/f$
and bound
\[
                 C\rho M_1^2L^\varepsilon
                           \varepsilon^{-1/2}\|a/M_1\|^2.
\]
The $ab$ term costs
$C\rho M_1\varepsilon^{-1/2}$. The surface derivative of
$S$ is controlled by \eqref{eq:gluing-chart-surface-gain} and
\eqref{eq:gluing-surface-composition}; its scalar coefficient is
$\rho f$. Its base derivative and the positive nonlinear row
use the strong/weak product estimate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:small-fractional-exponents}. Thus the local
cap equation remainder has quadratic difference constant
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-local-cap-nonlinearity}
 C\varepsilon^{-2}R_1^{K_1\varepsilon}
                         (1+\rho M_1^2R_1^{K_1\varepsilon}).
\end{equation}
There is no nonlinear surface derivative of $a$: its affine
linear derivative was subtracted in $N_\rho$. No full additional
derivative of the exact surface part is used. The background and
target coefficients have the bounds just proved, including the
normalized pregluing source on its transition. The same estimates
therefore apply to the raw domain without a base Coulomb condition.

On the fixed four-dimensional residual body, curvature products obey
$H^{1+\varepsilon}\times H^{1+\varepsilon}\to H^\varepsilon$
uniformly: interpolate $H^1\times H^1\to L^2$ and
$H^2\times H^2\to H^1$, both consequences of $H^1\to L^4$.
For an integer surface source order $j$, the principal part of
$\Gamma^j$ is $\chi^j\Lambda_\Sigma^j$. Elliptic order-zero
comparison gives $\chi^j\nabla_\Sigma^j\xi\in H^1$
from the $\Gamma^jE^1$ input bounds, with lower powers and
smooth finite-rank terms included. The comparison is normally
constant, so this never divides by $\chi$. In a quadratic
product distribute $\chi^j=\chi^a\chi^{j-a}$ and use
$H^1\cdot H^1\subset L^2$. The chart remainder is supported
where $\varphi=1$ and gains a surface derivative, so direct
differentiation bounds its source and ambient graph norms. The
positive equation uses the two-dimensional strong/weak estimates.
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-holonomy-estimate} bounds the perturbations.
Together these give constant $C\varepsilon^{-1}$ on the fixed
residual pieces. In this boundedness calculation the chart remainder
need not have linear boundary trace; no integration by parts against
an adjoint with that trace is used.

At a nonzero end parameter, subtract the exact $B_p$ family first.
Products with a decaying field are smooth coefficient products.
The pure parameter source has its linear term
$D_\rho(\tau p)$ subtracted. Its quadratic remainder is supported
at the fixed cutoff or decays like $C|p|^2e^{-\lambda|\theta|}$,
because the limiting configurations solve the exact equation and
the background converges with all fixed derivatives. Choose the
weight below $\lambda$. Differences have the same bound.
Linear holonomy coefficients at the smooth end family use the
differentiated coarea estimate; decaying remainders use
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-holonomy-estimate}. This proves the residual
estimates including \eqref{eq:gluing-chart-end-subtraction}.

It remains to combine the two norms. Write $U_i=\theta_1U$ and
$U_o=(1-\theta_1)U$. Their defining norms are bounded by
$\|U\|_{X_\rho}$, and
\[
                  \|(b,\nu)\|_{C_B^0H^k_\Sigma}
                               \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|U\|_{X_\rho}.
\]
On a fixed outer compact annulus of radius $R_1^{-1}$,
\[
 \|v(R_1^{-1}\cdot)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}}
                         \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\|v\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}(B)}.
\]
The value term uses $C^0$, the derivative uses the unchanged
Dirichlet integral, and its fractional part gains $R_1^{-\varepsilon}$.
Cut $U_i$ to an annular field $U_i^a$ which agrees with it for
$|w|\geq R_1/3$ and vanishes for $|w|\leq R_1/4$.
It already vanishes beyond $R_1/2$. Since $f_\infty\asymp M_1$
there, conversion and physical scaling give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-outer-annulus-strong}
 \|U_i^a\|_{H_{B,\rm phys}^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
       \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}(\rho R_1)^{-\varepsilon}
                                             \|U_i\|_{X^{\rm raw}_{\rm cap}}.
\end{equation}
Zero extension at a radial boundary is bounded here because the
value trace is zero and $1+\varepsilon<3/2$. At the straight
seam use a fixed extension. Only the strong coefficient triple,
not a full extra derivative of exact $b$, is asserted in this
comparison. For a surface-gaining chart remainder the same calculation
also controls its stronger residual surface norm.

The cross difference $N(U_i+U_o)-N(U_i)-N(U_o)$ is supported
on this outer cap annulus. There $U_i$ can be replaced by $U_i^a$:
where the replacement changes it, $U_o$ and its derivatives vanish.
All nonlocal perturbation and end cross terms are zero because
$U_i$ vanishes on their outputs and dependencies. The local physical
nonlinear estimate and \eqref{eq:gluing-outer-annulus-strong} bound
the cross difference with constant
$C\varepsilon^{-2}\rho^{-2\varepsilon}R_1^{K_2\varepsilon}$.
For the pure inner term use \eqref{eq:gluing-local-cap-nonlinearity}
and the source conversion $C\rho^{-\varepsilon}$; for the pure
outer term use the fixed residual estimate. These give
\eqref{eq:gluing-nonlinear-difference} with the coarser
\eqref{eq:gluing-nonlinear-constant}.

The same decomposition controls the ambient chart remainder.
Its inner term has the full surface gain for the reverse annular
comparison; its outer term transfers to the raw cap norm by
\eqref{eq:gluing-field-conversion}. The cross term has that same
surface gain, so its inner transfer costs at most a further
$\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_1^\varepsilon$. The largest fractional
loss is $\varepsilon^{-5/2}\rho^{-C\varepsilon}$ with
$C\leq3$, dominated by \eqref{eq:gluing-nonlinear-constant}.
The ambient near-identity map therefore has a contraction inverse
on a ball of radius $c/B_\rho$, after fixing $p$ and subtracting
its exact profile. Recover $p$ from the exact end value $q_p$.
The surface and positive inverse charts identify the exact nonlinear
boundary conditions with the linear ones in $X_\rho$. This
proves the chart assertion and its derivative $I$ at zero.
Finally, the fixed-scale surface and positive maps are smooth on
their strong Banach algebras, curvature is a continuous quadratic
map into the source, and the end families are smooth. The separate
$C^1$ holonomy conclusion of Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-holonomy-estimate}
therefore gives $F_\rho\in C^1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[first exact correction]\label{cor:gluing-first-correction}
There is $U_\rho\in X_\rho$ with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-first-correction}
 \mathcal F(\Phi_\rho(U_\rho))=0,\qquad
 \|U_\rho\|_{X_\rho}\leq CT^{-38}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}.
\end{equation}
The equation linearization in this graph chart has a right inverse
of norm $CT^2$ at the corrected point.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Write $q_\rho=\|Q_\rho\|$ and
$e_\rho=\|\mathcal F(x_{\rm pre})\|$. The range of $Q_\rho$
is closed because $Q_\rho D_\rho$ is a bounded projection.
On its closed ball of radius $2q_\rho e_\rho$, define
\[
                \mathcal T(U)=-Q_\rho\mathcal F(x_{\rm pre})-Q_\rho N_\rho(U).
\]
The inverse, error and nonlinear bounds give
$B_\rho q_\rho^2e_\rho=O(T^{-32})$ and
$q_\rho e_\rho=o(B_\rho^{-1})$. Thus this ball lies in the
chart, $\mathcal T$ preserves it, and its Lipschitz constant is
at most $4B_\rho q_\rho^2e_\rho<1/2$. Completeness gives
a fixed point, and applying $D_\rho Q_\rho=I$ proves its
exact equation and the stated bound. Uniqueness at this stage is
only within that inverse-range ball.

Since the equation is $C^1$, the quadratic difference estimate
implies $\|dN_\rho(U_\rho)\|\leq2B_\rho\|U_\rho\|$.
For $L_\rho=D_\rho+dN_\rho(U_\rho)$, the Neumann formula
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-corrected-geometric-inverse}
 \widetilde Q_\rho=Q_\rho(I+dN_\rho(U_\rho)Q_\rho)^{-1}
\end{equation}
is a right inverse with norm $CT^2$, because its correction
operator is $O(T^{-32})$. This conclusion concerns the geometric
graph operator; the ordinary Fredholm quotient is addressed next.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Smooth representatives and the ordinary Fredholm problem}

\begin{proposition}[ordinary regularity of the corrected solutions]
\label{prop:gluing-standard-regularity}
Any zero $x=\Phi_\rho(U)$ of the equation constructed in the preceding
chart with $\|U\|_{X_\rho}\leq CT^{-37}$ represents a smooth
mixed moduli solution at each fixed positive scale. It has finite
analytical energy, the prescribed ordinary end classes, and the exact
mixed end classes selected by its finite parameters. For a sufficiently
small positive weight on the mixed ends only, its ordinary augmented
Fredholm operator is onto and has real index four.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We first establish smoothness and the ends. Fix $\rho>0$ and
$\varepsilon>0$. The cap coefficient conversions are smooth and
bounded at this fixed scale. For $k\geq2$ the Fourier inequality
\[
 (1+|\xi_B|^2+|\xi_\Sigma|^2)^{1+\varepsilon}
 \leq C(1+|\xi_B|^2)^{1+\varepsilon}(1+|\xi_\Sigma|^2)^k
\]
puts the negative strong cap coefficients in ordinary four-dimensional
$H^{1+\varepsilon}$. The residual factor and ambient chart
remainder have the same local regularity. Choose $p=2+\varepsilon/4$;
then $2<p<4/(2-\varepsilon)$ and $p<2/(1-\varepsilon)$.
The negative connection and positive map are both locally $W^{1,p}$.
The stronger collar continuity already gives the actual weak matching.

The local $W^{1,p}$ Coulomb gauge theorem described after
\cite[Theorem~1]{DaeFukLip21b} supplies a $W^{2,p}$ gauge
relative to a smooth reference, with covariant Coulomb and zero
normal boundary conditions. This use is at a fixed physical scale.
The gauge is continuous and preserves the matching class.
The mixed regularity theorem
\cite[Theorem~1]{DaeFukLip21b}, equivalently
\cite[Theorem~5.4]{DaeFukLip21a}, makes the unperturbed seam
pair smooth there. Standard interior and totally real boundary
regularity make the positive map smooth on its remaining domain.

In the perturbed negative interior, start with the pointwise bound
for the holonomy perturbation, independent of the unitary connection
\cite[Proposition~6.1(ii)]{DaeFukLip21a}. The secondary forms
have the same bound by unitarity and their compact defining data.
In a local Coulomb gauge the first-order elliptic equation has a
quadratic connection term and bounded forcing. If $2<p<4$,
\[
 a\in W^{1,p}\ \Longrightarrow\ a\in L^{4p/(4-p)},\qquad
                  a\wedge a\in L^{2p/(4-p)},\qquad 2p/(4-p)>p.
\]
Iteration on smaller compact sets reaches $W^{1,6}$, choosing
intermediate exponents to avoid the endpoint four. Only boundedness
of the perturbation is used before this stage. The number of steps
may depend on the fixed $\varepsilon$.

Once $p=6$, differentiate the path ODE and use coarea to obtain
$W^{1,6}$ regularity of the perturbation. At order $m$ its
Duhamel terms contain input derivatives of total order at most $m$.
A factor of order $m$ is in $L^6$ and the remaining factors
are bounded; if there are several differentiated factors all have
order at most $m-1$. The integrated primary traces use H\"older
in their transverse variable. Their fixed constants are precisely
those included in \eqref{eq:gluing-primary-weights}. Thus every
fixed differentiated series converges, and elliptic bootstrapping
improves $W^{m,6}$ to $W^{m+1,6}$. Each compact output set
is treated together with its full compact dependency neighborhood;
for a primary series on a bounded flow interval this includes the
whole fixed three-dimensional body on that interval. Local gauge
transitions satisfy $dg=gA_j-A_i g$ and improve with the connection
regularity. They are smooth at the end of the bootstrap, define a
smooth bundle in the original topological class, and yield a global
Sobolev gauge to a smooth representative. This is the perturbed
interior and patching step discussed after
\cite[Theorem~5.4]{DaeFukLip21a}. No uniform bound for this
gauge in $X_\rho$ is claimed.

On compact subsets, $H^1$ and $H^1\subset L^4$ give finite
curvature and map energy. On a mixed end write $A=B_p+\xi$
using the exact end representative, where $\xi\in H^1_\omega$.
Since $B_p$ solves the perturbed-flat equation,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-analytical-end-curvature}
 F_A+*_3\nabla_Ah
       =d_{B_p}\xi+\xi\wedge\xi
                           +*_3(\nabla_Ah-\nabla_{B_p}h).
\end{equation}
All terms on the right are $L^2$, the last by the $L^2$
Lipschitz estimate of \cite[Proposition~6.1(ii)]{DaeFukLip21a}
or its Duhamel--coarea proof. Secondary perturbations vanish on
far ends. The positive end has constant limit and $L^2$ derivative.
The ordinary gauge end uses its fixed critical representative and
the unweighted $H^1$ remainder in the same calculation; the
ordinary symplectic end has finite energy by its fixed background
and $H^1$ variation. Thus the analytical energy is finite.
The unmodified $F_{B_p}$ need not be $L^2$ on an infinite
perturbed-flat cylinder, which is why
\eqref{eq:gluing-analytical-end-curvature} is required.

We can now apply \cite[Proposition~3.6]{DaeFukLip21a}: local
smoothness has supplied its initial local $H^2$ hypothesis and
the required regularity of $du$. More explicitly, the small-energy
tail estimates and temporal-gauge conclusion in
\cite[Propositions~5.24, 5.27 and 5.29]{DaeFukLip21a} give
exponential convergence at every fixed order. Compact secondary
perturbations do not change those tail equations. Choose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-moduli-weight}
                   0<\omega_{\rm mod}<\min(\omega,\delta_{\rm DFL})
\end{equation}
below the allowed decay rate on the compact end data. The positive
mixed limit is $q_p$ already: its original weighted base norm gives
uniform convergence on unit translates and a negative gauge does
not change it. The embedded three-manifold Lagrangian then fixes
the negative limiting class as $[B_p]$. The positive ordinary
limit is likewise prescribed. On the gauge end, translates where
the original $H^1$ remainder tends to zero converge to its
prescribed critical representative in the ordinary local quotient;
its Hausdorff irreducible slice identifies the class given by the
end theorem. These arguments prove membership in the ordinary
smooth configuration space with the stated end convention.

We next prove ordinary surjectivity. At any zero in the larger
$CT^{-37}$ ball, \eqref{eq:gluing-nonlinear-difference} gives
$\|dN_\rho(U)Q_\rho\|\leq CT^{-31}$. Thus
\eqref{eq:gluing-corrected-geometric-inverse} remains a right
inverse of size $CT^2$ there. Let $x=(A,u)$ now denote a
smooth representative and consider
\[
          \mathscr D_x(\xi,\nu)=(d_A^*\xi,D_x^{\rm geo}(\xi,\nu))
\]
on the actual normal, matching and outer Lagrangian domain, with
the finite tangent variables of the exact mixed end manifolds.
The Fredholm and weak adjoint theorems
\cite[Theorem~4.12]{DaeFukLip21a},
\cite[Theorem~5.9]{DaeFukLip21b} identify its cokernel with
the formal adjoint kernel in the positive-weight adjoint domain.
In particular a cokernel vector $(s,\eta)$ is smooth locally,
globally $L^2$, and positively weighted on mixed ends.
The secondary linearization and adjoint have every fixed integer
bound by differentiated coarea and are compact $H^1\to L^2$
on the full dependency set. Thus the same weak regularity and end
identification apply with that term. Retaining the finite end
variables is essential to the stated adjoint weight.

For a compactly supported gauge potential $\zeta$ with covariant
Neumann trace, $(d_A\zeta,0)$ belongs to the actual domain and
$D_x^{\rm geo}(d_A\zeta,0)=0$ by covariance at the exact
solution, including all perturbations. Pairing with the cokernel
