gives $\langle s,d_A^*d_A\zeta\rangle=0$. Interior tests and
the arbitrary boundary value of these Neumann tests imply
$d_A^*d_As=0$ with covariant Neumann condition. Cutoff integration
by parts, using the adjoint $H^1$ and $L^2$ bounds, yields
$d_As=0$. A parallel adjoint section restricts to a parallel section
on a flat irreducible seam fiber, so $s=0$.

For any smooth compact geometric source $F$ in the original
pregluing frames, apply the corrected graph inverse, the chart
derivative, and the fixed smoothing gauge to obtain a variation $V$
at $x$ with
\[
                         D_x^{\rm geo}V=SF.
\]
Here $S$ is a bounded invertible source multiplication on weighted
$L^2$: negative unitary conjugation and positive target transport.
The transformed variation is $H^1$ locally. Indeed its original
physical regularity is $H^{1+\varepsilon}$, while the smoothing
gauge is locally $W^{2,p}$ with $p>2$; its derivative and the
original variation have exponents greater than four, making the
differentiated conjugation $L^2$. Matching and normal traces are
preserved. On mixed ends $V$ has bounded $L^2$ size on unit
strips, since before conjugation it is a fixed finite end profile
plus a decaying remainder. It is $L^2$ on the other ends.

Choose an exhaustion cutoff $\chi_R$ common to the two sides of
each seam, constant on all far primary loop dependencies at each
flow coordinate, and one on the compact secondary dependencies.
All holonomy commutators with it vanish. The compact variation
$\chi_RV$ is in the ordinary first-order domain. The weak adjoint
identity therefore gives
\[
 0=\langle\eta,\chi_RSF\rangle
                         +\langle\eta,[D_x^{\rm geo},\chi_R]V\rangle.
\]
The remaining commutator is zeroth order with coefficient $Cd\chi_R$.
Its pairing tends to zero by the positive adjoint weight and bounded
unit-strip norm on mixed ends, and the two $L^2$ norms elsewhere.
Thus $\eta$ annihilates $SF$ for every smooth compact $F$.
Those images are dense in weighted $L^2$ because $S$ is bounded
and invertible, even if its gauge is initially Sobolev. Hence
$\eta=0$. This proves ordinary surjectivity without a uniform
physical Coulomb projection.

Finally compute the index, retaining the relative seam framing.
Choose a fixed positive-scale comparison circle outside the pregluing
transition, where the pregluing agrees exactly with the residual.
Join their mixed fillings to obtain a relative projective bundle
$P_{\rm rel}$ over $S^2\times\Sigma_g$. On the positive disc
pull back the universal projective bundle, choose flat surface
representatives, and extend the negative base components across the
seam by arbitrary smooth base components. The positive disc is
contractible, so the lift exists with the prescribed seam identification.
Since $F_\Sigma=0$, the mixed curvatures are derivatives of the
flat surface connection minus exact forms. Their derivatives are
$d_\beta$-closed, and integration over $\Sigma$ kills the
exact terms in the wedge pairing. With the established orientation,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-positive-sewing}
       \int_{D^+\times\Sigma}\operatorname{tr}(F\wedge F)
                                         =2\int_{D^+}u^*\Omega.
\end{equation}
This is the direct calculation of
\cite[Lemma~5.10, equation~(5.12)]{DaeFukLip21a}. Initially
piecewise smooth connections give the same integral by smoothing with
the seam identification fixed. This calculation does not require the
small-energy hypothesis of the annular argument in
\cite[Lemma~4.67]{DaeFukLip21b}.

The outer connections coincide, so transgression vanishes and
\[
 E_{\rm top}(x_{\rm pre})-E_{\rm top}(x_0)
                    =-\tfrac14\langle p_1(P_{\rm rel}),[S^2\times\Sigma]\rangle.
\]
Perturbation contributions cancel outside the comparison circle.
Within the fixed attaching charts, contract the small residual filling
to its central value and replace the cap's far tail by that same
constant value. Perform these homotopies with the relative boundary
identification fixed. The resulting closed projective bundle is
$\operatorname{ad}E$, so $P_{\rm rel}$ has its characteristic
class. A positive-half trivialization has not been extended across
the negative half. In particular a constant positive map in genus
one does not remove this relative clutching class.

For the primitive extension \eqref{eq:primitive-global-extension},
let $f$ be the positive generator from $S^2$ and let
$\lambda=c_1(\Lambda)$ be pulled back from $\Sigma$. Then
$f^2=\lambda^2=0$, $\langle f\lambda,[S^2\times\Sigma]\rangle=1$,
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-relative-characteristic-class}
        c_1(E)=f+\lambda,\qquad c_2(E)=f\lambda,\qquad
        p_1(\operatorname{ad}E)=c_1(E)^2-4c_2(E)=-2f\lambda.
\end{equation}
Thus the relative topological energy is $1/2$, for either pencil
rank. The configuration index formula
\cite[Proposition~3.20, equation~(3.21)]{DaeFukLip21a} is
\[
             \operatorname{ind}\mathscr D_x
                    =8E_{\rm top}(x)+\epsilon(\beta)-\epsilon(\alpha).
\]
The external end classes agree with the index-zero residual, so
the preglued configuration has index four. The compact secondary
term does not change that index.

The path $\Phi_\rho(tU)$ preserves the actual matching,
outer conditions and exact end critical manifolds. Its continuous
positive map and $H^k_\Sigma$-continuous seam data, together
with the local surface gauge charts, give a continuous homotopy of
the relative projective bundle. To use ordinary Fredholm invariance,
smooth these continuous boundary data in the same charts, relative
to smooth endpoints and retaining the end manifolds; extend the
negative connection coefficients affinely. This gives a smooth
configuration homotopy with the same clutching class. We have not
applied a high-order operator homotopy theorem directly to a rough
graph path. The smoothing gauge also preserves that class. Therefore
the corrected smooth solution has index four and, by the surjectivity
proved above,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-standard-kernel-dimension}
                         \dim_\mathbb R\ker\mathscr D_x=4.
\end{equation}
The effective local slice divides by the central stabilizer
$\{\pm1\}$, as required for its free action.
\end{proof}

The ordinary local slice and Fredholm implicit function theorem
\cite[Propositions~3.18 and 4.6]{DaeFukLip21a} now give a
smooth four-dimensional moduli neighborhood. Equality of this dimension
with the four expected gluing parameters does not establish that the
constructed family parametrizes it. We next prove that assertion using
four gauge-invariant holonomy functions.

\subsection{Affine normalization and four holonomy functions}

Use the holomorphic coordinate $w=-\theta+is$, whose negative
half-plane has $\operatorname{Im}w<0$ and whose seam is real.
This rotation must be retained in a later orientation comparison.
Write a primitive extension pencil as
\[
 e(w)=vw+w_0,\qquad [v,w_0]\in\mathbb P(V_L\oplus V_L),
                       \qquad V_L=H^1(\Sigma,\Lambda^{-1}L^2).
\]
The maximal relative $\mathcal O(1)$ line identifies $L$;
these spaces have constant dimension $g$ and form a smooth bundle
over $\operatorname{Pic}^0(\Sigma)$. Choose a smooth Hermitian
metric, linear in its first argument. Stable attachment requires
$v\ne0$. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-affine-slice-functions}
 \kappa(E)=\frac{\langle w_0,v\rangle}{\|v\|^2},\qquad
 w_\perp=w_0-\kappa v,\qquad
 s(E)=\left((\operatorname{Im}\kappa)^2+
                          \frac{\|w_\perp\|^2}{\|v\|^2}\right)^{1/2}.
\end{equation}
They are invariant under common complex scaling. The condition $s=0$
means a rank-one pencil with real basepoint $-\kappa$, excluded
by seam admissibility. Thus they are smooth with $s>0$ on the
stated locus, including interior rank drop. Under pullback by
$w\mapsto aw+b$, $a>0$, $b\in\mathbb R$, the coefficients
become $(av,w_0+bv)$ and
\[
                         \kappa\longmapsto(\kappa+b)/a,
                              \qquad s\longmapsto s/a.
\]
There is a unique orbit representative in the slice
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-affine-slice}
                            \operatorname{Re}\kappa=0,\qquad s=1,
\end{equation}
obtained by $a=s(E)$, $b=-\operatorname{Re}\kappa(E)$.
The infinitesimal translation $T_E$ and dilation $D_E$ give,
at this slice, the normal matrix
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-affine-normal-matrix}
 \begin{pmatrix}
 d\operatorname{Re}\kappa(T_E)&d\operatorname{Re}\kappa(D_E)\\
 ds(T_E)&ds(D_E)
 \end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&-1\end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
Both preserve evaluation at infinity. The regular attaching fiber
has real dimension four, so its intersection with this slice is
smooth of real dimension two.

\begin{lemma}[holonomy coordinates for the limiting kernel]
\label{lem:gluing-limiting-observations}
Locally on the compact regular primitive family, four real linear
combinations of traces of holonomy, supported on a fixed compact
rescaled negative region, have independent differentials on the
four-dimensional zero-attaching limiting kernel. They may be averaged
over small real affine motions while retaining independence.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First let $A$ be a smooth irreducible unitary rank-two connection
on a connected region. If a variation has zero first variation of
every closed-loop trace, it is an infinitesimal gauge variation.
To verify this, fix a basepoint and write $H_\gamma$ for the
based holonomy. Their complex span is $M_2(\mathbb C)$:
a nonscalar unitary supplies its two spectral projectors, and
irreducibility supplies another unitary with nonzero off-diagonal
entries. Multiplication by the projectors gives both off-diagonal
matrix units. If $\sum c_\gamma H_\gamma=0$, differentiation
of traces of concatenated loops gives
\[
 \operatorname{tr}\left(\sum c_\gamma\dot H_\gamma\,H_\eta\right)
                  =-\operatorname{tr}\left(\sum c_\gamma H_\gamma\dot H_\eta\right)=0.
\]
Nondegeneracy of the trace pairing makes
$H_\gamma\mapsto\dot H_\gamma$ a well-defined derivation of
the matrix algebra. It is inner: with matrix units, the derivation
identity gives $D(X)=[K,X]$ for
$K=\sum_iD(E_{i1})E_{1i}$. Preservation of the unitary involution
makes $K+K^*$ scalar, so $K$ may be taken skew-adjoint and
trace-free. Subtract this basepoint conjugation. Now every based
holonomy variation is zero. Differentiated parallel transport along
a path from the basepoint defines a path-independent potential
$\zeta$, and differentiation along its final segment gives the
variation $d_A\zeta$. Smooth local path families give smoothness.
Piecewise smooth loops can be smoothed without changing this conclusion.
For fixed-determinant $U(2)$ connections take both real and imaginary
trace parts.

Apply this fact in the based unitary trivialization of the limiting
base-flat connection $A_E^0$. Choose the determinant reference pulled
back from $\Sigma$ on the negative product. Its base component,
as well as the trace-free base component, is zero. The surface
$(0,1)$ connection is holomorphic in $w$. A zero-attaching
bundle tangent $v$ has representative $(0,b_v,\nu_v)$ with
$b_v^{0,1}$ holomorphic and $b_v(\infty)=0$.
Choose a relatively compact negative base region $O$ close to a
finite seam point and put $K=O\times\Sigma$. The surface
zero-form gaps near the flat irreducible seam make the connection
on $K$ irreducible. If all its trace derivatives vanish, the
preceding argument gives $(0,b_v)=d_{A_E^0}\zeta$ on $K$.
The base equation makes $\zeta$ independent of $w$.
The identity $b_v^{0,1}=d_{\beta_E(w)}^{0,1}\zeta$ extends
to the whole negative half-plane by holomorphic uniqueness in
surface Sobolev spaces. At infinity it gives $d_\alpha\zeta=0$,
so $\zeta=0$ and $b_v=0$. Matching makes $\nu_v$ zero
on the seam. Stable fibers give a holomorphic extension of its
classifying map and tangent bundle through a seam neighborhood.
In local holomorphic frames, extension of $\nu_v$ by zero is
weakly holomorphic across a boundary arc; uniqueness makes it zero
everywhere. The limiting kernel/cohomology identification then gives
$v=0$.

Thus the real trace differentials separate this four-dimensional
space. Select four real linear combinations spanning its dual;
they remain independent on a smaller parameter patch. Averaging
the loops over a small smooth affine neighborhood converges with
its first finite-family derivative to the unaveraged functions,
so a fixed sufficiently small average retains independence. A compact
family is covered by finitely many such choices; no global frame
of a possibly nontrivial kernel bundle is asserted.
\end{proof}

Fix these four combinations, their loops and a smooth density
$\chi(a,b)$ supported near $(1,0)$ in $(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R$,
with integral one. All moved loops stay in one fixed compact negative
region. With real or imaginary parts and the selected combinations
understood, define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-affine-observations}
 \mathcal O_{c,\rho}(A)
   =\int\chi(a,b)\operatorname{Tr}
       \operatorname{Hol}_{(w,x)\mapsto(c+\rho(aw+b),x)\circ\gamma}(A),da\,db
                      \quad\in\mathbb R^4.
\end{equation}
Write $\mathcal O_0(E)$ for their value at the limiting connection
in rescaled coordinates. These are local moduli coordinates, not
point-incidence conditions. For any signed smooth density $\sigma$
use $\mathcal O^\sigma$ for the corresponding average. Changing
variables in the compact affine integral gives the exact identities
at a fixed input connection
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-explicit-observation-derivatives}
 \rho\partial_c\mathcal O^\chi_{c,\rho}
                         =\mathcal O^{\sigma_T}_{c,\rho},\qquad
 \rho\partial_\rho\mathcal O^\chi_{c,\rho}
                         =\mathcal O^{\sigma_D}_{c,\rho},\qquad
 \sigma_T=-\partial_b\chi,\quad
 \sigma_D=-\partial_a(a\chi)-\partial_b(b\chi).
\end{equation}
The scale formula includes the two-dimensional Jacobian in $(a,b)$.
These identities hold for continuous connections and iterate in the
stated order for any fixed word of parameter vector fields. No input
spatial derivative along a moving rough loop is needed.

Duhamel's formula bounds observation differences by
$C\|A-A'\|_{C^0}$ and derivative differences by
$C\|A-A'\|_{C^0}\|h\|_{C^0}$, also for these signed densities.
On the fixed rescaled loop region the actual variation has coefficients
$(\rho a/f,b)$, with $f$ and $f^{-1}$ bounded there. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-observation-restriction}
 \|(\rho a/f,b)\|_{C^0(K)}
       \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}(1+\rho M_1)\|U\|_{X_\rho}
                                      \leq CT^{1/2}\|U\|_{X_\rho}.
\end{equation}
In particular the linear observation norm is $CT^{1/2}$.
Its ordinary connection quadratic remainder costs $CT$; composition
with the ambient chart remainder will be estimated below.

\subsection{Finite bundle derivatives and the standard kernel}

\begin{lemma}[finite pregluing derivatives]\label{lem:gluing-finite-derivatives}
Fix $c,\rho$ and a smooth four-real-dimensional family $E(t)$
in its attaching fiber, with the actual attaching representative
$\alpha(c)$ independent of $t$. Let $W_j=\partial_{t_j}x_{\rm pre}$
in the actual linear domain and fixed target transport. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-finite-derivative-bounds}
 \|W_j\|_{X_\rho}\leq C,\qquad
 \|D_\rho W_j\|_{Y_\rho}\leq CT^{-40}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}.
\end{equation}
On the fixed rescaled observation region $W_j$ converges in $C^0$
to its zero-attaching limiting bundle representative.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Include one bundle derivative in the finite-order cap estimates.
The derivative vanishes outside $|w|\leq2R_2$, hence only the
raw cap factor of $X_\rho$ is used. With $r_2=R_2^{-1}$,
the surface and positive cap derivatives vanish at infinity and
are $O(|\zeta|)$. Their tail cutoff has strong
$H^{1+\varepsilon}$ norm $O(r_2^{1-\varepsilon})$.
The differentiated matching interpolation remainder has two
$O(r_2)$ difference factors, hence norm $O(r_2^{2-\varepsilon})$.
Consequently the strong surface and positive norms are bounded.
The normalized base derivative is
$a_j=\rho^{-1}\chi\partial_{t_j}A_{B,\rm cap}$ and satisfies
\[
 \|a_j/M_1\|_{H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
                       \leq C\frac\delta{\rho M_1}R_2^\varepsilon,
 \qquad
 \|\rho d_\alpha a_j\|_{H_B^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}
                                              \leq C\delta R_2^\varepsilon.
\]
The cutoff derivative's size cancels its two-dimensional $L^2$
scale before the fractional factor is included. These quantities
tend to zero. For the last weak row use the full surface smoothness
of these finite parameter representatives. Extend $f_\infty$
through the deleted tail with size $CR_2^2$ and derivative
$CR_2^3$. Its fractional multiplier has norm $CR_2^{2+\varepsilon}$,
so $\|\rho f_\infty d_\alpha b_j\|_{H^\varepsilon H^k}
\leq C\rho R_2^{2+\varepsilon}$. This tends to zero and
imposes no new derivative on arbitrary graph inputs.

On the interpolation annulus, both the cap/residual surface difference
and its bundle derivative are $O(q)$, and the base difference
derivative is $O(\delta)\leq O(\eta)$, with the notation
\eqref{eq:gluing-overlap-coefficients}. Differentiate the affine
cancellation of the exact cap and residual equations before estimating
the error. It is still exact because the cap solves its equation
for every $t$. The quadratic interpolation terms retain two
difference factors after differentiation. The scalar error is therefore
bounded by the same
$C((v/\rho)\eta+(v^2/\rho)\eta^2+(\rho/v^2)q^2)$,
and the mixed and positive errors by the same quantities as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-pregluing-error}. Differentiated target frames
multiply the already bounded error by bounded coefficients. This
proves the second estimate in \eqref{eq:gluing-finite-derivative-bounds}.
On fixed rescaled compact sets the pregluing is the exact cap, and
its metric and finite bundle derivative converge to their limiting
base-flat representatives by the Dirichlet estimates. This proves
$C^0$ convergence on the loops. No scale derivative has been used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[fixed-scale reduction of geometric kernel fields]
\label{lem:gluing-linear-gauge-reduction}
At a smooth representative of a corrected graph solution, every
geometric kernel variation from the complete graph chart can be changed
by an infinitesimal gauge into the ordinary augmented kernel, at a
sufficiently small mixed-end weight. On the observation region this
change preserves the observation differentials. The assertion is at
fixed positive scale.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On a mixed end take the smooth representative $A^s$ in temporal
gauge, with limit $B_\infty$, and write the original graph
connection as $A^o=B_p+\xi$ with weighted $H^1$ remainder.
The smoothing gauge satisfies $\partial_\theta g=-A^o_\theta g$,
up to the action convention. Unitarity gives, for $s>t$,
\[
 \|g(s)-g(t)\|_{L^2(Y)}
 \leq C_\omega e^{-\omega t}
                    \|e^{\omega\theta}A^o_\theta\|_{L^2([t,\infty)\times Y)}.
\]
Thus it has an almost everywhere unitary $L^2$ limit $g_\infty$
and weighted $L^2$ convergence for every smaller weight.
Passing to the spatial gauge equation along slices where the original
remainder tends to zero gives
$d_Yg_\infty=B_\infty g_\infty-g_\infty B_p$ in distributions.
Its smooth coefficients and bounded solution imply smoothness by
first-order bootstrapping. Subtracting the two spatial equations yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-smoothing-gauge-end}
 \begin{split}
 d_Y(g-g_\infty)={}&B_\infty(g-g_\infty)-(g-g_\infty)B_p\\
                 &+(A_Y^s-B_\infty)g-g(A_Y^o-B_p).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Together with the temporal equation this proves weighted $H^1$
convergence, without a supremum decay assertion for the gauge.

Conjugating a decaying $H^1$ variation preserves that regularity:
the only rough differentiated product is bounded by
$\|A^o-B_p\|_{L^4}\|V_0\|_{L^4}$, with a smaller weight.
For a finite smooth tangent first subtract its conjugate by
$g_\infty$, and use \eqref{eq:gluing-smoothing-gauge-end}.
The transformed field is therefore a finite smooth end tangent
plus a weighted $H^1$ remainder. On the ordinary gauge end
use the same equation with its unweighted $H^1$ bounds and
smooth bounded limiting coefficients. The positive variation already
has the corresponding end form.

At a mixed limit decompose its critical tangent as
$v_\infty=h_\infty+d_{B_\infty}\zeta_\infty$ by the compact
three-dimensional covariant Neumann Laplacian. This zero-form
operator is invertible: a parallel section vanishes on the flat
irreducible boundary fiber. Gauge covariance preserves the linearized
critical equation, so $h_\infty$ is exactly the harmonic critical
tangent, with the same surface class and positive value. Subtract
$d_A(\tau\zeta_\infty)$ globally. The normal trace and geometric
equation remain unchanged, and the remaining field $V'$ has
precisely the allowed finite harmonic tangent. Its Coulomb source
$d_A^*V'$ is weighted $L^2$ on mixed ends and ordinary $L^2$
on the other ends.

Solve
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-fixed-neumann-gauge}
                  d_A^*d_A\zeta=d_A^*V',\qquad \nabla_n\zeta=0.
\end{equation}
At this fixed scale the operator is invertible from weighted $H^2$
to $L^2$ for a small positive mixed-end weight and zero ordinary
end weight. Here are its Fredholm and kernel checks. The limiting
compact cross-sectional zero-form Laplacians have positive gaps by
irreducibility. On each product tail, the operator is
$-\partial_\theta^2+\Delta_B$; the separated Green kernels
have indicial exponents $\pm\sqrt{\lambda_j}$ bounded away
from zero. They give inverses at sufficiently small positive,
negative or zero weights. Cut them off against a compact-core
Neumann parametrix. Commutators are compact by Rellich, and the
smoothly converging tail coefficients give arbitrarily small remaining
$H^2\to L^2$ error. This proves Fredholmness on the actual
normal domain. At weight zero it is self-adjoint, and the energy
identity and irreducibility kill its kernel; its index is zero.
Weight deformation below the indicial gap keeps this index.

One can also eliminate the possible negative-weight formal cokernel
directly. A weak cokernel section is smooth by local Neumann regularity.
On a temporal tail put $f(\theta)=\|\zeta(\theta)\|_{L^2(Y)}$.
The equation and boundary condition give, weakly also at zeros,
\[
 f f''=\|\partial_\theta\zeta\|_2^2-(f')^2
                      +\|d_{B(\theta)}\zeta\|_2^2\geq\lambda f^2.
\]
Compare on $[t_0,S]$ with $h''=\lambda h$ having the same
endpoint values. Negative weighted $L^2$ gives a sequence $S$
with $f(S)e^{-\omega S}$ bounded. For $\omega<\sqrt\lambda$,
the far endpoint contribution tends to zero, giving
$f(\theta)\leq f(t_0)e^{-\sqrt\lambda(\theta-t_0)}$.
Unit-strip elliptic estimates give decay of first derivatives as well.
On the ordinary end they already have $L^2$ bounds. Exhaustion
and integration by parts now give $d_A\zeta=0$, hence zero.
The positive-weight kernel is killed by the same energy identity.
This verifies \eqref{eq:gluing-fixed-neumann-gauge}; no bound
uniform in $\rho$ has been asserted.

Subtracting $d_A\zeta$ gives an ordinary first-order augmented
kernel element. Weak mixed regularity makes it a smooth kernel
representative. The observations are unchanged also for the initially
weak potential. On their compact region the original connection
and variation are continuous; the gauge is initially $W^{2,p}$,
$p>2$, hence continuous. Its first-order equation then makes it
$C^1$. The transformed variation is continuous, and its difference
from the final smooth one is a continuous $d_A\zeta$.
Since locally $\zeta\in H^2$ in dimension four, it is in
every finite $L^q$; the identity
$d\zeta=d_A\zeta-[A,\zeta]$ gives $W^{1,q}$, then
continuity and $C^1$. The ordinary loop derivative of this gauge
variation vanishes. The finite smooth end potentials do not change
that local argument.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[observations on the ordinary moduli tangent]
\label{cor:gluing-observation-coordinates}
At any corrected zero of graph size $CT^{-37}$, the differential
of $\mathcal O_{c,\rho}$ is an isomorphism from the ordinary
four-dimensional moduli tangent to $\mathbb R^4$, for sufficiently
small $\rho$ on the chosen patch.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let $L=D_\rho+dN_\rho(U)$ and use its geometric right inverse
$\widetilde Q$ of size $CT^2$. Put
$K_j=(I-\widetilde QL)W_j$. Then $LK_j=0$, and
\[
 \|K_j-W_j\|_{X_\rho}
      \leq CT^2(T^{-40}+T^4T^{-37})+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}
                                           \leq CT^{-31}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}.
\]
By \eqref{eq:gluing-observation-restriction}, the rescaled
connection error is $CT^{-61/2}$. The chart derivative differs
from the identity on the loops by its pointwise quadratic estimate;
the corrected surface displacement is $O(T^{-73/2})$, and the
cap metric error is exponentially small. Duhamel's derivative
estimate therefore makes the observation matrix on
$D\Phi_\rho(U)K_j$ converge to the invertible limiting matrix
of Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-limiting-observations}.
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-linear-gauge-reduction} identifies these as
independent ordinary kernel classes with the same observations.
Their dimension is four by
\eqref{eq:gluing-standard-kernel-dimension}, so they form a basis.
These kernel vectors have not been asserted to be derivatives of
a particular choice of corrected solution.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The observation-normalized exact solution}

\begin{proposition}[normalized correction]\label{prop:gluing-normalized-correction}
At fixed $(c,\rho,E)$ in the chosen patch there is an exact
solution $x^{\mathcal O}_{c,\rho,E}=\Phi_\rho(U^{\mathcal O})$
with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-normalized-correction}
 \mathcal O_{c,\rho}(x^{\mathcal O}_{c,\rho,E})=\mathcal O_0(E),
                           \qquad \|U^{\mathcal O}\|_{X_\rho}\leq CT^{-37}.
\end{equation}
It is a regular ordinary moduli point of dimension four, and the
observation values determine it uniquely in an ordinary moduli
neighborhood at that positive scale.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Set $H_\rho(U)=\mathcal O_{c,\rho}(\Phi_\rho(U))-\mathcal O_0(E)$
and augment the equation to
$\mathbb F_\rho=(F_\rho,H_\rho):X_\rho\to Y_\rho\oplus\mathbb R^4$,
with the sum target norm. On the fixed rescaled loops the pregluing
is the exact cap and differs from its limit by $C\delta$.
Thus $|H_\rho(0)|\leq C\delta$ and
$\mathfrak e_\rho=\|\mathbb F_\rho(0)\|
\leq CT^{-40}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}$.

Write $D=D_\rho$, $Q=Q_\rho$, and $\ell=dH_\rho(0)$,
so $\|\ell\|\leq CT^{1/2}$. For the four finite derivatives
put $Z_j=(I-QD)W_j$ and $Zt=\sum t_jZ_j$.
Then $DZ=0$, $\|Z\|\leq C$, and
$\|Z_j-W_j\|\leq CT^{-38}$. The matrix $M=\ell Z$
differs from the invertible limiting observation matrix by
$O(T^{-75/2})+O(e^{-T}T^{C_J})$, and hence $\|M^{-1}\|\leq C$.
The space
\[
                       \mathcal B_\rho=\operatorname{im}Q\oplus Z\mathbb R^4
\]
is a closed direct sum in the actual complete domain: $QD$
is a bounded projection, and applying $D$ and then $M$ proves
the intersection is zero. Its coordinate inverse is
$V\mapsto(DV,M^{-1}\ell(V-QDV))$.
On this space $\mathbb A_\rho=(D,\ell)$ is an isomorphism,
with explicit inverse
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-augmented-inverse}
 \mathbb Q_\rho(f,h)=Qf+ZM^{-1}(h-\ell Qf),\qquad
 \|\mathbb Q_\rho(f,h)\|\leq CT^{5/2}\|f\|+C|h|.
\end{equation}
In particular $\mathfrak q_\rho=\|\mathbb Q_\rho\|\leq CT^3$.
This is a specified complement, not an inverse on the whole
geometric domain with its gauge kernel.

The ambient chart remainder has quadratic constant $CT^4$.
Restriction to the loops costs $CT^{1/2}$, so composition in
the observation remainder costs at most $CT^{9/2}$. Its
ordinary connection quadratic term costs only $CT$.
Duhamel's higher derivatives and the chart difference estimate give,
on the complete ball of radius $cT^{-5}$,
\[
 \|\mathbb N_\rho(U)-\mathbb N_\rho(V)\|
       \leq CT^5(\|U\|+\|V\|)\|U-V\|,
 \qquad
 \mathbb F_\rho(U)=\mathbb F_\rho(0)+\mathbb A_\rho U+\mathbb N_\rho(U).
\]
The equation is $C^1$ by the preceding proof; the observations
are $C^1$ by continuous restriction to $C^0$ and the smooth
path ODE maps. The finite end corrections lie outside the loop region.

On the closed $\mathcal B_\rho$ ball of radius
$2\overline{\mathfrak q}_\rho\overline{\mathfrak e}_\rho$
use the contraction
$U\mapsto-\mathbb Q_\rho(\mathbb F_\rho(0)+\mathbb N_\rho(U))$.
Fixed upper bounds permit this radius even if the actual initial
error vanishes. Its size is $CT^{-37}$ and its Lipschitz
constant is bounded by $CT^{5+6-40}=CT^{-29}<1/2$.
The ball lies in the chart. Completeness and the isomorphism
\eqref{eq:gluing-augmented-inverse} give the exact augmented zero,
unique within that ball. This choice may differ from the first
correction, which used only $\operatorname{im}Q$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-standard-regularity} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:gluing-observation-coordinates} apply at its
$CT^{-37}$ size. The ordinary inverse function theorem makes
$\mathcal O_{c,\rho}$ a coordinate map near this point, proving
the stated local moduli uniqueness. This does not assert that its
neighborhood contains all solutions near a broken configuration.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Continuity of the normalized solutions at positive scale}

For parameter dependence we fix the optional finite cap normalization.
Let $K_{\rho,E}$ be the four-dimensional cap kernel with both
attaching values zero, and let $\Pi_K$ be its projection for
the surface and positive $L^2$ inner product. The Gram matrix
is uniformly invertible and its smooth basis has bounded graph norm
by Proposition~\ref{prop:attaching-kernel-normalization}. Replace
$Q^0$ in the preceding construction by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-canonical-cap-inverse}
                            Q^{\rm can}_{\rho,E}=(I-\Pi_K)Q^0_{\rho,E}.
\end{equation}
Its equation, ordinary base gauge, normal trace and zero attaching
values are unchanged, with bound $C\varepsilon^{-1}R_1^\varepsilon$.
It is now the unique output of its equation with $\Pi_KU=0$:
a difference is a kernel vector annihilated by its kernel projection.
In particular it is independent of the temporary spectral truncation
and, on common inputs, of the permitted exponent $\varepsilon$.
Use this choice in $P_\rho$, $Q_\rho$ and
\eqref{eq:gluing-augmented-inverse} from now on. All preceding
bounds and identities persist. This specifies the selected solution;
it need not agree with a choice that omitted the finite projection.

\begin{proposition}[positive-scale continuity]\label{prop:gluing-parameter-continuity}
With \eqref{eq:gluing-canonical-cap-inverse}, the normalized solutions
$[x^{\mathcal O}_{c,\rho,E}]$ are continuous in the ordinary
moduli topology on each chosen local parameter patch with $\rho>0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First allow the simultaneous range
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-exponent-window}
                      (2T)^{-1}\leq\varepsilon\leq2/T.
\end{equation}
All preceding estimates hold there with the same powers of $T$:
$\varepsilon^{-1}\asymp T$, $\rho^{-\varepsilon}\leq e^2$,
and $R_j^{C\varepsilon}$ is bounded. The cap inverse is the
same on common inputs by uniqueness, and the augmented fixed residual
inverse is the same by its index-zero uniqueness. The formulas for
cutoffs, profiles, based gauge potentials and paired transports contain
no exponent. Thus $P_\rho$ is the same distributional operator,
and its Neumann series has identical terms and converges in every
norm in \eqref{eq:gluing-exponent-window}. The same is true of
$Q_\rho$, $Z$, $M$ and $\mathbb Q_\rho$. The nonlinear
chart and almost everywhere holonomy formulas are also independent
of the exponent. Starting the augmented iteration at zero gives
identical iterates in the highest space and every lower space in
this interval. Their limits agree as distributions. Hence the chosen
solution is independent of this auxiliary exponent.

Fix $\lambda_0=(c_0,\rho_0,E_0)$ with $\rho_0>0$ and use
one fixed $\varepsilon_*>0$ in every nearby exponent window.
At fixed positive scale the cap metric depends smoothly on the finite
parameters: its Dirichlet linearization is elliptic and invertible,
so the usual implicit function theorem applies. The normalized base
gauge and its finite attaching conditions have the same property.
Polar matching maps identify the nearby cap domains, preserve the
ordinary base gauge and total zero attaching value, and gain one
surface derivative in their differences. Thus the coefficients and
finite Gram projection give a continuous family of bounded isomorphisms
$(\mathcal A_{\rm cap},\Pi_K)$ in fixed-exponent operator charts.
The inverse identity gives continuous, in fact smooth, local dependence
of \eqref{eq:gluing-canonical-cap-inverse}. This uses no derivative
of Sobolev rescaling on a rough input.

We identify the global spaces next. Let $h_\lambda=\rho R_1(\rho)$.
Choose smooth physical base diffeomorphisms supported inside the fixed
perturbation-free collar, preserving the seam, its normal lines, and
both sides, which align the $\theta_1$ interfaces with those at
$\lambda_0$. Near the interface use translation to $c$ and
dilation $h_\lambda/h_{\lambda_0}$. On the smaller observation
region use translation and the physical scale ratio $\rho/\rho_0$,
so that all observation loops become fixed. Radial interpolation between
these separated regions and the identity gives diffeomorphisms near
$\lambda_0$; even normal cutoffs preserve normal lines. Use the
same map on the positive side and the actual polar transports to
identify matching domains. The ends, perturbation dependencies and
finite end coordinates stay fixed.

If the based residual representative of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-residual-background} changes with the center,
include its smooth unitary gauge in these identifications. It is
supported in a fixed collar and is identity at the ends and dependency
regions. Covariance conjugates its augmented residual operator and
inverse to the single fixed residual problem. Thus references to
the fixed residual inverse here concern a fixed gauge class with
these explicit smooth representative identifications, not an assumption
that the point-normalized representative is independent of $c$.

These maps give locally uniformly bounded identifications with fixed
complete spaces $X_*,Y_*$. Aligning $\theta_1$ aligns its two
unequal-regularity factors. On the raw cap factor, base pullback
preserves separate Sobolev orders and does not change exterior surface
differentiation into a full gradient. Changing $d_\alpha$ adds
only a zeroth-order coefficient, and $d_\alpha(W-I)b$ is
order zero. On the overlap and residual factor the full additional
surface derivative is already present. The support of the coordinate
change lies strictly where $\chi=\varphi=1$, so the transition
of the surface regularizer and all weighted ends stay fixed. At
this positive scale all retained coefficient maps and their inverses
are smooth and finite. Their pullbacks are strongly continuous,
by approximation in the ambient localized graph spaces and the local
uniform bounds. One may first regularize in the surface, commuting
its elliptic regularizer past the exterior derivatives, and then
regularize in the base. The lower-order commutators are controlled
by the strong norms. No operator-norm derivative of a rough pullback
is asserted.

In these charts $D_\lambda:X_*\to Y_*$ is continuous in
operator norm. Its transported rows have smoothly varying coefficients
of controlled base derivatives and the exterior derivatives
$d_\alpha a,d_\alpha b$; the polar error is surface order zero.
The residual factor controls the stronger local derivatives, and all
nonlocal perturbation regions are unchanged. This is an operator
coefficient assertion after transport, distinct from norm continuity
of raw pullback.

The constructed $P_\lambda$ is strongly continuous. Source
cutoffs and conversions have that property; the fixed residual inverse
is bounded and the canonical cap inverse has the local dependence
just established. The only additional issue is residual evaluation
on a moving homogeneous disc. Near $\lambda_0$ all such discs
contain a common smaller positive-size disc. After the fixed residual
identification, differences of the inverse outputs solve the same
homogeneous equation there. Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-residual-homogeneous}
upgrades their graph convergence to every fixed local base and surface
order on a smaller disc. Moving point values and derivatives in the
based gauge are therefore continuous. Dilation turns its moving
Dirichlet and Neumann potential problems into fixed-disc problems
with continuously varying normalized coefficients. The exact and
harmonic profiles then vary continuously. The cap output has a strict
support margin $|w|\leq R_1/4$ inside $\theta_1=1$, so it
never acquires a stronger exterior norm when the interface moves.
The residual field already has that norm where it crosses the interface.

For completeness, the density used in this strong-continuity argument
can be checked in the actual source norm. Truncate weighted ends by
flow cutoffs, which commute with all surface and finite-rank terms;
fractional truncations converge by integrable tails. Regularize next
in $\Sigma$ by a fixed elliptic resolvent $J_\eta$, which
commutes with $\chi$. Expanding $\Gamma^j$ gives
$\chi^j\Lambda_{\Sigma,\theta}^j$ plus lower surface-order
and finite-rank terms. Since the principal symbol is independent
of $\theta$, its commutator with $J_\eta$ has at most
$j-1$ surface orders and is controlled by lower $\Gamma$
norms. The commutators are uniformly bounded and tend strongly
to zero, first on smooth surface fields and then by the lower-order
bounds. This preserves $\varphi F\in H_B^{\varepsilon_*}H^k_\Sigma$.
After surface regularization all surface orders are finite; base and
interior smoothing then converge in the source norm, using bounded
extension across the seam below its trace threshold. A diagonal
choice gives smooth compact source approximation. For the ambient
domain, a base derivative of these commutators adds
$\chi^{j-1}d\chi$ times at most $j-1$ surface derivatives,
again controlled by the lower norms. There is no division by $\chi$.
This also justifies the ambient approximation used above.

Put $R_\lambda=D_\lambda P_\lambda-I$. It is strongly
continuous. Its norm in an arbitrary equivalent common norm need
not be below $1/2$, but its powers satisfy
\[
                        \|R_\lambda^n\|_{Y_*\to Y_*}
                                            \leq C_{\lambda_0}2^{-n}.
\]
Conjugate the whole power to the intrinsic $Y_\lambda$ norm;
the two identification bounds occur once, not once per factor.
The Neumann series therefore converges locally uniformly in operator
norm and is strongly continuous, as is $Q_\lambda$.
The finite $W_j$ and their sources are continuous. The observation
functional $\ell_\lambda$ is norm-continuous because its loops
are fixed in the chosen chart. Consequently $Z_\lambda$,
$M_\lambda^{-1}$ and \eqref{eq:gluing-augmented-inverse} are
strongly continuous.

The transported nonlinear remainders are jointly continuous in
$\lambda,U$. On local regions use the fixed-exponent product and
surface-gaining composition estimates; on the unchanged dependency
regions use Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-holonomy-estimate} and the
fixed summability class. Exact end subtraction uses one smooth
finite family, and observations use continuous $C^0$ restriction.
Starting the augmented iteration at zero thus gives continuous
$X_*$-valued iterates. The intrinsic contraction estimate implies
\[
                   \|U_{\lambda,n}-U_\lambda^{\mathcal O}\|_{X_*}
                                               \leq C_{\lambda_0}2^{-n}.
\]
Uniform convergence proves graph continuity, including the finite
end coordinates, without requiring a contraction constant below one
in the particular common norm.

We now pass to ordinary moduli continuity, which requires control
of the ends as well as compact sets. For a sequence
$\lambda_i\to\lambda_0$, graph convergence gives physical
$W^{1,p}$ convergence on compact sets for a fixed $p>2$ and
positive $C^0$ convergence. On each mixed end subtract its own
exact representative $B_{p_i}$; the finite parameters converge,
and the remainders converge in weighted $H^1$. On ordinary ends
there is ordinary $H^1$ convergence. Formula
\eqref{eq:gluing-analytical-end-curvature}, the $L^2$ holonomy
Lipschitz estimate and $H^1\subset L^4$ show that the analytical
energy densities are uniformly integrable with uniformly small tails.
For the ordinary gauge end use the fixed residual solution and its
integrable perturbed curvature; the positive chart gives the same
conclusion for map energy.

On compact sets the $W^{1,p}$ bound gives an $L^p$ curvature
bound because $a^2\in L^{2p/(4-p)}$ with exponent larger than
$p$, taking $p<4$. Hence energy cannot concentrate at an interior
point or on a shrinking matching neighborhood. The positive
$W^{1,p}$ bound excludes positive and boundary concentration as well.
The compactness theorem \cite[Theorem~5.2]{DaeFukLip21a},
with the all-order perturbation class and empty concentration sets,
gives subsequential smooth convergence on compact sets modulo gauge.
It includes the full compact secondary dependency sets.

This smooth limit represents the graph limit. For the gauges relating
the original $A_i$ to the smoothly convergent $A_i'$, the equation
$dg_i=g_iA_i'-A_i g_i$ and unitarity give local $W^{1,q}$
bounds, $q=4p/(4-p)>4$. Differentiating gives $W^{2,p}$
bounds since $q/2>p$. Compact embedding and a diagonal subsequence
give a limiting unitary gauge relating the smooth limit to $A_0$.
The positive limit is already $u_0$. Thus no continuous prior
choice of smoothing gauges was needed.

On a mixed tail the topological integral required for the small-tail
theorem is also uniformly small. Write $A=B_p+\xi$ and note
that $F_{B_p}\wedge F_{B_p}=0$ because it is pulled back
from a three-manifold. Expanding leaves
$F_{B_p}\wedge d_{B_p}\xi$, $F_{B_p}\wedge\xi^2$,
$d_{B_p}\xi\wedge d_{B_p}\xi$, $d_{B_p}\xi\wedge\xi^2$
and $\xi^4$. With weighted $H^1$ bound $K$, weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the first absolute integral by
$Ce^{-\omega R}K$, and weighted $H^1\subset L^4$ bounds
the others by $Ce^{-\omega R}(K^2+K^3+K^4)$, after decreasing
the weight. Positive area is bounded by energy. The uniform tail
therefore meets the fixed threshold of
\cite[Proposition~5.29]{DaeFukLip21a}. Temporal gauges give
uniform smooth exponential decay to the individual critical limits.
The argument of \cite[equation~(5.36)]{DaeFukLip21a} promotes
compact convergence to weighted convergence after subtracting those
limits: split at a large $R$, use compact convergence before $R$,
and bound the weighted tail in each fixed order by $C_l e^{-cR}$.
The limiting classes are the original $p_i$ classes, fixed by
the positive limits and embedded critical Lagrangians, and converge
to $p_0$.

Uniformly small ordinary analytical tails exclude a nonconstant
trajectory or strip escaping to infinity: translating a compact piece
of such a limit would have positive energy in a uniformly vanishing
tail. Global $H^1$ control relative to the residual also fixes
the limiting critical classes. To make the ordinary Sobolev conclusion
explicit, choose cross-sectional gauge slices near the fixed
nondegenerate critical points, or the corresponding strip coordinates.
Their self-adjoint first-order Hessians have a fixed spectral gap.
The inverse estimate and a small-neighborhood absorption bound the
$H^1$ displacement by the $L^2$ gradient. The local action
is quadratic in the displacement, so remaining energy satisfies
$E'(t)\leq-cE(t)$ along the gradient equation. Every sufficiently
far strip stays in that neighborhood: otherwise compactness of a
translated sequence, uniform small tails, and the fixed class would
give a zero-energy limit with a nonzero critical displacement.
Unit-strip elliptic estimates upgrade uniform exponential energy
decay to every fixed smooth order. Splitting into compact and tail
parts gives the ordinary unweighted Sobolev convergence. This is the
no-energy-loss case of the ordinary-end discussion in
\cite[Section~5]{DaeFukLip21a}; its later index-at-most-one
exclusion is not used here.

The smooth limit is related to the smooth representative of $x_0$
by an allowed gauge: on temporal tails their relating gauge has zero
temporal derivative and the required limiting cross-sectional value.
After the prescribed ordinary-end identifications that value is a
stabilizer. Its spatial equation gives smoothness. Every subsequence
therefore has a further subsequence converging to $[x_0]$ in
the ordinary moduli topology, at any fixed finite Sobolev order and
a sufficiently small common mixed-end weight. The effective local
slice makes this topology metrizable. The full sequence converges,
proving the proposition.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Smoothness and nonsingularity of the parameter map}

Evaluation at infinity is a submersion on the regular primitive patch,
and the two affine normal functions have independent differentials
inside its fibers by \eqref{eq:gluing-affine-normal-matrix}.
The finite-dimensional submersion theorem therefore gives a local
smooth family $E=E(c,t_1,t_2)$ with
\[
          \operatorname{ev}_\infty E(c,t)=[\alpha(c)],\qquad
                   \operatorname{Re}\kappa(E)=0,\qquad s(E)=1.
\]
Use the based surface representative $\alpha(c)$ in this family.
It is independent of the scale. The $t$ derivatives span the
two-dimensional normalized fiber tangent. Shrink the coordinate
neighborhood so that the fixed four observations above work throughout,
and take constants on its compact closure. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-local-parameter-map}
       \Glue_{\rm loc}(c,\rho,t)
                  =[x^{\mathcal O}_{c,\rho,E(c,t)}].
\end{equation}

\begin{proof}[Completion of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-local-gluing}]
At every positive scale the image point is an ordinary regular
four-dimensional moduli point, and $d_x\mathcal O_{c,\rho}$
is an isomorphism by
Corollary~\ref{cor:gluing-observation-coordinates}. On its ordinary
moduli neighborhood consider
\[
           G(c,\rho,t,x)=\mathcal O_{c,\rho}(x)-\mathcal O_0(E(c,t)).
\]
It is jointly smooth. Choose high Sobolev slice orders for any fixed
number of moving-loop derivatives; elliptic regularity identifies
these finite-dimensional moduli charts. The limiting Dirichlet metric
is a smooth finite family as well. The implicit function theorem
gives a unique smooth zero branch near each parameter point.
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-parameter-continuity} puts the selected
solutions in that same neighborhood, and their defining observation
equations identify them with this branch. Thus
\eqref{eq:gluing-local-parameter-map} is smooth for $\rho>0$.

We identify its differential by affine equivariance of the limiting
reconstruction. For $g(w)=aw+b$, holomorphic pullback preserves
the trace-free base-flat equation and pulls its stable boundary
projectively flat metric to the corresponding metric of $g^*E$.
Dirichlet uniqueness therefore identifies the projective zero-scale
metric of $g^*E$ with $g^*H_0(E)$, and hence
\[
                              [A^0_{g^*E}]=[g^*A_E^0].
\]
The determinant reference was chosen pulled back from $\Sigma$
on the negative product, so it is invariant under this affine
pullback. Locally the projective gauge lifts to a determinant-preserving
unitary identification. Thus this equality applies to the actual
trace functions, with no central base-curvature contribution.
The positive classifying map has the same equivariance.

Let $\widehat A_\lambda$ be the normalized solution pulled back
by $(w,x)\mapsto(c+\rho w,x)$ to the loop region. The graph
correction and \eqref{eq:gluing-observation-restriction} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-rescaled-observation-error}
        \|\widehat A_\lambda-A^0_{E(c,t)}\|_{C^0(K)}
                         \leq CT^{-73/2}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}.
\end{equation}
The chart's pointwise quadratic term is smaller, and the pregluing
is exactly the cap there. Apply Duhamel's value estimate with
$\sigma_T,\sigma_D$ in
\eqref{eq:gluing-explicit-observation-derivatives}. Equivariance
and the same change of variables in the limiting average imply
\[
              \mathcal O_0^{\sigma_T}(A_E^0)=d\mathcal O_0(E)T_E,
              \qquad
              \mathcal O_0^{\sigma_D}(A_E^0)=d\mathcal O_0(E)D_E.
\]
These are left compositions on the loops: translation changes
$(a,b)$ to $(a,b+h)$ and dilation to $(e^ha,e^hb)$,
which fixes both signs and the two-dimensional Jacobian.

Put $L_\lambda=d_x\mathcal O_{c,\rho}$ on the ordinary moduli
tangent. Differentiate the exact normalization
$\mathcal O_{c,\rho}(\Glue_{\rm loc}(c,\rho,t))=\mathcal O_0(E(c,t))$,
now justified by smoothness. On the parameter frame
$(\rho\partial_c,\rho\partial_\rho,\partial_{t_1},\partial_{t_2})$
the columns of $L_\lambda d\Glue_{\rm loc}$ are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gluing-parameter-columns}
 \begin{aligned}
 J_c&=\rho\,d\mathcal O_0(E)\partial_cE
                        -\mathcal O^{\sigma_T}_{c,\rho}(x^{\mathcal O}_\lambda),\\
 J_\rho&=-\mathcal O^{\sigma_D}_{c,\rho}(x^{\mathcal O}_\lambda),\\
 J_{t_1}&=d\mathcal O_0(E)\partial_{t_1}E,\qquad
 J_{t_2}=d\mathcal O_0(E)\partial_{t_2}E.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
There is no scale derivative of $E$. Its bounded $c$ derivative
is multiplied by $\rho$. By
\eqref{eq:gluing-rescaled-observation-error}, these columns differ
by at most $C\rho+CT^{-73/2}+Ce^{-T}T^{C_J}$ from
\[
              d\mathcal O_0(E)(-T_E,-D_E,\partial_{t_1}E,\partial_{t_2}E).
\]
This limiting matrix is invertible by
\eqref{eq:gluing-affine-normal-matrix} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-limiting-observations}, uniformly on the
smaller compact patch. Thus \eqref{eq:gluing-parameter-columns}
is invertible for every sufficiently small positive scale.
Since $L_\lambda$ is an isomorphism, $d\Glue_{\rm loc}$
is invertible. The displayed parameter frame is a basis whenever
$\rho>0$, so the ordinary inverse function theorem gives the
claimed local diffeomorphism. Its correction is $CT^{-37}$ by
\eqref{eq:gluing-normalized-correction}, and its topological
component is the one computed in
\eqref{eq:gluing-relative-characteristic-class}. This completes
the local theorem.
\end{proof}

The two minus signs in the limiting parameter matrix do not by
themselves give the determinant-line orientation comparison. The
coordinate rotation, ordered gluing parameters and coherent orientations
must also be retained.
Proposition~\ref{prop:analytic-kernel-orientation} identifies the
derivative with an explicit analytic kernel map and computes the
finite complex sign. Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-orientation}
then establishes the excision comparison, and
Section~\ref{sec:reference-comparison} identifies the coherent signs.
The four holonomy functions serve as coordinates; the point-incidence
class is constructed in Section~\ref{sec:local-seam-incidence}.
Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-chart-compatibility} gives injectivity
on a whole small parameter product. The hypotheses for converse
gluing are stated at the start of Section~\ref{sec:regular-converse}.



\subsection{The constructed broken limit}\label{sec:constructed-limit}

We now identify the limit of the local construction. Throughout this
subsection the parameters stay in a compact subset of one sufficiently
small regular seam-admissible patch from
Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-local-gluing}. The residual $x_0=(A_0,u_0)$ is
fixed. Write $x_\rho=(A_\rho,u_\rho)$ for the observation-normalized
exact solution, $x_{\rm pre}$ for its pregluing, and
\[
                  s_\rho=T^{-37},\qquad T=\log(1/\rho).
\]
The notation $x_E^0=(A_E^0,u_E)$ denotes the smooth compactified
base-flat reconstruction of the primitive bundle $E$. In its prescribed
unitary frame the negative base component is zero. A hat denotes physical
pullback under $z=c+\rho w$.

Convergence of holomorphic bundles on compact sets means convergence of
their holomorphic transition functions on a finite product cover, after
bundle identifications. In particular this notion retains an unstable
surface fiber and is stronger than convergence of classifying maps on the
stable locus alone. No insertion points are marked in the following result.

\begin{theorem}[limit of the constructed family]
\label{thm:constructed-broken-limit}
Suppose $\rho_\nu\to0$, $c_\nu\to c_*$, and $E_\nu\to E_*$ in the
above patch, with its prescribed affine normalization and attaching data.
Then the constructed solutions have the following limits.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Away from $\{c_*\}\times\Sigma_g$, the residual limit is $x_0$,
smoothly on compact sets modulo gauge. On the mixed ends there is
convergence in every prescribed weighted Sobolev order after subtraction
of the individual critical limits, for one sufficiently small positive
weight. On the ordinary ends the corresponding convergence is unweighted.
\item On every fixed rescaled compact set, including the seam, the pair
$\widehat x_{\rho_\nu}$ converges modulo local unitary gauges to
$x_{E_*}^0$ in every prescribed finite differentiability order. The doubled
holomorphic projective bundles converge to $\PP(E_*)$ in the sense just
defined, including near an interior unstable fiber.
\item The whole neck has vanishing limiting energy and surface/map
diameter. The rescaled normalized energy measures converge in total
variation to the smooth measure of $x_{E_*}^0$, of mass $\frac12$.
The component at infinity has the prescribed attaching identification and
extends holomorphically as $\PP(E_*)$, with no additional modification.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently $\Glue_{\rm loc}$ extends continuously at scale zero, on
its own image, by the prescribed residual-plus-primitive configuration.
This assertion gives neither a neighborhood of that image nor a converse
or a differentiable collar at scale zero.
\end{theorem}

\subsubsection{Compatibility of the surface completions}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:constructed-higher-surface-orders}
The observation-normalized construction is independent of the integer
surface completion. For every fixed $k\geq2$, its correction satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-higher-surface-orders}
                 \|U_\rho^{\cO}\|_{X_\rho^{(k)}}\leq C_kT^{-37}
\end{equation}
for $0<\rho<\rho_k$. The threshold may depend on $k$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Keep the canonical cap inverse, its finite $L^2$ kernel projection, the
four observations, cutoffs, paired transports, and the operators
$\Gamma_X,\Gamma_Y$ fixed before choosing a completion. In particular
their finite end projections and added scalar shifts do not depend on
$k$. The exact cap metric is also independent of the derivative order
used to estimate it. For any fixed $k$, its error is bounded by
$C_k\rho^2T^{N_k}$ for a finite $N_k$. All four radius powers in
\eqref{eq:gluing-four-radii} remain unchanged. Factors
$R_1^{C_k\varepsilon}$ are bounded for each fixed $k$, and the
exponential factor $\rho^2$ absorbs every fixed additional power of $T$.
Thus all the preceding estimates hold at this surface order.

The higher-order cap solution lies in the lower-order domain. The cap
inverse is unique after base gauge, zero total attaching value, and the
fixed kernel projection; its outputs therefore agree. The augmented
residual inverse is already unique in its ordinary weighted $H^1$
domain, including its finite end variables. Every term in the geometric
parametrix and its Neumann series is consequently independent of the
completion. The surface chart is defined by the same uniquely solvable
small elliptic equations, and the end profiles are the same exact
critical representatives. Starting nonlinear iteration at zero gives
identical iterates at each order. Their limits agree as distributions.
This proves \eqref{eq:constructed-higher-surface-orders} without comparing
contraction-ball radii or assuming uniqueness in a larger moduli
neighborhood. No single threshold valid for all $k$ is asserted.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Energy on the whole neck}

Fix a paired physical disc $D(c,r_*)$ contained in the unperturbed
Euclidean matching collar, uniformly for the allowed centers. For the
paired annulus $A(c;a,b)$ let $\mathfrak E_\rho(a,b)$ be the analytical
energy: the negative integral of $|F_{A_\rho}|^2$ and the positive
integral of $|du_\rho|^2$, with the fixed conventions of
\cite[equation~(3.5)]{DaeFukLip21a}. Perturbations vanish on this disc.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:constructed-neck}
There is $\eta_\rho\to0$, uniform on the parameter patch and independent
of subsequently chosen $R,r$, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-neck-energy}
 \mathfrak E_\rho(R\rho,r)
       \leq CR^{-2}+Cr^2+\eta_\rho,
 \quad 2\leq R\leq R_2/2,\quad 4\rho R_2\leq r\leq r_*.
\end{equation}
In the actual attaching surface frames, for every fixed $k\geq2$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-neck-diameter}
 \begin{split}
 &\sup_{A(c;R\rho,r)^-}
       \|\beta_\rho-\alpha(c)\|_{H^k_\Sigma}
   +\sup_{A(c;R\rho,r)^+}d(u_\rho,[\alpha(c)])\\
 &\hspace{35mm}\leq C_k(R^{-1}+r)+C_kT^{-73/2}+o_\rho(1).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
The final error is an exponential times a fixed power of $T$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The physical product metric in a compact cap coordinate is
$(\rho f)^2g_B+g_\Sigma$, with $1\leq f\leq M_1$. Its curvature norm
retains the three weights
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-curvature-weights}
 \|F\|_{L^2_{\rm phys}}^2
  =\| (\rho f)^{-1}F_{BB}\|_2^2
       +\|F_{B\Sigma}\|_2^2+\|\rho fF_{\Sigma\Sigma}\|_2^2,
\end{equation}
using orthonormal component conventions. The mixed integral, like the
positive Dirichlet integral, is conformally invariant in the base. For a
normalized cap base variation $\rho a$, its physical coefficient is
$a/f$. Change of variables and the two-dimensional $H^1$ embedding give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-base-lfour}
 \|a/f\|_{L^4_{\rm phys}}
       \leq C\rho^{1/2}M_1\|a/M_1\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}.
\end{equation}
Indeed the fourth power on the left is
$\rho^2\int f^{-2}|a|^4$, and $f\geq1$.

Write the actual ambient displacement as $\xi=A_\rho-A_{\rm pre}$,
including the nonlinear surface correction, and
$u_\rho=e(u_{\rm pre},\nu)$. Its norm is at most
$C(s_\rho+T^4s_\rho^2)\leq Cs_\rho$. Localize it as
$\xi_i=\theta_1\xi=(\rho a_i,b_i)$ and
$\xi_o=(1-\theta_1)\xi$. The complete norm gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-localized-displacement}
 \begin{split}
 &\|(a_i/M_1,b_i)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_BH^k_\Sigma}
   +\|\rho d_\alpha a_i\|_{L^2_BH^k_\Sigma}
   +\|\rho f d_\alpha b_i\|_{L^2_BH^k_\Sigma}
                                           \leq Cs_\rho,\\
 &\|\xi_o\|_{H^1_{\rm phys}}
       +\|\xi_o\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_{B,\rm phys}H^k_\Sigma}
                                           \leq Cs_\rho.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
All physical norms in this proof are on the fixed collar or the indicated
localized support. Its cutoff functions are one on a slightly larger
collar; finite end variables contribute only bounded smooth fields here.
Both localized fields already include their differentiated cutoffs.
Differentiating their sum requires no additional estimate of
$(d\theta_1)\xi$ in an unlocalized norm. The two-dimensional evaluation
estimate yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-pointwise-displacement}
 \|b_i\|_{C^0_BH^k_\Sigma}
    +\|\xi_o\|_{C^0_{B,\rm phys}H^k_\Sigma}
       \leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}s_\rho=CT^{-73/2}.
\end{equation}
The positive displacement has the same localized estimates.

On the inner support the preglued surface coefficients and their fixed
surface derivatives are bounded, while its compact base coefficient is
bounded by $C(\delta+\rho^2R_1^3)=o(1)$. For the residual contribution,
the based physical coefficient is $O(|z-c|)$ and conversion multiplies
it by $\rho f$, giving $O(\rho^2|\zeta|^{-3})$. The cap estimate and
interpolation give the other contributions. On the outer support the
pregluing is exactly the smooth residual.

The surface curvature difference is
\[
 d_{\beta_{\rm pre}}(b_i+b_o)+(b_i+b_o)\wedge(b_i+b_o).
\]
The differentiated inner term is bounded by $Cs_\rho+C\rho M_1s_\rho$
in physical $L^2$, including the reference change in $d_\alpha$.
Its square costs $C\rho M_1\varepsilon^{-1/2}s_\rho^2$. The outer
derivative and square cost $C(s_\rho+s_\rho^2)$; the cross term costs
$C\varepsilon^{-1/2}s_\rho^2$. Since $\rho M_1\to0$, this proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-surface-curvature}
 \|F_{\Sigma\Sigma}(A_\rho)
          -F_{\Sigma\Sigma}(A_{\rm pre})\|_{L^2_{\rm phys}}
                                                        \leq Cs_\rho.
\end{equation}
Only the exterior surface derivative in
\eqref{eq:constructed-localized-displacement} is used.

For mixed curvature the differentiated inner terms are
$d_Bb_i-\rho d_\alpha a_i$; the remaining linear terms contain either
the small compact base coefficient times $b_i$ or a bounded surface
coefficient times $\rho a_i$. Their norms are $O(s_\rho)$.
The inner quadratic term costs
$C\rho M_1\varepsilon^{-1/2}s_\rho^2$, and the outer terms cost
$C(s_\rho+s_\rho^2)$. The two cross terms, in physical coefficients,
are $(a_o)_{\rm phys}b_i$ and $(a_i/f)b_o$. Equations
\eqref{eq:constructed-base-lfour}--\eqref{eq:constructed-pointwise-displacement}
bound them by $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}s_\rho^2$ and
$C\rho^{1/2}M_1s_\rho^2$, respectively. Therefore the mixed curvature
difference also has physical $L^2$ norm at most $Cs_\rho$.

The base curvature must use the exact scalar equation:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-scalar-energy-recovery}
 (\rho f)^{-1}*_BF_{BB}(A_\rho)
                    =-\rho f *_\Sigma F_{\Sigma\Sigma}(A_\rho).
\end{equation}
The pregluing scalar error has physical $L^2$ norm $O(T^{-40})$.
Subtract its equation from
\eqref{eq:constructed-scalar-energy-recovery} and use
\eqref{eq:constructed-surface-curvature}. This proves the full estimate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-curvature-comparison}
 \|F_{A_\rho}-F_{A_{\rm pre}}\|_{L^2_{\rm phys}(D(c,r_*))}
                                                        \leq Cs_\rho.
\end{equation}
This estimate concerns exact zeros. Directly differentiating a general
raw base field would instead lose $M_1$: an interior supported field
$a=M_1s_\rho a_0$, $b=0$, has raw graph size $O(s_\rho)$, whereas
its normalized base curvature has size $M_1s_\rho$. Such a field does
not satisfy the scalar equation.

The first compact derivatives of $u_{\rm pre}$ are bounded on the inner
support. They are bounded for the cap; in the residual region they cost
$O(\rho f)$; and on the transition $\zeta=vZ$, $v=R_2^{-1}$, the
values differ by $O(v+\rho/v+\delta)=O(v)$ while the cutoff derivative
costs $v^{-1}$. On the outer support use the fixed residual derivatives.
In smooth ambient target coordinates,
\[
 |du_\rho-du_{\rm pre}|
       \leq C\bigl(|\nabla\nu|+|\nu||du_{\rm pre}|\bigr).
\]
Apply the localized estimates and conformal invariance to obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-positive-comparison}
 \|du_\rho-du_{\rm pre}\|_2\leq Cs_\rho,
       \qquad \sup d(u_\rho,u_{\rm pre})\leq CT^{-73/2}.
\end{equation}
The product in the derivative estimate uses the $L^2$ norm of $\nu$,
so it has no extra evaluation loss.

It remains to integrate the pregluing itself. Divide the annulus into
the exact cap $|w|\leq R_2$, the interpolation annulus, and the exact
residual $|z-c|\geq2\rho R_2$. Smoothness of the limiting cap at
$\zeta=0$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-cap-tail}
 \int_{|w|\geq R}|d_B\beta_E^0|^2
                  +\int_{|w|\geq R}|du_E|^2\leq CR^{-2}.
\end{equation}
The cap mixed curvature converges in compact $C^0$ to $d_B\beta_E^0$,
with error $O(\delta)$. Its weighted surface curvature has norm at
most $C\rho M_1$, as does its base curvature by exact ASD. Thus its
annular contribution is $CR^{-2}+o(1)$.

On $\zeta=vZ$ the surface and positive deviations from attachment are
$O(v)$ with bounded first compact derivatives. Their mixed and positive
energies are $O(v^2)$. The weighted surface curvature norm is
$O(\rho/v)$; scalar base curvature is bounded by this term and the
pregluing error. The transition energy is therefore at most
\[
                  Cv^2+C(\rho/v)^2+CT^{-80}+o(1),
\]
where the last term is exponential times a fixed power of $T$.
The smooth residual contributes at most $Cr^2$. This proves the
pregluing version of \eqref{eq:constructed-neck-energy} and a uniform
bound for its energy on $D(c,r_*)$. Equations
\eqref{eq:constructed-curvature-comparison} and
\eqref{eq:constructed-positive-comparison} transfer both assertions to
the exact solution. They also give $L^1$ convergence of the two
analytical energy densities on this fixed collar, by factoring the
difference of squares.

Finally the cap surface and positive values differ from attachment by
$C/R+C\delta$, the transition by $O(v+\rho/v+\delta)$, and the
residual by $Cr$. Add
\eqref{eq:constructed-pointwise-displacement} and
\eqref{eq:constructed-positive-comparison} to prove
\eqref{eq:constructed-neck-diameter}. There is no sum over a growing
number of annuli in either argument.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:constructed-energy-measure}
On $|z-c|\leq\rho R_1/2$, pull the normalized topological energy
measure back to the rescaled base and extend it by zero over the omitted
neighborhood of infinity. These measures converge in total variation to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-energy-measure}
 \mu_E^0=\frac1{8\pi^2}\int_\Sigma
                 \tr(F_{A_E^0}\wedge F_{A_E^0})
                     +\frac1{4\pi^2}u_E^*\Omega,
              \qquad \mu_E^0(\PP^1)=\frac12,
\end{equation}
where the two terms are placed on their respective hemispheres. For
$E_\nu\to E_*$ the limit is $\mu_{E_*}^0$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
On every fixed rescaled compact set the pregluing is the cap and
$\theta_1=1$. The compact base displacement is $\rho a_i$, whose
$H^1_BH^k_\Sigma$ norm is $O(\rho M_1s_\rho)$; the surface and
positive displacements have size $O(s_\rho)$. Consequently the
coefficients converge in these first-order norms to $x_E^0$.
On the whole retained disc, the pregluing cap mixed curvature converges
in compact $L^2$ to the limiting mixed curvature. The omitted tail has
energy $O(R_2^{-2})$, the transition energy tends to zero, and the
residual part has energy $O((\rho R_1)^2)$. Its weighted base and
surface curvatures tend to zero, by exact ASD off the transition and
the vanishing scalar error on it.

Wedge products are continuous from $L^2\times L^2$ to $L^1$.
Thus \eqref{eq:constructed-curvature-comparison} also gives convergence
of the negative Chern--Weil densities. The $L^1$ norm of a top-degree
form is unchanged under pullback, so no rescaling factor remains.
Equation~\eqref{eq:constructed-positive-comparison} and smoothness of
$\Omega$ give convergence of the positive area forms. This proves
total-variation convergence, including the omitted region at infinity.
The same argument before integrating over $\Sigma$ excludes
concentration at an interior point of a rescaled product chart.

The limiting measure is positive: its negative mixed curvature has the
ASD mixed-type relation, its base curvature is zero, and its positive
map is holomorphic. Sew the negative connection to a flat surface family
on the positive disc, retaining the prescribed seam identification.
The flat-family formula \cite[Lemma~5.10 and equation~(5.12)]{DaeFukLip21a}
identifies its positive Chern--Weil integral with
twice the symplectic area. The resulting adjoint bundle is $\ad E$.
Since $p_1(\ad E)=-2$, the normalization in
\eqref{eq:constructed-energy-measure} has mass
$-\frac14(-2)=\frac12$. An interior rank-drop fiber causes no
singularity in this smooth negative reconstruction. In genus one a
constant positive map does not trivialize the seam clutching bundle.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Regularity on fixed rescaled domains}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:constructed-rescaled-regularity}
On nested fixed rescaled compact sets $K\subset K'$, including a seam
segment, there are local unitary base gauges such that, for every fixed
$m,k$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-rescaled-regularity}
 \|g_\rho^*\widehat A_\rho-A_E^0\|_{W^{m,4}_BH^k_\Sigma(K)}
       +\|\widehat u_\rho-u_E\|_{W^{m,4}(K^+)}\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
The gauges are close to the identity in $H^2_BH^k_\Sigma$ on their
fixed defining domains. Each fixed order has its own small-scale
threshold.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On a fixed rescaled domain the pregluing is the cap and $\theta_1=1$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-higher-surface-orders} and the cap estimates
give, with $\widehat A_\rho=A_{B,\rho}+\beta_\rho$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-initial-rescaled}
 \begin{split}
 \|A_{B,\rho}\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}
       &\leq C_k\rho M_1s_\rho+C_k\rho^2T^{N_k}=o(1),\\
 \|\beta_\rho-\beta_E^0\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}
       +\|\widehat u_\rho-u_E\|_{H^1}&=o(1).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
The coefficient differences also tend to zero in $C^0_BH^k_\Sigma$,
using the evaluation loss $\varepsilon^{-1/2}=T^{1/2}$.

Choose a fixed smooth negative base domain $D$ with any artificial
boundary separated from $K$. It may have a straight seam segment.
The ordinary zero-mean Neumann problem with one-form source $a$ has
\[
 \|\phi\|_{H^2_BH^k_\Sigma}\leq C_k\|a\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma},
 \quad \Delta_B\phi=-d_B^*a,\quad \partial_n\phi=-a(n).
\]
The two signs are understood through the corresponding variational
problem; the compatibility condition is the divergence theorem. The
scalar inverse acts coefficientwise on the Hilbert surface space and
commutes with surface derivatives. For $g=e^\phi$ write
$g^*a=a+d_B\phi+R(a,\phi)$. Two-dimensional multiplication gives
\[
 \|R(a,\phi)-R(a,\psi)\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}
 \leq C_k(\|a\|_{H^1}+\|\phi\|_{H^2}+\|\psi\|_{H^2})
                                      \|\phi-\psi\|_{H^2}.
\]
Here $H^2_B\cdot H^1_B\to H^1_B$ and
$H^1_B\cdot H^1_B\to L^2_B$ control, in particular, the differentiated
term $d\phi\,d\phi$. The entire remainder is in $H^1$, so its normal
trace is controlled as well. Applying the Neumann inverse to
$a+R(a,\phi)$ is a contraction. For $a=A_{B,\rho}$ this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-small-base-gauge}
 d_B^*(g_\rho^*a)=0,\qquad (g_\rho^*a)(n)=0,\qquad
 \|g_\rho-I\|_{H^2_BH^k_\Sigma}\leq C_k\|a\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}.
\end{equation}
Its logarithm has zero mean. Identical iterations identify the gauges
at different surface orders. On the seam $a(n)=0$ originally, so the
new normal condition implies $\partial_ng_\rho=0$. The matching class
is preserved and the positive map is unchanged. The transformed surface
connection contains $g_\rho^{-1}d_\Sigma g_\rho$; use one additional
surface order in \eqref{eq:constructed-small-base-gauge}. Hence, writing
the transformed pair as $a_\rho+\beta_E^0+b_\rho$ and
$\widehat u_\rho$, its differences are still small in the $H^1$ and
$C^0$ norms of \eqref{eq:constructed-initial-rescaled}.

Fold the positive half-disc across the seam. The limiting linear
boundary condition is the smooth real subspace
\[
          C_{\beta_E^0}b=0,\qquad H_{\beta_E^0}b=\ell\nu.
\]
Its local complex straightening, followed by complex-conjugate
reflection, gives a Cauchy--Green parametrix for the limiting operator
$L_E$. It is bounded from $L^p_BH^k_\Sigma$ to
$W^{1,p}_BH^k_\Sigma$ for $p=2,4$, with the same integral formula.
The scalar singular-integral estimate extends to the Hilbert space by
random signs and square-function inequalities. Smooth straightening
adds bounded zeroth-order coefficients; the local remainders improve
base regularity. No attaching-point evaluation is required in this
local estimate.

Use the nonlinear matching chart of
Proposition~\ref{prop:nonlinear-cap-chart}, centered at the limiting
pair. Its derivative is the identity and its negative correction gains
one surface derivative. The difference coordinate $v_\rho$ satisfies
the fixed linear boundary condition, is small in $H^1_BH^k_\Sigma$
and $C^0_BH^k_\Sigma$, and solves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-local-cr}
 L_Ev_\rho=B(z,v_\rho)D_Bv_\rho+f_\rho,\qquad
      \|B(z,v_\rho)\|_{L^\infty_B\mathcal L(H^k_\Sigma)}=o(1).
\end{equation}
The source $f_\rho$ contains $d_\Sigma a_\rho$, bounded coefficients
times $a_\rho,v_\rho$, and their zeroth-order products, but no derivative
of $v_\rho$. It vanishes at the limiting pair. The higher surface
orders and $H^1_B\subset L^4_B$ give $\|f_\rho\|_{L^4_BH^k_\Sigma}=o(1)$.
Localizing with a fixed cutoff and applying the parametrix $R$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-cr-improvement}
            (I-RBD_B)(\chi v_\rho)=h_\rho,\qquad
                      \|h_\rho\|_{W^{1,4}_BH^k_\Sigma}=o(1).
\end{equation}
The right side includes the source, cutoff commutators applied to
$v_\rho\in L^4$, and regularizing remainders. For small $\rho$ the
operator $RBD_B$ has norm less than $\frac12$ on both $W^{1,2}$ and
$W^{1,4}$. Its inverse is the same Neumann series on common inputs.
The given weak $W^{1,2}$ solution therefore belongs to $W^{1,4}$ on
the smaller domain and tends to zero there. Interior patches use the
same argument without reflection. No fixed $p>2$ estimate has been
inferred from the shrinking fractional exponent.

The base field now satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-base-bootstrap}
 d_B^*a_\rho=0,\qquad a_\rho(n)=0,\qquad
 d_Ba_\rho=-a_\rho\wedge a_\rho
       -\rho^2f_\infty^2*_B*_\Sigma F_\Sigma(\beta_E^0+b_\rho).
\end{equation}
The conformal factor and all fixed derivatives are bounded on the
chosen rescaled compact sets. The local base Hodge estimate is
\[
 \|a_\rho\|_{W^{m+1,p}_BH^k_\Sigma(K)}
 \leq C\bigl(\|d_Ba_\rho\|_{W^{m,p}_BH^k_\Sigma(K')}
                          +\|a_\rho\|_{L^p_BH^k_\Sigma(K')}\bigr).
\]
At $m=0,p=4$, the square of $a_\rho$ is small in $L^4$ by
$H^1_B\subset L^8_B$, and the surface term is $O(\rho^2)$ using the
just proved estimate at a higher surface order. Hence $a_\rho\to0$
in $W^{1,4}$. Since this space is a two-dimensional algebra, the same
equation gives $a_\rho\to0$ in $W^{2,4}$.

Differentiate \eqref{eq:constructed-local-cr} tangentially at the seam.
The principal coefficient remains small in $L^\infty$. The new product
$(D_BB)D_Bv_\rho$ is small in $L^2$, using two $L^4$ factors;
derivatives of $f_\rho$ are controlled in the same space. Tangential
difference quotients justify applying the $p=2$ estimate before the
new derivative is known: each nonzero quotient is already $W^{1,2}$,
its new source is uniformly bounded in $L^2$, and the resulting estimate
passes to the weak derivative. Straightening has made tangential
differentiation preserve the real boundary condition. The normal
principal coefficient is invertible, so the equation recovers normal
derivatives. This proves $v_\rho\to0$ in $W^{2,2}$.

Now $D_Bv_\rho$ belongs to every finite $L^q_B$. Use $q=8$ for both
factors in $(D_BB)D_Bv_\rho$ and apply the $p=4$ estimate to obtain
$v_\rho\to0$ in $W^{2,4}$. For $m\geq2$, assume both fields converge
in $W^{m,4}$ at each required fixed surface order.
Equation~\eqref{eq:constructed-base-bootstrap} first gives
$a_\rho\to0$ in $W^{m+1,4}$. After $m$ derivatives of
\eqref{eq:constructed-local-cr}, the only unestimated derivative is
$BD_BD^mv_\rho$. All other terms lie in $L^4$ by two-dimensional
Sobolev multiplication and the improved base estimate. Absorb this
small principal term and use difference quotients as before. This
gives $v_\rho\to0$ in $W^{m+1,4}$ and proves the induction.
Each step uses finitely many higher surface orders already supplied by
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-higher-surface-orders}. It requires no
uniform four-dimensional elliptic constant as $\rho\to0$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The residual component and its ends}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:constructed-residual-limit}
The residual and end conclusions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit} hold for the constructed family.
On a fixed exterior matching collar the gauges identifying the smooth
limit with $x_0$ can be chosen to converge to the identity in $C^0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Undo the smooth center-dependent based gauge before comparison with
$A_0$. Outside $|z-c|\leq\rho R_1/2$ the pregluing equals the residual
and the graph norm is its residual factor. Thus on every fixed negative
compact set $K$ disjoint from $\{c_*\}\times\Sigma$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-exterior-hone}
                      \|A_\rho-A_0\|_{H^1(K)}\leq C_Ks_\rho.
\end{equation}
On a fixed exterior matching collar, at each fixed surface order,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-exterior-pointwise}
       \|A_\rho-A_0\|_{C^0_BH^k_\Sigma}\leq C_kT^{1/2}s_\rho=o(1).
\end{equation}
The positive maps converge in $H^1$ and $C^0$ in their construction
charts. All these statements include the nonlinear ambient correction.

On the mixed ends write $A_\rho=B_{p_\rho}+\xi_\rho$ and
$A_0=B_0+\xi_0$, subtracting the individual exact critical
representatives. With $v_\rho$ denoting the positive displacement from
its own $q_{p_\rho}$, the graph estimate gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-end-subtraction}
 |p_\rho|+\|\xi_\rho-\xi_0\|_{H^1_\omega}
                +\|v_\rho-v_0\|_{H^1_\omega}\leq Cs_\rho.
\end{equation}
On the ordinary ends the analogous estimate is unweighted, with fixed
ordinary critical classes. In particular all these remainders have
uniformly small tails.

Strong physical $H^1$ convergence gives strong $L^2$ curvature
convergence on fixed exterior sets, since in four dimensions
\[
 F_{A_0+a}-F_{A_0}=d_{A_0}a+a\wedge a,\qquad
 \|F_{A_0+a}-F_{A_0}\|_2\leq C(\|a\|_{H^1}+\|a\|_{H^1}^2).
\]
The holonomy gradients converge on their compact outputs by their
$L^2$ Lipschitz estimates; their entire dependency neighborhoods are
outside the modified collar. The finite secondary perturbations have
the same property. Smooth positive charts give convergence of the
positive derivatives in $L^2$. Hence analytical energy densities are
uniformly integrable on fixed exterior sets.

On a mixed end exact criticality is needed to cancel the constant term:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-perturbed-end-curvature}
 \begin{split}
 F_{A_\rho}+*_3\nabla_{A_\rho}h
  ={}&d_{B_{p_\rho}}\xi_\rho+\xi_\rho\wedge\xi_\rho\\
    &+*_3\bigl(\nabla_{B_{p_\rho}+\xi_\rho}h
                            -\nabla_{B_{p_\rho}}h\bigr).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
The weighted $H^1$ embedding and the $L^2$ Lipschitz estimate control
this expression in weighted $L^2$, after decreasing the positive weight.
On an ordinary end compare with the fixed residual's integrable
perturbed curvature and use its unweighted $H^1$ displacement. Together
with the positive estimates this proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-small-end-tails}
 \lim_{L\to\infty}\limsup_{\rho\to0}
       \mathfrak E(x_\rho;\text{all ends beyond }L)=0.
\end{equation}
The unmodified critical curvature has not been assigned an infinite
cylinder $L^2$ norm. Proposition~\ref{prop:constructed-neck} now gives
a uniform global analytical energy bound.

Apply the standard-perturbation compactness theorem
\cite[Theorem~5.2]{DaeFukLip21a} to the smooth representatives. The
interior and positive concentration sets are empty and the seam set is
contained in $\{c_*\}$. Its smooth conclusion off that set uses the
all-order primary summability fixed before selecting the residual, and
the complete exterior dependency sets. The uniform integrability above
supplies absence of exterior concentration without a uniform $p>2$
curvature estimate.
