We identify its smooth exterior limit $A'$ at the critical $H^1$
order. The relating unitary gauges satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-exterior-gauge}
                         dg_\rho=g_\rho A_\rho'-A_\rho g_\rho,
                 \qquad A_\rho'\longrightarrow A'\text{ smoothly}.
\end{equation}
Unitarity and \eqref{eq:constructed-exterior-hone} give a local
$W^{1,4}$ bound for $g_\rho$. Rellich compactness gives a subsequence
converging in every finite $L^q$ and almost everywhere to a unitary
$g$. The right side of \eqref{eq:constructed-exterior-gauge} converges
strongly in $L^4$: use $A_\rho\to A_0$ in $L^4$ and dominated
convergence against the fixed smooth coefficients. Thus the gauges
converge strongly in $W^{1,4}$. Differentiation gives strong $W^{2,2}$
convergence, using products of two $L^4$ terms and bounded gauges
times the strongly convergent $L^2$ derivatives of $A_\rho$.
The limiting equation $dg=gA'-A_0g$ has smooth coefficients and
bounded $g$; it first gives $W^{1,\infty}$ and then smoothness by
differentiation. Hence the residual limit is $x_0$ modulo a smooth
gauge. On an exterior matching collar,
\eqref{eq:constructed-exterior-pointwise} bounds the coefficients in
\eqref{eq:constructed-exterior-gauge} uniformly. After composition with
the limiting gauge, the relating gauges tend to $I$ in $L^2$ with
uniformly bounded first derivatives, and therefore in $C^0$. This last
assertion uses the extra collar estimate, not a false embedding of
$W^{2,2}$ into $C^0$ in four dimensions.

For mixed-end convergence at higher orders, we also check the
topological small-tail hypothesis of DFL's decay theorem. Since
$B_p$ is pulled back from three dimensions, $F_{B_p}\wedge F_{B_p}=0$.
The other curvature-square terms have the forms
\[
 F_{B_p}\wedge d_{B_p}\xi,\quad F_{B_p}\wedge\xi^2,\quad
 d_{B_p}\xi\wedge d_{B_p}\xi,\quad d_{B_p}\xi\wedge\xi^2,\quad\xi^4.
\]
If $K$ bounds the weighted $H^1$ remainders, their absolute integrals
past $L$ are at most $Ce^{-cL}(K+K^2+K^3+K^4)$, by weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz and $H^1$ multiplication. The constants are uniform
for $p$ near zero. Positive area is bounded by derivative energy, and
the perturbation terms are controlled by
\eqref{eq:constructed-perturbed-end-curvature}. Thus all required
small-tail thresholds hold uniformly after increasing $L$.

In temporal gauge, DFL's estimates
\cite[Propositions~5.24 and~5.29]{DaeFukLip21a} give uniform exponential
decay. The higher-order argument retains the initial decay rate at each
derivative order, while shrinking the allowed energy threshold. The
preceding tail estimate supplies that threshold at each fixed order.
Choose one weight $\omega_*>0$ below this common rate. The critical
classes are the prescribed $p_\rho$ classes: their positive values
$q_{p_\rho}$ are fixed by the end chart, and the critical Lagrangians
are embedded. Split an end at large $L$, use smooth convergence before
$L$, and integrate the uniform exponential estimate after it. This
gives convergence in every prescribed $H^l_{\omega_*}$ order after
subtracting individual critical limits, as in
\cite[equation~(5.36)]{DaeFukLip21a}.

On ordinary ends \eqref{eq:constructed-small-end-tails} excludes a
translated nonconstant trajectory or strip, since a compact piece of
such a limit would have positive energy. The global $H^1$ displacement
fixes its zero-energy limiting critical class. In its ordinary Coulomb
slice, or the chart at the transverse Lagrangian intersection, the
self-adjoint critical Hessian has a spectral gap. Its inverse and
absorption of the quadratic remainder give
\[
 \|b\|_{H^1}\leq C\|\operatorname{grad}\mathcal A(b)\|_2,\qquad
             |\mathcal A(b)-\mathcal A(0)|\leq C\|b\|_{H^1}^2.
\]
The family stays in this neighborhood on every sufficiently far tail:
otherwise a translated first-exit strip has energy tending to zero,
converges locally to a constant critical solution, and has displacement
tending to zero in $L^2$ by the global comparison. Local elliptic
compactness in the fixed critical slice contradicts the nonzero exit
displacement. Along the flow toward that critical point, the remaining
energy therefore satisfies $e'(t)\leq-ce(t)$.

Uniform unit-strip elliptic estimates follow by compactness near this
critical solution, as in the proof of DFL's Proposition~5.27. On the
far end the equation and perturbations are translation invariant, so
the temporal derivative solves the homogeneous linearized equation.
Its high Sobolev norms on smaller strips are bounded by its $L^2$
norm, already exponentially decaying. Integrating the temporal
derivative gives the corresponding displacement bound. Splitting the
end at large $L$ now proves every prescribed unweighted $H^l$
convergence. These ends retain weight zero. No index-at-most-one
exclusion is used in this proof.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Holomorphic bundle convergence}

We first make the stable surface slice quantitative. Let $V$ be a stable
rank-two surface bundle with fixed determinant and put
\[
 D=\bar\partial_V:\Omega^0(\End_0V)\longrightarrow\Omega^{0,1}(\End_0V),
                         \qquad H=\ker D^*.
\]
Stability gives $\ker D=0$. Hodge decomposition on the compact curve
therefore makes
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-complex-slice-linear}
 H^{k+1}(\End_0V)\oplus H\longrightarrow H^k(\Omega^{0,1}\End_0V),
                     \qquad (\eta,h)\longmapsto D\eta+h
\end{equation}
an isomorphism, for $k\geq2$. The nonlinear map
\[
        (\eta,h)\longmapsto
        e^{-\eta}(\bar\partial_V+h)e^\eta-\bar\partial_V
\]
is holomorphic between these Banach spaces and has derivative
\eqref{eq:constructed-complex-slice-linear}. The complex implicit-function
theorem gives unique small parameters satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-complex-slice-bound}
               \|\eta(b)\|_{H^{k+1}}+|h(b)|\leq C_k\|b\|_{H^k}.
\end{equation}
Every surface $(0,1)$ connection is integrable, since the surface has
complex dimension one. Shrink the slice so its members are stable and
distinct $h$ represent distinct isomorphism classes. This is the local
slice for the effective complex gauge action on stable bundles
\cite[Section~4, pp.~769--770]{Owens01}. The inverse maps agree at
higher orders, have smooth parameter dependence, and are uniformly
bounded on a compact stable reference family. The gauge is chosen near
$I$, fixing the choice between the central lifts $\pm I$.

Consider now an integrable product Dolbeault operator whose surface
part $D+b(z)$ stays in this neighborhood. The slice parameters are
$\eta(z),h(z)$. Integrability says that $\partial_{\bar z}b$ is an
infinitesimal complex gauge variation. The derivative of $h$ annihilates
that variation, since its value is constant along a sufficiently small
gauge orbit. The slice map is holomorphic, so the chain rule gives
$\partial_{\bar z}h=0$. Apply $g=e^\eta$ to the full product operator.
Its surface part is $D+h(z)$ and its base part has trace-free coefficient
$\gamma$. Integrability then gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-slice-base-coefficient}
                            D\gamma+[h(z),\gamma]=0.
\end{equation}
Stability implies $\gamma=0$. Thus the slice identifies the complete
projective holomorphic family with the pullback slice family, including
its base coefficient. The central part is a base-line factor, removed
by projectivization. Fiberwise surface isomorphisms alone would not
supply this identification.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:constructed-holomorphic-limit}
The finite-scale doubled projective bundles of the constructed family
converge to $\PP(E_*)$ on every fixed rescaled compact set, with the
prescribed attaching identification. The assertion holds through an
interior unstable fiber and identifies the stable extension at infinity.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The small gauges of Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-rescaled-regularity}
are compatible on overlaps. Indeed their relative gauges $h_\nu$ tend
uniformly to $I$ and obey $dh_\nu=h_\nu A_\nu'-A_\nu h_\nu$,
where both connections converge to the same limiting coefficients.
This gives convergence of $dh_\nu$ to zero; successive differentiation
gives every fixed higher-order convergence. The cocycle identities are
exact before taking a limit and limit to the identity.

On a fixed rescaled seam segment, finitely many stable reference
fibers supply the preceding slices. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-rescaled-regularity} and smooth dependence of
the slice inverse, the negative surface isomorphisms and classifying
maps converge in every fixed finite order. The positive classifying
maps converge to $u_{E_*}$. Matching identifies their continuous values
on the seam. In a complex chart their union is holomorphic by Morera's
theorem. Uniform convergence on a paired neighborhood and Cauchy
estimates give all finite orders of holomorphic convergence on smaller
neighborhoods. Pulling back the slice family supplies holomorphic bundle
frames on both halves; equation
\eqref{eq:constructed-slice-base-coefficient} identifies the full
negative product operator. The slice and frame transition functions
converge to those of $E_*$. The same argument applies on positive
interior compact sets.

An unstable surface fiber in the negative interior requires a different
argument. Choose a product polydisc $D_z\times D_\zeta$ and a
holomorphic frame of $E_*$ on a slightly larger polydisc. In the
smooth unitary identifications already obtained, the varying Dolbeault
operators have coefficients
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-interior-dolbeault}
 \bar\partial+B_{z,\nu}\,d\bar z+B_{\zeta,\nu}\,d\bar\zeta,
          \qquad B_{z,\nu},B_{\zeta,\nu}\longrightarrow0
\end{equation}
at every prescribed finite order. Use a fixed mixed H\"older norm
$C^{m,\alpha}_z(C^{m,\alpha}_\zeta)$, $0<\alpha<1$, controlled by
a sufficiently high ordinary smooth norm. The Cauchy--Green inverse
$T_z$ on a fixed disc is bounded in this parameter-dependent norm:
the usual kernel-difference estimate holds for functions with values
in the Banach space $C^{m,\alpha}_\zeta$. It commutes with parameter
derivatives. On nested fixed discs the contraction
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-holomorphic-frames}
             g_\nu=I-T_z(B_{z,\nu}g_\nu),\qquad
             k_\nu=I-T_\zeta(C_{\zeta,\nu}k_\nu)
\end{equation}
constructs an invertible $g_\nu\to I$ first. In its frame the $z$
coefficient vanishes and the remaining coefficient $C_{\zeta,\nu}$
is holomorphic in $z$ by integrability. It tends to zero, using one
additional coefficient order for $\partial_{\bar\zeta}g_\nu$.
The second contraction in
\eqref{eq:constructed-holomorphic-frames} has iterates holomorphic in
$z$, since $T_\zeta$ acts on a fixed disc and commutes with
$\partial_{\bar z}$. Thus $g_\nu k_\nu$ gives a holomorphic frame
converging to the chosen limiting frame. Higher orders follow in the
successively higher fixed norms. No stability of the surface fiber is
used here. On a finite product cover, the resulting holomorphic
transition matrices converge, including on overlaps with the stable
seam neighborhoods. Fixed-determinant central lifts may be used locally;
equivalently this is a projective frame construction.

It remains to check attachment on the whole neck. Put
$\alpha_\nu=\alpha(c_\nu)$. Equation
\eqref{eq:constructed-neck-diameter} places every negative surface
operator and positive class on
$A(c_\nu;R\rho_\nu,r)$ in one fixed stable slice, for $R$ large,
$r$ small, and then $\nu$ large. Apply
\eqref{eq:constructed-complex-slice-bound} there to obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:constructed-holomorphic-neck}
 \sup_{A^-}\|\eta_\nu\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}
       +\sup_A|h_\nu-h(c_\nu)|
                    \leq C_k(R^{-1}+r)+o_\nu(1).
\end{equation}
The first term is in the slice centered at $\alpha_\nu$, or has its
small reference gauge subtracted in a fixed slice. The negative
classifying map is holomorphic, and it glues to the positive map across
both seam intervals. Consequently the doubled family on the full
annulus is the pullback of that one slice family. The slice map and its
small logarithm are single-valued in the actual attaching frame, even
though the annulus is not simply connected. Hence no additional
projective transition arises from unrelated gauge choices on different
arcs. This argument controls values and surface derivatives; it does
not assume uniform physical base derivative bounds on the shrinking
annulus.

The limit on $\CC\times\Sigma$ is now the restriction of the specified
primitive bundle $E_*$. Its fibers near infinity are stable, and its
classifying coordinate tends to $[\alpha(c_*)]$. Let $R\to\infty$ and
$r\to0$ in \eqref{eq:constructed-holomorphic-neck} after taking the
sequence limit. This identifies precisely its original attaching value
and stable family at infinity. Only the limiting bundle is extended.
A finite annulus has not been replaced by a punctured disc, and no
holomorphic cap for each finite-scale residual variation is asserted.

The isomorphisms used here are inherited from the chosen fixed-determinant
rank-two families and small complex gauges. Two such isomorphisms to
the same stable family differ by a scalar, so agree projectively.
This does not assert that every abstract projective automorphism lifts
to the fixed rank-two bundle. Base-line twists have no adjoint effect.
On the compact sphere the normalization
\[
              \det E=\pi_\Sigma^*\Lambda\otimes\pi_B^*\cO(1)
\]
removes their remaining degree ambiguity: a base twist of degree $d$
changes the determinant degree by $2d$, and a degree-zero line on
$\PP^1$ is trivial. Thus the normalized rank-two limit is $E_*$ as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Completion of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit}]
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-residual-limit} identifies the residual and
all ends. Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-rescaled-regularity} supplies every
fixed finite order on rescaled compact sets, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-holomorphic-limit} identifies the doubled
bundle, including an interior unstable fiber. The whole-neck estimate
\eqref{eq:constructed-neck-energy} gives
\[
 \lim_{r\downarrow0}\lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_{\nu\to\infty}
                  \mathfrak E_{\rho_\nu}(R\rho_\nu,r)=0.
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:constructed-neck-diameter} gives the corresponding
diameter assertion. Corollary~\ref{cor:constructed-energy-measure}
retains all the half-unit mass. At rank drop the modification remains
in the limiting holomorphic bundle; a stable-map sphere describing its
algebraic degeneration is not an additional analytic component.
The stable whole-neck family inserts no modification at infinity.

The component gauges retain the same attaching frame. For completeness,
choose successively larger fixed rescaled domains and thresholds at
which each finite list of estimates holds, and let these domains grow
by a diagonal choice. Decrease the thresholds to keep their physical
diameters tending to zero and their supports inside the cap. The small
base gauge can be extended as the identity by cutting off its logarithm
near the artificial boundary. On a regular exterior matching collar,
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-residual-limit} gives relating gauges close
to $I$. These may likewise be cut off toward the neck. In relative
normal boundary gauge both original and transformed seam normal
coefficients vanish, so their relating gauges have zero normal
derivative there; cutoffs constant in that direction preserve it.
For each retained compact truncation the two component regions are
disjoint for small scale, and these Sobolev bundle identifications
extend across the discarded annulus. No estimate for their derivatives
on that entire annulus is needed. The gauges at different fixed orders
agree where both constructions apply.

These are the stated analytic, energy, and holomorphic convergence
properties of the broken configuration. They are uniform for each
finite collection of compact domains and derivative orders and on the
compact parameter patch. Such collections give a countable exhaustion
defining this convergence topology, so the local construction extends
continuously at scale zero. Constant unmarked components with only
their attaching node are omitted; insertion ratios and their possible
marked components are not part of this theorem. No assertion of
converse entry or collar uniqueness has entered the proof.
\end{proof}

\section{Converse gluing on regular primitive strata}
\label{sec:regular-converse}

\subsection{Stable families and one elementary modification}

We first state the converse with explicit hypotheses on the doubled
families and their exterior representatives. We group them into four
conditions, retaining the fixed data throughout this section.
Section~\ref{sec:physical-sector-coverage} derives the technical
exterior and end conditions from convergence and energy exhaustion.

\paragraph{(C1) Residual and convergence.}
The pair $x_0=(A_0,u_0)$ is a fixed smooth irreducible regular residual
of index zero. The primary perturbations satisfy the all-order
summability used above. The product matching neighborhood is disjoint
from all perturbation supports and dependency regions. We retain the
fixed-determinant lifts and use the real seam coordinate in
Section~\ref{sec:regular-local-gluing}.

Let $x_\nu$ be smooth mixed solutions converging to $x_0$ on compact
sets away from one seam point.

\paragraph{(C2) Doubled families and outer framings.}
On shrinking doubled discs suppose
Lemma~\ref{lem:doubling} and the coherent framing and capping convention
of Proposition~\ref{prop:framed-splitting} apply, with
$n(E_\nu)=1$. The outer classifying loops and an annulus about them
lie in a shrinking stable chart about the residual attaching class.
Their caps in that chart have area tending to zero. On the negative
outer arcs there are actual unitary surface representatives converging
to the residual attaching connection in every prescribed fixed surface
order. These are data on the chosen growing rescaled circles, not a
consequence of convergence on a fixed rescaled circle.

\paragraph{(C3) Family type and regular attaching data.}
After a subsequence, fix one of the following alternatives:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $d_\nu=0$, $\ell_\nu=1$. There is one Langton step with quotient
of degree zero. Its point is in the open negative half-plane. Translate
by its physical real part and divide by the absolute value of its
physical imaginary part, so its normalized position is $-i$.
\item $g=2$, $d_\nu=1$, $\ell_\nu=0$. All finite surface fibers are
stable. The limiting image line obtained below corresponds to
$L_*\in\operatorname{Pic}^0(\Sigma)$ with
$H^0(\Sigma,\Lambda L_*^{-2})=0$. Normalize the comparison
pencil by \eqref{eq:gluing-affine-slice-functions}, as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family} below, and assume its
limit is seam-admissible.
\end{enumerate}
For each $\nu$, write $E_\nu^0$ for the normalized comparison
primitive associated with $x_\nu$: the inverse elementary transform
with the same modification data in the first case, and the
degree-one extension pencil in the second. These constructions
are given in Propositions~\ref{prop:converse-one-modification}
and~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}.
In both cases assume the normalized comparison data lie in a
relatively compact regular seam-admissible patch with one observation
system. Write their physical centers and scales as $c_\nu,\rho_\nu$,
and put $w=(z-c_\nu)/\rho_\nu$. The retained doubled discs contain
$D_{2R_\nu}\times\Sigma$, with fixed additional radius margins, where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-radii}
 T_\nu=\log(1/\rho_\nu),\qquad
 R_\nu\geq c\rho_\nu^{-1/2},\qquad
 r_\nu=\rho_\nu R_\nu\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
All finite-family hypotheses must hold on these retained discs.
The enlargement argument below explains when coherent charge-one
outer data preserve the second alternative.

\paragraph{(C4) Exterior and end estimates.}
In normal gauges, convergence to the based residual representative
holds on
\[
 r_\nu/32\leq |z-c_\nu|\leq r_\nu/8
\]
with physical $C^1_BC^k_\Sigma$ connection error at most $r_\nu$,
for each fixed $k$ and sufficiently large $\nu$. The other required
finite physical derivatives have their exterior convergence bounds.
There is no other concentration on those compact sets. On the ends
assume weighted smooth convergence after subtracting each solution's
own exact critical representative, retaining its finite critical
parameters. The weight is fixed below the residual asymptotic gaps
and the given convergence rate. Smooth convergence off the atom
allows a sufficiently slow diagonal choice of exterior radii.
The bundle and charge comparison on an enlargement, and the end
convergence under exhaustion of the global defect, are proved in
Propositions~\ref{prop:automatic-outer-data} and
\ref{prop:automatic-end-convergence} below.

\begin{theorem}[converse for stable families and one elementary modification]
\label{thm:regular-sector-converse}
Suppose conditions \textup{(C1)--(C4)} hold.
Then $x_\nu$ eventually belongs, modulo the allowed
gauge group, to the local gluing family of
Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-local-gluing}.
Write $(q,t_1,t_2)$ for local coordinates on the normalized
primitive space, with $q$ its attaching class. Let $t_\nu$ be the
last two coordinates of $E_\nu^0$. There are unique nearby solutions
$(\widehat c_\nu,\widehat\rho_\nu,\widehat t_\nu)$ of the four
observation equations such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-parameter-bound}
\begin{split}
 [x_\nu]&=\Glue_{\rm loc}(\widehat c_\nu,\widehat\rho_\nu,
                                      \widehat t_\nu),\\
 \left|\frac{\widehat c_\nu-c_\nu}{\rho_\nu}\right|
 +\left|\log\frac{\widehat\rho_\nu}{\rho_\nu}\right|
 +|\widehat t_\nu-t_\nu|&\leq CT_\nu^{-79/2}.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The original solution has a representative in the full gluing chart
at these parameters with coordinate norm at most $CT_\nu^{-38}$.
The second alternative includes stable finite families approaching
negative rank drop.
\end{theorem}

The theorem does not classify every index-four degeneration. In
particular it excludes positive rank-drop limits and special genus-two
lines, and it retains the exterior and end hypotheses just stated.
We first construct the comparison bundles, then prove common analytic
estimates, and finally prove full-chart entry and uniqueness.

\subsection{A single elementary modification}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:converse-one-modification}
In the first alternative the stable replacements have image tending
uniformly to the attaching class. After a subsequence the modification
sublines converge smoothly. The doubled bundles converge
holomorphically on fixed compact products, including the modification
fiber, to the primitive inverse transform determined by those limiting
data. There is a comparison primitive $E_\nu^0$ with the same modification
point and subline, and an identification of its base-holomorphic
structure with that of $E_\nu$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The stable-reduction map $f_\nu$ has area tending to zero, since
$d_\nu=0$ and its cap has vanishing area. Small area and the outer
image condition imply uniform image convergence. Indeed, a holomorphic
curve through the center of a target coordinate ball, with no domain
boundary mapping into that ball, has area at least $c\delta^2$ in
a ball of radius $\delta$. In Euclidean coordinates, Stokes and
coarea give $2A(s)\leq sA'(s)$ at regular radii; the density at a
nonconstant branch is a positive integer multiple of $\pi$.
Uniform comparison with the Kähler metric gives the assertion.
A point a fixed distance from the attaching class would provide
such a ball missing the outer boundary, contradicting small area.

Use a stable complex slice about the attaching bundle $V_*$.
Its coordinate $t_\nu(w)$ is holomorphic and satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-slow-parameter}
 \|t_\nu\|_\infty\leq\eta_\nu\to0,\qquad
 \|\partial_w^jt_\nu\|_\infty\leq C_j\eta_\nu R_\nu^{-j}
\end{equation}
on smaller retained discs. The slice pullback represents the stable
replacement up to a line from the disc, which is holomorphically
trivial. Its trace-free base operator is $\bar\partial_w$:
the remaining base coefficient is a holomorphic trace-free
endomorphism of a stable surface bundle, hence zero.

Let $a=-i$, $V_\nu=F_\nu|_{\{a\}\times\Sigma}$, and let
$G_\nu\subset V_\nu$ be the degree-zero subline supplied by the
Langton inclusion. After taking a subsequence, the classes of $G_\nu$
converge in the compact Picard torus. Normalize their inclusions
to have $L^2$ norm one. Uniform surface Dolbeault estimates give a
smoothly convergent subsequence of those maps. Its nonzero limit
$G\to V_*$ has no zero: saturation at a nonzero effective divisor
would give a line of degree at least one in the stable degree-one
rank-two bundle. Thus the orthogonal projections onto $G_\nu$,
in the NS metrics of $V_\nu$, converge in every surface order.

Writing $t=w-a$, the inverse-transform lattice is generated by
$e_1/t,e_2$ when $e_1$ spans $G_\nu$ at $t=0$. If the stable
transition matrix is
\[
 Q_\nu=\begin{pmatrix}q_{11}&q_{12}\\tc_{21}&q_{22}\end{pmatrix},
\]
the actual transition matrix is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-modification-transitions}
 \begin{pmatrix}t&0\\0&1\end{pmatrix}Q_\nu
 \begin{pmatrix}t^{-1}&0\\0&1\end{pmatrix}
 =\begin{pmatrix}q_{11}&tq_{12}\\c_{21}&q_{22}\end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
Division of the vanishing entry by $t$ is controlled by its
base derivative on smaller charts. These matrices converge with
nonzero determinant and prove locally free holomorphic convergence
through the divisor.

Replace the stable family by the constant family $V_\nu$ and keep
$G_\nu$ and $a$. Its inverse transform $E_\nu^0$ has extension
\[
 0\longrightarrow\mathcal O(1)\boxtimes G_\nu
 \longrightarrow E_\nu^0
 \longrightarrow\mathcal O\boxtimes\Lambda G_\nu^{-1}
 \longrightarrow0,
\]
with coefficient pair $e(w)=(w-a)v$. The extension compactifies
directly on $\PP^1\times\Sigma$; it has $c_2=1$ and
$p_1(\ad E_\nu^0)=-2$. The same smooth splitting and lattice
$e_1/t,e_2$ identify the actual and comparison base-holomorphic
structures. The surface operators differ. Since
$t_\nu(w)-t_\nu(a)=(w-a)k_\nu(w)$, this difference in the divisor
frame has entries
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}
 (w-a)k_{11}&(w-a)^2k_{12}\\
 k_{21}&(w-a)k_{22}
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \|\partial_w^jk_\nu\|_{C^m_\Sigma}
             \leq C_{j,m}\eta_\nu R_\nu^{-1-j}.
\]
It is small and nonsingular on the fixed divisor chart. At large
radius use the stable frame, rather than this polynomial bound.
The current rank-one pencil already satisfies
\eqref{eq:gluing-affine-slice}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Degree-one stable families in genus two}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:converse-stable-family}
In the second alternative, the classifying image approaches a line
in $M_2$. Near a regular limiting line, the actual family has slowly
varying Jacobian and extension coordinates. Their values at a
unique projective pole define a comparison primitive
$E_\nu^0$ without division by its transverse coefficient.
The affine normalization produces $c_\nu,\rho_\nu$ with
$c_\nu,\rho_\nu\to0$ and a compact normalized primitive family.
On a seam-admissible subsequence the actual and comparison
base-holomorphic structures are identified uniformly through
negative rank drop.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use the realization of $M_2$ as the intersection of two quadrics
in $\PP^5$, with hyperplane class the primitive class
\cite[Example~24]{Munoz99b}. Initially write the physical domain
as $D_{8r_\nu}$, with outer annulus $r_\nu\leq|z|\leq8r_\nu$
mapping close to $p=[1:0:\cdots:0]$. Let $\epsilon_\nu\to0$
bound its five affine coordinates. The section $Z_0$ of the
pullback of $\mathcal O(1)$ has exactly one simple zero
$a_\nu\in D_{r_\nu}$: its relative zero count is the capped
degree one, using $Z_0$ as a nonvanishing trivialization on
the small-chart cap. A holomorphic lift, divided by the
nowhere-zero function $s_0/(z-a_\nu)$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-projective-pole}
 f_\nu(z)=[(z-a_\nu)(1,h_\nu(z))+(0,v_\nu)],
                         \qquad v_\nu\in\CC^5\setminus\{0\}.
\end{equation}
The residue formula on $|z|=7r_\nu$, the maximum principle,
and Cauchy's estimate give
\[
 |v_\nu|\leq Cr_\nu\epsilon_\nu,\quad
 \|h_\nu\|_{D_{6r_\nu}}\leq C\epsilon_\nu,\quad
 \|\partial_z^jh_\nu\|_{D_{4r_\nu}}
                        \leq C_j\epsilon_\nu r_\nu^{-j}.
\]
Put $p_0=(1,0,\ldots,0)$ and $u_\nu=v_\nu/|v_\nu|$.
The two summands in $(z-a_\nu)p_0+(0,v_\nu)$ are orthogonal,
and its perturbation $(z-a_\nu)(0,h_\nu)$ has relative norm
at most $C\epsilon_\nu$. Hence the projective distance to
the line $\PP\operatorname{span}\{p_0,(0,u_\nu)\}$ is uniformly
$O(\epsilon_\nu)$, independently of $|v_\nu|$.

For each defining quadric $Q_j$, with polar form $B_j$,
evaluation and differentiation of \eqref{eq:converse-projective-pole}
at $a_\nu$ give
\[
 Q_j(0,v_\nu)=0,\qquad
 B_j((0,v_\nu),(1,h_\nu(a_\nu)))=0.
\]
After $u_\nu\to u_*$ these exact identities and $Q_j(p_0)=0$
show that $\ell_*=\PP\operatorname{span}\{p_0,(0,u_*)\}$
lies in $M_2$. The image of the whole smaller disc tends
uniformly to $\ell_*$. The only division by $|v_\nu|$ here
is in the exact identity identifying a direction.

Let $N=\PP(\mathcal V)\to\operatorname{Pic}^0(\Sigma)$,
$\mathcal V_L=H^1(\Sigma,L^2\Lambda^{-1})$, and let
$i:N\to M_2$ send an extension to its middle bundle.
The primitive classification identifies $\ell_*=i(N_{L_*})$
\cite[Propositions~2--3 and~5]{Munoz99b}.
The relative $\mathcal O(1)$ subline recovers $L_*$ uniquely:
push the classified product bundle twisted by
$\mathcal O(-1)$ to $\Sigma$.
At a pair $L\subset V$, the infinitesimal subline variations
with fixed $V$ are $H^0(\Sigma,\Lambda L^{-2})$, by the graph
description of a subbundle. Stable automorphisms are scalar.
Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-extension-inverse}
                \ker di=H^0(\Sigma,\Lambda L^{-2}).
\end{equation}
The source and target have complex dimension three.
Our regularity hypothesis makes $di$ invertible along the
whole compact fiber $N_{L_*}$. It is injective on that fiber:
the extension tensored by $L_*^{-1}$ gives
$h^0(VL_*^{-1})=1$, so the subline is unique.
It is then injective on a neighborhood of the fiber.
Otherwise pairs of distinct points with the same image in
shrinking neighborhoods would limit to the same fiber point,
contradicting local invertibility there. Consequently, for a
small ball $B$ about $L_*$, $i:N|_B\to U$ is biholomorphic.
The actual maps lift to $N|_B$, with Jacobian coordinate
$L_\nu(z)\to L_*$ uniformly.

Trivialize $\mathcal V$ on $B$. The pullback of the projective
hyperplane bundle has degree one on each fiber $\PP^1$,
so its coordinate sections are a holomorphic rank-two matrix
$A(L):\CC^2\to\CC^6$, after trivializing a base line.
Choose holomorphic extension vectors $e_1(L),e_2(L)$ on which
its first row is $1,0$, and choose $e_1(L_*)$ to represent $p$.
The extension coordinate has the same unique simple pole:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-extension-pole}
 q_\nu(z)=\frac{b_\nu}{z-a_\nu}+k_\nu(z),\qquad b_\nu\ne0.
\end{equation}
The inverse $i^{-1}$ and the outer data make $q_\nu$ small
on the outer annulus. The residue and Cauchy estimates give,
on smaller discs and for $\eta_\nu\to0$,
\[
 |b_\nu|\leq C\eta_\nu r_\nu,\quad
 \|L_\nu-L_*\|+\|k_\nu\|\leq C\eta_\nu,\quad
 \|\partial_z^jL_\nu\|+\|\partial_z^jk_\nu\|
                   \leq C_j\eta_\nu r_\nu^{-j}\quad(j\geq1).
\]
The projective residue in \eqref{eq:converse-projective-pole}
is $b_\nu$ times the last five components of $A(L_\nu(a_\nu))e_2$;
their norm is bounded above and below.

Put $L_\nu^0=L_\nu(a_\nu)$, $k_\nu^0=k_\nu(a_\nu)$, and
$v=e_1(L_\nu^0)+k_\nu^0e_2(L_\nu^0)$. The current comparison
pencil is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-current-pencil}
              e_\nu^0(z)=(z-a_\nu)v+b_\nu e_2(L_\nu^0).
\end{equation}
To identify the actual bundle, choose the local Poincaré family
on one fixed smooth line bundle, with line Dolbeault operator
holomorphic in $L$. Choose representatives $\psi_j(L)$ of $e_j(L)$
holomorphic in $L$ and uniformly smooth in $\Sigma$. Two fixed
$(0,1)$-forms spanning the negative-degree $H^1$ at $L_*$
remain a basis nearby; a holomorphic change of basis gives
these representatives. All $(0,1)$-forms are closed on a curve,
$H^0$ is zero, and $h^1=2$ is constant.
The actual operator has base part $\bar\partial_z$ and surface part
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-stable-operator}
\begin{pmatrix}
 \bar\partial_{L_\nu(z)}
 &(z-a_\nu)\psi_1(L_\nu(z))
       +[b_\nu+(z-a_\nu)k_\nu(z)]\psi_2(L_\nu(z))\\
 0&\bar\partial_{\Lambda L_\nu(z)^{-1}}
\end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
Its coefficients are holomorphic in $z$, so it is integrable.
Its stable fibers have precisely the lifted extension classes.
The fine-moduli property identifies it projectively with the
original doubled bundle; the remaining line from the disc is
trivial. Replacing $L_\nu,k_\nu$ by their current values at
$a_\nu$ gives $E_\nu^0$ with the same base operator.

For $e_\nu^0(z)=zv+w_0$ set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-normalized-center}
 c_\nu=-\operatorname{Re}\frac{\langle w_0,v\rangle}{\|v\|^2},
 \qquad
 \rho_\nu=s(e_\nu^0)>0.
\end{equation}
The prescribed metric in \eqref{eq:gluing-affine-slice-functions}
gives uniformly
\[
 \rho_\nu\asymp
       (|\operatorname{Im}a_\nu|^2+|b_\nu|^2)^{1/2},
 \qquad |c_\nu-\operatorname{Re}a_\nu|\leq C|b_\nu|.
\]
Indeed the projection of $e_2$ perpendicular to $v$ is bounded
below, and $\kappa+a_\nu=O(b_\nu)$.
In $w=(z-c_\nu)/\rho_\nu$, divide the pencil by its common
scalar $\rho_\nu$ and put
$A_\nu=(a_\nu-c_\nu)/\rho_\nu$, $B_\nu=b_\nu/\rho_\nu$.
These coefficients are bounded and satisfy the affine slice
exactly. The slope remains nonzero. If the limit has $B=0$,
normalization forces $A=\pm i$; $A=-i$ is the negative
rank-drop limit, whereas $A=i$ is the excluded positive one.

On the normalized domains the slow parameters
$t_\nu=(L_\nu,k_\nu)$ satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-divided-parameters}
 |t_\nu(w)-t_\nu(A_\nu)|
       \leq C\eta_\nu|w-A_\nu|/R_\nu,\qquad
 |\partial_w^jt_\nu|\leq C_j\eta_\nu R_\nu^{-j}.
\end{equation}
Near the bounded $A_\nu$ the operator difference in the extension
frame is nonsingular, with all surface derivatives $O(\eta_\nu/R_\nu)$.
On the outer region use the common $\mathcal O(1)$ frame at infinity,
conjugating by $\operatorname{diag}(w^{-1},1)$. Its off-diagonal entry is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-infinity-operator}
 (1-A_\nu/w)[\psi_1(L_\nu(w))+k_\nu(w)\psi_2(L_\nu(w))]
                       +(B_\nu/w)\psi_2(L_\nu(w)).
\end{equation}
The whole-disc surface-operator error is $O(\eta_\nu)$.
On a logarithmic disc it and its fixed derivatives are
$\eta_\nu/R_\nu$ times a fixed polynomial in the radius.
No division by $B_\nu$ occurs. The common base operators and
transitions identify the base-holomorphic structures, and the
converging integrable coefficients give holomorphic bundle
convergence on fixed compact products, including negative rank drop.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[enlarging the stable-family disc]
\label{rem:converse-enlargement}
Suppose a larger shrinking doubled disc contains the original one,
has the same coherent charge one, and has an outer cap of vanishing
area about the same attaching class. Its stable-reduction map
restricts to the original stable map and has area at least
$1/2+o(1)$. Its integer degree is therefore at least one for
large $\nu$. The identity $d+\ell=1$ gives $d=1,\ell=0$.
The intervening annulus has area tending to zero and both boundary
images near the attaching point; the target-ball monotonicity
argument gives uniform image smallness on that annulus.
The inverse extension-family branch extends, and its pole,
residue, and current values at the pole are unchanged.
Thus center and scale are fixed before enlargement. When these
coherent outer data are available, slow diagonal radii at least
$\sqrt{\rho_\nu}$ give \eqref{eq:converse-radii}, after fixed
margins and recentering. Boundary values on one preassigned disc
alone do not justify this enlargement.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Comparison metrics on expanding half-discs}

Let $H_\nu$ be the actual projective HYM metric. We use metrics on
the same actual holomorphic bundle, rather than compare two surface
operators as if they were holomorphically identical.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:converse-reference-metric}
In either sector there is a metric $K_\nu$ on the actual negative
bundle with zero trace-free base curvature and uniformly bounded
surface curvature in every fixed surface order. In a base-parallel
frame it is independent of $w$ and has attaching NS connection
$\alpha_\nu$. The actual Dolbeault operator in that frame is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-base-flat-frame}
 \bar\partial_w+\bar\partial_{\alpha_\nu}+c_\nu(w,x),
                         \qquad \bar\partial_wc_\nu=0.
\end{equation}
The coefficients have uniformly bounded fixed surface derivatives
on the growing half-disc and uniformly bounded fixed mixed derivatives
on its unit neighborhoods away from the artificial outer edge.
For $s_\nu=\log(K_\nu^{-1}H_\nu)$, determinant normalized, the
boundary values satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-metric-boundary}
\begin{split}
 \|s_\nu(x,\cdot)\|_{C^k_\Sigma}
        &\leq C_k\eta_\nu(1+|x|)/R_\nu
                           \qquad(x\in[-R_\nu,R_\nu]),\\
 \sup_{\substack{|w|=R_\nu\\\operatorname{Im}w\leq0}}
       \|s_\nu(w,\cdot)\|_{C^k_\Sigma}
        &\leq C_k(b_{k,\nu}+\eta_\nu+R_\nu^{-1}).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Here $b_{k,\nu}\to0$. On every logarithmic real interval the seam trace has the analogous
small bounds for each fixed tangential and surface derivative,
with a fixed polynomial in that interval's radius.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In the one-modification case let $P_\nu$ be the NS-orthogonal projection
onto $G_\nu\subset V_\nu$ and set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-blaschke-metric}
 S_\nu(w)=(I-P_\nu)+\frac{w-a}{w-\bar a}P_\nu,\qquad
                      K_\nu=S_\nu^*H_{\mathrm{NS},\nu}.
\end{equation}
On the inverse-transform frame $e_1/(w-a),e_2$, the two factors
of $S_\nu$ are $1/(w-\bar a),1$. Thus it is invertible on the
actual bundle throughout the closed negative half-plane. It is
holomorphic in the base and smooth in the surface. Consequently
the base Chern curvature of $K_\nu$ is zero. On the seam
$|(w-a)/(w-\bar a)|=1$; at infinity $S_\nu=I+O(1/|w|)$.
For $E_\nu^0$ this is its unique base-flat Dirichlet metric.
For the actual bundle the retained surface-operator error in the
divisor and stable frames proves all required surface-curvature
bounds.

In the stable-family case transfer the base-flat Dirichlet metric
of $E_\nu^0$ by the base-holomorphic identification in
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}. The compact Dirichlet
construction and its parameter dependence give uniform smooth
metrics on the closed negative hemisphere. All boundary fibers,
including infinity, stay in a compact stable family; an unstable
limiting interior fiber is irrelevant to the base Dirichlet problem.
The two uniform extension charts and
\eqref{eq:converse-infinity-operator} bound the surface error.
This proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-reference-curvature}
 F_{K_\nu,BB}^{\perp}=0,\qquad
 \sup|\nabla_\Sigma^j F_{K_\nu,\Sigma\Sigma}^{\perp}|\leq C_j.
\end{equation}
Parallel transport for the flat projective base connection from
infinity identifies the bundle with the pullback of its attaching
surface bundle. This identification is base-holomorphic and unitary
for $K_\nu$, and smooth in the compact family. It gives
\eqref{eq:converse-base-flat-frame} in both cases.
The extension charts on a fixed core and at infinity, together with
the slow-parameter Cauchy bounds, verify the stated coefficient bounds.

On the seam both metrics are NS metrics for nearby surface operators.
The trace-free NS metric linearization has zero kernel on the compact
stable family, so its surface implicit-function theorem has uniform
parameter and surface bounds. The difference of the actual and current
parameters vanishes at $a$ or $A_\nu$ and is bounded by
$C\eta_\nu(1+|x|)/R_\nu$. This proves the first line of
\eqref{eq:converse-metric-boundary} and its local tangential versions.
For the first sector use also the unitary $S_\nu(x)$ on the seam;
for the second use the common infinity extension chart at large $x$.

On the outer negative semicircle the stipulated unitary connections
are close to the residual attaching connection. A stable complex
slice supplies a close isomorphism to their holomorphic representative.
The comparison frame at infinity is in that same small stable
neighborhood and its metric differs from its attaching NS metric by
$O(R_\nu^{-1})$. These maps compare the actual metrics there in all
fixed surface orders. To compare their identifications with the
chosen global one, observe that both are isomorphisms of the same
stable rank-two surface bundle. Their ratio is scalar and disappears
from the projective metric ratio. This proves the second line of
\eqref{eq:converse-metric-boundary}. No norm of the scalar, or
determinant square root on an annulus, is needed.
\end{proof}

Set
\[
 U_\nu=\log\frac{\tr(K_\nu^{-1}H_\nu)}
                   {2\sqrt{\det(K_\nu^{-1}H_\nu)}}.
\]
For determinant-one ratio with eigenvalues $e^\lambda,e^{-\lambda}$,
$U_\nu=\log\cosh\lambda$. The Chern trace inequality on the actual
holomorphic bundle and \eqref{eq:converse-reference-curvature} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-scalar-inequality}
       (\Delta_w+\rho_\nu^2\Delta_\Sigma)U_\nu\leq C\rho_\nu^2,
\end{equation}
with nonnegative-spectrum scalar Laplacians. The boundary estimate
and the barrier $C\rho_\nu^2(R_\nu^2-|w|^2)$ imply
\[
 \sup U_\nu\leq C(b_{0,\nu}+\eta_\nu+R_\nu^{-1}+r_\nu^2)\to0.
\]
In particular $s_\nu\to0$ uniformly on the entire growing half-disc.

A sharper seam barrier supplies the rate needed near the core.
Write $w=x-it$, $t\geq0$, choose fixed $A_0\geq1$, and put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-sharp-barrier}
\begin{split}
 P_R(w)&=-\frac2\pi\operatorname{Im}\log\frac{R+w}{R-w},\\
 J_R(w)&=x^2-t^2+Rt+A_0^2,\qquad
 V_R(w)=\tfrac12t(R-t).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The function $P_R$ is harmonic, zero on the diameter and one on
the semicircle; $P_R\leq Ct/R$ on $D_{R/2}^-$. The function
$J_R$ is nonnegative harmonic with seam value $x^2+A_0^2$,
and $V_R\geq0$, $\Delta_wV_R=1$.
The first boundary estimate gives
$U(x)\leq C\eta^2(x^2+A_0^2)/R^2$.
If $m_R$ is the bounded outer discrepancy, comparison with
$C\eta^2R^{-2}J_R+m_RP_R+C\rho^2V_R$ yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-inner-scalar}
 \sup_{D_L^-\times\Sigma}U\leq C(L/R+\rho^2RL),\qquad
 \|s\|_{L^\infty(D_L^-\times\Sigma)}
        \leq C(L/R+\rho^2RL)^{1/2}\leq CL^{1/2}\rho^{1/4}
\end{equation}
for $1\leq L\leq R/2$. At the two corners continuity and the
seam estimate give the required boundary upper limits.
The last inequality uses \eqref{eq:converse-radii} and $r\leq1$.
No convergence rate of the exterior data occurs in this bound.

\subsection{Surface derivatives and the inner connection norm}

The following derivative argument keeps surface orders unweighted;
rescaled four-dimensional elliptic estimates alone would lose them.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:converse-surface-derivatives}
For every fixed $k$, all surface derivatives of $s_\nu$ through
order $k$ tend uniformly to zero on the growing half-disc.
For every fixed $A>0$ and finite $m$, on $|w|\leq T_\nu^A$
the actual solution can be put in a unitary normal axial gauge with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-inner-jets}
\begin{split}
 \|\widehat\beta_\nu-\beta_\nu^0\|_{C^m}
       +\|u_\nu-u_\nu^0\|_{C^m}&\leq C_{A,m}\rho_\nu^{1/10},\\
 \|\widehat A_{B,\nu}\|_{C^m}&\leq C_{A,m}\rho_\nu^2T_\nu^A.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The base component in the second line is expressed in $w$.
Map differences use the exponential chart along the compact primitive
family. The localized ambient raw cap difference from its exact
physical cap is $o(T_\nu^{-N})$ for every fixed $N$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In the frame \eqref{eq:converse-base-flat-frame} write $H=K^0h$,
and let $\nabla$ be the attaching flat adjoint connection and the
fixed surface tensor connection. Put
\[
 q=h^{-1}\nabla_\Sigma h,\qquad
 \mathcal D_\xi v=\nabla_\xi v+\tfrac12[q(\xi),v],\qquad
 v_1=q,\quad v_{j+1}=\mathcal D_\Sigma v_j.
\]
This connection preserves the real $H$-self-adjoint bundle.
Let $A$ be the actual Chern connection,
$d_{A,\rho}=(d_{A,B},\rho\nabla_{A,\Sigma})$, and
$L_{A,\rho}=\nabla_{A,\rho}^*\nabla_{A,\rho}/2$.
The differentiated metric equations have the estimate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-metric-variation}
 |L_{A,\rho}v_k|\leq C_k\rho^2(1+|v_k|)
  +C_k\sum_{\substack{1\leq i,j<k\\i+j\leq k}}
                         |d_{A,\rho}v_i|\,|d_{A,\rho}v_j|.
\end{equation}
Here the constants use bounded lower surface derivatives and
the bounded surface coefficients of $c$, but no separate bound
for a base derivative of the unknown metric.

We justify this form. Under unitary metric transport a metric
velocity $v=H^{-1}\dot H$ changes the connection by
$(\partial_A-\bar\partial_A)v/2$. A change $\gamma$ of the
Dolbeault operator adds $\gamma-\gamma^{*H}$. For surface
differentiation $\gamma$ has only surface components. The
variation of $L$ in a skew connection direction $\Theta$ is,
in parallel tangent frames,
\[
 \dot Lw=-\sum_\mu\lambda_\mu[\Theta_\mu,\nabla_{A,\mu}w]
       -\tfrac12[\sum_\mu\lambda_\mu\nabla_{A,\mu}\Theta_\mu,w],
             \qquad \lambda_B=1,\quad\lambda_\Sigma=\rho^2.
\]
Here is the second variation and the structure preserved by
further differentiation. Write
$\mathfrak m=(i\Lambda_\rho F_A)_0=0$, and use $s,t$ for
surface derivative indices. Put
\[
 K(v)_\mu=-\tfrac i2J_\mu{}^\nu\nabla_{A,\nu}v,\qquad
 C_s=\gamma_s-\gamma_s^{*H},\qquad \gamma_s=\nabla_s c.
\]
The reference variation $C_s$ has only surface components.
On endomorphism coefficients the transported connection variation is
\[
 [\mathcal D_s,\nabla_{A,\mu}]
       =\operatorname{ad}B_{s,\mu},\qquad
 B_s=K(v_s)+C_s.
\]
On tensor-valued fields this commutator also contains the
fixed surface tensor-curvature action in a surface slot.
It vanishes in a base slot.
The two identities
\[
 (i\Lambda_\rho d_AK(v))_0=L_{A,\rho}v,\qquad
 \operatorname{div}_{A,\rho}K(v)=-\tfrac12[\mathfrak m,v]
\]
give the first differentiated equation
$L_{A,\rho}v_t+\rho^2S_t=0$, where
\[
 S_t=(i\Lambda_\Sigma d_AC_t)_0+S_t^{\rm geom},
 \qquad |S_t^{\rm geom}|\leq C|v_1|.
\]
The term $S_t^{\rm geom}$ is the fixed surface
tensor-curvature contribution. Thus
$|S_t|\leq C(1+|v_1|)$. For $v_{st}=\mathcal D_sv_t$,
the second differentiated equation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-second-metric-variation}
\begin{split}
 L_{A,\rho}v_{st}
 ={}&\sum_\mu\lambda_\mu
        [K(v_s)_\mu,\nabla_{A,\mu}v_t]\\
 &+\rho^2\sum_{\mu\in T\Sigma}
        [C_{s,\mu},\nabla_{A,\mu}v_t]
   +\tfrac{\rho^2}{2}
        [\operatorname{div}_{A,\Sigma}C_s,v_t]\\
 &-\rho^2\mathcal D_sS_t+\rho^2R^{\rm geom}_{st}.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The omitted metric divergence is a commutator with
$\mathfrak m$ and vanishes. The tensor $R^{\rm geom}_{st}$
contains only fixed surface geometry and surface metric jets.
Moreover,
\[
\begin{split}
 \mathcal D_sC_t
   &=\nabla_s\gamma_t-(\nabla_s\gamma_t)^{*H}
       +\tfrac12[v_s,\gamma_t+\gamma_t^{*H}],\\
 \mathcal D_sS_t
   &=(i\Lambda_\Sigma
       (d_A\mathcal D_sC_t+[B_s\wedge C_t]))_0
        +\text{surface geometry terms}.
\end{split}
\]
Only the surface restriction of $B_s$ occurs in the
second line.
The surface Chern formula is
\[
 A_\Sigma=\alpha+\tfrac12q-\tfrac i2q\circ J_\Sigma+c-c^{*H},
 \quad
 \nabla_{A,\Sigma}v_j
   =v_{j+1}+[-\tfrac i2q\circ J_\Sigma+c-c^{*H},v_j].
\]
It bounds all reference terms in
\eqref{eq:converse-second-metric-variation} by
$C\rho^2(1+|v_2|)$ once $v_1$ is bounded.
The first sum in that equation pairs two base gradients with
weight one or two surface gradients with weight $\rho^2$.
It is bounded by $C|d_{A,\rho}v_1|^2$. This proves
\eqref{eq:converse-metric-variation} at order two.

For the induction, retain the stronger form
$L_{A,\rho}v_k=\rho^2R_k+P_k$.
The expression $R_k$ has no base derivative of the unknown
metric and is at most linear in $v_k$, with coefficients
controlled by lower surface jets and the reference data.
Each term of $P_k$ is bilinear in
$\nabla_Av_i,\nabla_Av_j$, where $i,j<k$ and $i+j\leq k$;
the derivative indices are contracted within the same
product factor. Base pairs have weight one and surface
pairs have weight $\rho^2$.

Apply one more ordered surface derivative. Variation of $L$
adds the paired product $K(v_1)\nabla_Av_k$ and
$\rho^2$-weighted reference terms. In differentiating $P_k$ use
\[
 \mathcal D_s(\nabla_{A,\mu}v_i)
   =\nabla_{A,\mu}v_{si}+[K(v_s)_\mu+C_{s,\mu},v_i]
        +R^\Sigma_{s\mu}v_i.
\]
Here $R^\Sigma_{s\mu}$ is the fixed tensor-curvature
action and is zero for a base index $\mu$.
The first term raises one derivative index by one.
The $K(v_s)$ term replaces one gradient by a gradient of
$v_1$ times a bounded lower jet, preserving the paired
contraction. The $C_s$ term occurs only in a surface slot:
its partner is therefore also a surface gradient and the
whole term retains $\rho^2$. The surface Chern formula
absorbs it in $R_{k+1}$.
Differentiating coefficients and $R_k$ introduces only
surface jets, with $v_{k+1}$ at most linear.

Fixed tensor-curvature commutators also have the surface
factor $\rho^2$. Ordered differentiation requires no
interchange of parameter indices. If they are interchanged,
the parameter curvature $-[q\wedge q]/4$ acts on
$\mathfrak m$ or a lower differentiated equation,
preserving the same form. Thus the induction closes
before taking norms and proves
\eqref{eq:converse-metric-variation} at every order.

Let $G$ be the scalar Dirichlet Green operator of
$\Delta_B+\rho^2\Delta_\Sigma$ on $D_R^-\times\Sigma$.
It is positive, $\|G1\|_\infty\leq CR^2$, and
\[
 \|G\sqrt{fg}\|_\infty
       \leq\|Gf\|_\infty^{1/2}\|Gg\|_\infty^{1/2}
                         \qquad(f,g\geq0).
\]
These assertions follow from the base barrier and Cauchy--Schwarz
in the positive Green measure. Put
$M_k=\sup|v_k|$, $\beta_k=\sup_{\partial}|v_k|$,
$I_k=\|G|d_{A,\rho}v_k|^2\|_\infty$.
Kato's inequality and the identity
\[
 (\Delta_B+\rho^2\Delta_\Sigma)|v_k|^2
     =4\langle L_{A,\rho}v_k,v_k\rangle-2|d_{A,\rho}v_k|^2
\]
give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-green-induction}
\begin{split}
 M_k&\leq\beta_k+C_kr^2(1+M_k)
                         +C_k\sum\sqrt{I_iI_j},\\
 I_k&\leq\tfrac12\beta_k^2+
             C_kM_k\bigl(r^2(1+M_k)+\sum\sqrt{I_iI_j}\bigr).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The sums have the indices in \eqref{eq:converse-metric-variation}
and are empty for $k=1$. Since $r\to0$ and the boundary
derivatives tend to zero, induction gives
$M_k\leq C_k B_k$ and $I_k\leq C_kB_k^2$,
where $B_k=\max_{j\leq k}\beta_j+r^2$.
Ordinary derivatives of $s$ are polynomial expressions in the
$v_j$ and bounded $h,h^{-1}$. Zeroth order is also controlled:
on the compact irreducible attaching family
$\|s\|_{C^0_\Sigma}\leq C\|\nabla_\alpha s\|_{C^0_\Sigma}$,
since otherwise a normalized sequence would limit to a nonzero
parallel trace-free section. This proves the first assertion.

On $L=T^A$, interpolate \eqref{eq:converse-inner-scalar} with
the uniform $C_\Sigma^{2k}$ bound to obtain
$\|s\|_{C_\Sigma^k}\leq C_kL^{1/4}\rho^{1/8}$.
The logarithmic metric equation in the base-parallel frame is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-base-metric-equation}
                \Delta_ws=Q(s)(d_ws,d_ws)+\rho^2f.
\end{equation}
Every fixed surface derivative of $f$ is bounded using two
further surface orders; $f$ has no base derivative of $h$.
On translated unit discs and seam half-discs, the two-dimensional
interpolation estimate
\[
 \|d_ws\|_{L^{2p}}^2\leq
     C\|s\|_\infty\|d_w^2s\|_{L^p}+C\|s\|_\infty^2,\qquad p>2,
\]
absorbs the quadratic term in the localized Laplace estimate.
At the seam subtract the known small Dirichlet trace. Surface
differentiation first gives the same estimate at every prescribed
surface order; further base differentiation then uses the already
obtained orders in $f$ and the two-dimensional $W^{2,p}$ algebra.
The coefficient and seam-jet bounds are uniform on the unit cover.
Taking suprema, rather than summing its cardinality, proves mixed
derivative bounds by a logarithmic polynomial times $\rho^{1/8}$.
The actual and comparison surface operators differ by
$\eta/R$ times such a polynomial. Unitary metric transport
by $h^{1/2}$ therefore gives the surface comparison.
In the positive hemisphere the primitive family is uniformly
stable, and its classifying map is smooth in $w$ and the slow
parameters; the same argument gives the positive comparison.

Finally use parallel transport along the seam from its center,
then along normal segments, with the convention
$A^g=gAg^{-1}-dg\,g^{-1}$. The gauge differs from the initial
one by a logarithmic polynomial times $\rho^{1/8}$, at every
fixed order. In $w=x-it$ it satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-axial}
 A_t^g=0,\quad A_x^g(x,0)=0,\quad
 A_x^g(x,t)=\int_0^t F_{A^g,tx}(x,\tau)\,d\tau,\quad
 F_{A^g,tx}=\rho^2\mathcal C(F_{A^g,\Sigma\Sigma}),
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal C$ is the fixed signed contraction. All fixed
surface and unit base derivatives of the last curvature are
bounded. Differentiating the integral proves the base estimate
in \eqref{eq:converse-inner-jets}. The strict exponent gap
from $1/8$ to $1/10$ absorbs every fixed logarithmic polynomial.
The gauge preserves the actual matching condition.

The exact physical caps have error $\rho^2T^J$ from this same
compact base-flat family, at every prescribed finite order.
Apply the same axial construction on their Euclidean regions.
For their difference from the actual solution,
$\Delta A_w=O(\rho^2T^J)$ and the surface/map difference is
$O(\rho^{1/10}T^J)$. The raw cap base coefficient is
$a=(f_\infty/\rho)O_a\Delta A_w\circ\Phi$, as in the source
conversion above. Thus $a/M_1$ is a positive power of $\rho$
times a polynomial in $T$, even after its physical $\rho^{-1}$
conversion. The other strong terms and the weak terms
$\rho d_\alpha a$, $\rho f_\infty d_\alpha b$ have the same
property. Fixed compact-coordinate derivatives of the inner
cutoff and frame conversions cost only powers of $T$ on
$|w|\leq T^{81}$. Bounding these exponential errors at two
integer base orders proves their ambient raw norm is
$o(T^{-N})$ for every fixed $N$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The critical seam trace and the larger axial gauge}

Set $Z=w/R=X-iY$. We need an $H^{3/2}$ bound for the seam
logarithm after this dilation; small pointwise values alone do not
give this trace estimate.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:converse-critical-trace}
On every fixed smaller real $Z$ interval and for each fixed surface
order $k$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-critical-trace}
              \|s(R\,\cdot)|_{\RR}\|_{H_X^{3/2}H_\Sigma^k}
                                                 \leq C_k\eta.
\end{equation}
In the base-parallel frame write $c=c^0+e$, where $c^0$ is the
comparison-primitive coefficient. On a fixed smaller negative $Z$
half-disc,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-large-coefficients}
\begin{split}
 \|e\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}
       +\|d_Ze\|_{L_Z^2H_\Sigma^k}&\leq C_k\eta,\\
 \|c^0\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}
       +\|d_Zc^0\|_{L_Z^2H_\Sigma^k}&\leq C_k.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The integrals of $|d_Ze|_{C_\Sigma^k}$ and
$|d_Zc^0|_{C_\Sigma^k}$ along every vertical segment of a
smaller fixed rectangle are bounded by $C_k\eta$ and $C_k$,
respectively.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In either case the seam metric logarithm is a smooth function
$F_E(x,d)$, with $F_E(x,0)=0$, of the compact comparison primitive
$E$ and the difference $d(x)$ of its slow parameters.
It is smooth also at $x=\infty$ in the coordinate $1/x$,
with uniformly bounded parameter and surface derivatives.
For the first sector this follows from the NS family and
conjugation by $S(x)$ in \eqref{eq:converse-blaschke-metric}.
For the second it follows from the common extension frames and
the compact stable seam family. Only a function on the compact
real boundary is being extended at infinity.

The Taylor integral gives $F_E(x,d)=Q_E(x,d)d$, with $Q_E$
real-linear in its vector argument. The exact divided difference
is $d(w)=(w-A)a(w)$, with
$|\partial_w^ja|\leq C_j\eta R^{-1-j}$ and bounded $A$
($A=-i$ in the first sector). In real coordinates let $M_A$
be multiplication by $A$. Taylor expansion at infinity gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-seam-expansion}
 Q_E(x,d)(xI-M_A)
       =xQ_\infty(d)+Q_0(d)+K_E(x,d),\qquad
 |\partial_x^j\partial_d^\gamma\nabla_\Sigma^kK_E|
                    \leq C_{j,\gamma,k}(1+|x|)^{-1-j}.
\end{equation}
The two ends of the real line have the same expansion in $1/x$.
The bound on a fixed interval follows by smoothness.
Put $b(X)=Ra(RX)$. Both $b$ and $d(RX)$ have all fixed
$X$ derivatives $O(\eta)$ on the retained interval. The seam
logarithm is the sum
\[
 XQ_\infty(d(RX))b(X)+R^{-1}Q_0(d(RX))b(X)
                   +R^{-1}K_E(RX,d(RX))b(X).
\]
The first two terms have $C_X^2C_\Sigma^k$ size $O(\eta)$.
For the last, insert a fixed outer cutoff. Change of variables
and \eqref{eq:converse-seam-expansion} give
\[
 \|R^{-1}K_E(R\,\cdot,d(R\,\cdot))\|_{H_X^1H_\Sigma^k}
       \leq C_kR^{-1/2},\qquad
 \|R^{-1}K_E(R\,\cdot,d(R\,\cdot))\|_{H_X^2H_\Sigma^k}
       \leq C_kR^{1/2}.
\]
The largest second derivative is $R\partial_x^2K_E$;
derivatives of $d(RX)$ are bounded and all parameter derivatives
of $K_E$ have the same decay. Multiplication by $b$ costs
$C_k\eta$, taking further surface orders where needed.
Interpolation between these fixed Hilbert norms proves
\eqref{eq:converse-critical-trace}. A higher uniform trace
order is not asserted.

For the first sector the surface error in the global $S$ frame
has the four blocks
\[
 e(w)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 (w-a)k_{11}&(w-a)^2(w-\bar a)^{-1}k_{12}\\
 (w-\bar a)k_{21}&(w-a)k_{22}
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
Polynomial division of $(w-a)^2/(w-\bar a)$ and the slow
derivatives of $k$ give
$|e|\leq C\eta(1+|w|)/R$, $|d_we|\leq C\eta/R$,
in every fixed surface order. The pole is outside the closed
negative half-plane. In the second sector write $e=G_E(w,d(w))$,
where $G_E$ is smooth on the compact negative hemisphere,
$G_E(w,0)=0$, and its fixed-parameter base derivative is
$O((1+|w|)^{-2})$. These facts follow from
\eqref{eq:converse-stable-operator} and
\eqref{eq:converse-infinity-operator} in the fixed primitive
base-parallel frame. The same bounds for $e,d_we$ follow.
In both cases
$|d_wc^0|_{C_\Sigma^k}\leq C_k(1+|w|)^{-2}$.
The Dirichlet integral is conformally invariant under $w=RZ$,
and the vertical integral of this last decay is uniformly
bounded after the substitution $t=RY$.
These observations prove all coefficient and segment assertions.
\end{proof}

Let $d_{k,\nu}$ be the supremum of the surface derivatives of
$s_\nu$ through order $k$. They tend to zero by
Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-surface-derivatives}. We write
$\tau_{k,\nu}\to0$ for a finite sum of $\eta_\nu,r_\nu^2$,
and sufficiently many of these $d_{j,\nu}$; this asserts no
logarithmic rate. On fixed nested $Z$ half-discs the metric equation
is
\[
                  \Delta_Zs=Q(s)(d_Zs,d_Zs)+r^2f.
\]
Use the fixed trace extension $H^{3/2}\to H^2$ and
$\|d_Zs\|_4^2\leq C\|s\|_\infty\|d_Z^2s\|_2+C\|s\|_\infty^2$
to absorb its quadratic term. Differentiating in $\Sigma$ first,
the highest new first-derivative term has the small coefficient
$d_Zs$ in $L^4$; all other products use preceding orders.
The bounded surface derivatives of $f$ and the small boundary
trace close the finite induction. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-large-metric}
                   \|s(R\,\cdot)\|_{H_Z^2H_\Sigma^k}
                                           \leq C_k\tau_{k,\nu}.
\end{equation}
Pointwise surface uses of this estimate take two further surface
orders. It does not commute a surface supremum with a base
$L^2$ norm for free.

After unitary metric transport, $A'_Z$ is a smooth expression
in $s,d_Zs$, so $\|A'_Z\|_{H_Z^1H_\Sigma^k}\leq C_k\tau_{k,\nu}$.
For an $H^1$ function on a fixed rectangle the $L^2$ trace on
each parallel line segment of a smaller rectangle is uniformly
bounded by its $H^1$ norm: apply the one-dimensional $H^1$
estimate with values in the other variable's $L^2$ space.
The same statement holds at the real boundary. Consequently
the seam and normal transport integrals of $A'_Z$ are small,
in every prescribed surface order. The axial gauge $g$ based
at the seam center satisfies
$\|g-I\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}\leq C_k\tau_{k,\nu}$.

The curvature identity now gives $A_Z^g=O(r^2)$.
To differentiate its axial integral, the vertical integral of
$d_ZF_{\Sigma\Sigma}$ is uniformly bounded: differentiate the
surface Chern formula, use \eqref{eq:converse-large-metric}
and the segment trace for $d_Zs$, and use
\eqref{eq:converse-large-coefficients} for $d_Zc$.
The first-order gauge equation gives the integrals of $d_Zg$
after the $A_Z^g$ bound is known. It therefore also bounds
the differentiated conjugation of surface curvature.
Differentiating the axial integral proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-large-axial}
\begin{split}
 \|A_Z^g\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}
       +\|d_ZA_Z^g\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}&\leq C_kr^2,\\
 \|\beta^g-\beta^0\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}
       +\|\beta^g-\beta^0\|_{H_Z^1H_\Sigma^k}
                                             &\leq C_k\tau_{k,\nu}.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
For the second line, before $g$ the difference consists of
$e$, surface derivatives of $h$, and small metric factors
times bounded reference coefficients. Differentiating in $Z$
uses \eqref{eq:converse-large-coefficients},
\eqref{eq:converse-large-metric}, and the finite Dirichlet norm
of $c^0$. After $g$ use the differentiated first-order gauge
equation and the small factor $g-I$ multiplying $d_Z\beta^0$.
This does not require a uniform $H^1$ multiplier bound for
the rapidly varying reference.
In physical coordinates, the first line says
$A_B^{\rm phys}=O(r)$, $d_zA_B^{\rm phys}=O(1)$ and its
physical $H^1$ norm on the shrinking disc is $O(r)$.

On the positive side the difference from the comparison primitive
has the same $C^0$ and $H_Z^1$ smallness. In the stable-family
case its parameter variation is smooth on the compact positive
hemisphere; in the first case it is the slow stable slice.
In both cases smoothness of the primitive at infinity gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-primitive-tail}
 |\nabla_w^j(\beta^0-\alpha_\nu,u^0-p_\nu^0)|_{C_\Sigma^k}
                   \leq C_{j,k}|w|^{-1-j},\qquad
 p_\nu^0=[\alpha_\nu],\quad |w|\geq C_*.
\end{equation}
For the positive map use the attaching chart and omit the
surface norm. The map need not be constant in the second sector.

\subsection{Removing the primitive core}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:converse-core-removal}
Put $C_\nu=T_\nu^{40}$ and $h_\nu=\rho_\nu C_\nu$.
There are smooth mixed configurations $y_\nu$, equal to
$(\alpha_\nu,p_\nu^0)$ on $|z-c_\nu|\leq h_\nu$ and equal
to $x_\nu$ outside $2h_\nu$, which preserve exact matching,
zero normal trace, and all ends. Their geometric equation source
has complete residual norm $O(T_\nu^{-40})$ at
$\varepsilon_\nu=1/T_\nu$. They have global representatives
converging strongly to $x_0$ in the critical residual ambient
norms and in $C^0$ after each fixed surface derivative on the
collar. Their background divergence, with its exact end limit
subtracted, tends to zero in every required fixed $L^p$ norm.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the annulus $|w|\sim C_\nu$, set $v=w/C_\nu$.
Equations~\eqref{eq:converse-inner-jets} and
\eqref{eq:converse-primitive-tail} give, in every fixed annular
and surface order,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-removal-annulus}
 \|(\beta-\alpha_\nu,u-p_\nu^0)\|\leq q_\nu,\qquad
 \|A_v\|\leq p_\nu,\quad
 q_\nu=C(T_\nu^{-40}+\rho_\nu^{1/10}T_\nu^J),\quad
 p_\nu=C\rho_\nu^2T_\nu^J.
\end{equation}
The exponent $J$ may increase with the fixed derivative count.
In the exact nonlinear matching chart about the flat pair,
interpolate all surface and positive coordinates by a fixed
radial cutoff $\chi(v)$, zero inside radius one and one outside
radius two. Interpolate the base one-form by the same cutoff.
The common harmonic coordinates on the seam remain equal,
and the coexact seam coordinate remains zero. The resulting
surface connection is
$\alpha_\nu+\chi(\beta-\alpha_\nu)+\mathcal R$,
with all fixed derivatives of $\mathcal R$ bounded by $Cq_\nu^2$.
There is the same quadratic remainder on the positive side.

The exact input equations cancel. In physical coefficients
the scalar-row source is bounded by
$C[h_\nu^{-2}(p_\nu+p_\nu^2)+q_\nu^2]$, and the mixed row by
$Ch_\nu^{-1}(q_\nu+p_\nu q_\nu+q_\nu^2)$.
The positive row obeys the latter bound without the $p_\nu$ term.
For a coefficient of fixed-annulus $H^1_vH_\Sigma^k$ norm $K$,
physical dilation and interpolation give
\[
                    \|f((z-c)/h)\|_{H_B^\varepsilon H_\Sigma^k}
                                  \leq Ch^{1-\varepsilon}K,
                         \qquad 0\leq\varepsilon\leq1/4.
\]
The annular support margins permit extension by zero.
All residual source rows, including their surface operators,
are controlled by this local norm, with one further fixed
surface order when needed. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-removal-source}
 \|\mathcal F(y_\nu)\|_{Y^{\rm res}_{\varepsilon_\nu,k}}
 \leq C_k\bigl[
 h_\nu^{-1-\varepsilon_\nu}(p_\nu+p_\nu^2)
 +h_\nu^{1-\varepsilon_\nu}q_\nu^2
 +h_\nu^{-\varepsilon_\nu}(q_\nu+p_\nu q_\nu+q_\nu^2)\bigr]
 \leq C_kT_\nu^{-40}.
\end{equation}
The source is identified by the current attaching surface frame
and the bounded positive target frame on its support.
Here $h_\nu^{-\varepsilon_\nu}$ is bounded and $p_\nu/h_\nu$
is exponentially small. The perturbation dependency assumption
ensures there is no source elsewhere.

The gauge in \eqref{eq:converse-large-axial} agrees with the
inner axial gauge by uniqueness of their seam-then-normal
transport. On the transition its base coefficient is
$O(r^2C_\nu/R)$ in $Z$, so a derivative of the removal cutoff
preserves its $O(r^2)$ derivative bound. The reference's
Dirichlet tail outside $|w|\geq C_\nu$ is $O(C_\nu^{-1})$.
Together with \eqref{eq:converse-large-axial} this gives, on
a fixed smaller physical relative disc,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-erased-local-norm}
\begin{split}
 \|A_{y,B}^{\rm phys}\|_\infty&\leq C_kr,\quad
 \|d_zA_{y,B}^{\rm phys}\|_\infty\leq C_k,\quad
 \|A_{y,B}^{\rm phys}\|_{H_B^1H_\Sigma^k}\leq C_kr,\\
 \|\beta_y-\alpha_\nu\|_{H_B^1H_\Sigma^k}
        +\|u_y-p_\nu^0\|_{H_B^1}
                       &\leq C_k(\tau_{k,\nu}+T_\nu^{-40}+r),\\
 \|\beta_y-\alpha_\nu\|_{C_B^0C_\Sigma^k}
        +\|u_y-p_\nu^0\|_\infty
                       &\leq C_k(\tau_{k,\nu}+T_\nu^{-40}).
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Suprema of base fields in the first line include the displayed
surface order. On the shrinking transition, estimate derivatives
of the cutoff using \eqref{eq:converse-removal-annulus};
one must not apply an unbounded cutoff multiplier to the
qualitative error $\tau_{k,\nu}$.

On the exterior overlap compare this axial representative with
the stipulated representative converging to the based residual.
If $A_2=A_1^G$, the gauge identities are
\[
 d_\Sigma G=G\beta_1-\beta_2G,\qquad
 d_BG=GA_{1,B}-A_{2,B}G.
\]
Unitarity and the first identity give bounded surface derivatives.
The physical base bounds and their first derivatives give
$d_BG=O(r)$, $d_B^2G=O(1)$, in all fixed surface orders.
Fix one base value $G_0$ on the connected overlap. Integration
along paths of length $Cr$ gives
\[
 H=GG_0^{-1},\qquad H-I=O(r^2),\quad d_BH=O(r),\quad
                                      d_B^2H=O(1).
\]
First retain $G_0$ as a constant surface gauge on the inner
representative, then cut off only $\log H$ on the overlap.
The cutoff gauge has base derivative $O(r)$ and second
derivative $O(1)$; its physical $H^1$ contribution tends to
zero. Choose it normal-constant on the seam. Undo the common
based residual gauge using its fixed-collar extension.
This proves global critical and $C^0$ convergence.

The background divergence uses first derivatives of the base
component, which are bounded on a region of area $O(r^2)$,
and surface derivatives of the surface difference, which tend
uniformly to zero. Thus it tends to zero in each fixed finite
$L^p$ norm on compact sets. The joined configurations equal
the original ones on all ends. If $B_{p,e}$ is the exact
critical family, define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-end-divergence}
 e_e(p)=d_{B_{0,e}}^*(B_{p,e}-B_{0,e}),\qquad
 \sigma_p=\sum_e\chi_e e_e(p).
\end{equation}
Since $B'_0=p$ is co-closed, $e_e(p)=O(|p|^2)$.
Subtracting $\sigma_{p_\nu}$ gives the asserted weighted
divergence convergence. Its unsubtracted nondecaying value
need not belong to a weighted source space.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A fixed gauge and the quantitative residual estimate}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:converse-fixed-gauge}
Let $a_\nu=A_{y_\nu}-A_0$ in the preceding global representatives
and $f_\nu=d_{A_0}^*a_\nu-\sigma_{p_\nu}$. There are smooth
gauges $g_\nu=\exp\phi_\nu$, with zero covariant normal derivative
and tending to the identity on all ends, such that
\[
 d_{A_0}^*(A_{y_\nu}^{g_\nu}-A_0)=\sigma_{p_\nu},\qquad
 \|\phi_\nu\|_{W_\omega^{2,2}\cap W_\omega^{2,6}}\to0.
\]
The same convergence holds after every fixed surface derivative
on a smaller fixed matching collar. The gauges preserve critical
convergence, all exact critical profiles, and the geometric
source estimate \eqref{eq:converse-removal-source}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The fixed operator $P=d_{A_0}^*d_{A_0}$ with covariant Neumann
condition has a positive global $L^2$ gap. On each end cross-section
irreducibility excludes a parallel zero-form and gives a tangential
Poincaré inequality. It controls the far tails of a putative
normalized sequence with derivative tending to zero. Rellich
compactness on the remaining fixed compact part would then give
a nonzero parallel zero-form for $A_0$, a contradiction.
For $q=2,6$, integration against $|v|^{q-2}v$ gives
\[
 \int\langle Pv,|v|^{q-2}v\rangle
 \geq \frac{4(q-1)}{q^2}\|d_{A_0}(|v|^{q/2-1}v)\|_2^2
 \geq \frac{4(q-1)\lambda}{q^2}\|v\|_q^q.
\]
Fixed Neumann estimates therefore give
$\|v\|_{W^{2,q}}\leq C_q\|Pv\|_q$.
For compact smooth forcing, start with the $L^2$ inverse,
use fixed local regularity, and insert end cutoffs in this
inequality; density gives the agreeing $L^2$ and $L^6$
inverses. Conjugation by a sufficiently small fixed weight
$e^{\omega t_0}$ changes $P$ by a small first-order operator
and leaves its Neumann domain unchanged. A Neumann series yields
\[
 Q_N=P^{-1}:L_\omega^2\cap L_\omega^6
                  \longrightarrow W_{\omega,N}^{2,2}\cap
                                         W_{\omega,N}^{2,6}.
\]
The notation denotes the one bounded inverse on the intersection.

For the gauge action used above set
$M_\phi=e^{\ad\phi}$, $J_\phi=\int_0^1e^{s\ad\phi}\,ds$.
Then $a^g=M_\phi a-J_\phi d_{A_0}\phi$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-gauge-remainder}
\begin{split}
 d_{A_0}^*a^g-\sigma_p&=f-P\phi+N(\phi),\\
 N(\phi)&=(M_\phi-I)(f+\sigma_p)
       -(\nabla_{A_0}M_\phi)\mathbin{\lrcorner}a\\
 &\quad -(J_\phi-I)P\phi
       +(\nabla_{A_0}J_\phi)\mathbin{\lrcorner}d_{A_0}\phi .
\end{split}
\end{equation}
The only derivative of $a$ is its known divergence. In
$Y_g=L_\omega^2\cap L_\omega^6$ and
$Z_g=W_{\omega,N}^{2,2}\cap W_{\omega,N}^{2,6}$, the fixed
$W^{2,6}\to C^1$ embedding gives
\[
 \|N(\phi)-N(\psi)\|_{Y_g}
 \leq C(\|a\|_\infty+\|\sigma_p\|_\infty+\|f\|_{Y_g}
                    +\|\phi\|_{Z_g}+\|\psi\|_{Z_g})
                                    \|\phi-\psi\|_{Z_g}.
\]
The factor $\sigma_p$ is used only in its unweighted supremum;
a decaying $\phi$ supplies the weighted factor. The contraction
$\phi=Q_N(f+N(\phi))$ on the ball $2\|Q_N\|\|f\|_{Y_g}$
proves the claim at order zero. Normal trace follows directly
from the exact gauge formula.

For surface differentiation first rearrange the equation as
\[
 P\phi=J_\phi^{-1}\{M_\phi f+(M_\phi-I)\sigma_p
       -(\nabla M_\phi)\mathbin{\lrcorner}a
       +(\nabla J_\phi)\mathbin{\lrcorner}d_{A_0}\phi\}.
\]
Its principal operator is fixed and its right side contains
only first derivatives of $\phi$. After $k$ surface derivatives,
the highest new field and its first derivative are controlled
by the preceding surface level's two physical derivatives.
The commutator with $P$ has that same preceding-order control.
The remaining terms use the small surface derivatives of
$a,f$, and bounded derivatives of $\sigma_p$ with a decaying
$\phi$ factor. Fixed local Neumann estimates on nested collars
give the finite induction. Smoothness for each $\nu$ follows
from this semilinear equation and ordinary regularity.
The gauges tend to identity on weighted ends by embedding;
on unweighted ordinary ends their unit-region $W^{2,6}$
norms tend to zero and give the same conclusion.
The exact formula, the derivative bounds, and covariance
preserve critical convergence and the localized geometric
source bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:converse-residual-rate}
Let $V_\nu$ be the inverse nonlinear residual coordinate of
the gauged $y_\nu$ about $x_0$, with all finite end variables.
For every fixed $k\geq2$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-residual-rate}
              \|V_\nu\|_{X^{\rm res}_{1/T_\nu,k}}
                                      \leq C_kT_\nu^{-40}.
\end{equation}
The same ambient residual bound holds after subtracting the
exact critical profiles. The attaching class and the central
flat surface representative satisfy errors $O(T_\nu^{-79/2})$
relative to the fixed residual at $c_\nu$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proved $C^0$ surface and map convergence permits the inverse
nonlinear matching chart; no critical Sobolev embedding into
$C^0$ is assumed. Its coordinate $V$ has small critical norm,
and all its required $C^0$ surface coefficient norms tend to
zero. Let their finite sum, together with $|p|$, be $s_m(V)$.
The residual inverse in
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-fixed-residual-inverse} also holds at
$\varepsilon=0$: its initial first-order inverse, its integer
surface commutators, and the reflected Bessel multiplier proof
all include this endpoint on the same domain.

Write $A_{\Phi_0(V)}-A_0=\xi+C(V)$ for its connection component.
The nonlinear correction $C$ on the collar is surface-valued,
quadratic, and gains one surface derivative; its end value is
$B_p-B_0-p$. Define
\[
 N_{\rm geom}(V)=\mathcal F(\Phi_0(V))-D_{\rm geom}V,\qquad
 R_{\rm C}(V)=\sigma_p-d_{A_0}^*C(V).
\]
For sufficiently many fixed surface supremum orders,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-critical-remainder}
 \|N_{\rm geom}(V)\|_{Y^{\rm res}_{0,k}}
       +\|R_{\rm C}(V)\|_{Y^{\rm res}_{0,k}}
                 \leq C_ks_m(V)\|V\|_{X^{\rm res}_{0,k}}.
\end{equation}
Here each source is placed only in its geometric or Coulomb row.
To verify the estimate, distribute the surface weights
$\chi^j$ in the $\Gamma$ norms between the two factors
of each product. Put one factor and its fixed surface derivatives
in the small supremum norm and the other in weighted $L^2$.
Surface ellipticity of the principal part $\chi\Lambda_\Sigma$
and the smooth finite-rank end corrections give the required
weighted derivative bounds. There is no division by $\chi$.
Where the chart correction is supported, the unweighted
$H_B^1H_\Sigma^k$ and $L_B^2H_\Sigma^{k+1}$ norms are available.
One base derivative of its surface correction has form
$DS(b)d_Bb+(\partial_BS)(b)$, with the second term quadratic.
The Coulomb row takes only a surface derivative of this correction.
The positive remainder has a small coefficient multiplying
$d_Bv$, plus quadratic terms with fixed background derivatives.
For holonomy, the second Duhamel expansion, one supremum
insertion, and submersion coarea give
$\|\mathcal H(A_0+a)-\mathcal H(A_0)-D\mathcal H_{A_0}a\|_2
\leq C\|a\|_\infty\|a\|_2$.
The prescribed primary series sums these bounds, and its
dependencies miss the surface-localized collar.

To check decay, choose a smooth linear extension $V_p$ of
the finite end tangent and write $V=V_p+W$, with $W$ decaying.
The exact critical geometric equation implies
$N_{\rm geom}(V_p)=O(|p|^2)$ with exponential decay before
taking the weighted norm. Also
$\sigma_p-d_{A_0}^*C(V_p)$ has the same bound: its two
nondecaying limits cancel exactly. Subtracting these expressions
from those at $V_p+W$ leaves a decaying $W$ factor and a
small coefficient. For the chart derivative, split
$DS(b_p+b_W)d_Bb_W$ from
$[DS(b_p+b_W)-DS(b_p)]d_Bb_p$; the latter uses the smooth
$O(|p|)$ derivative of the finite profile. This proves
\eqref{eq:converse-critical-remainder} with exact ends retained.

The actual augmented equation is
\[
 DV_\nu=(\mathcal F(y_\nu^{g_\nu})-N_{\rm geom}(V_\nu),
                                                R_{\rm C}(V_\nu)).
\]
The fixed endpoint inverse and \eqref{eq:converse-removal-source}
give
$\|V_\nu\|_{X^{\rm res}_{0,k}}\leq
C_kT_\nu^{-40}+C'_ks_m(V_\nu)\|V_\nu\|_{X^{\rm res}_{0,k}}$.
Absorb the final coefficient. No factor $T_\nu$ multiplies
the qualitative smallness in this endpoint argument.

For fractional order a polynomial second-order bound suffices.
Before core removal, the coefficient and seam-jet bounds and
\eqref{eq:converse-base-metric-equation} give uniformly bounded
fixed $w$ derivatives on unit discs throughout a smaller retained
region. Integrated norms cost powers of $R\leq\rho^{-1}$.
The axial identity and its first-order gauge equation, differentiated
successively, retain $A_w^{\rm ax}=O(\rho^2R)$ and bounded
fixed mixed derivatives of the gauge and surface fields.
This is a finite induction using unitary transport and the
curvature integral, not exponential growth with the path length.
Physical differentiation costs fixed powers of $\rho^{-1}$.
The two cutoff radii satisfy $h\geq\rho$ and
$r\geq c\sqrt{\rho}$, so their differentiated contributions
are also polynomial. The exterior joining gauge has the same
property by its first-order equation.

One further derivative of the fixed gauge equation gives a
polynomial $H^3$ bound: its principal operator is fixed, its
coefficients have small $C^1$ norms, and the differentiated
forcing and connection use the just established bounds.
The surface-order version is obtained first, before the extra
base derivative. Differentiating the inverse surface chart twice
then gives
\[
 \|V_\nu\|_{E_\omega^2}
 +\|\varphi V_\nu\|_{H_B^2H_\Sigma^k;\omega,\mathrm{as}}
                                    \leq C_k\rho_\nu^{-J_k}.
\]
The suffix means exact profile subtraction with the finite
coordinates added. The chart is a surface chart; no
four-dimensional $H^2$ algebra estimate is used.
Interpolation in each complete residual component yields
\[
 (T^{-40})^{1-1/T}(\rho^{-J_k})^{1/T}
             =T^{-40}\exp(J_k+40\log T/T).
\]
It proves \eqref{eq:converse-residual-rate}, including the
$\Gamma$ terms, the next-surface-order weak term, and all
finite end variables. The argument works at any exponent
comparable to $1/T$.

Finally a normalized average on a half-disc of radius $h$
has $H^{-1}$ norm at most $C\sqrt{1+\log(1/h)}$.
Indeed its Fourier transform is $\widehat\eta(h\xi)$;
integrating $(1+|\xi|^2)^{-1}$ up to $|\xi|=1/h$
gives the logarithm, and the smooth Fourier tail is bounded.
Apply a fixed half-plane extension and outer cutoff.
