The erased positive map is exactly $p_\nu^0$ on that disc.
Averaging its critical $O(T_\nu^{-40})$ difference from the
residual gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-attaching-rate}
                 d(p_\nu^0,u_0(c_\nu))\leq CT_\nu^{-79/2}.
\end{equation}
The variation of $u_0$ across the disc is exponentially smaller.
For the surface representative after the fixed gauge, its
oscillation on the erased disc is $O(h_\nu)$ in every fixed
surface order, since the gauge has bounded first derivatives.
The same Hilbert-valued average therefore gives
$\|\beta_{y_\nu^{g_\nu}}(c_\nu)-\alpha_0(c_\nu)\|_{H_\Sigma^k}
\leq C_kT_\nu^{-79/2}$. This representative is flat.
The constant surface factor from the exterior join is retained
in this comparison.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Uniqueness in the full small chart}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:converse-full-chart-uniqueness}
Fix the parameters of a sufficiently small regular gluing chart.
Let $U_*$ be its normalized correction and let $U\in X_\rho$
solve the same geometric equation and the same four observation
equations. If $\|U\|_{X_\rho}\leq T^{-9}$, then
$\Phi_\rho(U)$ and $\Phi_\rho(U_*)$ represent the same ordinary
mixed moduli point.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write the augmented equation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-normalized-correction} as
\[
 \mathbb F(U)=\mathbb F(0)+\mathbb A U+\mathbb N(U),\qquad
 \mathbb A=(D,\ell),\qquad
 \mathcal B=\operatorname{im}Q\oplus Z\RR^4 .
\]
Its inverse on $\mathcal B$, denoted by $\mathbb Q$, satisfies
$\mathbb A\mathbb Q=I$ and $\|\mathbb Q\|\leq CT^3$.
The geometric inverse has norm $CT^2$,
$\|\ell\|\leq CT^{1/2}$, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-full-difference}
 \|\mathbb N(U)-\mathbb N(V)\|
 \leq CT^5(\|U\|+\|V\|)\|U-V\|,
       \qquad \|U\|,\|V\|\leq c_0T^{-5}.
\end{equation}
The equation is $C^1$, and its geometric part has quadratic
constant $CT^4$. The constructed correction belongs to
$\mathcal B$ and has norm at most $CT^{-37}$.

Set $V=U-U_*$, $\delta=\|U\|+\|U_*\|$, and decompose
$V=w+b$ by $b=\mathbb Q\mathbb A V$.
The endpoint equations give
\[
 b=-\mathbb Q(\mathbb N(U)-\mathbb N(U_*)),\quad
 \|b\|\leq CT^8\delta^2,\quad
 w\in\ker\mathbb A,\quad
 \|w\|\leq\delta+CT^8\delta^2 .
\]
For $0\leq t\leq1$, solve in $\mathcal B$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-exact-path}
 v(t)=-\mathbb Q\{\mathbb N(U_*+tw+v(t))-\mathbb N(U_*)\}.
\end{equation}
The ball of radius $C_1T^8\delta^2$ is invariant for $C_1$
large, and the contraction constant is at most $CT^8\delta=o(1)$.
The solution depends continuously on $t$, has $v(0)=0$,
$v(1)=b$, and gives an exact path
$U(t)=U_*+tw+v(t)$ with the four observations fixed and
$\sup_t\|U(t)\|\leq4\delta$.
Only the difference estimate at the original origin is used here.

We check ordinary regularity at every point $U_0$ of this path.
For $L=D+dN_{\rm geo}(U_0)$ the formula
\[
 \widetilde Q_{U_0}=Q(I+dN_{\rm geo}(U_0)Q)^{-1},
          \qquad \|\widetilde Q_{U_0}\|\leq CT^2
\]
is valid because $\|dN_{\rm geo}(U_0)Q\|\leq CT^6\delta=o(1)$.
The fixed-scale regularity argument in
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-standard-regularity} gives a smooth
finite-energy representative with its exact critical limits.
The small seam chart retains an irreducible flat fiber.
For an ordinary augmented cokernel pair $(s,\eta)$, testing
compact covariant-Neumann gauge variations gives
$d_A^*d_As=0$, hence $s=0$ by irreducibility and the end
conditions. Pair $\eta$ with $\widetilde Q_{U_0}$ applied to
compact smooth geometric sources. The end cutoffs can be chosen
constant on each far primary dependency family and equal to one
on all secondary dependencies. Their remaining commutators tend
to zero against the positively weighted adjoint solution; a
graph variation has only the allowed finite critical tangent
and a weighted remainder. Density gives $\eta=0$.
The index computation of the local gluing theorem still applies:
the bundle, positive relative class, and end components have
not changed. Thus the ordinary moduli space is smooth of
dimension four at every $U_0$.

Let $W_j$ be the four fixed-attaching pregluing derivatives
used for \eqref{eq:gluing-parameter-columns}. They satisfy
$\|W_j\|\leq C$, $\|DW_j\|\leq CT^{-40}$, and
$(\ell W_j)_j=M_0(E)+o(1)$ with uniformly invertible $M_0$.
Set $K_j=(I-\widetilde Q_{U_0}L)W_j$.
Then $LK_j=0$ and
$\|K_j-W_j\|\leq C(T^{-38}+T^6\delta)$.
For $H(U)=\mathcal O_{c,\rho}(\Phi_\rho(U))-\mathcal O_0(E)$,
the observation part of \eqref{eq:converse-full-difference}
gives
\[
 (dH(U_0)K_j)_j=M_0(E)+o(1)+O(T^{13/2}\delta).
\]
This matrix is invertible. The fixed-scale reduction in
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-linear-gauge-reduction} transfers these
vectors to the ordinary augmented kernel. It retains the
critical tangent and solves the decaying covariant Neumann
equation after removing its limiting infinitesimal gauge.
Irreducibility and the end spectral gaps justify that solve
at this positive scale. Gauge invariance retains the displayed
matrix. The observations are therefore local coordinates on
the ordinary moduli space at every point of the path.

Finally, graph continuity of this exact path implies continuity
of its ordinary moduli classes by the argument in
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-parameter-continuity}. Indeed,
at fixed $\rho$ it gives local physical $W^{1,p}$ convergence
for some $p>2$, positive $C^0$ convergence, convergence of
the finite end coordinates, and weighted $H^1$ convergence
after exact profile subtraction. These imply uniform
integrability of energy on compact sets and uniformly small
end tails. Compactness has no concentration, the comparison
gauge equation identifies every smooth limit with the graph
limit, and the end gaps give the ordinary weighted convergence.
This argument only uses exactness and graph convergence.
The continuous path has constant observations, hence is
locally constant in the ordinary moduli space. Its endpoint
classes agree.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exact parameters and compatible inner and outer gauges}

We now complete the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse}.
Write $\Delta_\nu=T_\nu^{-79/2}$. Evaluation and the two
affine normal functions have jointly surjective differential,
by \eqref{eq:gluing-affine-normal-matrix}.
Choose local primitive coordinates $(q,t_1,t_2)$ on the
normalized slice, with $q$ the attaching class.
Let $\overline E_\nu=E(c_\nu,t_\nu)$ have the fixed residual
attaching value. Equation~\eqref{eq:converse-attaching-rate}
gives $d(E_\nu^0,\overline E_\nu)\leq C\Delta_\nu$.
This adjustment is allowed to leave the rank-one locus.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:converse-exact-parameters}
The parameters can be changed by
\eqref{eq:converse-parameter-bound} so that the original solution
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-exact-observations}
 \mathcal O_{\widehat c_\nu,\widehat\rho_\nu}(x_\nu)
            =\mathcal O_0(E(\widehat c_\nu,\widehat t_\nu)).
\end{equation}
The solution of these four parameter equations is unique in
a fixed sufficiently small box in scaled center, log scale,
and primitive coordinates.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Set
\[
 J_\nu(c,\rho,t)=\mathcal O_{c,\rho}(x_\nu)-\mathcal O_0(E(c,t)),
 \qquad (c,\rho,t)=(c_\nu+\rho_\nu b,\rho_\nu e^h,t_\nu+\tau).
\]
The inner comparison on the fixed observation-loop region
and the attaching estimate give $|J_\nu(0)|\leq C\Delta_\nu$.
The affine averaging formula
\eqref{eq:gluing-explicit-observation-derivatives}
differentiates only the fixed smooth averaging densities.
Their supports stay in one compact loop region on a small
fixed box in $(b,h)$. The inner comparison therefore controls
these differentiated observations without differentiating an
incoming gauge with respect to the trial parameters.
At the box center the derivative converges to the observation
matrix on
\[
 T_E,\quad D_E,\quad-\partial_{t_1}E,\quad-\partial_{t_2}E .
\]
The $c$ derivative of $E(c,t)$ contributes $O(\rho_\nu)$.
Affine equivariance of the limiting Dirichlet metric, as in
the proof of \eqref{eq:gluing-parameter-columns}, identifies
the first two columns. These four vectors form a basis of
the fixed-attaching tangent space, so the matrix $M_\nu$
is uniformly invertible.

Repeated changes of variables again differentiate only the
densities. On the box,
$\|dJ_\nu-M_\nu\|\leq C\,(\text{box radius})+o(1)$.
The map $z\mapsto z-M_\nu^{-1}J_\nu(z)$ is a contraction
on a ball of radius $C\Delta_\nu$. It supplies the stated
parameters. Integrating $dJ_\nu-M_\nu$ on segments proves
injectivity on the smaller fixed convex box as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:converse-chart-entry}
In one allowed gauge the original solution lies in the full
configuration chart at the parameters of
Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-exact-parameters}, with
$\|U_\nu\|_{X_{\widehat\rho_\nu}}\leq CT_\nu^{-38}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We suppress $\nu$ and repeat the interpolation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-residual-rate} at
$\varepsilon=1/\widehat T$. Since $\widehat T/T\to1$,
the factors $\rho^{-J/\widehat T}$ remain bounded.
The core-erased solution $y$ in the fixed residual gauge
still has complete residual norm $CT^{-40}$.
Use the residual frame based at $\widehat c$ and let
$\ell=\widehat\rho\,\widehat T^{81}$.
It contains the entire support of the inner gluing cutoff.
Write $y=y_{\rm ax}^G$, where both representatives have
zero normal base trace. The axial estimate through the
erased annulus gives
$A_{y_{\rm ax},B}=O(\rho T^J)$ on this disc.
In the fixed based frame $A_{0,B}=O(\ell)$.
The Hilbert-valued base embedding, of norm $C\sqrt T$
at this exponent, gives
$\|A_{y,B}-A_{0,B}\|_{C^0_BC_\Sigma^m}\leq C_m\Delta$.
The gauge equation consequently implies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-joining-gauge}
 d_BG=G A_{y_{\rm ax},B}-A_{y,B}G,\qquad
 \|d_BG\|_{C^0_BC_\Sigma^m}\leq C_m d_T,\qquad
 d_T=\Delta+\rho T^{J_m}.
\end{equation}
The global joining construction has uniformly bounded
surface derivatives of $G$ and bounded first base derivatives.

The localized $H_B^{1+\varepsilon}H_\Sigma^m$ norm of
$d_BG$ is also bounded by $C_md_T$.
To verify this assertion, localization of the residual
estimate costs $C\sqrt T\,\ell^{-\varepsilon}$.
In differentiating a product $fG$, the term $(df)G$
uses the $H^\varepsilon$ multiplier bound for uniformly
$C^1$ functions. Substitute \eqref{eq:converse-joining-gauge}
in the other term $f\,dG$; the resulting products of base
coefficients use their supremum and $H^\varepsilon$ bounds.
The axial term is exponentially small with all required
fixed derivatives. Background terms localized to this disc
are $O(\ell^{1-\varepsilon})$. Additional surface derivatives
use the same equation at the next fixed surface orders.
All factors $\ell^{-\varepsilon}$ are bounded.

Let $G_c=G(\widehat c)$ and, on this disc, define
\[
 q(z)=\log(G(z)G_c^{-1}),\qquad H(z)=\exp(-\chi(z)q(z)),
\]
where $\chi$ is radial, one on the half-radius disc and
zero near its outer circle. Then
$\|q\|_{C_\Sigma^m}\leq C\ell d_T$,
$\|dq\|_{C_\Sigma^m}\leq Cd_T$, and
$\|D_B^2q\|_{H^\varepsilon_BH_\Sigma^m}\leq Cd_T$.
The normal derivative vanishes on the seam. Extending $H$
by the identity gives an allowed gauge with $HG=G_c$
on the inner disc. The order of these factors follows
from $(A^G)^H=A^{HG}$.

The cost of this change in the complete residual norm is
at most $CT^{-39}$. For the largest cutoff term,
$D_B^2\chi\,q$ has size $C\ell^{-1}d_T$ on area
$O(\ell^2)$, hence $L^2$ norm $Cd_T$ and fractional
factor $\ell^{-\varepsilon}$. Terms with one differentiated
cutoff use $dq$; the remaining terms use the preceding
fractional estimate. Allowing one additional $\sqrt T$
factor gives $C\sqrt T\,\Delta=CT^{-39}$.
Surface terms have the additional factor $\ell$.
The same product estimates apply to conjugating $y-x_0$.
Thus, after this change,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-compatible-residual}
 \|y'-x_0\|_{\mathrm{res},1+\varepsilon;\mathrm{amb}}
       \leq CT^{-39},\qquad
 x'=y'\ \text{outside the old removal disc}.
\end{equation}
On the inner disc $x'$ is the original axial solution
in the constant surface frame $G_c$. All ends are unchanged.

Since $\widehat c$ lies inside the old erased core,
its flat surface representative is
$\gamma=G_c\alpha_\nu G_c^{-1}-d_\Sigma G_cG_c^{-1}$.
Before the last gauge change, the $CT^{-40}$ residual
estimate gives
$\|\gamma-\alpha_0(\widehat c)\|_{C_\Sigma^m}\leq C_m\Delta$.
The fixed irreducible surface slice expresses $\gamma$
in the chosen flat family by a surface gauge $k$ with
$\|k-I\|_{C_\Sigma^m}\leq C_m\Delta$.
The comparison primitive in the new coordinate is the pullback
of $E_\nu^0$ by
$w=b+a\widehat w$, with $|b|+|a-1|\leq C\Delta$.
It differs from $\widehat E=E(\widehat c,\widehat t)$
by $C\Delta$ in the full finite-dimensional family.

Use the compact smooth family of limiting base-flat
representatives in base-parallel frames fixed at infinity.
The remaining identification of frames is precisely the
base-constant surface gauge $k$. Hence the limiting surface
connections and positive maps differ in every fixed compact
norm by $C_m\Delta$, while both base connection coefficients
vanish. The exact small-metric caps in axial gauge have
base coefficient $O(\widehat\rho^2\widehat T^J)$ in
rescaled coordinates, or $O(\widehat\rho\widehat T^J)$
physically. Their surface and map errors retain
$C_m\Delta+O(\widehat\rho^2\widehat T^J)$.
It follows that the inner ambient norm satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:converse-compatible-cap}
 \|\theta_1(x'-x_{\mathrm{cap},\widehat E})\|_{\mathrm{cap,amb}}
            \leq C\sqrt T\,\Delta+\rho^{1/20}T^J .
\end{equation}
For the $C\Delta$ term this is the fractional cutoff
estimate on a smooth compact surface/map family difference;
its cost is $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}R_1^\varepsilon$.
The physical base conversion includes $\rho^{-1}$,
but its numerator is $O(\rho^2T^J)$.
Thus it is included in the last term of
\eqref{eq:converse-compatible-cap}. No such conversion
is applied to the $C\Delta$ surface difference.

The pregluing cap need not originally use axial gauge.
Apply its inverse axial gauge to both $x'$ and $y'$.
Its logarithm and required rescaled derivatives are
$O(\rho^2T^J)$. A radial extension to a slightly larger
disc, followed by a cutoff, preserves the zero normal
derivative on the straight seam rays. The resulting
physical base change has $H^1$ norm $\rho T^J$
and $H^2$ norm $T^J$; interpolation gives
$\rho^{1-\varepsilon}T^J$ in $H^{1+\varepsilon}$.
All needed surface orders satisfy the same estimates.
Consequently \eqref{eq:converse-compatible-residual} and
\eqref{eq:converse-compatible-cap} persist in the actual
pregluing representative, and $x'=y'$ off the removal disc.

The primitive tail in \eqref{eq:converse-primitive-tail}
and the smooth residual attaching expansion give
\[
 \|\theta_1(x_{\mathrm{cap},\widehat E}-x_{\rm pre})\|
                 _{\mathrm{cap,amb}}
                     \leq CT^{-40}+\rho^{1/20}T^J .
\]
Indeed the strong tail bound is $CR_2^{-1+\varepsilon}$;
the residual variation and based base coefficient are
$\rho T^J$. The nonlinear matching interpolation is
quadratic in $R_2^{-1}+\rho R_2+\rho^2T^J$,
with fractional factor $R_2^\varepsilon$.
Weak rows retain their additional physical factors.
On the support of $1-\theta_1$ the old removed core
is absent. There $x'=y'$ and $x_{\rm pre}=x_0$.
The residual cutoff costs $C\sqrt T$ in
\eqref{eq:converse-compatible-residual}, giving
$CT^{-77/2}$. These estimates yield
\[
                  \|x'-x_{\rm pre}\|_{\widehat X_{\widehat\rho}}
                                      \leq CT^{-38}.
\]
Both configurations satisfy exact matching and zero normal
trace, with the same ordinary outer conditions and individually
subtracted exact end profiles. The inverse nonlinear chart of
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-nonlinear-chart} applies:
its quadratic constant is $C\widehat T^4$, and its pointwise
chart condition follows from the $C\sqrt T$ embedding.
It gives $U=\Phi_{\widehat\rho}^{-1}(x')$ with norm
at most $CT^{-38}$. The inverse retains actual linear
matching, normal trace, outer conditions, and finite end
tangents. Thus $U$ belongs to the full domain $X_{\widehat\rho}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Completion of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse}]
Propositions~\ref{prop:converse-one-modification} and
\ref{prop:converse-stable-family} supply the comparison primitive
models in the two sectors. The metric, connection, core-removal,
and fixed-gauge estimates prove
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-residual-rate}.
Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-exact-parameters} solves the observations,
and Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-chart-entry} puts the
original solution in the full chart at those parameters.
The observations are gauge invariant, so they still hold
there. Its norm $CT^{-38}$ is smaller than $T^{-9}$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-full-chart-uniqueness} identifies
its ordinary moduli class with the normalized constructed
solution, proving the theorem.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Energy and diameter in the converse sectors}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:converse-whole-neck}
Retain the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse}.
Suppose in addition that the normalized analytical energy measures
near the attaching point satisfy
\[
 \mu_\nu\rightharpoonup\mu_0+\tfrac12\delta_{c_*},
                         \qquad \mu_0(\{c_*\})=0 .
\]
Pull back $\mu_\nu|_{D(c_\nu,r_\nu)}$ by
$w\mapsto c_\nu+\rho_\nu w$ and extend it by zero;
denote the resulting measure by $\nu_\nu$.
If $E_\nu^0\to E_*$, then
$\|\nu_\nu-\mu_{E_*}\|_{\rm TV}\to0$, where $\mu_{E_*}$
is the smooth primitive measure of mass $\frac12$.
In particular, for all retained $L$,
\[
 \mu_\nu(D(c_\nu,r_\nu)\setminus D(c_\nu,L\rho_\nu))
                       \leq CL^{-2}+o(1).
\]
In the unitary reference frames used above, the surface
connection and positive map on the whole retained annulus
have distance at most $C_kL^{-1}+o(1)$ from the attaching
pair, in every fixed surface order.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The local connection estimates include the seam and any
interior unstable fiber. In rescaled coordinates the three
negative curvature contributions to physical energy have
weights $\rho^{-2}$, $1$, and $\rho^2$ on $F_{BB}$,
$F_{B\Sigma}$, and $F_{\Sigma\Sigma}$ respectively.
The scalar ASD equation gives
$F_{BB}=O(\rho^2F_{\Sigma\Sigma})$, with bounded surface
curvature on compact sets. Thus both pure contributions
vanish locally, and the mixed contribution converges.
Positive energy is conformally invariant. This proves local
total variation convergence to the primitive measure.
The flat-family Chern--Weil identity used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit} gives its mass
$-\frac14p_1(\operatorname{ad}E_*)=\frac12$.
The derivative of the tail
\eqref{eq:converse-primitive-tail} gives
$\mu_{E_*}(\CC\setminus D_L)\leq CL^{-2}$.

The additional weak energy hypothesis bounds the limiting
mass in the shrinking physical disc above by $\frac12$:
first enclose it in a fixed continuity disc around $c_*$
and then shrink that disc. Local convergence bounds the
liminf below by $\mu_{E_*}(D_L)$ for every fixed $L$.
Consequently the total masses tend to $\frac12$.
Splitting into $D_L$ and its complement now proves global
total variation convergence and the simultaneous tail bound.
For the diameter assertion, the actual surface connection
in its reference unitary frame is a smooth expression in
$c$, $h-I$, and one surface derivative of $h$.
On the entire exterior half-annulus their estimates above
give $C_k(L^{-1}+\eta_\nu)+o(1)$.
The positive reference map has the same $1/w$ tail and
the actual map differs from it by $o(1)$ there.
This proves the assertion without selecting intermediate
circles. The analytical defect was imposed separately
from the algebraic charge identity.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Injectivity and transitions between regular charts}

The next result concerns the constructed charts themselves.
All charts use the same fixed residual, determinant and
clutching conventions, and the same Hermitian metric in
\eqref{eq:gluing-affine-slice-functions}.
They may use different local primitive coordinates and
different sets of admissible observations. Primitive
isomorphisms below retain the inherited attaching data.
Each chart is restricted to a relatively compact subset
of a slightly larger regular seam-admissible chart.
Denote these local gluing maps by $\Glue_i$.

\begin{lemma}[comparison of two rescalings]
\label{lem:collar-two-rescalings}
Suppose two constructed sequences represent the same
ordinary moduli point at each $\nu$:
\[
 \Glue_i(c_\nu,\rho_\nu,t_\nu)
             =\Glue_j(c'_\nu,\rho'_\nu,t'_\nu),
              \qquad \rho_\nu,\rho'_\nu\longrightarrow0 .
\]
Assume their remaining parameters stay in the compact
subsets just specified. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:collar-relative-scales}
 \frac{c'_\nu-c_\nu}{\rho_\nu}\longrightarrow0,\qquad
 \frac{\rho'_\nu}{\rho_\nu}\longrightarrow1 .
\end{equation}
Their normalized primitive parameters approach the same
bundle: any convergent subsequences have isomorphic limits
with the prescribed attaching data.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Pass to a subsequence on which the centers and primitive
parameters converge. The energy defect in
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit} first identifies
the two limiting physical centers. Choose a physical
disc about that common center, with radius tending to
zero sufficiently slowly to contain both centers and
every fixed multiple of both core radii. Restrict the
common analytical energy measure to this disc.
In either rescaling it converges in total variation
to the corresponding primitive measure of mass $\frac12$.
Indeed local convergence gives the lower mass bound,
whereas the weak physical defect gives the upper bound,
exactly as in Corollary~\ref{cor:converse-whole-neck}.

Write $a_\nu=\rho'_\nu/\rho_\nu$ and
$b_\nu=(c'_\nu-c_\nu)/\rho_\nu$.
If $a_\nu\to0$, a fixed disc in the second coordinate
is a disc of radius $a_\nu L$ in the first.
Choose $L$ so that it contains, for instance, more than
$\frac38$ of the second limiting mass.
Total variation convergence to the first smooth measure
implies that the masses of all discs of radius $a_\nu L$
tend uniformly to zero, regardless of their centers.
To see this uniformity, first discard a small tail of
the limiting integrable density and then use absolute
continuity of its integral on a compact set.
This is a contradiction. Interchanging the two
descriptions rules out $a_\nu\to\infty$.
With $a_\nu$ bounded above and below, $|b_\nu|\to\infty$
would send that same positive mass outside every fixed
first-coordinate disc, contrary to tightness.
After a further subsequence, $a_\nu\to a>0$ and
$b_\nu\to b\in\RR$.

The two limiting pairs are isomorphic after pullback
by $g(w)=aw+b$. Here it is necessary to identify the
connections, rather than just their energy densities.
The rescaled smooth convergence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit} holds on every
fixed negative product chart, including seam segments.
The gauge relating the two exact representatives is
unitary. In their convergent local frames it satisfies
$dh_\nu=h_\nu A'_\nu-A_\nu h_\nu$.
Its values lie in a compact group, and this equation
bounds every fixed derivative on compact sets.
A diagonal subsequence gives a smooth unitary
isomorphism of the limiting negative connections on
the whole half-plane. The local transition identities
pass to the limit. In the limiting base-parallel frames
the base components both vanish, so the base part of
this gauge equation says that the isomorphism is
independent of $w$. In particular it extends smoothly
to the negative compactifying point. The positive maps agree after the
same affine change because they came from the same
solution.

The limiting negative isomorphism is holomorphic for
the product Dolbeault operators and includes an unstable
surface fiber. On the stable seam and positive side,
the universal surface family identifies the two
holomorphic projective bundles. Scalar uniqueness
makes that identification agree with the negative one
on their overlap. Near infinity both families are
stable and extend in the fixed attaching slice.
The same universal-family identification extends there,
with no modification. These are the surface-slice and
extension arguments used in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit}.
Thus $E'_*\simeq g^*E_*$ in the primitive family,
with the common determinant and attaching conventions.
The local primitive charts retain the same lift of
this projective identification.

Both bundles satisfy $\operatorname{Re}\kappa=0$ and
$s=1$. The affine transformation law immediately gives
$a=1$, $b=0$. Every convergent subsequence of the
bounded relative parameters has these limits, proving
\eqref{eq:collar-relative-scales} and the asserted
primitive identification.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[injectivity and boundary limits of regular charts]
\label{thm:regular-chart-compatibility}
After reducing its upper scale bound, each regular chart
$\Glue_i$ above is injective on its entire parameter
product and is a smooth open embedding at positive scale.
Adjoin its normalized primitive configurations at scale
zero. The extended map is a homeomorphism onto its own
image in the unmarked broken-convergence topology of
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit}.

On an existing overlap of two positive-scale images
the coordinate change is smooth. Along its closure
at regular boundary parameters in the specified compact
patches, it extends continuously by the natural
identification of normalized primitive bundles, and
satisfies \eqref{eq:collar-relative-scales}.
These assertions neither give coverage of a full
neighborhood of the boundary stratum nor assert
differentiability at scale zero.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose that a single chart is noninjective for every
upper scale bound. There are distinct parameter pairs
with the same image and both scales tending to zero.
By Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-two-rescalings}, their relative
center and log scale tend to zero and their primitive
coordinates have the same limit. They therefore lie
in one of the fixed scaled boxes used in
Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-exact-parameters}.
For the common constructed solution, the observation
comparison \eqref{eq:gluing-rescaled-observation-error}
and differentiation of the averaging densities give
the same uniform derivative estimate for $J$ as in
that lemma. Both parameter pairs solve $J=0$, by the
definition of the normalized construction. Injectivity
of $J$ on the fixed convex box contradicts their
distinctness. Hence some positive upper bound gives
injectivity throughout the product. The local
diffeomorphism of Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-local-gluing}
then makes its positive-scale restriction an open
embedding.

Continuity at zero is
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit}.
For inverse continuity, consider a sequence in this
image converging to one of its broken configurations,
and keep the boundary parameters in a smaller compact
patch. Its scales must tend to zero: a subsequence
with a positive limiting scale would have a smooth
ordinary limit and no half-unit atom.
Any subsequential limit of the remaining parameters
has the prescribed physical center by the energy defect.
The rescaling comparison in the preceding lemma also
applies to its given broken convergence: the two
rescalings have the same full primitive mass, local
smooth limits, and the same compatible attaching
identifications. It therefore gives the same
normalized primitive bundle. Compactness of the
parameter closure now proves convergence of all
parameters. At a positive-scale point the inverse
is already smooth; a sequence with scales tending
to zero cannot converge there because of its atom.
This proves inverse continuity on the image, with
the usual sequential broken topology on these
fixed compact patches.

The transition on a positive overlap is the composition
of one open embedding with the inverse of the other,
so it is smooth. Consider any sequence in this overlap
whose first parameters tend to a regular boundary
point and whose second parameters remain in the
specified compact patch. A positive limiting second
scale would again contradict the energy defect.
Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-two-rescalings} identifies every
subsequential limit of its second parameters, proving
the continuous boundary extension and the relative
scale limits. Coordinate cocycles hold on positive
overlaps by equality of the moduli points and on their
regular boundary closures by continuity.
This argument applies only where such overlaps
accumulate; it does not prove that two image sets
cover a common neighborhood of every shared boundary
point.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Deriving the outer and end hypotheses}
\label{sec:physical-sector-coverage}

We now derive the technical exterior hypotheses of the converse from
physical convergence. Retain the fixed smooth irreducible regular
index-zero residual $x_0$, determinant lifts, perturbations, and
integrable product collar of this section. Suppose $x_\nu$ are smooth
mixed solutions converging to $x_0$, modulo the allowed gauges,
smoothly on every compact set off one seam point $c_*$. The convergence
includes all fixed physical and surface derivative orders. Assume
that on the unperturbed matching neighborhood their normalized
analytical energy measures satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:physical-primitive-defect}
 \mu_\nu\rightharpoonup\mu_0+\tfrac12\delta_{c_*},
                         \qquad \mu_0(\{c_*\})=0,
\end{equation}
where $\mu_0$ is the smooth residual measure there. No shrinking
radius, outer framing, or convergence norm on an infinite end is
prescribed in these assumptions.

\begin{proposition}[outer data from physical convergence]
\label{prop:automatic-outer-data}
Under these assumptions, the finite doubles can be taken on one
fixed neighborhood of $c_*$. On sufficiently slowly shrinking
outer circles they have coherent relative charge one, stable
outer fibers, caps of vanishing area in a fixed fine-moduli
chart, and the actual unitary outer representatives required
by Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse}.
After a subsequence their finite-family splitting is
$(d_\nu,\ell_\nu)=(0,1)$ or $(1,0)$. The first alternative
has one degree-zero Langton quotient in the open negative
half-disc; it is the only alternative in genus one.

In the first alternative, or in the second when $g=2$ and
the limiting image line is regular, extract the current
primitive by the holomorphic constructions in
Propositions~\ref{prop:converse-one-modification} and
\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}. Its physical center and
scale satisfy $c_\nu\to c_*$ and $\rho_\nu\to0$, with
$\rho_\nu>0$. The outer circles can then be enlarged so
that all the retained-domain and exterior-annulus assumptions
of Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse} hold, including
\eqref{eq:converse-radii}, without changing that comparison primitive.
Regularity and seam-admissibility of its normalized limit
remain separate algebraic restrictions.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Translate the seam coordinate so $c_*=0$. The residual double
has stable fibers near zero, by openness of stability at its
seam fiber. Choose a fixed disc $D$ on which all these fibers
are stable and its classifying map $f_0$ lies in a contractible
fine-moduli coordinate chart $U$ about $p=f_0(0)$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:doubling} applies to each actual smooth solution
on this same disc. It requires no uniform width of the stable
seam collar as $\nu$ varies. On every compact punctured
annulus, physical convergence and the stable complex slice
used above make the actual surface fibers stable for large
$\nu$. They give convergence of the classifying maps to $f_0$
in every fixed physical derivative order.

We first identify the relative charge of the double.
On the negative half its underlying bundle is the bundle of
the actual connection. On the positive half it is the
pullback of the universal stable bundle. Equip the latter
with the smooth family of Narasimhan--Seshadri metrics.
At the seam, the projective holomorphic isomorphism is
unique and is unitary: both metrics are projectively flat,
and uniqueness of the determinant-normalized stable metric
identifies them. Scalar automorphisms disappear on the
adjoint bundle. The projective seam transition of the
double is therefore the actual unitary matching transition.

Inserting the flat cylinder in the connection-valued
filling implements this same transition; collapsing its
collar recovers the underlying projective bundle of the
double. This is the annular bundle construction in
\cite[Lemma~4.67, equations~(4.68)--(4.69)]{DaeFukLip21b}.
For each finite $\nu$, smoothness of the solution at the
seam origin and a stable-family trivialization extend this
comparison across the center. We use the branch fixed by
this actual filling, without applying the small-energy
branch choice in the removable-singularity argument to
the concentrating sequence. The normalization of the
positive contribution is
\cite[Lemma~5.10]{DaeFukLip21a}.

The outer identification must also be retained. In the
physical convergence gauge on a fixed stable annulus,
the complex slice normalizations for the actual and
residual surface operators differ by gauges tending
to the identity. Their polar decompositions give a
homotopy between the universal-family outer identification
and the physical convergence identification. This is a
statement about their ratio in the common convergence
frame. Neither identification is assumed to extend over
the original disc. In particular any large component of
the convergence gauge occurs in both relative bundles.
Since the residual doubled disc lies in $U$, it is
homotopic there to the constant cap at $p$. Thus the
capped adjoint bundle of the actual double has precisely
the Pontryagin number of $P_{\nu,r}$ in
Theorem~\ref{thm:local-charge}. At a sufficiently small
fixed regular radius, that theorem and
\eqref{eq:physical-primitive-defect} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:automatic-framed-charge}
                   n(E_\nu)=-\tfrac12p_1(P_{\nu,r})=1
\end{equation}
for all sufficiently large $\nu$.

Choose a smaller fixed outer radius $r_0$ within $D$.
A diagonal choice $s_\nu\downarrow0$ gives convergence
gauges on $s_\nu\leq |z|\leq r_0$ with every prescribed
finite derivative error at most $s_\nu^2$. Explicitly,
choose radii $s_j\downarrow0$ and thresholds $N_j$
so convergence through order $j$ on the corresponding
fixed annulus has this bound for $\nu\geq N_j$,
then set $s_\nu=s_j$ between consecutive thresholds.
All actual fibers on this annulus are stable and their
classifying maps lie in $U$.

For an outer circle of radius comparable to $r\geq Cs_\nu$
and contained in $D_{Cr}$, its classifying loop is
$O(r)$ from $p$ and has length $O(r)$. The first assertion
uses $f_0(z)-p=O(|z|)$; the second uses the uniformly
bounded physical first derivative. Contract the loop to
$p$ in $U$. Its radial and angular derivatives are both
$O(r)$ in local coordinates, so its signed normalized
area satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:automatic-small-cap}
             |\operatorname{Area}(\operatorname{cap}_{\nu,r})|
                                                       \leq Cr^2.
\end{equation}
The derivative estimate is needed here; small image alone
does not bound the area of a rapidly winding loop.

Between two such circles surrounding $D_{s_\nu}$, retain
the actual annular restriction and the same contraction
in $U$. The annulus with its two caps is a sphere in a
contractible chart, whose universal adjoint pullback has
zero Pontryagin number. Consequently
\eqref{eq:automatic-framed-charge} holds on every retained
circle. The same argument allows displaced circles
surrounding $D_{s_\nu}$. There is no new Chern--Simons
branch when a circle is moved.

Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:disc-reduction} and use
Proposition~\ref{prop:framed-splitting}. On a shrinking
retained disc its stable replacement has
\[
 1=d_\nu+\ell_\nu,\qquad
 \frac{d_\nu}{2}
   =\operatorname{Area}(f_\nu)
             +\operatorname{Area}(\operatorname{cap}_{\nu,r}).
\]
The interior area is nonnegative, the cap area tends to
zero, and $d_\nu$ is an integer. Thus $d_\nu\geq0$
for large $\nu$, giving the two alternatives. If
$\ell_\nu=1$, positivity of every modification charge
gives exactly one step with degree-zero quotient.
Its point is negative, since every positive and seam
fiber is stable, and it is inside $D_{s_\nu}$.
If $\ell_\nu=0$, there is no unstable fiber on the
retained disc. For $g=1$, the classifying map is constant,
so $d_\nu=0$.

The holomorphic part of the current model construction
can now be performed before imposing a lower bound on
the retained radius. In the first alternative the
stable replacement has area tending to zero and small
outer image. The monotonicity argument in
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-one-modification} makes
its whole image tend to $p$ and gives compactness of
the modification subline. If $a_\nu$ is the modification
point, set $c_\nu=\operatorname{Re}a_\nu$ and
$\rho_\nu=-\operatorname{Im}a_\nu>0$.
Both tend to zero.

In genus two with $d_\nu=1$, choose the projective
hyperplane avoiding $p$ used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}.
Its unique intersection with the classifying disc
is a simple pole $a_\nu$ inside $D_{s_\nu}$.
The residue calculation and exact quadratic identities
there extract its limiting image line. If that line
is regular, the unique inverse extension neighborhood
gives $L_\nu(z)$ and
\[
                  q_\nu(z)=\frac{b_\nu}{z-a_\nu}+k_\nu(z).
\]
Freezing the two slow parameters at $a_\nu$ and applying
\eqref{eq:gluing-affine-slice-functions} gives the current
center and scale, with $c_\nu\to0$ and $\rho_\nu\to0$,
$\rho_\nu>0$. These holomorphic steps use only the
charge and small outer data already obtained.

Choose a sufficiently large fixed constant $C_0$ and put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:automatic-radius-choice}
 r_\nu=C_0\max\{s_\nu,\sqrt{\rho_\nu},|c_\nu|\},
                         \qquad R_\nu=r_\nu/\rho_\nu.
\end{equation}
Then $r_\nu\to0$ and $R_\nu\geq C_0\rho_\nu^{-1/2}$.
All fixed radius margins lie inside the original doubling
domain for large $\nu$. Taking $C_0$ large enough puts
the physical annulus
$r_\nu/32\leq|z-c_\nu|\leq r_\nu/8$ outside
$D_{s_\nu}$. Its physical $C^1_BC^k_\Sigma$ error
is at most $s_\nu^2\leq r_\nu$ for every fixed $k$
and large $\nu$. The smooth uniformly bounded gauge
family basing the residual at $c_\nu$ preserves this
bound after adjusting the diagonal accuracy by a fixed
factor. The actual outer surface representatives and
positive maps tend to the attaching pair, and
\eqref{eq:automatic-small-cap} applies.

No new modification appears on enlargement, since the
whole added annulus has stable fibers. In the stable
genus-two case, its added image is near $p$, while the
inner image is near the limiting line. Both lie in the
same inverse extension neighborhood. Uniqueness of that
inverse makes $L_\nu,q_\nu$, their pole and residue,
and the frozen data agree on the overlap. In the
modification case the stable replacement and its
modification data agree by restriction. The current
primitive and its normalization are therefore unchanged.
This proves all the claimed outer and radius hypotheses.
\end{proof}

For complete end convergence one also needs the global energy
budget. In addition to \eqref{eq:physical-primitive-defect}, assume
the prescribed ordinary ends agree with those of $x_0$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:physical-global-defect}
                   \cE(x_\nu)-\cE(x_0)\longrightarrow\tfrac12.
\end{equation}
Proposition~\ref{prop:index-exhaustion} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:primitive-atom} supply this situation
for the index-four primitive degeneration under consideration.

\begin{proposition}[end convergence from exhaustion of the defect]
\label{prop:automatic-end-convergence}
Under the physical hypotheses above, including
\eqref{eq:physical-global-defect}, there are allowed gauges
in which the mixed ends converge in every fixed weighted
Sobolev order after subtracting each solution's exact
critical representative. Their finite critical parameters
converge to those of $x_0$. The weight can be fixed positive
below the common exponential decay rate and the residual
asymptotic gaps. On the ordinary ends the corresponding
convergence holds in every fixed unweighted order.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $K_R$ be a compact truncation containing the matching
atom and every secondary perturbation support, with all
cylindrical ends starting beyond $R$. Smooth convergence
on the compact complement of a small matching disc gives
convergence of the topological integrals there, including
the compact perturbation terms. On that unperturbed disc,
topological energy equals normalized analytical energy.
Equation~\eqref{eq:physical-primitive-defect} therefore gives,
for each sufficiently large fixed $R$,
\[
           \cE(x_\nu;K_R)\longrightarrow\cE(x_0;K_R)+\tfrac12.
\]
Subtract this from \eqref{eq:physical-global-defect}, using
the same restriction convention for the perturbed energy
on the cylinders. The topological energy of an exact
solution on a far end is nonnegative: its gauge term is
the square of the perturbed gradient and its symplectic
term is the holomorphic energy. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:automatic-small-tails}
 \lim_{R\to\infty}\limsup_{\nu\to\infty}
                \sum_e\cE(x_\nu;e_{[R,\infty)})=0.
\end{equation}
This also bounds every tail separately. Compact secondary
terms have already been handled by convergence on $K_R$.
No unmodified curvature-square integral of a nonflat
critical representative is used on an infinite cylinder.

For each finite derivative order $l$, choose a fixed
tail start $R_l$ so that \eqref{eq:automatic-small-tails}
meets the topological small-energy threshold in
\cite[Proposition~5.29]{DaeFukLip21a} for all large $\nu$.
Its proof retains the positive decay rate from
\cite[Proposition~5.24]{DaeFukLip21a}, while decreasing
the threshold at higher orders. In temporal gauges,
patched to the compact exterior gauges as in
\cite[Section~5.4]{DaeFukLip21a}, there are exact critical
mixed pairs $(B_\nu,q_\nu)$ such that, on far unit strips,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:automatic-uniform-end-decay}
 \|A_\nu-B_\nu\|_{C^l(e_t)}
            +\|u_\nu-q_\nu\|_{C^l(e_t)}
                 \leq C_l e^{-\delta_0(t-R_l)}.
\end{equation}
All time derivatives are supplied by the same equation
and unit-strip estimates. A common rate does not require
a single threshold for all $l$.

Compactness of the critical family gives a convergent
subsequence of its classes. Anchor temporal gauges at
a fixed cross-section and use a smooth critical slice.
For any fixed large time $t$, compact convergence
identifies the limits of the actual sections there.
The distance from each section to its own critical limit
is bounded by \eqref{eq:automatic-uniform-end-decay}.
Letting first $\nu$ and then $t$ tend to infinity shows
that the limiting critical pair is $(B_0,q_0)$.
In the chosen smooth family of exact representatives
$(B_p,q_p)$, embeddedness of the critical Lagrangian
therefore gives $p_\nu\to0$.

Subtract these exact representatives, retaining their
full dependence on $p_\nu$. For a weight $\omega$ below
$\delta_0$, compact convergence and the integrable
exponential majorant give, in smooth end coordinates,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:automatic-weighted-ends}
\begin{split}
 &\|(A_\nu-B_{p_\nu})-(A_0-B_0)\|_{H^l_\omega(e)}\\
 &\qquad +\|(u_\nu-q_{p_\nu})-(u_0-q_0)\|_{H^l_\omega(e)}
                                                     \longrightarrow0.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
Indeed, split the end at a fixed large time, use smooth
convergence on the finite strip, and then make the
uniformly dominated tail small. This is the argument of
\cite[equation~(5.36)]{DaeFukLip21a}, now with its
small-tail premise supplied by
\eqref{eq:automatic-small-tails}. Decrease the weight
also below the residual asymptotic gaps.

On an ordinary end the prescribed critical point is
isolated in its ordinary slice and has a fixed spectral
gap. Choose a small slice neighborhood whose closure
contains no other critical point. Compact convergence
places a sufficiently late fixed section in a smaller
neighborhood. A sufficiently small remaining energy
prevents a first exit from the chosen neighborhood.
Otherwise, for a sequence of remaining energies tending
to zero, translate unit strips to the first exit.
Ordinary local compactness and regularity give a constant
critical limit. Its central section lies on the fixed
boundary of the slice neighborhood, which contains no
critical point, a contradiction. This argument takes
place in the local gauge quotient and gives a positive
exit-energy threshold met by
\eqref{eq:automatic-small-tails}.

In this neighborhood, the inverse Hessian and quadratic
action estimates are
\[
 \|b\|_{H^1}\leq C\|\operatorname{grad}\mathcal A(b)\|_2,
       \qquad |\mathcal A(b)-\mathcal A(0)|
                                      \leq C\|b\|_{H^1}^2.
\]
The exact limit is the prescribed critical point, so the
remaining energy satisfies $e'(t)\leq-ce(t)$.
Uniform exponential energy decay follows. The temporal
derivative solves the homogeneous linearized equation
on the translation-invariant far end. Uniform elliptic
estimates on unit strips, the all-order primary
perturbation bounds, and integration of that derivative
give decay at every fixed derivative order. Splitting
into a finite strip and a tail proves the required
ordinary-end convergence, where the complete domain
uses weight zero. These end arguments use the estimates
preceding the low-index compactness conclusion in
\cite[Section~5.4]{DaeFukLip21a}; they do not apply that
index-at-most-one conclusion to the present sequence.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[coverage for sequences with regular algebraic limits]
\label{thm:physical-sector-coverage}
Retain the fixed residual and equation data of \textup{(C1)}.
Assume the following:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The solutions converge smoothly to $x_0$ on every compact
set off $c_*$, in every fixed physical and surface derivative
order. The local and global energy defects are both one-half,
as in \eqref{eq:physical-primitive-defect} and
\eqref{eq:physical-global-defect}.
\item The finite-family reduction has one degree-zero elementary
modification, or $g=2$ and the finite families are stable with
regular limiting image line.
\item The normalized comparison primitives supplied by
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-outer-data} converge to a regular
seam-admissible primitive $E_*$.
\end{enumerate}

Every sufficiently late $[x_\nu]$ then belongs to any fixed
sufficiently small regular gluing chart whose primitive
patch contains $E_*$ in its interior and whose center
interval contains $c_*$. Its observation-corrected
parameters satisfy \eqref{eq:converse-parameter-bound},
and its full chart representative has norm $O(T_\nu^{-38})$.
Representation in that chart is unique modulo the allowed
gauge group. The retained rescaled energy measures converge
in total variation to the half-unit primitive measure, with
the whole-neck energy and diameter conclusions of
Corollary~\ref{cor:converse-whole-neck}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-outer-data} supplies the
finite doubles, coherent charge, caps, actual outer
representatives, exterior annulus, and retained radii.
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-end-convergence} supplies
the complete end convergence. The algebraic limit puts
the current data in a compact subset of a slightly
larger regular seam-admissible coordinate patch with
one observation system. All hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse} now hold.
That theorem gives exact representation and the
parameter and norm estimates. The corrected primitive
parameters tend to those of $E_*$, so the representation
lies in the prescribed smaller patch for large $\nu$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-chart-compatibility} gives
whole-patch uniqueness, and
Corollary~\ref{cor:converse-whole-neck} gives the
energy and diameter conclusions.
\end{proof}

The two finite-family alternatives can alternate along
a sequence. If their normalized data tend to the same
regular primitive, and the stable-family subsequences
satisfy the regular-line condition, apply the theorem
to each alternative. Both are eventually represented
in the same chart. Thus continuity of the finite-family
splitting is not assumed at negative rank drop.

This theorem supplies sequential coverage in the stated
regular sectors. Neither its physical hypotheses nor
the extraction of the algebraic data uses membership
in a gluing image. If two primitive coordinate patches
contain the same limit, each such incoming sequence is
eventually in both images. Their coordinate changes
have the boundary limits in
Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-chart-compatibility}.
It remains to establish which other algebraic limits
can approach these boundary points. In particular, at
negative rank drop a constant positive classifying
map does not determine the modification subline or the
limiting image line of stable finite families. Boundary
data must retain the full reduced primitive bundle and
its attaching identification. The theorem does not
identify a topology recording only the residual and
the energy atom with the required compactification.

\begin{corollary}[very stable attaching values]
\label{cor:very-stable-converse}
In genus two, the separate regular-image-line hypothesis in the
stable-family alternative of
Theorem~\ref{thm:physical-sector-coverage} may be replaced by very
stability of the residual attaching bundle $e_0(c_*)$.
All its other hypotheses on physical convergence and the
normalized primitive limit are retained. Special extension
points cannot approach a compact set of very stable attaching
values in either finite-family alternative.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The projective-pole part of
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family} does not use
regularity of the limiting line. It supplies a line
$\ell_*=i(N_{L_*})$ through $p=e_0(c_*)$.
If $L_*$ were special, the definition of $B$ would give
$p\in B$, contrary to Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-exceptional}.
Thus $H^0(\Sigma,\Lambda L_*^{-2})=0$, as required for
the remaining inverse and comparison argument. For a
one-modification primitive limit, use its retained attaching
coefficient $[e_\infty]$ over $p$; it too has nonspecial
subline parameter by Proposition~\ref{prop:very-stable-branches}.
That proposition and compactness also give the uniform exclusion.
No subline is recovered from a constant positive map in this argument.
\end{proof}

The corollary excludes special lines under a condition on the
residual value. Proposition~\ref{prop:global-residual-avoidance}
supplies that condition on the whole residual seam after a
sufficiently small finite perturbation enlargement.
The corollary alone retains the restrictions on positive rank drop
and convergence of the full normalized primitive data.
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-center-low-genus-coverage} below derives
the normalized limit at finite centers for the chosen genus-two
point cutdown and for genus-one sequences before incidence.

\subsection{Positive sphere limits and the insertion}
\label{sec:positive-sphere-repair}

Continue with the stable degree-one families of
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}, before restricting
to a seam-admissible normalized limit. In the rotated coordinate
the seam is $\operatorname{Im}z=0$ and the positive side has
$\operatorname{Im}z>0$. The actual lift is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-actual-lift}
 f_\nu(z)=i\bigl(L_\nu(z),
       [z-a_\nu:b_\nu+(z-a_\nu)k_\nu(z)]\bigr),\qquad b_\nu\ne0.
\end{equation}
On retained smaller discs the pole and Cauchy estimates give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-slow-bounds}
 \begin{gathered}
 |a_\nu|<r_\nu,\qquad |b_\nu|\leq C\eta_\nu r_\nu,\qquad
 |L_\nu-L_*|+|k_\nu|\leq C\eta_\nu,\\
 |\partial_z^jL_\nu|+|\partial_z^jk_\nu|
             \leq C_j\eta_\nu r_\nu^{-j},\qquad \eta_\nu\to0.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
The limiting line is $i(N_{L_*})$ and contains the residual
attaching value $p=i(L_*,[1:0])$.

\begin{proposition}[positive sphere limit in the stable-family sector]
\label{prop:positive-sphere-limit}
Assume the physical half-unit concentration and the stable
degree-one holomorphic hypotheses above, with $p\notin B$.
Write $a_\nu=c'_\nu+i d_\nu$, and suppose
$d_\nu>0$ and $|b_\nu|/d_\nu\to0$.
Then the positive maps at scale $|b_\nu|$ converge to a single
degree-one sphere through $p$, which exhausts the concentrating
energy. At scale $d_\nu$ the classifying maps converge to $p$
away from the sphere point, including on the seam.
The sphere operator and its node evaluation are regular.

For a positive insertion $z_\nu^{\rm mark}$ in the retained
disc, a limit $[z_\nu^{\rm mark}-a_\nu:b_\nu]\to[R:B']$
gives marked value $i(L_*,[R:B'])$.
The frozen holomorphic model has node equation $r\xi=b$,
where $r=z-a$; in a principal coordinate normalized by
the distance $d$ to the seam its coefficient is $b/d$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Positive rank drop after \eqref{eq:converse-normalized-center}
has $d_\nu/\rho_\nu\to1$ and $b_\nu/\rho_\nu\to0$,
so it satisfies the stated ratio. Here
$c'_\nu=\operatorname{Re}a_\nu$ differs from the normalized
center by $O(|b_\nu|)$; they need not coincide.
Set $z=a_\nu+b_\nu\zeta$. The actual lift becomes
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-extraction}
 f_\nu(a_\nu+b_\nu\zeta)
 =i\bigl(L_\nu(a_\nu+b_\nu\zeta),
       [\zeta:1+\zeta k_\nu(a_\nu+b_\nu\zeta)]\bigr)
 \longrightarrow F(\zeta)=i(L_*,[\zeta:1]).
\end{equation}
Every fixed compact $\zeta$-set lies on the positive side
for large $\nu$, since $d_\nu/|b_\nu|\to\infty$,
and in the retained disc since $|b_\nu|/r_\nu\to0$.
The slow bounds give convergence in every fixed $C^k$ norm.
Homogeneous coordinates include $\zeta=0$. The limit extends
to a degree-one sphere with $F(\infty)=p$ and energy $1/2$.
The phase of $b_\nu$ is retained in this conformal coordinate.

At scale $d_\nu$, set $z=c'_\nu+d_\nu w$. The affine
extension coordinate is
\[
       \frac{b_\nu/d_\nu}{w-i}+k_\nu(c'_\nu+d_\nu w).
\]
Since $d_\nu<r_\nu$, the slow bounds give $C^k$ convergence
to $p$ on compact sets away from $w=i$, including the seam.
On the negative side this concerns the classifying map,
not high-order convergence of the unitary connection.
For $|z|\leq2r_\nu$ and $|z-a_\nu|\geq R|b_\nu|$,
the same affine coordinate gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-neck-image}
                    d(f_\nu(z),p)\leq C(R^{-1}+\eta_\nu).
\end{equation}
This proves vanishing map-image diameter between scales.

Let $\mu_\nu$ be the nonnegative normalized energy measure,
pushed to the base on the gauge side. The energy on
$B_{R|b_\nu|}(a_\nu)$ converges to that of $F|_{|\zeta|\leq R}$,
which increases to $1/2$. Positivity and the exhausted
half-unit atom imply
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-energy-exhaustion}
 \lim_{R\to\infty}\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\limsup_{\nu\to\infty}
 \mu_\nu\bigl(D_\delta\setminus
                    B_{R|b_\nu|}(a_\nu)\bigr)=0.
\end{equation}
The smooth residual energy on $D_\delta$ tends to zero.
Thus no second concentrating component or additional neck
energy remains. The sphere and quasimap basepoint record
the same charge-one degeneration.

Very stability gives $H^0(\Sigma,\Lambda L_*^{-2})=0$.
The regular extension chart identifies a neighborhood of
$N_{L_*}$ with a neighborhood of its image. On this fiber
the tangent sequence is
\[
 0\longrightarrow\cO(2)\longrightarrow TN|_{N_{L_*}}
       \longrightarrow T_{L_*}\operatorname{Pic}^0(\Sigma)
                              \otimes\cO\longrightarrow0.
\]
Its quotient is $\cO^{\oplus2}$, and $H^1(\cO(2))=0$
splits the sequence. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:regular-sphere-tangent-splitting}
 F^*TM_2\cong\cO(2)\oplus\cO\oplus\cO,\qquad
 H^1(F^*TM_2)=H^1(F^*TM_2(-\infty))=0.
\end{equation}
These vanishings give sphere regularity and surjective
evaluation of its kernel at the node. Domain automorphisms
fixing the node have zero node value, so removing them
preserves surjectivity. For a regular residual and a node
at a fixed positive interior point, solve the residual and
sphere equation rows, then correct their matching row with
a sphere kernel vector. This proves regularity of that
linear nodal pair, but not an inverse estimate uniform as
the node approaches the seam.

Compactness of $\PP^1$ supplies the stated subsequential
limit of $[z_\nu^{\rm mark}-a_\nu:b_\nu]$. The slow bounds
in homogeneous coordinates give its marked value.
For $B'\ne0$ the sphere coordinate is $R/B'$; for $B'=0$
the value is $p$. In particular the path $z^{\rm mark}=it$
has sphere coordinate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-mark-ratio}
       \frac{-\operatorname{Re}a_\nu+i(t_\nu-d_\nu)}{b_\nu}.
\end{equation}
Both real positions must be retained.

For frozen $L,k$, evaluation $i(L,[r:b+kr])$ extends
over $\operatorname{Bl}_{(0,0)}\CC^2$. Its charts
$r=b\zeta$ and $b=r\xi$ have evaluations
\[
             i(L,[\zeta:1+k\zeta]),\qquad i(L,[1:\xi+k]).
\]
The second chart has the exact equations
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-sphere-node-coordinates}
                    r\xi=b,\qquad (w-i)\xi=b/d.
\end{equation}
At $b=0$ the branches $\xi=0$ and $r=0$ are the principal
component and sphere, with common node value $i(L,[1:k])$.
A mark colliding with the node requires a constant
three-pointed component in marked stable reduction;
its value remains $p$ and it adds no energy.
The ratio $\lambda=d/|b|$ specifies only the modulus
$|b/d|=\lambda^{-1}$, not the complex phase.
These holomorphic model identities do not identify
corrected mixed-chart transitions with a nodal chart.
\end{proof}

On the positive side DFL's evaluated mixed bundle is
$u^*\mathbb F_q$, where $\mathbb F_q$ is the universal
adjoint bundle at the surface point $q$
\cite[equation~(7.9)]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Its pullbacks by the two evaluation charts extend the
positive model over the sphere and node. Actual and frozen
evaluations are uniformly close by
\eqref{eq:positive-sphere-slow-bounds}; short-geodesic
transport in a fixed connection gives bundle identifications
on their retained domains. This does not imply that arbitrary
configuration-dependent incidence sections have the required limits.

\begin{proposition}[a symplectic representative avoiding primitive lines]
\label{prop:positive-primitive-incidence}
Let $\cC$ be a compact smooth residual family of real dimension
at most one with $e_x(0)\notin B$ for every $x\in\cC$.
There is an arbitrarily small change of the symplectic
section pair defining the point representative such that it
is independent along every primitive line through these
values, at the surface point $q$.
For this choice, no sequence satisfying the stable degree-one
holomorphic hypotheses of
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}, with atom at
the interpolation crossing and residual in $\cC$, can lie
in the point incidence if its insertion is in the retained
positive doubling disc.

In particular, the positive sphere of
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-sphere-limit} cannot carry
the insertion. For parameterized section pairs the choice
can be made relative to a prescribed parameter collar
where this independence already holds.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Pull back the cover of Proposition~\ref{prop:very-stable-branches}
along $p(x)=e_x(0)$:
\[
 Q=\{(x,L,[v]):i(L,[v])=p(x)\}\longrightarrow\cC .
\]
Let $W\to Q$ have projective fiber $\PP(\mathcal V_L)$.
It is smooth and compact, of real dimension
$\dim\cC+2\leq3$, and evaluates by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:primitive-line-evaluation-family}
 \Psi:W\longrightarrow M_2\times\Sigma,\qquad
 (x,L,[v];[w])\longmapsto(i(L,[w]),q).
\end{equation}
This image can have overlaps and need not be a submanifold.
Other target factors are carried at the corresponding
residual values. A line can pass through $B$ because a
different subline there is special; it need not lie
entirely in $M_2\setminus B$.

At every point of $W$, global section variations supported
near its image vary the two values through all of
$\operatorname{Hom}(\CC^2,\CC^3)$. Finitely many suffice over
compact $W$. Parametric Sard makes the pulled-back pair
transverse to the rank-one and rank-zero strata, of real
codimensions four and twelve, including on $\partial W$.
Both inverse images are empty. For nonempty $W$, compactness
in a fixed Hermitian metric gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:primitive-line-section-margin}
       \min_{w\in W}
       |\sigma_1(\Psi(w))\wedge\sigma_2(\Psi(w))|>0.
\end{equation}
For empty $W$ the assertion is vacuous. This open condition
allows sufficiently small further genericity changes and
does not change the characteristic class or coorientation.
For relative data, retain the independent pair on the
prescribed collar and multiply the remaining finite
variations by parameter cutoffs. Independence is open near
the compact fixed part; its complement projects outside
the collar, where the same finite-cover argument applies.

Choose these as the symplectic sections $s_i^S$ before
choosing DFL's mixed interpolating sections. Their positive
restriction is exactly $\sigma_i(u(z),q)$
\cite[Section~7.2, equation~(7.9)]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The image extraction in
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family} places every
retained marked value at distance tending to zero from a
limiting line through $p(x)$. Very stability places that
line among the four fibers defining $W$.
A subsequence of marked values therefore limits to $\Psi(W)$.
The margin \eqref{eq:primitive-line-section-margin} and
continuity contradict dependence of the two actual positive
section values. Compactness gives the same conclusion
for parameterized pairs.

A mark entering the smaller sphere scale has
$|z_\nu^{\rm mark}-a_\nu|=O(|b_\nu|)$, so it is
automatically in the retained positive disc.
Escaping sphere coordinates are also excluded while the
insertion remains in that disc. The proof uses the whole
limiting line and applies as well to the positive part
of a full-rank stable-family primitive.
\end{proof}

For a negative insertion, DFL's sections on the compact gauge
part can depend on the connection on that whole part
\cite[Section~7.2, condition~(iii)]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The following convergence result controls this dependency.

\begin{proposition}[negative connection at positive rank drop]
\label{prop:positive-negative-collapse}
Retain the physical and stable finite-family hypotheses of
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-sphere-limit}.
In the real affine normalization write
$z=c_\nu+\rho_\nu w$ and
$e_\nu^0(w)=(w-A_\nu)v_\nu+B_\nu e_{2,\nu}$, with
$A_\nu\to i$ and $B_\nu\to0$.
The original negative connections admit representatives converging
to the residual connection in $C^0(K)\cap H^1(K)$ on every
fixed compact gauge region $K$, including its seam boundary.
On the product collar convergence holds in
$C_B^0C_\Sigma^k\cap H_B^1H_\Sigma^k$ for each fixed finite $k$.
The local identifications are normal along the seam and join
to the prescribed exterior representatives. No negative core
is removed.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $\operatorname{Im}A\geq1/2$, trivialize the degree-one
base line on the closed negative hemisphere by the section
whose only zero is $A$. The extension class in this frame is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-negative-trivialization}
                  v+\frac{B}{w-A}e_2.
\end{equation}
Its expression at infinity is $v+B\zeta(1-A\zeta)^{-1}e_2$.
These two charts form a smooth compact family through $B=0$,
where the negative bundle is the pullback of the stable attaching
extension. Its NS boundary metric and base-flat Dirichlet metric
depend smoothly on the parameters. At $B=0$, Dirichlet uniqueness
identifies this metric with the constant attaching NS metric.
In the base-parallel unitary frame, normalized at infinity,
its surface connection therefore satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-negative-model-decay}
 |\nabla_w^j(\beta^0-\alpha)|_{C_\Sigma^k}
       \leq C_{j,k}|B|(1+|w|)^{-1-j},\qquad j\geq0.
\end{equation}
The estimate on a bounded affine region follows from smooth
parameter dependence; at infinity the difference vanishes and
has a smooth Taylor expansion. The trace-free base coefficient
is zero. This construction concerns only the negative hemisphere.
The positive classifying map still has a basepoint at $B=0$.

Choose the coherent enlarged radius as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-outer-data}, with
$R_\nu\geq c\rho_\nu^{-1/2}$ and
$r_\nu=\rho_\nu R_\nu\to0$.
Transfer the base-flat metric to the actual negative extension
frames. The slow-parameter bounds
\eqref{eq:converse-divided-parameters} still hold: their
divided difference is taken on the doubled disc, which
contains the positive point $A_\nu$.
The negative frames in \eqref{eq:positive-negative-trivialization}
are uniform, so the reference-curvature and NS boundary arguments
of Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-reference-metric} apply unchanged
on this side. In particular the seam metric logarithm has
size $C_k\eta_\nu(1+|x|)/R_\nu$ in $C_\Sigma^k$, and the
actual outer metric error tends to zero.
The scalar comparison and
Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-surface-derivatives} give smallness of
the metric logarithm in every fixed surface order.
They use no positive interior metric or map estimate.

For completeness, the larger-disc estimate also extends to this
family. Its seam NS logarithm is $F_E(x,d)$ with $F_E(x,0)=0$,
smooth on the compactified real line through $B=0$.
Writing $d=(x-A)a(x)$ gives the expansion
\eqref{eq:converse-seam-expansion}, uniformly in this neighborhood.
The concentrated remainder has $H^1$ norm $O(R^{-1/2})$ and
$H^2$ norm $O(R^{1/2})$ after dilation, so interpolation gives
the same $H^{3/2}$ trace bound as
Lemma~\ref{lem:converse-critical-trace}.
The actual surface-operator error has size
$C_k\eta(1+|w|)/R$ and base derivative $C_k\eta/R$.
The frozen coefficient has derivative
$C_k|B|(1+|w|)^{-2}$ by the compact negative family.
Thus the coefficient and segment estimates used in
\eqref{eq:converse-large-metric} and
\eqref{eq:converse-large-axial} hold here as well.

Set $Z=w/R$. Those estimates, on fixed smaller relative
half-discs, give for some $\tau_{k,\nu}\to0$
\[
 \|\beta_\nu^g-\beta_\nu^0\|_{L_Z^\infty C_\Sigma^k
                                     \,\cap\,H_Z^1H_\Sigma^k}
       \leq C_k\tau_{k,\nu},\qquad
 \|A_{Z,\nu}^g\|_{C_Z^1C_\Sigma^k}\leq C_kr_\nu^2.
\]
The axial transport is close to the base-parallel identification
by the $H_Z^1$ segment trace estimate for its base coefficient.
This does not infer transport bounds from the metric supremum alone.
Combine these inequalities with
\eqref{eq:positive-negative-model-decay} and the conformally
invariant base Dirichlet integral. In physical coordinates,
$A_z=r^{-1}A_Z$ and $d_zA_z=r^{-2}d_ZA_Z$, hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-negative-physical-bounds}
\begin{split}
 \|\beta_\nu^g-\alpha_\nu\|_{L_z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}
       +\|\beta_\nu^g-\alpha_\nu\|_{H_z^1H_\Sigma^k}
       &\leq C_k(|B_\nu|+\tau_{k,\nu}+r_\nu),\\
 \|A_{z,\nu}^g\|_{L_z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}
       +\|A_{z,\nu}^g\|_{H_z^1H_\Sigma^k}
       &\leq C_kr_\nu,\qquad
 \|d_zA_{z,\nu}^g\|_{L_z^\infty C_\Sigma^k}\leq C_k.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
No positive-map comparison or core-removal step is used.

Join to the exterior gauge by the connection argument in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-core-removal}.
On its shrinking overlap both representatives have bounded
surface jets, physical base coefficient $O(r)$ and bounded
first physical derivative. Their transition satisfies
$d_BG=O(r)$ and $d_B^2G=O(1)$.
Retain its base-constant surface factor $G_0$ in the inner
identification. Then $GG_0^{-1}-I=O(r^2)$, and cutting off
its small logarithm contributes $O(r)$ to the physical base
coefficient. The transformed attaching connection converges
to the residual one by comparison at an overlap anchor.
The radial cutoff preserves the normal seam condition.
Equation~\eqref{eq:positive-negative-physical-bounds}, the
shrinking area and exterior smooth convergence prove the
asserted compact $C^0$ and $H^1$ convergence. The same
argument retains every fixed surface order and leaves the
exterior end representatives unchanged.
\end{proof}

This result concerns the original negative connection on the
whole compact dependency region. It is not convergence of the
bubbling mixed pair in its original configuration norm.
Nor does it give all higher physical Sobolev norms: a profile
of amplitude $B_\nu$ on scale $\rho_\nu$ has $j$th base
derivative of $L^2$ size $|B_\nu|\rho_\nu^{1-j}$.
For $j\geq2$ the stated hypotheses need not make that expression
small. We next choose sections continuous in the proved topology.

\begin{proposition}[compact negative insertion intervals]
\label{prop:positive-negative-incidence}
Let $C$ be a compact smooth residual family of real dimension
at most one, with irreducible restrictions to a common compact
gauge region. Fix a compact interval $I\subset(-\infty,0)$
on the negative insertion path and a surface point $q$.
The compact gauge-side interpolating pair may be chosen so
that no point-cut sequence with insertion in $I$ has a
positive-rank-drop limit as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse} with
residual in $C$. This choice preserves the prescribed
symplectic pair and the far negative gauge pullback, after
increasing its finite compact cutoff if necessary.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Take a connected compact region $X_c$ containing the path
segment and all holonomy dependencies to be chosen. In the
fixed-determinant matrix convention, normalize the determinant
of a based loop holonomy by one fixed square root of its
fixed determinant holonomy. The resulting $SU(2)$ matrix
$H_\gamma(A)$ gives the gauge-equivariant vector
\[
                 v_\gamma(A)=\tfrac12(H_\gamma(A)-H_\gamma(A)^*)
                       \in\mathfrak{su}(E_{y_0}).
\]
Irreducibility supplies two loops with noncommuting holonomies.
Their vectors $v_1,v_2$ are not parallel, so
$v_1,v_2,[v_1,v_2]$ span the real adjoint fiber.
Transport these vectors along fixed families of paths to
points in small spatial charts, multiply by spatial cutoffs,
and extend by zero. These are sections of the universal
adjoint bundle over $\mathcal B^*(X_c)\times X_c$,
defined even where their values fail to span.
Compactness supplies finitely many complexified sections
$E_1,\ldots,E_N$ spanning at every point
$([A_x|_{X_c}],y(t,q))$, $(x,t)\in C\times I$.

For two rows of complex coefficients set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:negative-holonomy-pair}
                         s_i^a=\sum_{j=1}^N a_{ij}E_j,
                                  \qquad i=1,2.
\end{equation}
Coefficient variation spans the whole pair fiber
$\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb C^2,\mathbb C^3)$.
Parametric Sard on $C\times I$ and its boundary strata
therefore makes the pair transverse to ranks one and zero.
Their real codimensions are four and twelve, whereas
$\dim(C\times I)\leq2$. For generic $a$ the pair is
independent everywhere, with a positive wedge minimum
if the parameter space is nonempty.

To impose this choice on the actual mixed interpolation,
increase the finite cutoff $T$ to contain $I$, the spatial
supports and all loops in $X_T$. Let $\chi$ be a spatial
cutoff equal to one near $y(I,q)$ and supported strictly
inside the negative compact region. Replace the old pair by
\[
                    \widetilde s_i
                         =\chi s_i^a+(1-\chi)s_i^{\mathrm{old}}.
\]
This factors through $A|_{X_T}$, as required by DFL's
condition (iii), and preserves the seam, positive restriction
and pullback for $t\leq-T$
\cite[Section~7.2, equations~(7.9)--(7.10)]{DaeFukLip21a}.
It is exactly \eqref{eq:negative-holonomy-pair} on a
neighborhood of the specified insertion segment.

Parallel transport has, in common unitary representatives,
the estimate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:negative-holonomy-continuity}
 \|U_{A'}(\gamma)-U_A(\gamma)\|
       \leq C\operatorname{length}(\gamma)\|A'-A\|_{C^0(X_T)}.
\end{equation}
It follows by variation of constants; the two transport
factors in its integral have norm one. This estimate is
uniform for the finitely many compact path families.
Matrix operations and finite sums retain the continuity,
and their ODE formulas give the required differentiability
at sufficiently high Sobolev regularity.
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse} thus
identifies the actual section limits with their residual
values, also when $t_\nu\to t_0$ in $I$.
Their positive wedge minimum contradicts the point incidence.
\end{proof}

The preceding proposition chooses the pair for a specified compact
negative interval. To make one choice for the full path, we retain
the actual prescriptions at its two ordinary ends and its seam.

\begin{proposition}[exclusion of the positive rank-drop end]
\label{prop:positive-end-exclusion}
Let $C$ be a compact smooth residual family of real dimension
at most one, with uniform ordinary-end convergence, a common
compact perturbation support, and prescribed ends in finite sets
of irreducible nondegenerate gauge and transverse symplectic
critical points. Assume the compact gauge restrictions and all
sufficiently far incoming slices are irreducible, and the residual
values at the insertion crossing are very stable.
There is one choice of symplectic pair, gauge pair and mixed
interpolating pair satisfying DFL's prescriptions for which no
point-cut sequence with residual in $C$, the same ordinary ends,
and an exhausted global half-unit defect has a positive-rank-drop
limit at a finite seam center in the regular stable finite-family
sector of Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse}.
This holds for every sequence of insertion parameters in $\mathbb R$,
including limits at the seam and at either infinity.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose the symplectic pair independent both on the compact
line family $W$ of \eqref{eq:primitive-line-evaluation-family}
and on all residual positive insertion evaluations
$(u_x(t,0),q)$, $x\in C$, $t\geq0$, and their critical limits.
Finitely many global section variations span the pair fiber on
the compact target. Apply parametric Sard to $W$, to
$C\times[0,n]$ for each positive integer $n$, and to the
finite critical set, including their boundary strata.
Their dimensions are at most three, two and zero, below
the rank-one codimension four. The countable union of
exceptional parameter sets still has measure zero.
Uniform ordinary-end convergence makes the completed residual
evaluation image compact. Thus one pair $\sigma^S$ has a
positive wedge minimum on this image and on $W$.
No differentiability at compactified infinity is required.

For large fixed $T$, the completed image of the incoming
restrictions $[A_x(t)]$, $x\in C$, $t\leq-T$, is a compact
subset of $\mathcal B^*(Y_\#,E_\#)$.
It includes the prescribed critical limits. Use the holonomy
frames in the preceding proof on $Y_\#$ to choose finitely
many sections spanning the evaluated fiber on this compact
image. The same countable Sard argument on
$C\times[-n,-T]$ and the critical set gives a finite holonomy
pair $\sigma^G$ independent there, with a positive wedge
minimum. It defines sections of the universal bundle on
$\mathcal B^*(Y_\#,E_\#)\times Y_\#$ even where its
constituent frames cease to span.

Near the negative seam define the pair by normal transport:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-end-seam-sections}
 s_i^\partial(A,t,q)
       =P_A(y(0,q),y(t,q))\,
                      \sigma_i^S([\beta(0,0)],q),
                      \qquad -2\epsilon\leq t\leq0.
\end{equation}
The seam connection is flat and DFL identifies its evaluated
bundle with the symplectic universal bundle. This is a
gauge-equivariant formula with the same wedge norm as the
seam pair. It agrees with the positive prescription at zero.

We check its compact-dependency requirement on the configuration
space. On the stable open set of unitary surface connections,
the normalized NS metric and its canonical unitary transport
define a smooth equivariant retraction $r(\beta)$ to the
projectively flat connections. It is the identity on flat
connections, by uniqueness of the NS metric. The surface
implicit-function theorem used in the metric comparisons
gives its local smoothness. Pull $\sigma^S$ back by $r$
on the same fixed Hermitian adjoint fibers. In the ordinary
surface slice quotient choose a smooth cutoff equal to one
near the compact flat moduli space, with support contained
in the stable open set, and extend the product by zero.
Finitely many small slice neighborhoods with closures in
this open set give such a cutoff. This extends the seam
section to arbitrary surface connections. Restriction from
$A|_{X_T}$ followed by normal transport extends
\eqref{eq:positive-end-seam-sections} to the compact universal
bundle. Work at a sufficiently high Sobolev order for the
restrictions and the finite differentiability used here.
On mixed configurations the surface cutoff equals one.
Thus the formula depends only on $A|_{X_T}$ and is continuous
in the compact connection and seam-surface convergence of
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse}.

On the far incoming end use the exact slice pullback of
$\sigma^G$. Near the seam use
\eqref{eq:positive-end-seam-sections}. A partition in $t$
gives a background pair on a tube around the compact middle
of the negative insertion path, equal to these prescriptions
near its two ends. Both are independent on the residual
family there. Add finitely many holonomy variations supported
in the remaining middle, retaining smaller fixed collars.
Their pair values span by the preceding proposition.
Relative parametric Sard on the compact parameter space
$C$ times the middle interval, of dimension at most two,
makes the pair independent everywhere; sufficiently small
variations preserve the margins on the collars.
Extend from this tube by a spatial cutoff and an existing
interpolating pair. The new and old boundary values agree
with $\sigma^S$ at the seam and $\sigma^G$ on the far end,
so the extension preserves both prescriptions. Each term
factors through $A|_{X_T}$. This produces one allowed mixed
pair, independent on the entire residual insertion path,
whose negative evaluations have the proved continuity.

We next treat positive insertions approaching the atom but
outside the originally retained disc. Suppose the atom is
at the interpolation crossing, and write $p=u_0(0,0)$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse} and exact
matching give uniform convergence of seam classes on a
fixed compact seam interval. On a small fixed positive
outer semicircle the original smooth convergence holds.
For fixed large $R$, the sphere circle
$|z-a_\nu|=R|b_\nu|$ has image at distance at most
$C/R+o(1)$ from $p$, by
\eqref{eq:positive-sphere-neck-image}.
All boundary images of
$\Omega_{\nu,\delta,R}=D_\delta^+\setminus
B_{R|b_\nu|}(a_\nu)$ are consequently near $p$.
Its energy tends to zero in the iterated limit of
\eqref{eq:positive-sphere-energy-exhaustion}.
Interior target-ball monotonicity gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-end-full-neck-image}
 \lim_{R\to\infty}\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}
       \limsup_{\nu\to\infty}
       \sup_{z\in\Omega_{\nu,\delta,R}}d(u_\nu(z),p)=0.
\end{equation}
Indeed, a value a fixed distance $\varepsilon$ from $p$
would give a target ball of radius $\varepsilon/4$ missing
the whole boundary image, once $R$ is large and $\delta$
small. Its preimage component has compact closure in the
domain. Monotonicity would force energy at least
$c\varepsilon^2$, contradicting exhaustion.
