This uses the seam and both circular boundary controls,
not energy smallness alone.

For a positive mark tending to zero, a bounded sphere ratio
$(z_\nu-a_\nu)/b_\nu$ has its limit on the line in $W$.
An unbounded ratio has value tending to $p$ by
\eqref{eq:positive-end-full-neck-image}, even outside the
initial retained disc. Both are excluded by $\sigma^S$.
If the atom is at a different finite seam center, a mark
near the crossing lies away from the atom and smooth
convergence identifies its residual value.

Finally pass to a subsequence of hypothetical cut points
with $t_\nu\to t_0$ in the extended real line. A finite
negative $t_0$ uses compact negative connection convergence
and the chosen continuous formulas. A negative approach
to zero uses the unchanged wedge norm in
\eqref{eq:positive-end-seam-sections}. Positive approaches
to zero were just treated, and finite positive $t_0$ use
smooth convergence away from the seam atom.
For $t_\nu\to\pm\infty$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-end-convergence} gives
uniform ordinary-end convergence of the actual sequence
to its prescribed critical representatives. The finite
holonomy pair $\sigma^G$ or the smooth pair $\sigma^S$
therefore has its chosen nonzero critical wedge limit.
Every case contradicts incidence.
\end{proof}

The proposition chooses the end and mixed representatives;
it does not apply to arbitrary prescribed pairs.
Relative fixed parameter collars require independence and
compatible section limits already on those collars.
The sections represent the same universal characteristic class.
Their proper relative count and compatible transversality
are constructed in the subsequent incidence and boundary argument.
The nonconstant negative primitives do not satisfy
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse}, so their
contribution is not excluded. Positive rank drop remains an
unmarked degeneration. The proposition excludes it from
this chosen point cutdown in the stated sector, so an
ambient nodal collar is not needed for that exclusion.

\subsection{Normalized compactness at finite centers}

\begin{theorem}[finite-center coverage in genera one and two]
\label{thm:finite-center-low-genus-coverage}
Fix the equation and a finite regular index-zero residual set $C$
in a fixed energy and ordinary-end range. Let $J$ be a compact
interval of finite seam centers containing the insertion crossing.
Suppose smooth mixed solutions
converge to $x_0\in C$ smoothly modulo gauge off $c_*\in J$,
with the local and global half-unit defects
\eqref{eq:physical-primitive-defect} and
\eqref{eq:physical-global-defect}, and the same ordinary ends.
In genus one, their actual normalized primitive data have a
regular seam-admissible limit after passing to a subsequence.
In genus two the same conclusion holds for the point cutdown
chosen in Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-end-exclusion}, provided
the residual attaching values on $C\times J$ are very stable.

Every sufficiently late member of this subsequence is represented
in each sufficiently small regular gluing chart whose primitive
patch contains the limit in its interior. The representation has
the estimates of Theorem~\ref{thm:physical-sector-coverage} and
is unique in the patch modulo the permitted gauge and real affine
actions. Existence, regularity and seam admissibility of the
normalized limit are conclusions here.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For a primitive pencil $e(w)=vw+w_0$ retain its nonzero attaching
coefficient $[v]$ and subline $L$. In the affine slice
\eqref{eq:gluing-affine-slice-functions}, write
$\kappa=i\tau$, $\tau\in\mathbb R$, and let
$\xi:\mathbb Cv\to v^\perp$ send $v$ to $w_\perp$.
Common complex scaling changes neither $\tau$ nor $\xi$.
The slice equations are exactly
\begin{equation}\label{eq:normalized-primitive-sphere}
 \tau^2+\|\xi\|^2=1,\qquad
 e(w)=v(w+i\tau)+\xi(v),\qquad
 \xi\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Cv,v^\perp).
\end{equation}
In genus two this is the unit two-sphere in a real rank-three
bundle over the attaching extension space. Over a compact set
of very stable values the latter is a compact four-sheeted
cover by Proposition~\ref{prop:very-stable-branches}.
Thus the normalized coefficient completion is compact.

If $\xi\ne0$, the pencil has independent coefficients and
no basepoint. If $\xi=0$, its common zero is $-i\tau$, with
$\tau=\pm1$. These are precisely negative and positive
rank drop. A real common zero would require both
$\xi=0$ and $\tau=0$, contradicting the unit norm.
A zero at infinity would require $v=0$.
There is no additional seam-basepoint or coefficient-escape
stratum in this normalized single-pencil completion.

Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-outer-data} gives exactly
the finite-family alternatives $(d,\ell)=(0,1)$ and $(1,0)$.
In the one-modification alternative, compactness of its
degree-zero subline follows from the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-one-modification}:
normalize the inclusion into the converging stable replacement,
pass to a Picard limit, and apply elliptic compactness.
The nonzero limiting inclusion cannot have zeros, since
its saturation would have degree at least one in a stable
rank-two degree-one bundle. It remains a subbundle.
The actual center $\operatorname{Re}a_\nu$ and scale
$-\operatorname{Im}a_\nu$ put the modification point at
$-i$ exactly. This gives a converging negative-rank-drop
primitive, retaining its full subline data. In genus one
this is the only finite-family alternative, and
$\dim_\mathbb C V_L=1$ leaves no perpendicular coefficient.

In the stable genus-two alternative, the projective-pole
part of Proposition~\ref{prop:converse-stable-family}
extracts a line through the residual value before assuming
its regularity. Very stability makes its subline parameter
nonspecial by Corollary~\ref{cor:very-stable-converse}.
The regular inverse therefore supplies the actual current
pencil. Equation~\eqref{eq:normalized-primitive-sphere}
gives a convergent normalized subsequence. Its full-rank
and negative-rank-drop limits are seam admissible.
The only remaining limit is positive rank drop, excluded
for these actual cut points by
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-end-exclusion}.
Very stability similarly makes the subline nonspecial
in the genus-two one-modification alternative.

The obtained seam-admissible limits are regular.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fredholm-dolbeault} gives
$H^2(\mathbb P^1\times\Sigma,\operatorname{End}_0E)=0$.
By Proposition~\ref{prop:attaching-kernel-normalization},
the complex attaching-evaluation cokernel has dimension
$g-2+h^0(\Lambda L^{-2})$. It vanishes in genus one,
where the degree-one line has one section, and in genus
two for the nonspecial sublines just obtained.
These computations include negative rank drop.
Thus primitive evaluation is onto and the fiber product
with the fixed regular index-zero residual is regular.

The automatic enlargement and end results now supply
all other hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:physical-sector-coverage}. Apply that
theorem to obtain the asserted exact chart representation,
estimates and uniqueness. Alternating finite-family
alternatives can be treated on separate subsequences;
when their full limits agree, both enter the same chart.
The full attaching data are retained even when the
positive classifying map is constant.
\end{proof}

The point-cut hypothesis excludes the positive pole in genus two.
Section~\ref{sec:cut-compactification} treats centers escaping
down mixed ends and proves compactness of the remaining cut.
The relative incidence construction in
Section~\ref{sec:local-seam-incidence} determines the local
counts on the finite-center neighborhoods.

On the complement of the positive pole in
\eqref{eq:normalized-primitive-sphere}, stereographic coordinates give
the smooth bundle identification
\begin{equation}\label{eq:normalized-primitive-plane}
 \zeta=\frac{\xi}{1+\tau},\qquad
 \tau=\frac{1-\|\zeta\|^2}{1+\|\zeta\|^2},\qquad
 \xi=\frac{2\zeta}{1+\|\zeta\|^2}.
\end{equation}
Thus at a fixed very stable genus-two attaching value the four
seam-admissible normalized primitive branches are four planes.
Their origins are negative rank drop, and their added points
at infinity are positive rank drop. Over varying attaching data
the planes form the complex line bundle
$\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Cv,v^\perp)$ on the four-sheeted
extension cover. This is a smooth identification determined by
the chosen metric; it does not identify the oriented relative
incidence class or a holomorphic structure on the real affine quotient.

\subsection{Coverage of the point-cut ends}\label{sec:primitive-gluing}

The local construction gives smooth parameters at every
sufficiently small positive scale on compact regular
seam-admissible patches.
Theorem~\ref{thm:constructed-broken-limit} identifies
their full broken limit.
Theorems~\ref{thm:regular-sector-converse}
and~\ref{thm:regular-chart-compatibility}
give converse entry, injectivity, and transition limits
on the stated regular sectors.
Theorem~\ref{thm:physical-sector-coverage} derives
the necessary outer and end hypotheses for physical
sequences with a regular reduced limit.

For the point cut, the regular-limit restriction is
removed by the subsequent evaluation and incidence
arguments. Very stable attaching values exclude
special genus-two lines. The finite-center theorem
covers the genus-one and genus-two alternatives,
and the point-incidence sections exclude positive
sphere escape. Propositions~\ref{prop:mixed-end-exclusion}
and~\ref{prop:all-bubbling-cut-ends} complete the
exhaustion for arbitrary sequences in the chosen cut.
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}
then uses these finitely many regular neighborhoods.
An ambient differentiable collar through every
uncut primitive and positive nodal configuration
is not required.

\section{Residual transversality and relative incidence}
\label{sec:boundary-formula}

We choose residual evaluation and incidence data so that the
primitive ends of the point cut lie in the regular neighborhoods
constructed above. The relative seam calculations give local
degrees eight and four. Section~\ref{sec:gluing-orientations}
compares their coordinate orientations with the actual mixed
gluing maps. Section~\ref{sec:cut-compactification} excludes
the other bubbling alternatives and proves the signed boundary
identity. Section~\ref{sec:reference-comparison} fixes its
coherent orientation signs and proves the integral point theorem.

\subsection{Evaluation transversality}\label{sec:evaluation-transversality}
Let $\cP$ consist of standard secondary holonomy perturbations and
domain-dependent almost-complex variations, with output and dependency
regions disjoint from the fixed matching neighborhood. We specify the
holonomy convention. On DFL's permitted gauge region $X_c$, take
submersions $\gamma:S^1\times B\to X_c$ with $\gamma(1,b)=b$ and set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:matrix-holonomy-directions}
 V_{\gamma,\omega}(A)=
 \pi_{\mathfrak{su}(2)}
 \bigl(\omega\otimes\operatorname{Hol}_\gamma(\widetilde A)\bigr),
 \qquad \omega\in\Omega_c^{2,+}(B;\CC).
\end{equation}
Here $\widetilde A$ is a unitary lift with fixed determinant connection,
and $\pi_{\mathfrak{su}(2)}$ is the real orthogonal projection.
Real and imaginary coefficients give independent real directions.
This is the convention of \cite[Section~3(i)]{Kronheimer05};
it includes DFL's displayed real-form multiples of projected holonomy.
We allow this standard enlargement explicitly. Projection commutes with
gauge transformations, $|V_{\gamma,\omega}(A)|\leq C|\omega|$,
and both support and dependency remain outside the collar.
Kronheimer's interior compactness theorem
\cite[Proposition~11]{Kronheimer05}, together with DFL's unchanged
matching and end analysis, gives index-zero compactness for a fixed
finite family of these directions. Their coefficient norms can be
made small enough to retain the nonnegative-energy bound in
\cite[Section~6.2]{DaeFukLip21a}. No assertion about a smaller
coefficient family is needed below.

Let $\cS$ be a stratum in an algebraic Whitney stratification for which
$i_g$ and the restrictions of $\ev_\infty$ to the full-rank and Segre
strata are stratified submersions.  On the universal residual moduli space
consider
\begin{equation}\label{eq:universal-seam-evaluation}
 \Ev_\gamma:\cM^{\rm univ}_0(\alpha,\beta)\times\RR_s\longrightarrow M_g,
 \qquad
 \Ev_\gamma([A,u],\eta,s)=[A|_{(s,0)\times\Sigma_g}].
\end{equation}
The matching condition gives the same value from $u$.

The required genericity concerns evaluation of actual residual
solutions. It does not follow from the dimension of an arbitrary
path in $M_g$. We prove the needed evaluation statement using two
consecutive Sobolev realizations of the mixed operator.

At a smooth regular index-zero residual use DFL's $l=2$ realization
of the full gauge-fixed operator, denoted by
$D:\mathcal E^2\to\mathcal Y^1$. The domain retains linear
matching, zero normal trace, outer boundary conditions, and the
exact end terms; the target is the unrestricted weighted $H^1$
sum of equation fields. DFL's configuration and Fredholm theory
allow this choice \cite[Definition~3.9 and Proposition~3.10]
{DaeFukLip21a}. Regularity and the gauge splitting make $D$
invertible with the stated end convention. For a fixed seam point
$s$, let $K=d\Ev_\gamma|_{\dot s=0}$. This is bounded on
$\mathcal E^2$, since $H^2$ point evaluation on the
two-dimensional map domain is bounded. It is onto on smooth
configuration variations: prescribe a harmonic matching trace
locally and extend it to both sides with compact support.
The connection restriction gives the same harmonic class.

Let $Z_s$ be the evaluation surface $\{s\}\times\Sigma$ in
the boundary of the gauge domain, together with its corresponding
point on the boundary of the map domain. Write $P$ for variation
of the equation in all allowed smooth secondary directions.
The adjoint equation for evaluation has a distributional source:
an annihilator consists of $\lambda\in(\mathcal Y^1)^*$ and
$\nu\in T^*_{\Ev_\gamma(s)}M_g$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:adjoint-evaluation-source}
 \langle\lambda,D\xi\rangle+\nu(K\xi)=0,
 \qquad \langle\lambda,P\dot\eta\rangle=0
\end{equation}
for all domain variations and all allowed perturbation directions.
We will show that $\lambda$ is supported on $Z_s$, including
possible distributions on the boundary, and then that it vanishes.

\begin{proposition}[residual seam evaluation]
\label{lem:seam-evaluation}
At every smooth regular index-zero residual and fixed finite seam
point, the operator
\begin{equation}\label{eq:augmented-evaluation-linearization}
 (\xi,\dot\eta)\longmapsto(D\xi+P\dot\eta,K\xi)
\end{equation}
is surjective. Finitely many directions of
\eqref{eq:matrix-holonomy-directions} suffice at the fixed residual
and seam point. In a fixed finite energy and end range and on a
compact seam interval $I$, a finite-dimensional enlargement gives
generic residual paths avoiding $B$ in genus two on $I$.
For $g\geq3$ the analogous conclusion is avoidance of $i_g(N_g)$.
These statements use only the index-zero compactness theorem.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We first regularize the adjoint away from $Z_s$. Denote the given
realization by $D_2:\mathcal E^2\to\mathcal Y^1$ and the realization
with the same matching and exact end conditions by
$D_1:\mathcal E^1\to\mathcal Y^0$. DFL prove Fredholmness starting
at $k=1$, with regularity at all higher orders
\cite[Theorem~4.12 and Lemma~4.34]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Finite secondary holonomy derivatives are compact
$\mathcal E^1\to\mathcal Y^0$. Indeed, at the fixed smooth
connection the derivative of parallel transport has the form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:linear-holonomy-transport}
 D\operatorname{Hol}_\gamma|_A(a)(b)=
 -\int_0^1 U_A(1,t;b)\,
   a_{\gamma(t,b)}(\partial_t\gamma)\,U_A(t,0;b)\,dt .
\end{equation}
Pullback by the submersion, smooth multiplication, and integration
in $t$ are bounded on $H^j$ for $j=0,1$; compact support and
Rellich give compactness into $L^2$.
The fixed cylinder Hessians have the same local bounds, with the
longitudinal derivative retained. These are linear estimates at a
smooth connection, not assertions of nonlinear smoothness on $H^1$.
If $D_1\xi=0$, the higher-order estimate gives $\xi\in\mathcal E^2$,
so $\xi=0$. The closed image of $D_1$ contains the dense subspace
$\mathcal Y^1$ because $D_2$ is onto. Thus $D_1$ is invertible and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lower-mixed-inverse}
 \|D_1^{-1}f\|_{\mathcal E^1}\leq C\|f\|_{\mathcal Y^0},
 \qquad D_1^{-1}f=D_2^{-1}f\quad(f\in\mathcal Y^1).
\end{equation}

Let $\chi$ be a smooth compact cutoff zero near $Z_s$, constant
in the surface variable at the seam and equal on its two sides.
Multiplication by $\chi$ preserves the matching, normal trace,
and outer conditions and removes the exact end terms. Such cutoffs
can equal one near every point off $Z_s$; near another seam point
they are chosen on the base and include the whole surface factor.
For $f\in\mathcal Y^1$, the first adjoint identity applied to
$\chi D_2^{-1}f$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:localized-adjoint-ltwo}
 \begin{aligned}
  (\chi\lambda)(f)&=-\lambda([D,\chi]D_2^{-1}f),\\
  |(\chi\lambda)(f)|&\leq C_\chi
    \|\lambda\|_{(\mathcal Y^1)^*}\|f\|_{\mathcal Y^0}.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Here $K(\chi D_2^{-1}f)=0$. The differential commutator has
order zero; the nonlocal commutators also map
$\mathcal E^1\to\mathcal Y^1$, by
\eqref{eq:linear-holonomy-transport}. They need not be differential
operators. Equation~\eqref{eq:lower-mixed-inverse} proves the bound.
Density and the $L^2$ representation theorem now identify
$\chi\lambda$ with an $L^2$ field, with the dual weight. This is an
identity on the unrestricted $H^1$ target, including its boundary
traces, so no additional boundary functional is omitted.

On a seam segment disjoint from $Z_s$, variations supported in
the collar have no perturbation output: the dependency regions
also avoid the collar. The weak adjoint equation there is
homogeneous. The $L^2$ field just obtained satisfies
\cite[Theorem~5.9(ii)]{DaeFukLip21b}, which gives $H^1$ regularity
and the adjoint matching condition. The higher-order part of that
theorem, applied to the formal adjoint, gives smoothness up to
the segment. This proves the negative-order boundary regularity
needed here without asserting $L^2$ regularity at $Z_s$ itself.

Write $\lambda=(\phi,\beta,z)$ for its scalar gauge, self-dual,
and map components, initially as functionals. For any scalar
$H^1$ target $f$, put $\xi=D_2^{-1}(f,0,0)$. Its physical
equation derivative is zero. Since the residual has zero kernel
on the gauge quotient, $\xi$ is an infinitesimal gauge variation;
use the Coulomb chart and gauge splitting in
\cite[Propositions~3.10 and~4.6]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Evaluation is gauge invariant, so $K\xi=0$. The adjoint identity
implies $\phi(f)=0$, hence $\phi=0$. This step uses no integration
by parts at the evaluation surface.

The restriction of $A$ to $X_c$ is irreducible, as in
\cite[Section~6.2]{DaeFukLip21a}. Locally in $X_c$, choose
finitely many based loops whose holonomies span the complex
endomorphism algebra, and thicken them to submersions. Their
holonomies remain a smooth spanning family on a smaller output
ball. Varying the complex self-dual coefficients in
\eqref{eq:matrix-holonomy-directions} then produces every smooth
compactly supported adjoint self-dual section on that ball.
This is the local spanning argument in
\cite[Lemma~13]{Kronheimer05}. The perturbation annihilation
therefore gives $\beta|_{X_c}=0$ as a distribution.

For a gauge variation compactly supported in the interior, the
fixed and secondary holonomy terms have output in $X_c$ and
are annihilated by $\beta$. The first adjoint identity reduces to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:separated-gauge-adjoint}
                 d_A^{+,*}\beta=0\quad\hbox{in }\operatorname{int}X .
\end{equation}
On self-dual forms this operator has injective principal symbol;
$d_A^+d_A^{+,*}$ has scalar Laplace principal part, up to the
factor $1/2$. Distributional elliptic regularity makes $\beta$
smooth in the interior. Unique continuation for this
scalar-principal system \cite[Remark~3]{Aronszajn57} and its
vanishing on $X_c$ give $\beta=0$ throughout the gauge interior.
Every connected gauge component meets the permitted region.
The continuation is applied after eliminating the nonlocal terms.

Choose a seam arc away from $s$. The smooth adjoint matching
condition there gives zero trace for $z$, since the gauge field
is zero. In the map interior $D_u^*z=0$. In a unitary
trivialization this has Cauchy--Riemann principal part. Extend
$z$ by zero across the arc and extend the smooth coefficients.
Its zero trace removes the boundary distribution, so the
extension solves the homogeneous equation weakly. Interior
regularity and unique continuation make it zero, first near the
arc and then throughout the connected map interior. This applies
also when $u$ is constant; no almost-complex variation is required.
All interior components of $\lambda$ vanish.
Equation~\eqref{eq:localized-adjoint-ltwo} excludes boundary
functionals at every point off $Z_s$. Thus
$\operatorname{supp}\lambda\subset Z_s$.

Finally, a continuous functional on the unrestricted $H^1$ target
supported on $Z_s$ is zero. Choose a smooth cutoff
$\chi_\epsilon$ in half-disc coordinates transverse to $Z_s$,
zero for $r\leq\epsilon^2$ and one for $r\geq\epsilon$, with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:evaluation-capacity-cutoff}
 |\nabla\chi_\epsilon|\leq
       \frac{C}{r|\log\epsilon|},\qquad
 \int|\nabla\chi_\epsilon|^2\leq
       \frac{C}{|\log\epsilon|}.
\end{equation}
For every smooth compactly supported target section $\psi$,
$\chi_\epsilon\psi\to\psi$ in $H^1$. The $L^2$ error and
the term $(1-\chi_\epsilon)\nabla\psi$ tend to zero by the
shrinking tube volume; the remaining term is controlled by
\eqref{eq:evaluation-capacity-cutoff}. Integrating over $\Sigma$
leaves the same estimate on the gauge side. A finite partition
of unity handles the bundles, and the weights are bounded on
this compact neighborhood. The functional vanishes on every
$\chi_\epsilon\psi$, hence on $\psi$, and is zero by density.

We have proved that an annihilator of
\eqref{eq:augmented-evaluation-linearization} has $\lambda=0$.
Then $\nu K=0$, and surjectivity of configuration evaluation
gives $\nu=0$. There is no closed-range issue: $D$ is invertible
and the remaining factor is finite dimensional. Equivalently,
the values of $KD^{-1}P$ span that factor, so finitely many
directions suffice. Their solution variations are smooth by mixed
elliptic regularity. Thus the same finite-dimensional derivative
is obtained in the higher Sobolev realizations used to construct
smooth universal moduli spaces.

For the final assertion, the regular index-zero residual moduli
space in the given range is compact and discrete, hence finite
\cite[Proposition~3.26]{DaeFukLip21a}. Its product with $I$
is compact. Choose a finite union of the local separating
directions. Surjectivity persists for small parameters;
parameterized low-index compactness excludes additional
residuals outside these neighborhoods. The perturbations
remain in a fixed finite smooth family and can be chosen
small enough to preserve DFL's nonnegative-energy bound.
Parametric Sard applied to a Whitney stratification of $B$,
including the endpoint restrictions on $I$, makes each
residual path transverse to every stratum. Every stratum
has real codimension at least two by
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-exceptional}, so a transverse
one-dimensional path misses it. Equation~\eqref{eq:image-codim}
gives the same argument for $g\geq3$.
\end{proof}

We next extend avoidance to the noncompact seam. Put
$L_+=L(Y,E)$ and $L_-=L(Y',E')$, retaining the fixed primary
perturbations. The seam parameter $s$ here is denoted by $\theta$
in \cite{DaeFukLip21a}. The two mixed-end limits are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mixed-end-evaluation}
 q_\pm(x)=\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}e_x(s)\in L_\pm,
 \qquad e_x(s)=u(0,s).
\end{equation}
They can vary independently in the configuration space with fixed
Floer generators $\alpha,\beta$; see
\cite[Proposition~3.6(iii) and the following configuration
definition]{DaeFukLip21a}. Write
$Z_2=B$ and $Z_g=i_g(N_g)$ for $g\geq3$.
These are proper closed complex algebraic subsets of $M_g$.

\begin{proposition}[avoidance on the whole residual seam]
\label{prop:global-residual-avoidance}
At a smooth regular index-zero residual, let
$K_\partial=d(q_+,q_-)$ be the configuration endpoint derivative.
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:augmented-end-evaluation}
 (\xi,\dot\eta)\longmapsto
 (D\xi+P\dot\eta,K_\partial\xi)
 \quad\text{in }\mathcal Y^1\oplus
       T_{q_+}L_+\oplus T_{q_-}L_-
\end{equation}
is onto, using finitely many of the permitted holonomy directions.
In a fixed finite energy and end range, an arbitrarily small
finite-dimensional enlargement can be chosen so that every
index-zero residual seam avoids $Z_g$, including its two mixed-end
limits. For the resulting perturbation, the finite union of these
completed seam images is a compact subset of $M_g\setminus Z_g$.
This avoidance persists under sufficiently small changes in any
subsequent fixed finite permitted family.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The exact critical end coefficients in DFL's domain give bounded maps
\[
 K_\partial:\mathcal E^j\longrightarrow
             T_{q_+}L_+\oplus T_{q_-}L_-,
 \qquad j=1,2.
\]
Their norms are explicit in \cite[equation~(4.5)]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Boundary restriction identifies the perturbed harmonic end spaces
with the tangent spaces of the embedded Lagrangians. The end charts
in \cite[Section~4.1]{DaeFukLip21a} vary the two representatives
independently. Extend their matching variations on the respective
ends and cut them off towards the compact part; the chart
trivializations give the decaying corrections at the actual
residual. A smooth infinitesimal gauge transformation whose scalar
vanishes on the boundary removes the normal component without changing
the tangential matching trace or the endpoint classes; its end correction
decays since the exact harmonic representatives have zero normal trace.
Thus $K_\partial$ is onto on smooth
configuration variations. It vanishes on compactly supported variations.

Suppose $\lambda\in(\mathcal Y^1)^*$ and
$\nu\in(T_{q_+}L_+\oplus T_{q_-}L_-)^*$ annihilate
\eqref{eq:augmented-end-evaluation}. The compatible lower-order
inverse in \eqref{eq:lower-mixed-inverse} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:end-adjoint-ltwo}
 \lambda(f)=-\nu(K_\partial D_1^{-1}f),\qquad
 |\lambda(f)|\leq C|\nu|\,\|f\|_{\mathcal Y^0},
 \quad f\in\mathcal Y^1.
\end{equation}
Consequently the entire functional is represented by a weighted
$L^2$ field. The weight is dual to that of $\mathcal Y^0$ and
is equivalent to the ordinary weight on compact sets. We have
not imposed a vanishing adjoint end coefficient: the endpoint
source is still present in this identity.

The separation argument in Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation}
now applies without a finite evaluation set. More explicitly,
write $\lambda=(\phi,\beta,z)$. For a scalar target $f$,
$D_2^{-1}(f,0,0)$ has zero physical derivative, hence is
infinitesimally gauge by regularity and index zero.
Gauge invariance of the endpoint classes gives $\phi(f)=0$.
Holonomy annihilation gives $\beta|_{X_c}=0$. Since all holonomy
outputs lie in $X_c$, compact interior connection tests give
$d_A^{+,*}\beta=0$; elliptic regularity and unique continuation
give $\beta=0$ throughout the gauge interior.

Every compact domain test has zero endpoint derivative. Near a
finite seam arc the adjoint equation is therefore homogeneous.
Equation~\eqref{eq:end-adjoint-ltwo} supplies its $L^2$ hypothesis
in \cite[Theorem~5.9(ii)]{DaeFukLip21b}, and higher regularity
gives the smooth adjoint matching condition. The zero gauge trace
forces the map adjoint trace to vanish. Zero extension across the
arc and Cauchy--Riemann unique continuation give $z=0$ in the
connected map interior. This includes a constant residual map.
Thus the representing $L^2$ field is zero almost everywhere,
so $\lambda=0$. There is no additional boundary or infinity
functional on the unrestricted weighted $L^2$ target.
Surjectivity of $K_\partial$ gives $\nu=0$.
Invertibility of $D_2$ reduces the range question to the finite
endpoint target; hence finitely many directions suffice.
Their solution variations are smooth by mixed regularity, as
in Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation}.

We use an elementary consequence of the Lagrangian condition:
if $L$ is a smooth Lagrangian in a K\"ahler manifold $M$ of
complex dimension $n$, and $Z\subset M$ is a proper closed
complex analytic subset, then $L\setminus Z$ is open dense.
To prove density, suppose a nonempty open subset of $L$ lies in
$Z$. In a coordinate neighborhood use a finite complex
stratification and its closed filtration by dimension.
Remove the closed lower-dimensional part whenever the open subset
meets its complement; otherwise descend the filtration.
This produces a nonempty open subset of $L$ contained in a
single smooth complex stratum $S$. Then $TL\subset TS$ is an
isotropic subspace of the symplectic space $(TS,\omega|_S)$.
Its dimension is $n$, whereas an isotropic subspace of $TS$
has dimension at most $\dim_\CC S<n$, a contradiction.
Closedness gives openness. No real analyticity of $L$ or
transversality of $L$ to $Z$ is required. Applying this with
the standard complex structure $J_*$ gives the open dense set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:good-mixed-end-values}
             (L_+\setminus Z_g)\times(L_-\setminus Z_g).
\end{equation}

Start at the given regular perturbation $\eta_0$. The residual
set in the specified range is finite. Choose the finite union
of the endpoint directions just obtained. For small parameters,
each residual continues uniquely by the implicit function
theorem, and each of its endpoint maps is a submersion.
Fixed-family index-zero compactness excludes additional
residuals, as in Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation}.
The inverse image of \eqref{eq:good-mixed-end-values} under
each endpoint map is open dense, since a submersion is open.
The finite intersection of these sets is open dense. Choose a
parameter $\eta_1$ in it, arbitrarily close to $\eta_0$.
Joint submersion for the endpoints of distinct residuals
is unnecessary.

Write $\overline{\RR}=[-\infty,+\infty]$. The completed
seam map $\bar e_x$ depends continuously on $x$ as a map
to $C^0(\overline{\RR},M_g)$ in a high-order weighted
configuration chart. Indeed, the exact end coefficients
control $q_\pm(x)$. The weighted $H^2$ norm of the decaying
map remainder controls its supremum on each unit half-strip,
with a uniform Sobolev constant after translation. A bounded
set in an end chart therefore satisfies, for $t\geq t_0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform-residual-seam-tail}
 \sup_{s\in[t,t+1]}d(e_x(s),q_+(x))\leq C e^{-\delta t},
\end{equation}
and the analogous negative-end estimate. The reference
solution converges exponentially by
\cite[Proposition~5.29]{DaeFukLip21a}; smooth end chart
constants are absorbed in $C$. Compactly supported secondary
perturbations do not change the equation sufficiently far out
on either end. Ordinary $H^2$ embedding treats the compact part.

There is consequently a common $S$ such that the tails
$|s|\geq S$ of all residuals at $\eta_1$ have positive
distance from $Z_g$. Continuity of the completed seam maps
gives configuration neighborhoods on which these same tails
retain a smaller positive margin. These are neighborhoods in
configuration space, chosen before the next finite family.

Apply Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation} to $[-S,S]$ at
$\eta_1$ and adjoin its finitely many directions. Shrink the
parameter ball so that all continued residuals stay in the
preceding neighborhoods; compactness again excludes additional
residuals. Generic parameters make every residual path on
$[-S,S]$ transverse to every stratum of $Z_g$, also at the
interval endpoints. The strata have real codimension at least
two, so every path avoids them. The tails already avoid $Z_g$.
The union of the two finite families has compact support and
dependency disjoint from the fixed matching collar, and its
coefficients can retain the required energy bound.

For the final perturbation $\eta_2$, the finite union of the
completed seam images is compact and disjoint from the closed
set $Z_g$. If both are nonempty, this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:global-residual-separation}
 \inf_{\substack{x\in\cM^{\eta_2}_0\\
                  s\in\overline{\RR}}}
                  d(\bar e_x(s),Z_g)>0 .
\end{equation}
The same configuration continuity, regularity and index-zero
compactness preserve avoidance under sufficiently small changes
in a subsequent fixed finite family. This proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

In genus two, Proposition~\ref{prop:very-stable-branches}
now gives uniform local inverse bounds over all the completed
residual seam images. Corollary~\ref{cor:very-stable-converse}
excludes special limiting lines at every finite attaching center,
retaining the other hypotheses on the full normalized primitive.
The separation also holds at the limiting mixed-end values.
It does not give a gluing chart for a center escaping down an end.

The relative parameter problem remains distinct. Density of the
complement in a Lagrangian need not permit paths between prescribed
endpoints: locally, for $L=\RR^n\subset\CC^n$ and
$Z=\{z_1=0\}$, a path in $L$ changing the sign of its first
coordinate must meet $Z$. Moreover, seam evaluation for a
one-parameter residual family has a two-dimensional domain,
so transversality to real codimension two does not imply
avoidance. These observations limit the density argument;
they assert no obstruction for a particular DFL family.
When $g=1$, the target is a point and evaluation has no role;
the excess-incidence problem remains separate.

\paragraph{Regular parameterized residual problems.}
The evaluation argument also applies when the linearized operator
has a kernel. We keep the primary perturbations and Floer
generators fixed. Let $P$ be a finite-dimensional parameter
manifold for the secondary equation data, with compact output
and dependency away from the matching collar. At a solution over
$p\in P$, write the full parameterized derivative as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:parameterized-residual-operator}
 T_j:\mathcal V^j=\mathcal E^j\oplus T_pP\longrightarrow
       \mathcal Y^{j-1},\qquad
 T_j(\xi,\tau)=D_j\xi+R\tau,\qquad j=1,2.
\end{equation}
The parameter columns $R\tau$ are smooth equation fields.
Regularity of the parameterized problem means that $T_2$ is onto;
it does not require $D_2$ to be onto.

\begin{proposition}[evaluation for a regular parameterized problem]
\label{prop:parameterized-residual-evaluation}
Suppose $T_2$ in \eqref{eq:parameterized-residual-operator}
is onto. Let $K$ be joint configuration evaluation at finitely
many distinct finite seam points, optionally including either
or both mixed-end values. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:parameterized-augmented-evaluation}
 (v,\dot\eta)\longmapsto(T_2v+P_x\dot\eta,Kv)
\end{equation}
is onto, using finitely many permitted additional holonomy
directions. Its finite-point targets are tangent spaces to
$M_g$ and its endpoint targets are tangent spaces to $L_\pm$.
Here $P_x$ denotes equation variation, not the parameter manifold.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The operators $T_j$ are finite-rank extensions of the Fredholm
operators $D_j$. If $T_1(\xi,\tau)=0$, mixed regularity applied
to $D_1\xi=-R\tau$ gives $\xi\in\mathcal E^2$, and then
smoothness. This uses the linear holonomy bounds in
\eqref{eq:linear-holonomy-transport} when applying the
unperturbed regularity theorem. Thus
$H=\ker T_1=\ker T_2$ is finite dimensional. The closed image
of $T_1$ contains the dense subspace $\mathcal Y^1$, since
$T_2$ is onto, so $T_1$ is onto.

Choose continuous linear observations
$\ell:\mathcal V^1\to\RR^{\dim H}$ restricting to an
isomorphism on $H$. Compactly supported field observations
together with observations of $\tau$ suffice: they separate
every nonzero smooth vector, hence finitely many separate $H$.
The operators $(T_j,\ell)$ are isomorphisms. Their right
inverses with zero observation are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:compatible-parameterized-inverses}
 Q_jf=(T_j,\ell)^{-1}(f,0),\qquad
 T_jQ_j=\id,\qquad
 Q_2f=Q_1f\quad(f\in\mathcal Y^1),\qquad
 \|Q_1f\|_{\mathcal V^1}\leq C\|f\|_{\mathcal Y^0}.
\end{equation}
This construction also gives local smooth choices with uniform
bounds on compact regular families.

An annihilator $(\lambda,\nu)$ of
\eqref{eq:parameterized-augmented-evaluation} satisfies
\[
 \lambda(T_2v)+\nu(Kv)=0,\qquad
 \lambda(P_x\dot\eta)=0.
\]
In particular $\nu K$ vanishes on $H$. Let $Z$ be the finite
union of evaluation surfaces and map boundary points.
For a compact domain-preserving cutoff $\chi$ zero near $Z$,
put $C_\chi(\xi,\tau)=(\chi\xi,0)$. Then $KC_\chi=0$,
including its endpoint components, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:parameterized-cutoff-commutator}
 (TC_\chi-\chi T)(\xi,\tau)
             =[D,\chi]\xi-\chi R\tau .
\end{equation}
Both terms map $\mathcal V^1$ to $\mathcal Y^1$.
Consequently
\[
 (\chi\lambda)(f)
       =-\lambda((TC_\chi-\chi T)Q_2f),\qquad
 |(\chi\lambda)(f)|\leq C_\chi
      \|\lambda\|_{(\mathcal Y^1)^*}\|f\|_{\mathcal Y^0}.
\]
This is the local $L^2$ representation used in
Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation}. If only endpoints
are evaluated, $K$ is bounded on $\mathcal V^1$ and
$\lambda(f)=-\nu KQ_1f$ gives the global representation directly.

The scalar gauge argument must retain the kernel. For a scalar
target $f$, put $v=Q_2(f,0,0)$. Its combined physical
linearization is zero. Coulomb splitting gives
$v=v_C+v_G$, where $v_G$ is infinitesimally gauge with zero
parameter component and $v_C$ has zero gauge row.
Gauge equivariance gives zero physical rows for $T_2v_G$,
so $T_2v_C=0$. Therefore $\nu Kv_C=0$ and $Kv_G=0$.
The annihilator identity gives $\lambda(f,0,0)=0$.
Thus the scalar component vanishes without asserting that
every physical kernel vector is gauge.

The separation and continuation proof of
Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation} now applies to tests
with parameter component zero. Holonomy annihilation gives
$\beta|_{X_c}=0$; removing all holonomy outputs gives
$d_A^{+,*}\beta=0$ in the interior, hence $\beta=0$.
The local $L^2$ adjoint theorem supplies matching regularity
on an arc off $Z$. Zero gauge trace, zero extension and
Cauchy--Riemann continuation give zero map adjoint.
The parameter derivative of the almost complex structure,
if present, is a column of $R$ and does not occur in these
tests. Boundary functionals off $Z$ are excluded by the
local $L^2$ representation. The logarithmic capacity
argument \eqref{eq:evaluation-capacity-cutoff}, with finitely
many cutoffs, removes the remaining source on $Z$.
Compact smooth tests up to the boundary are dense in the
weighted $\mathcal Y^1$ target; it has no exact critical
coefficients and supports no separate functional at infinity.
Thus $\lambda=0$.

Configuration evaluation $K$ is onto. Prescribe its finite
values using disjoint compact matching variations. Prescribe
the exact end coefficients independently and correct their
finite values by these compact variations. Hence $\nu=0$.
Since $T_2$ is onto, subtracting its right inverse reduces
the range question to the finite evaluation factor, including
$K(H)$; its range is a finite-dimensional subspace and is
closed. Trivial annihilator proves surjectivity, and finitely
many added directions suffice. Higher regularity identifies
the same derivatives in smooth universal moduli charts.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[relative evaluation on a compact regular family]
\label{cor:relative-residual-evaluation}
Let $\cC$ be a compact regular moduli space of parameterized
residuals over a compact parameter manifold $P$, with boundary
coming from $\partial P$ and with regular restricted problems
there. Let $A$ be a prescribed closed collar of $\partial P$.
Suppose all mixed-end values of $\cC$ avoid $Z_g$, and seam
evaluation is transverse to a complex Whitney stratification of
$Z_g$ over $A$, including the boundary restriction.
Then arbitrarily small finite secondary perturbations depending
smoothly on $p$, unchanged over $A$, give transversality on the
whole seam of the continued family $\cC_a$. The mixed-end
values and both sufficiently long tails still avoid $Z_g$.
This concerns the deformation in a neighborhood of $\cC$.

If $\dim\cC=1$, the exceptional inverse image is finite and
interior for $g=2,3$, and empty for $g\geq4$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
A compact regular zero set has a uniform implicit-function
neighborhood. Choose a smooth complement to its tangent bundle
in the configuration and parameter space. The equation
derivative on that complement is an isomorphism; the implicit
function theorem solves for a small normal section uniformly
over $\cC$. Thus its continued zero sets are compact manifolds
$\cC_a$ diffeomorphic to $\cC$. They agree over $A$, where
the equation is unchanged. The construction at $\partial P$
uses the restricted charts and is fixed on its collar.
This is a local deformation assertion, not a compactness
assertion for additional components of the full moduli space.

Weighted $H^2$ embedding and the exact end coefficients make
completed seam evaluation continuous on
$\cC\times\overline{\RR}$. Compactness and the endpoint
hypothesis give a common $S$ and configuration neighborhoods
on which the tails $|s|\geq S$ stay away from $Z_g$.
For a point over $p\notin A$ and $s\in[-S,S]$, multiply
the directions in
Proposition~\ref{prop:parameterized-residual-evaluation}
by a smooth parameter function supported outside $A$ and
equal to one near $p$. The evaluation derivative there
is unchanged. Transversality on the prescribed collar extends
to an open neighborhood of it and persists on a smaller such
neighborhood for small deformations. This uses compactness
and Whitney condition~(a). The remaining compact set projects
outside $A$. A finite cover of that set
supplies finitely many directions making the universal
evaluation a submersion there. The fixed collar remains
transverse. Whitney condition~(a) and compactness preserve
this simultaneous transversality near its boundary.

Parametric Sard for the finitely many strata gives the
assertion on $\cC_a\times[-S,S]$, including its boundary
faces. The tails retain avoidance. All spatial output and
dependency remain compact and away from the matching collar,
and the parameter coefficients vanish on $A$.

If $\dim\cC=1$, the evaluation domain has dimension two.
For $g=2,3$, the top exceptional strata have real codimension
two and their inverse images have dimension zero. Lower
complex strata have real codimension at least four and are
missed. The fixed boundary restriction has dimension one
and also misses $Z_g$. The closed exceptional set and
tail avoidance make the inverse image compact, hence finite
and interior. For $g\geq4$ its minimal real codimension is
at least four, so the whole inverse image is empty.
\end{proof}

The endpoint hypothesis holds automatically for small
continuations around the perturbation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:global-residual-avoidance}. In any
further fixed finite family, take a sufficiently small
parameter ball $U$. The index-zero residuals are finitely
many smooth branches $x_j(a)$, and their completed seam
images stay away from $Z_g$. Smooth compact parameter
families in this ball therefore satisfy the corollary;
their entire residual seams already avoid $Z_g$.
The point-interpolation parameter $t$ in
\eqref{eq:point-cuts} varies the incidence data, not the
residual equation. Its residual evaluation images are
unchanged, for all $t$.

In genus two, pull back the degree-four cover in
Proposition~\ref{prop:very-stable-branches} along
$U\times\overline{\RR}\to M_2\setminus B$ for each residual
branch. This contractible domain gives four continuous
branches after labeling one fiber, smooth at finite seam
points. A smaller closed ball gives uniform local inverse
bounds by compactness. This is a labeling on the specified
parameter domain, not on all of $M_2\setminus B$. No
differentiability in a compactified seam coordinate or at
bubble scale zero follows.


\subsection{Seam sections and a relative local problem}
\label{sec:local-seam-incidence}

The following local constructions use the proved finite-center
comparisons and the chosen interpolating sections. Their limiting
relative class retains the actual universal fiber at the seam.
The subsequent determinant comparison and global boundary
argument identify this local count with its contribution
to the point homotopy.

\begin{proposition}[relative seam-section model]
\label{prop:relative-seam-model}
Fix a regular residual $x_0=(A_0,u_0)$ in the setting of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-center-low-genus-coverage}.
Use the section construction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-end-exclusion}, with an independent
pair at the point $M_1$ in genus one. Its compact-middle holonomy
loops and paths may be chosen in the interior gauge region.
There is a fixed compact negative insertion interval $I$ such
that every finite-center point-cut degeneration has its center
at the interpolation crossing, bounded ratio $s=-c/\rho$,
and insertion limit in $I$.

Let $P$ be the elliptic Jacobian in genus one, or one of the
four normalized two-spheres in genus two. In a fixed attaching
unitary frame put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:relative-seam-pair}
 S(E,s)=\widetilde\sigma^S(\beta_E(s),q),\qquad
 S_\infty=\widetilde\sigma^S(\alpha,q),
\end{equation}
where $\beta_E$ is the base-flat negative Dirichlet surface
connection and the tilde denotes the equivariant universal
surface section. The actual section pair converges, in the
residual point frame, to a pair of the form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:relative-local-pair}
 H(E,s,t)=R(t)+b(t)\bigl(S(E,s)-S_\infty\bigr)
                 \quad\text{on }P\times S^1\times I.
\end{equation}
Here $R$ is the independent residual pair, $b=0$ near the
far end of $I$, and $b=1$, $R=S_\infty$ near its seam end.
The pair defines a relative positive-$p_1$ degeneracy class
with compact support away from the independent boundary data.
Allowed compact section variations make its model degeneracy
locus an oriented compact zero-manifold.
On compact regular gluing patches the convergence is uniform
in $C^0$ after $c=-\rho s$. At a regular positive scale,
local intersection numbers equal the model local degrees
when oriented by these gluing coordinates.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Noncommuting loops and their connecting paths can be moved
slightly into the interior, retaining the finite spanning
property used in Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-incidence}.
They then have a fixed positive distance from the seam.
On a tube around a compact middle interval the mixed pair is
\[
 a(t)\sigma^G(A_t)
   +b(t)P_A(0,t)\widetilde\sigma^S(\beta(0,0),q)+K(A,t),
\]
where the first term is supported in an incoming product
neighborhood and $K$ is a finite interior holonomy sum.
The two end collars have the prescribed gauge pair and
the exact seam-transport pair respectively.

Choose a flat attaching representative $\alpha$ on a fixed
Hermitian bundle $V$. The negative Dirichlet family is smooth
on $P$, including negative rank drop; in genus two it also
extends through the positive pole by
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-negative-collapse}.
At that pole it is constant $\alpha$.
Its seam connection is smooth on the compactified real line,
with value $\alpha$ at infinity and uniform $O((1+|s|)^{-1})$
decay. Thus \eqref{eq:relative-seam-pair} is a smooth independent
pair in the fixed fiber $(\operatorname{ad}V)_q\otimes\mathbb C$.
It equals $S_\infty$ at seam infinity and, in genus two,
on the positive-pole circle. Its independence uses the entire
line in genus two and the unique class in genus one. The
equivariant fiber identification is part of the formula.

For an actual sequence with compact regular primitive limit
$E$ and $-c_\nu/\rho_\nu\to s$, the inner connection comparison
gives $\beta_\nu(0,0)\to\beta_E(s)$ in every fixed surface order.
Retain the joining gauge's constant surface factor and the
convergent attaching representative. This compares the full
point fiber; a residual stabilizer acts trivially on its adjoint.
Normal transport to $t\in I$ converges to the residual transport:
the physical base coefficient on the shrinking disc is $O(r)$
by \eqref{eq:converse-large-axial}, also after joining, so its
segment integral is $O(r^2)$. On the rest of the segment use
the diagonal exterior convergence and the unitary transport ODE.
The fixed interior holonomies converge to their residual values.
Transporting the point fiber back by $P_{A_0}(0,t)$ now gives
\eqref{eq:relative-local-pair}. On a compact gluing patch the
evaluated bundle on $I$ is identified with the residual bundle
outside the pregluing disc; the nonlinear correction is on
that same bundle. This gives a continuous point-fiber
identification in which the estimates are uniform on the patch.
The converse parameter estimate
identifies current and observation-normalized ratios in this
limit; no derivative at $\rho=0$ is used.

If the finite atom center differs from the crossing, every
dependency just listed is separated from it. The limiting
pair is then the independent residual pair. If the center
tends to zero but $|c_\nu|/\rho_\nu\to\infty$, enlarge the
outer radius to include the crossing in a smaller relative
disc. The original negative comparison in
\eqref{eq:converse-large-axial} and the uniform primitive tail
give $\beta_\nu(0,0)\to\alpha$. The same transport and
holonomy argument again gives the residual pair.
The positive pole is excluded by
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-end-exclusion}.
These arguments prove center and normalized-parameter localization.
The short negative seam collar is independent by exact transport.
Positive marked values near zero lie near the limiting line
inside the enlarged retained disc, by the actual line estimate,
or near the residual value in the diagonal exterior region.
In genus one the stable replacement is uniformly constant in
this limit. Finite marks away from zero use smooth convergence;
insertion infinity uses the actual uniform end convergence.
Thus the remaining insertion lies in the compact middle $I$.

On $Z=P\times S^1\times I$, the pair $H$ is independent
near both $t$ boundaries and on $P\times\{\infty\}\times I$;
in genus two it is also independent on the positive-pole
cylinder. Compactness gives a closed neighborhood $A$ of
these subsets with independent values. The rank-one
degeneracy condition defines its relative class in
$H^4(Z,A;\mathbb Z)$ with DFL's coorientation.

To make the model transverse, take finitely many surface
universal sections spanning the point fiber along the compact
line image, or at $M_1$. Extend them by the NS retraction
used in \eqref{eq:positive-end-seam-sections} and transport
them normally. Multiply by a $t$ cutoff vanishing on the
fixed end collars and positive on the possible degeneracy
region. Two independent rows of complex coefficients span
the pair fiber at every such model point. Small coefficients
preserve the margins on $A$ and the residual pair.
Parametric Sard gives a transverse rank-one locus of dimension
zero and no rank-zero points. These variations are allowed
compact mixed sections and preserve the two end pairs.
They may be chosen generic simultaneously on countably
many regular positive-scale gluing charts.

Equation~\eqref{eq:relative-local-pair} describes the initial
representative. These added terms have their corresponding
seam-section limits by the same convergence argument.
They give a transverse representative of the same relative
class, since the whole small homotopy stays independent on $A$.

Finally take a small four-ball about an isolated transverse
model zero, with independent pair on its boundary. Uniform
$C^0$ convergence makes the straight boundary homotopy to
the actual small-scale pair independent. At a regular scale
its intersection number in the ball equals the model degree.
This uses the chosen local coordinate orientation; comparison
with the physical determinant orientation remains separate.
\end{proof}

Polar orthonormalization of the seam pair, followed by its
determinant-one third column, defines a map
$\Gamma:P\times S^1\to SU(3)$, constant on
$P\times\{\infty\}$. In genus two it is also constant
on the positive-pole circle and therefore factors through
$S^2\wedge S^1\cong S^3$.
The fibration $SU(2)\to SU(3)\to S^5$ gives
$\pi_3(SU(3))\cong\mathbb Z$.
The usual complex generator is represented by the pair
$(e_1,z_1e_2+z_2e_3)$ on a four-ball, with one transverse
rank-one degeneracy. Thus the relative number above is
the obstruction of this specific seam frame, with DFL's
coorientation sign. Equivalently, clutch trivial rank-three
bundles over $P\times D^2$ by $\Gamma$ to obtain
$V_\Gamma\to P\times S^2$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:seam-clutch-relative-number}
 \#D_H=\pm\langle c_2(V_\Gamma),[P\times S^2]\rangle.
\end{equation}
The sign records factor order, boundary orientation and DFL's
opposite $c_2$ convention. The far insertion boundary has a
constant pair and contributes zero. This identifies an actual
relative boundary obstruction; it does not assign multiplicity
one to a genus-two branch merely because that branch is a plane.

\begin{proposition}[genus-one seam obstruction]
\label{prop:torus-seam-obstruction}
For the genus-one relative seam model in
Proposition~\ref{prop:relative-seam-model},
\[
                 |\langle c_2(V_\Gamma),[J\times S^2]\rangle|=8.
\]
This is the absolute integral relative obstruction. Its sign
in the physical DFL boundary formula requires the remaining
orientation comparison.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $V$ be the fixed stable rank-two degree-one elliptic
attaching bundle and $\mathscr P$ the normalized Poincar\'e
line over $\Sigma\times J$. For every $L\in J$,
$H^1(VL^{-1})=0$ by duality and stability of the negative-slope
bundle $V^*L$. Riemann--Roch gives $h^0(VL^{-1})=1$.
Thus $T=\pi_*(V\otimes\mathscr P^{-1})$ is a line bundle,
and evaluation gives the universal subbundle
\[
                  G=\mathscr P\otimes\pi^*T
                            \subset\operatorname{pr}_\Sigma^*V.
\]
It is nowhere zero: a zero of a fiber inclusion would give
a positive-degree saturated subline in $V$.
With $\int_J\theta=1$, the usual Poincar\'e normalization
has $\pi_*(c_1(\mathscr P)^2)=-2\theta$.
Since $\operatorname{Td}(\Sigma)=1$ and
$\operatorname{ch}(V)=2+\omega_\Sigma$, the direct
Grothendieck--Riemann--Roch calculation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:elliptic-evaluated-subline}
 \operatorname{ch}(T)
  =\pi_*\bigl((2+\omega_\Sigma)e^{-c_1(\mathscr P)}\bigr)
  =1-2\theta,\qquad c_1(G_q)=-2\theta.
\end{equation}
The restriction $\mathscr P_q$ is topologically trivial.
In particular $L\mapsto G_{L,q}\in\mathbb P(V_q)$ has
degree two. No torsion quotient of the retained Jacobian
subline parameter is taken.

Let $P_L$ be orthogonal projection onto $G_L$ in the NS
metric. For the normalized modification point $-i$, the
actual base-flat Dirichlet identification is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:elliptic-seam-gauge}
 S_L(w)=(I-P_L)+\frac{w+i}{w-i}P_L,\qquad
                         K_L=S_L^*H_{\mathrm{NS}}.
\end{equation}
It is nonsingular in the inverse-transform bundle frame,
as in \eqref{eq:converse-blaschke-metric}.
On the real seam it is unitary. Hence in its base-parallel
target frame $\beta_L(s)=\alpha^{S_L(s)}$, and equivariance
gives the pair $\operatorname{Ad}_{S_L(s,q)}\sigma_0$.
Polar orthonormalization commutes with this unitary action,
so $\Gamma$ is its complexified adjoint clutching map, up
to a constant change of frame.

Choose the sphere orientation so the scalar clutching
factor gives $\mathcal O(1)$, with positive class $h$.
The rank-two point clutching bundle is
\[
        U=(G_q\boxtimes\mathcal O(1))\oplus
                                   (G_q^\perp\boxtimes\mathcal O).
\]
For $g=c_1(G_q)=-2\theta$, its complement has class $-g$,
and $g^2=h^2=0$. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:elliptic-adjoint-clutch}
 c_1(U)=h,\quad c_2(U)=-gh,\quad
 c_2(\operatorname{End}_0U)=4c_2(U)-c_1(U)^2
                                           =8\theta h.
\end{equation}
The bundle $V_\Gamma$ is $\operatorname{End}_0U$ by the
actual seam formula. Integration gives eight in this
clutching orientation. Reversing the seam orientation
reverses its sign, so the asserted absolute value is eight.
The physical relation $s=-c/\rho$, DFL's coorientation
and the mixed determinant orientation have not been
combined here.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[genus-two seam obstruction]
\label{prop:genus-two-seam-obstruction}
Fix a very stable genus-two attaching value and one of its
four normalized coefficient spheres $P$.
Orient $P$ by the complex normal extension coordinate $\xi$
at its positive pole, and orient the base sphere by its complex
coordinate $w$. With clutching convention $x_-=\Gamma x_+$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:genus-two-seam-degree}
             \langle c_2(V_\Gamma),[P\times S^2]\rangle=1.
\end{equation}
This convention is intrinsic on all four branches, so their
total is four. In particular each branch has absolute
relative obstruction one. The physical DFL orientation is
not asserted to be this product orientation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix the attaching subline $L$, put $Q=\Lambda L^{-1}$, and
choose an orthonormal basis $v,v_2$ of $H^1(LQ^{-1})$ with
$v$ representing the attaching extension. The actual normalized
family has
\[
       \tau^2+|\xi|^2=1,\qquad
       e_{\tau,\xi}(w)=(w+i\tau)v+\xi v_2.
\]
Fixed surface cocycles for $v,v_2$ construct the smooth
parameter family
\begin{equation}\label{eq:genus-two-raw-point-family}
 0\longrightarrow \mathcal O_w(1)\boxtimes L
   \longrightarrow\mathcal E
   \longrightarrow\operatorname{pr}_\Sigma^*Q
   \longrightarrow0
       \quad\text{over }P\times\mathbb P^1_w\times\Sigma.
\end{equation}
Its coefficient at infinity is the fixed $v$. There is no
additional line from $P$ in this family.

Every nonzero surface extension is stable. Its only split
fiber in the positive region $P\times D_+$ is
$z_+=(\tau=-1,\xi=0,w=i)$; the other zero lies in
the negative region. The positive classifying map is
$[w+i\tau:\xi]$ into the fixed extension line.
Near $z_+$, use the coordinates $r=w+i\tau(\xi),\xi$.
They preserve the chosen product orientation, and the
extension class is exactly $rv+\xi v_2$.
Blow up this point in these coordinates:
\[
        \pi:\widetilde M\longrightarrow
                   M=P\times\mathbb P^1_w,\qquad
        D=\pi^{-1}(z_+).
\]
The normal line of $D$ is $\mathcal O_D(-1)$, so $D^2=-1$.
The negative region and seam are unchanged.

The pulled-back extension splits on $D\times\Sigma$.
Take its elementary transform using projection to the
$L$ summand there. In a blowup chart write $r=tR,\xi=tX$.
The original upper-right surface transition coefficient
is $t(Rv_{ij}+Xv_{2,ij})$. The new frame
$(t e_L,e_Q)$ replaces it by $Rv_{ij}+Xv_{2,ij}$.
This proves local freeness and the exact sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:genus-two-resolved-point-family}
 0\longrightarrow
   \bigl(\pi^*\mathcal O_w(1)\otimes\mathcal O_{\widetilde M}(-D)\bigr)
                                         \boxtimes L
   \longrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal E}
   \longrightarrow\operatorname{pr}_\Sigma^*Q
   \longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
On $D$ the class is $[R:X]$, hence is stable. The positive
classifying map therefore extends over its resolved region
as a map $\widehat f$ to the same extension line. The
adjoint of this stable family is the pullback of the
universal adjoint; any base-line ambiguity has disappeared.

The chosen symplectic pair is independent on the whole
extension line. Its polar completion is a global complex
frame of the universal adjoint point bundle there.
Pull it back by $\widehat f$ to frame
$\operatorname{End}_0\widehat{\mathcal E}_q$ on the resolved
positive region. This is a complex frame, not a real
$SO(3)$ frame: the exceptional restriction has nontrivial
real $w_2$, while its complexification
$\mathcal O(1)\oplus\mathcal O\oplus\mathcal O(-1)$ is trivial.

On the negative region the family is unchanged, and its
base-flat Dirichlet metric gives the attaching point frame
used in \eqref{eq:relative-seam-pair}. On the seam the
coordinates of the positive frame in the negative frame
are exactly $\Gamma$. In the chosen positive frame the
complex bundle descends to a trivial bundle on
$P\times D_+$. Thus, with the stated clutching convention,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:resolved-bundle-seam-clutch}
       \operatorname{End}_0\widehat{\mathcal E}_q
                                      \cong\pi^*V_\Gamma.
\end{equation}
This is a complex-bundle comparison. It neither descends
the real adjoint across $D$ nor constructs an analytic
positive nodal collar.

Put $h=\pi^*c_1(\mathcal O_w(1))$ and $d=\operatorname{PD}[D]$.
At the fixed point $q$, the spaces $L_q,Q_q$ are constant.
Equation~\eqref{eq:genus-two-resolved-point-family} gives
$c_1(\widehat{\mathcal E}_q)=h-d$ and
$c_2(\widehat{\mathcal E}_q)=0$. Furthermore $h^2=0$,
$hd=0$ since the base projection is constant on $D$,
and $\int_{\widetilde M}d^2=-1$.
The degree-one blowdown and
\eqref{eq:resolved-bundle-seam-clutch} now give
\[
 \langle c_2(V_\Gamma),[P\times S^2]\rangle
   =\int_{\widetilde M}
       \bigl(4c_2(\widehat{\mathcal E}_q)
                        -c_1(\widehat{\mathcal E}_q)^2\bigr)
   =-\int_{\widetilde M}(h-d)^2=1.
\]
A change of complex extension frame preserves the positive-pole
orientation. Changing the independent pair changes its completed
frame by a map $S^2\to SU(3)$, which is null-homotopic.
Thus the number is independent of these choices and applies
with the same convention on all four branches.
\end{proof}

The compact section variations can also be chosen transverse on
the negative-pole cylinder $\{(\tau,\xi)=(1,0)\}\times S^1\times I$.
Its dimension is two, below the rank-one codimension four, and
the same coefficient variations span. Its cutdown is therefore
empty, with an independent neighborhood by compactness.
For this refinement all genus-two model cut points lie in the
full-rank primitive locus away from both poles. The actual section
limits exclude finite-center cut sequences approaching the negative
pole as well. Genus one retains its entire rank-drop Jacobian family.

The orientation in Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-seam-obstruction}
is opposite to the complex stereographic coordinate
$\zeta=\xi/(1+\tau)$ at the negative pole. Together with
$s=-c/\rho$ and DFL's positive-$p_1$ coorientation, this must
still be compared with the physical mixed determinant.
The following proposition assembles the local degrees into an
actual truncation at the covered finite-center primitive ends.

\begin{proposition}[finite-center truncation]
\label{prop:finite-center-truncation}
Retain the fixed equation, residual and physical hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-center-low-genus-coverage}, with the
transverse section system above. There are finitely many disjoint
positive-scale gluing neighborhoods of the model zeros, with
independent side boundaries, which exhaust the stated finite-center
primitive cut degenerations. They admit common regular scale
truncations. Write $n(x_0)=\pm1$ for the sign of the residual.
With outward normal pointing toward the removed smaller scales,
their total artificial boundary count is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-center-signed-boundary}
 B_{\mathrm{finite}}(x_0)=
 \begin{cases}
  8n(x_0)\eta_1(x_0),&g=1,\\
  n(x_0)\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^4\eta_{2,j}(x_0),&g=2.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
Here $\eta_1$ and each $\eta_{2,j}$ are constant signs on
the corresponding connected primitive branch. They compare the
physical DFL orientation with the ordered local parameters
$(P,c,\rho)$, after factoring out $n(x_0)$.
Their values and agreement across branches and residuals
are not asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Orient $P=J$ complexly in genus one and use the positive-pole
orientation in genus two. With complex base orientation and
clutching convention $x_-=\Gamma x_+$, put
$k(P)=\langle c_2(V_\Gamma),[P\times S^2]\rangle$.
Then $k(J)=8$ and $k(P_j)=1$. For genus one this sign is
fixed by the actual inverse transform at $-i$: its determinant
transition is the Blaschke factor and its base determinant is
$\mathcal O(1)$.

Give the model the orientation $o_Z=o_P\wedge ds\wedge dt$.
Cap its constant far insertion boundary inside the negative
hemisphere, with $t$ increasing toward the seam. The oriented
seam boundary of that hemisphere has decreasing $s$, so the
annular orientation is $ds\wedge dt$. The independent positive
frame has transition $\Gamma$ on the seam. The ordinary
complex degeneracy count is therefore $k(P)$, and DFL's
positive-$p_1$ count is $-k(P)$.

First check orientation compatibility of local gluing charts.
A smaller compact common regular primitive patch lies in both
constructed images at all sufficiently small scales: otherwise
a sequence of constructed solutions with its proved regular
primitive limit would contradict the physical regular-sector
converse. This does not use a positive nodal collar.
The transition estimates give
\[
 \theta'=\psi(\theta)+o(1),\qquad
 c'-c=\rho\,o(1),\qquad \rho'/\rho=1+o(1).
\]
In $c=\delta b$, $\rho=\delta e^h$ they converge uniformly
on compact sets to $(\psi(\theta),b,h)$ as $\delta\to0$.
This limiting map preserves $o_P\wedge db\wedge dh$.
The boundary homotopy on a small four-ball shows that the
smooth positive-scale transition has degree $+1$.
Thus it preserves $o_P\wedge dc\wedge d\rho$; no derivative
limit at zero is required.

On any such chart define $\eta$ by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-center-orientation-sign}
 o_{\mathrm{DFL},\mathcal M}
       =n(x_0)\eta\,o_P\wedge dc\wedge d\rho.
\end{equation}
Positive-scale regularity makes this sign locally constant,
and the overlap argument makes it well-defined on the
connected primitive branch. This gives the signs in the
statement. It does not identify different branches.

Choose disjoint closed four-balls $B_a$ around the finitely
many transverse model zeros in coordinates $(\theta,s,t)$.
Their boundaries have independent pair. Define the actual
neighborhoods by
\[
 (\theta,s,t,\rho)\longmapsto
          \bigl(\operatorname{Gl}_a(\theta,-\rho s,\rho),t\bigr),
                      \qquad 0<\rho\leq\epsilon.
\]
They are embeddings for positive scale. Uniform section
convergence makes their side boundaries independent for
small $\epsilon$. They are mutually disjoint after shrinking
that bound: a sequence of common configurations would force
the same full primitive limit, scale ratio tending to one,
center difference $o(\rho)$, and the same insertion limit,
contradicting disjointness of the closed model balls.

Choose a common regular $\epsilon$ for the scale functions
on the finitely many one-dimensional cuts. The slices are
compact zero-manifolds, and their local degrees equal the
model degrees by the independent boundary homotopy.
Conversely every stated finite-center cut degeneration
limits to a model zero by Proposition~\ref{prop:relative-seam-model}
and enters its neighborhood by the physical converse.
This exhausts these degenerations, without asserting
compactness of the remainder through other possible strata.

Finally, using \eqref{eq:finite-center-orientation-sign} gives
\begin{align*}
 o_{\mathcal M\times\mathbb R_t}
  &=n(x_0)\eta\,o_P\wedge dc\wedge d\rho\wedge dt\\
  &=-n(x_0)\eta\,\rho\,o_P\wedge ds\wedge d\rho\wedge dt\\
  &=n(x_0)\eta\,\rho\,d\rho\wedge o_Z .
\end{align*}
The outward normal of the remaining moduli space points
toward decreasing $\rho$, giving $-n(x_0)\eta\,o_Z$.
The incidence has even real codimension four, so it commutes
with this boundary operation without another sign.
Multiplying by the DFL model count $-k(P)$ gives
$n(x_0)\eta k(P)$. Sum over the balls and branches to obtain
\eqref{eq:finite-center-signed-boundary}.
\end{proof}

For comparison, the finite-dimensional complex orientation
of the fixed-attaching bubble fiber pulls back to
$-o_J\wedge dc\wedge d\rho$ in genus one:
its local coordinates are $(L,\kappa)$ with $\kappa=-c+i\rho$.
In genus two the physical coefficients are
$\kappa=-c+i\rho\tau$, $\chi=\rho\xi$.
At the negative pole, $d\tau=0$ and the chosen $o_P$ is
opposite to the complex $\xi$ orientation. The two reversals
cancel, giving $o_P\wedge dc\wedge d\rho$.
These coordinate orientations are compared with the mixed Fredholm
orientation in Section~\ref{sec:gluing-orientations}.
The truncation uses independent side boundaries and regular
positive-scale slices; it requires no differentiable collar at
scale zero. Section~\ref{sec:cut-compactification} supplies the
compactness and regularity needed for the global boundary identity.

\section{Orientations of the primitive gluing maps}
\label{sec:gluing-orientations}

We first construct determinant-line excision at positive scale.
The main analytic step identifies this excision with the kernel
map obtained by differentiating the corrected gluing family.
We then compare the compact primitive orientation with DFL's
coherent orientation, first on the selected genus-two component
and then with an ordinary closed insertion. The simultaneous
normalization on both matching components is established in
Section~\ref{sec:reference-comparison}.

\subsection{Excision of the mixed determinant line}
\label{sec:actual-determinant-excision}

The full-Coulomb realization used here places gauge fixing in the
target. Its ordinary source imposes the normal trace and canonical
matching conditions, but does not impose the Coulomb equation on
every input field. This distinction permits the following excision.

\begin{proposition}[positive-scale determinant excision]
\label{prop:actual-determinant-excision}
Fix the regular low-genus gluing data of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation}, with strictly positive
scale $\rho$ and attaching value $p=e_0(c)$. Let $D_{\rm gl}$ be
the ordinary mixed operator at the corrected solution and $D_0$
the residual operator, with their prescribed weighted domains.
On each sufficiently small compact regular attaching patch there is a continuous
comparison of orientation double covers
\begin{equation}\label{eq:actual-determinant-excision}
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_{\rm gl}\otimes\det T_pM_g)
 \cong
 \operatorname{Or}\bigl(\det D_0\otimes
   \det_{\mathbb R}H^1(\mathbb P^1\times\Sigma_g,\operatorname{End}_0E)
                 \bigr).
\end{equation}
It is defined by the configuration paths and cutoff rotation below,
and is unchanged under homotopies of these choices within the fixed
attaching chart and retained bundle identification. It includes
negative rank drop. No extension to $\rho=0$, identification with
the gluing-map derivative, or compatibility with DFL's coherent
orientation is asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write $C_E$ for the compact reconstructed mixed operator at the
positive scale and $C_\alpha$ for the constant compact operator
at the flat attaching representative $\alpha$.
The compact index and regularity results give
\[
 \ker C_\alpha=T_pM_g,\quad\operatorname{coker}C_\alpha=0,
 \qquad
 \ker C_E=H^1(\operatorname{End}_0E),\quad
                         \operatorname{coker}C_E=0.
\]

Multiplying the nonlinear-chart correction by a parameter in
$[0,1]$ first gives a path in the graph configuration topology.
As in the relative-index argument, smooth its positive and seam
data in the matching charts relative to smooth endpoint representatives,
retain the exact end critical manifolds, and extend the negative
connection coefficients affinely. Use this smooth configuration
path to join the corrected solution to the prescribed pregluing.
It need not consist of solutions. Along it the ordinary
Fredholm domains vary continuously: use the bounded local domain
identifications of \cite[Proposition~5.27]{DaeFukLip21b},
the retained asymptotic gaps, and the established low-order
holonomy bounds.
Orientability of the global mixed determinant line
\cite[Section~4.7]{DaeFukLip21a} makes its orientation transport
independent of the chosen smooth configuration path with these
endpoints. We do not apply ordinary operator transport directly
to the rough graph path.

In the common gluing annulus use the nonlinear matching chart
about $\alpha$. Let $z_E,z_0$ denote the primitive and residual
coordinate vectors. Replace the original interpolation by
$a z_E+b z_0$, where $a,b\geq0$, $a+b\leq1$, $a$ decreases
from one to zero before $b$ increases from zero to one,
and both vanish on an intervening open annulus. Convex
interpolation of these coordinate formulas gives a path in the
same chart. The seam equations are exactly $v=0$ and equality
of the harmonic coordinates on the two sides, so they are
preserved. Interpolate base components affinely in the retained
bundle frame; their zero normal component remains zero.

Simultaneously flatten the residual inside the outer transition
and the compact primitive toward its attaching cap. The latter
exterior remains in the chart: in $\zeta=1/w$, the base-flat
model has deviation $O(|\zeta|)$ from the attaching value, and
the capped reconstruction adds the vanishing fixed-coordinate
error from the pregluing estimates. Thus its surface and map
coordinates are small on the whole exterior of the chosen
large affine radius. The retained bundle identification is
unchanged. Choose equal product metrics on the common annulus.
All paths use smooth positive metrics and mixed configurations.

Denote the flattened operators by $D_{\rm gl}^f,D_0^f,C_E^f$.
Exchanging the inner mixed discs of $D_{\rm gl}^f\oplus C_\alpha$
gives $D_0^f\oplus C_E^f$. Their differential expressions and
canonical matching domains agree in the common constant annulus.
The equation's perturbation supports and dependencies are outside
this local unperturbed neighborhood and remain on the global exterior.

For the source rotation use
\[
 R(\vartheta)=
 \begin{pmatrix}\cos\vartheta&-\sin\vartheta\\
                 \sin\vartheta&\cos\vartheta\end{pmatrix},
\]
where $\vartheta$ is a radial base function, zero on the outer
edge and $\pi/2$ on the inner edge. Apply the same rotation
to the two gauge fields and the two positive map fields.
It is independent of the surface coordinate, and its two seam
traces agree. For the actual linearized boundary conditions,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-domain-rotation}
 \iota_{\mathbf n}(Ra)=R\iota_{\mathbf n}a=0,\qquad
 d_\alpha(Ra_\Sigma)=0,\qquad
 \Pi_\alpha(Ra_\Sigma)=\kappa_\alpha(R\nu).
\end{equation}
These are trace-space identities for the full mixed domain.
They do not require the cutoff field to solve the gauge-fixing
row. Extend the maps by the identity on the exteriors and
signed interchange on the interiors. The resulting bounded
invertible source and target maps $U_X,U_Y$ preserve the
global weights and finite exact end variables.

The equal differential expressions imply
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-commutator-homotopy}
 U_Y(D_{\rm gl}^f\oplus C_\alpha)U_X^{-1}
               =D_0^f\oplus C_E^f+K,
\end{equation}
where $K$ is a zero-order commutator supported in the common
annulus, on its four- and two-dimensional components.
Rellich compactness makes it compact from the ordinary $H^1$
mixed domain to the $L^2$ target. Hence
$D_0^f\oplus C_E^f+tK$ is Fredholm for every $0\leq t\leq1$.
Neither smallness nor surjectivity along this path is needed.

To transport orientations through possible kernel jumps, choose
a finite-dimensional target stabilization $F$ on a compact
parameter neighborhood such that $(D_t,j_t):X\oplus F\to Y$
is onto for every parameter. Cokernel representatives at one
point, openness of surjectivity, and a finite cover provide it.
Its kernels $W_t$ form a vector bundle, and
\[
 0\longrightarrow\ker D_t\longrightarrow W_t\longrightarrow F
   \longrightarrow\operatorname{coker}D_t\longrightarrow0
\]
identifies $\det D_t$ with $\det W_t\otimes(\det F)^*$.
This constructs the orientation double cover continuously
through kernel jumps. Enlarging $F$ adds cancelling factors;
homotopies give the same endpoint orientation transport.

Apply this construction to
\eqref{eq:excision-commutator-homotopy} and undo the configuration
and metric paths. It gives
\[
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_{\rm gl}\otimes\det C_\alpha)
       \cong\operatorname{Or}(\det D_0\otimes\det C_E).
\]
The compact kernel identifications prove
\eqref{eq:actual-determinant-excision}. All factors have even
real index, so reordering the displayed factors adds no
graded sign. Homotopies of the radial cutoffs, small chart
interpolations and positive metrics prove the asserted
choice compatibility. Smooth locally free bundle frames and
stable seam fibers make the same argument valid through
negative rank drop.
\end{proof}

Attaching evaluation on these regular compact kernels is onto.
Its exact sequence identifies the right-hand side of
\eqref{eq:actual-determinant-excision}, after cancelling
$\det T_pM_g$, with
$\det D_0\otimes\det_{\mathbb R}\ker d\operatorname{ev}_\infty$.
This constructs an excision comparison for the ordinary mixed
Fredholm operators. To compare it with the derivative of gluing,
we control the kernels along the compact commutator path.
Propositions~\ref{prop:excision-correction-orientation}
and~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-orientation} provide this control.
The coherent orientation convention is specified next.

\subsection{The coherent orientation normalization}
\label{sec:primitive-orientation-normalization}

DFL's gauge orientation system includes a choice of orientation
for a path $p_0$ from a reference generator to itself whose index
is $8k+4$. Reference paths from the other generators, cylinder
composition and the standard orientations for instantons on $S^4$
then determine the system
\cite[Section~2.4, pp.~21--22]{DaeFukLip21a}.
This choice affects the primitive signs even after the model
clutching numbers have been fixed.

\begin{proposition}[change of coherent orientation normalization]
\label{prop:primitive-orientation-normalization}
Keep the reference-path orientations and standard $S^4$
instanton orientations fixed, and reverse only the chosen
orientation of $p_0$. For the actual finite-center gluing
families of Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation},
the signs relative to their residuals change by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:primitive-normalization-reversal}
                 \eta'_1(x_0)=-\eta_1(x_0),\qquad
                 \eta'_{2,j}(x_0)=-\eta_{2,j}(x_0).
\end{equation}
The seam clutching numbers are unchanged. In particular, this
normalization change cannot alter the ratio of two branch
signs or two residual-relative signs.
Changing only the reference-path orientations, with the
orientation of $p_0$ fixed, leaves every $\eta$ unchanged.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In DFL's extension recipe, a path expression with $m$ copies
of $p_0$, allowing inverse paths, changes orientation by
$(-1)^m$. An $S^4$ insertion has index divisible by eight
and retains its fixed orientation. The reference-path
contributions cancel when comparing two paths with the
same endpoints. If their indices differ by four modulo
eight, their numbers of $p_0$ factors have opposite parity,
so their orientation-change factors have ratio $-1$.

DFL orient a mixed configuration by deforming it to a
symplectically constant pair and gluing a gauge cylinder
to the constant mixed solution
\cite[Section~4.7, pp.~59--60]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The latter operator has zero kernel and cokernel and its
