determinant has the natural real orientation. The glued
and residual mixed configurations have the same ordinary
generators and indices differing by four. Their gauge
cylinders in this prescription have the same index
difference, because the constant mixed factor has index zero.

Let $\chi_{\rm gl},\chi_0\in\{1,-1\}$ be the changes of
mixed orientation at these configurations. The preceding
parity calculation gives $\chi_{\rm gl}/\chi_0=-1$.
The residual sign becomes $n'(x_0)=\chi_0n(x_0)$.
Applying this to the defining equation
\eqref{eq:finite-center-orientation-sign} gives
$\eta'=(\chi_{\rm gl}/\chi_0)\eta=-\eta$.
The geometric bundles and their complex Chern numbers do
not depend on the coherent orientation choice.
Finally, if the reference-path orientations change by signs
$\epsilon(\alpha)$, the composition recipe multiplies the
orientation of a path from $\alpha$ to $\beta$ by
$\epsilon(\alpha)\epsilon(\beta)$. The mixed prescription
has the same endpoint factor. It cancels between the glued
configuration and its residual, proving the last assertion.
\end{proof}

For a selected surface component of genus at least two,
DFL fix the free choice by gluing its positive generator
sphere to a constant strip and using mixed shifting
\cite[Remark~4.76]{DaeFukLip21a}. Their scope is explicit:
if both components have nontrivial $\pi_2$, they guarantee
sphere-gluing compatibility for one chosen summand
\cite[Remark~2.39, pp.~16--17]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Simultaneous compatibility for both components does not
follow from that remark. A genus-one component has
$\pi_2(M_1)=0$, so a torus-only splitting has no generator
sphere for this prescription. In that case the orientation
of $p_0$, or an independently specified cylinder convention,
must be retained as gauge orientation data.
One cannot choose a new normalization separately for
each residual, branch or marked point in a single coherent
problem. Nor does fixing it identify the actual gluing
orientation with the complex primitive orientation.

\subsection{Comparison of the analytic kernel map with determinant-line excision}
\label{sec:analytic-kernel-orientation}

Let
\[
 K_E=\ker\bigl(d\operatorname{ev}_\infty:
          H^1(\operatorname{End}_0E)\longrightarrow T_pM_g\bigr)
\]
on the regular low-genus attaching patch. It has complex dimension two.
Write $W_\rho(v)$ for the derivative of the actual pregluing in
$v\in K_E$, keeping $c,\rho$ fixed and using all four real
fixed-attaching directions before taking the affine slice.
Let $L=dF_\rho(U)$ at the observation-normalized correction,
and let $\widetilde Q$ be its geometric right inverse. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:analytic-kernel-map}
 \mathcal T_\rho(v)
   =\bigl[D\Phi_\rho(U)(I-\widetilde QL)W_\rho(v)\bigr]
                                      \in\ker D_{\rm gl}.
\end{equation}
The brackets denote the ordinary full-Coulomb representative
given by Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-linear-gauge-reduction}.
The vector before reduction is a genuine geometric kernel
vector, and gauge reduction preserves its observations and
the exact finite end variables.

\begin{proposition}[orientation of the analytic kernel map]
\label{prop:analytic-kernel-orientation}
For sufficiently small positive scale on each compact regular
attaching patch, $\mathcal T_\rho$ is an isomorphism.
Let $o_{K_E}$ be its source's complex orientation and orient $P$
as in Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation}. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:analytic-gluing-orientation}
 d\operatorname{Gl}(o_P\wedge dc\wedge d\rho)
       =\sigma_g\,\mathcal T_\rho(o_{K_E}),\qquad
                  \sigma_1=-1,\quad \sigma_2=+1.
\end{equation}
This compares the derivative with the analytic map
\eqref{eq:analytic-kernel-map}; it does not identify that map
with the excision transport of
Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Set $M_E=d\mathcal O_0(E)|_{K_E}$ and
$\ell_\rho=d\mathcal O_{c,\rho}|_{\ker D_{\rm gl}}$.
These are isomorphisms to $\mathbb R^4$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-limiting-observations} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:gluing-observation-coordinates}.
The finite derivative estimate and the corrected right inverse give
\[
 \|(I-\widetilde QL)W_\rho(v)-W_\rho(v)\|_{X_\rho}
                          \leq CT^{-31}|v|,\qquad T=\log(1/\rho).
\]
Restriction to the rescaled loop region costs $T^{1/2}$.
The cap derivative and the connection there converge to their
base-flat limits. Duhamel's derivative formula therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:analytic-kernel-observations}
                  M_E^{-1}\ell_\rho\mathcal T_\rho=I+o(1)
\end{equation}
uniformly on the smaller compact patch. This proves
invertibility and shows that $\mathcal T_\rho$ has the same
orientation as $\ell_\rho^{-1}M_E$: the straight homotopy of
the matrix in \eqref{eq:analytic-kernel-observations} to $I$
is invertible once its error norm is less than one.
Observations are used here only on smooth finite-dimensional
kernels, with their gauge-invariance already proved.

On the parameter frame
\[
 (\rho\partial_c,\rho\partial_\rho,
  \partial_{\theta_1},\partial_{\theta_2}),
\]
define $B_\rho$ to have columns
$(-T_E,-D_E,\partial_{\theta_1}E,\partial_{\theta_2}E)$
in $K_E$. It is an isomorphism by the affine normalization.
Equation~\eqref{eq:gluing-parameter-columns}, retaining
$\rho\,d\mathcal O_0(E)\partial_cE$, gives
$\ell_\rho d\operatorname{Gl}=M_EB_\rho+o(1)$
in this frame. Thus
\[
       \mathcal T_\rho^{-1}d\operatorname{Gl}B_\rho^{-1}
                                                    =I+o(1).
\]
It is homotopic through invertible maps to the identity, so
$d\operatorname{Gl}$ and $\mathcal T_\rho B_\rho$
have the same orientation.

It remains to compute the sign of $B_\rho$. In genus one,
the fixed-attaching holomorphic coordinates are $(L,\kappa)$.
At $\kappa=i$, the vectors $(-T_E,-D_E)$ have
$\kappa$ coordinates $(-1,i)$, reversing the complex
$\kappa$ orientation; the Jacobian directions retain theirs.
In genus two, use $(\kappa,\chi)$ at negative rank drop.
The same reversal occurs in the $\kappa$ plane, while
the chosen orientation of $P$ is opposite to complex
$\xi$ there and $d\chi=d\xi$. These two reversals cancel.
The determinant of $B_\rho$ is nonzero on the connected
regular branch, so its sign is constant. Moving the two
$P$ directions past $c,\rho$, or multiplying those two
coordinate vectors by positive $\rho$, changes no sign.
This proves \eqref{eq:analytic-gluing-orientation}.
\end{proof}

The same observation estimate shows that two analytic kernel
maps on the same actual solution have orientation-preserving
transition if both have the stated limiting cap observations.
This includes changes of the right inverse that preserve the
estimates. It does not identify separately normalized solutions
without the previously proved chart-overlap comparison.

We next specify the remaining excision sign. Concatenate the
paths of Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision},
including the rotation identification, in the direction
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-oriented-path}
 \mathcal D_0=D_0\oplus C_E
       \longrightarrow \mathcal D_1=D_{\rm gl}\oplus C_\alpha.
\end{equation}
Trivialize its ordinary source and target Hilbert bundles over
the interval, retaining those identifications. Every operator
has index $m=4+\dim_{\mathbb R}T_pM_g$, and both endpoints
are surjective. Put $V=K_E\oplus T_pM_g$ with its complex
orientation and choose a complex splitting $j$ of attaching
evaluation. On the endpoint kernels prescribe rows
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-endpoint-rows}
 \begin{aligned}
 r_0(0,h)&=(h-j\,d\operatorname{ev}_\infty h,\
                                      d\operatorname{ev}_\infty h),\\
 r_1(v,a)&=(\mathcal T_\rho^{-1}v,a).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Extend them to bounded finite-rank maps on the ordinary sources
by first projecting onto the finite-dimensional endpoint kernels,
and join the extensions by a continuous family $r_t$.
No point or raw holonomy evaluation on arbitrary $H^1$ fields
is required. The augmented operators
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-index-zero-family}
             \mathcal A_t=(\mathcal D_t,r_t):
                          X\longrightarrow Y\oplus V
\end{equation}
have index zero and invertible endpoints.
Write $\operatorname{Exc}$ for the original determinant
comparison from $\det D_{\rm gl}$ to
$\det D_0\otimes\det K_E$, after cancelling the attaching
factor with its complex orientation.

For an index-zero Fredholm path with invertible endpoints, its
\emph{orientation parity} is the sign of its determinant transport
relative to the canonical orientations at those endpoints.

\begin{lemma}[criterion for the excision orientation]
\label{lem:excision-orientation-parity}
Let $\epsilon_{\rm exc}$ be the orientation parity of
\eqref{eq:excision-index-zero-family}. Then the excision
transport, after cancelling the $T_pM_g$ factor, satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-versus-analytic-kernel}
 \operatorname{Exc}^{-1}
       (o_{D_0}^{\rm can}\otimes o_{K_E})
                 =\epsilon_{\rm exc}\,\mathcal T_\rho(o_{K_E}).
\end{equation}
For a finite target stabilization
$(\mathcal A_t,j_t):X\oplus F\to Y\oplus V$
that is onto throughout, choose a continuous frame of its
kernel bundle $W_t$ and a fixed basis of $F$.
If $B_i$ is the matrix of the isomorphism
$\pi_F:W_i\to F$ at $i=0,1$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-parity-matrices}
             \epsilon_{\rm exc}
                      =\operatorname{sgn}(\det B_0\det B_1).
\end{equation}
The sign is independent of the row extension inside the
interval. In particular, invertibility of $\mathcal A_t$
throughout the interval would imply $\epsilon_{\rm exc}=+1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Finite cokernel representatives and a finite cover provide
the stated stabilization. Since $\mathcal A_t$ has index
zero, $W_t$ has rank $\dim F$ and has a continuous frame.
The stabilization sequence identifies
$\det\mathcal A_t=\det W_t\otimes(\det F)^*$.
At an invertible endpoint $\pi_F$ is an isomorphism, and
its determinant sign compares this transported frame with
the canonical determinant orientation. This proves
\eqref{eq:excision-parity-matrices}. A continuous change
of frame has the same sign at both ends and cancels.
Enlarging the stabilization adds cancelling factors.

Adding the row gives the exact sequence
\[
 0\to\ker\mathcal A_t\to\ker\mathcal D_t\to V
    \to\operatorname{coker}\mathcal A_t
    \to\operatorname{coker}\mathcal D_t\to0.
\]
With the kernel-first determinant convention it identifies
$\det\mathcal D_t$ with
$\det\mathcal A_t\otimes\det V$ continuously through
kernel jumps. At the endpoints, these are exactly the
orientations prescribed by \eqref{eq:excision-endpoint-rows}.
The first is the complex compact-kernel orientation; the
second is the analytic kernel orientation times the constant
complex factor. Since $\dim V$ is even, moving its factor
introduces no graded sign. This proves
\eqref{eq:excision-versus-analytic-kernel}.
Changing the row extension gives a homotopy with fixed
invertible endpoints. If the entire augmented path is
invertible, its canonical determinant orientations already
give continuous transport, proving the final assertion.
\end{proof}

Define the remaining coherent sign by
\[
 \operatorname{Exc}(o_{\rm DFL,gl})
       =n(x_0)\epsilon_{\rm coh}\,
                      o_{D_0}^{\rm can}\otimes o_{K_E}.
\]
Equations~\eqref{eq:analytic-gluing-orientation} and
\eqref{eq:excision-versus-analytic-kernel} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:primitive-orientation-three-factors}
                    \eta_g=\sigma_g\epsilon_{\rm exc}
                                            \epsilon_{\rm coh}.
\end{equation}
Formula \eqref{eq:excision-parity-matrices} specifies
the excision comparison whose sign is evaluated below.
The subsequent comparison with DFL's coherent cylinder
prescription determines $\epsilon_{\rm coh}$.

The existing geometric right inverse does not apply directly
to every ordinary operator in \eqref{eq:excision-oriented-path}.
In particular, the weak-adjoint surjectivity argument used
gauge covariance at an exact solution. Along a non-solution
configuration path the gauge variation instead includes the
equation error. The proof below controls the commutator in
the complete geometric norms and constructs the augmented
kernel frames along the path.

\subsubsection{The Neumann gauge block along excision}

The gauge part of this problem admits a uniform estimate.
Let $\nabla_t$ be the derivative on negative adjoint zero-forms
along the excision path. On its rotation segment this is a
metric connection on the direct sum, with an off-diagonal term
on the identified negative annuli. Impose covariant Neumann
boundary conditions and put
\[
 \Delta_t=\nabla_t^*\nabla_t,\qquad
 \|\phi\|_{\mathcal H_t}^2=\|\phi\|_2^2+\|\nabla_t\phi\|_2^2.
\]
These gauge spaces have no finite critical variables.

\begin{proposition}[uniform Neumann estimate along excision]
\label{prop:excision-neumann-gap}
For the deformations in
Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision},
the cap metrics of Lemma~\ref{lem:cap-dirichlet-metric},
and the fixed compact regular parameter patch, the paths may be
chosen so that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-neumann-gap}
       \|\nabla_t\phi\|_2^2\geq\lambda\|\phi\|_2^2
\end{equation}
with $\lambda>0$ independent of $t$ and sufficiently small $\rho$.
Consequently the weak Neumann inverse
$\mathcal H_t^*\to\mathcal H_t$ is uniformly bounded.
One sufficiently small common positive end weight, chosen at
the outset and equal to one on the compact component, has the
same property. The negative gauge projection is uniformly
bounded on $L^2$, also with that weight.
At each fixed positive scale an $L^2$ source
has an $H^2$ Neumann solution. No uniform $H^2$ bound is asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First consider the fixed residual connection $A_0$.
Its limiting zero-form operators on the ends have positive
gap by irreducibility. A normalized sequence whose covariant
derivatives tend to zero cannot lose mass on those ends.
Local Rellich compactness would therefore produce a nonzero
parallel adjoint section if the global gap vanished.
Irreducibility on a seam fiber rules this out.
The fixed Sobolev inequality and this gap give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-global-form-perturbation}
 \|[a,\phi]\|_2
 \leq C\bigl(\|a_{\rm dec}\|_{L^4}
                  +\|a_{\rm fin}\|_{L^\infty}\bigr)
                                   \|d_{A_0}\phi\|_2,
 \qquad a=a_{\rm dec}+a_{\rm fin}.
\end{equation}
Here $a_{\rm fin}$ will be a smooth extension of the changes
in limiting critical representatives.

In the retained base-flat negative frame, the primitive surface
connection is bounded and the reconstructed compact base
coefficient is $O(\delta)$, where
$\delta\leq C_J\rho^2R_1^{J+8}$ for a fixed sufficiently large $J$.
The global insertion is confined to radius $O(\rho R_2)$.
Its $L^4$ connection difference from $A_0$ is consequently
$O(\rho^{1/2}T^N)$ for a fixed $N$; the conversion of a compact
base coefficient contributes its factor $\rho^{-1}$.
The residual flattening has the same smallness.
For the correction path,
\eqref{eq:constructed-base-lfour} and
\eqref{eq:constructed-localized-displacement} give, with
$M_1\leq CR_1^2$,
\[
 \|a/f\|_{L^4_{\rm phys}}
       \leq C\rho^{1/2}M_1\|a/M_1\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma},
 \qquad
 \|b\|_{L^4_{\rm phys}}
       \leq C\rho^{1/2}M_1^{1/2}\|b\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}.
\]
The outer weighted $H^1$ remainder has global $L^4$ norm
$O(T^{-37})$. The finite exact end profiles have $L^\infty$
norm $O(T^{-37})$ in the fixed smooth end charts.
The nonlinear chart remainder satisfies the same bounds.
Thus throughout the chosen graph-configuration interpolation,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-global-coefficient-smallness}
 \|a_{\rm dec}\|_{L^4}+\|a_{\rm fin}\|_{L^\infty}
                       \leq CT^{-37}+C\rho^{1/2}T^N=o(1).
\end{equation}
Only the finite profiles, rather than an arbitrary decaying
$H^1$ remainder, are estimated in $L^\infty$.
Choose the smooth configuration replacement sufficiently close
in these coefficient norms in local unitary gauges, relative
to its endpoint representatives. The form inequality is open
in these norms and invariant under the gauge identifications.
Equations~\eqref{eq:excision-global-form-perturbation} and
\eqref{eq:excision-global-coefficient-smallness} prove the
uniform gap for the global configuration segments.

For the compact component put $R=R_1$ and $a=\rho R$.
The negative metric $\rho^2f_R^2g_B$ is $a^2h$, where $h$
is one fixed smooth hemisphere metric after the dilation $w=Rv$.
Indeed, near $\zeta=1/w=0$, write
$f_\infty=c(\zeta)|\zeta|^{-2}$, so that
$c^2g_B=|d\zeta|^2$. With $u=R\zeta$, the explicit cap formula gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rescaled-cap-metric}
 R^{-2}f_R^2g_B
   =\bigl[\chi(|u|)|u|^{-2}+1-\chi(|u|)\bigr]^2|du|^2.
\end{equation}
Away from this chart the metric in $v$ is Euclidean.
The expressions agree on their overlap.
The fixed product Sobolev inequality, followed by the surface
gap of the attaching connection $\alpha$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-sobolev}
 \|\phi\|_4
 \leq Ca^{-1/2}
            \bigl(\|\nabla^\alpha\phi\|_2+\|\phi\|_2\bigr)
 \leq Ca^{-1/2}\|\nabla^\alpha\phi\|_2 .
\end{equation}
The $L^4$ and $L^2$ scaling factors are $a^{1/2}$ and $a$;
the base derivative has its additional inverse factor $a^{-1}$.
The displayed coarser estimate is valid for $a\leq1$.

In the retained negative bundle frame, the limiting surface
coefficient satisfies $\beta_0-\alpha=O(|\zeta|)$ at infinity
and is bounded elsewhere. Its base coefficient is zero.
The reconstruction changes both compact coefficients by
$O(\delta)$. Since
\[
 \int_{|\zeta|<r_*} f_R^2|\zeta|^4\,d\operatorname{vol}_B\leq C,
 \qquad
 \int_B f_R^2\,d\operatorname{vol}_B\leq CR^2,
\]
with a bounded contribution from the remaining fixed chart,
the physical connection satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-coefficients}
 \|(A_E-\alpha)_\Sigma\|_4
          \leq C\rho^{1/2}+Ca^{1/2}\delta,\qquad
 \|(A_E)_B\|_4\leq C\rho^{-1/2}\delta.
\end{equation}
For the base term its fourth power is
$\rho^{-2}\int_B f_R^{-2}|(A_E)_B|^4$
in compact coordinates. Flattening toward $\alpha$ preserves
these coefficient bounds: the small surface chart coordinates
and base coefficients are multiplied by bounded cutoffs.
No derivative of a cutoff enters this estimate.
H\"older and \eqref{eq:excision-compact-sobolev} now give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-form-smallness}
 \|(\nabla^{A_E}-\nabla^\alpha)\phi\|_2
       \leq Cq_\rho\|\nabla^\alpha\phi\|_2,\qquad
 q_\rho=R^{-1/2}+\delta+\frac{\delta}{\rho\sqrt R}=o(1).
\end{equation}
This proves the compact gap, including negative rank drop.
No irreducibility of each interior surface connection is needed.

It remains to consider the rotation commutator.
Let $\nabla_{\rm sp}$ be the split flattened derivative.
On the common annulus both its summands equal $\nabla^\alpha$,
and its commutator path has derivative
\[
 \nabla_t=\nabla_{\rm sp}-tJ\,d\vartheta,\qquad
 J=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}.
\]
For negative zero-forms, the rotation extends across the paired
inner negative discs: use the retained identifications with
the attaching surface bundle, set $V_t=R(t\vartheta)$ on the
annulus, the identity outside, and $R(t\pi/2)$ inside.
The inner physical metrics agree because $R_2\ll R_1$.
Thus $V_t$ is unitary on the direct sum of negative $L^2$ spaces.
Its normal derivative is zero on the seam.
This extension makes no assertion about an identification
of the full mixed domains on the inner discs.

Conjugation cancels the derivative of $\vartheta$ exactly:
\[
               V_t^{-1}\nabla_tV_t=\nabla_{\rm ref}+B_t.
\]
The reference is the flattened residual on the global component
and $\nabla^\alpha$ on the compact component. Both equal
$\nabla^\alpha$ on the paired inner discs.
There $B_t$ is the orthogonal conjugate of the compact
connection difference; it vanishes on the common annulus.
Its exterior part is the original diagonal difference.
The identical inner metrics preserve
\eqref{eq:excision-compact-coefficients} for the off-diagonal
coefficients. Use \eqref{eq:excision-compact-sobolev} for
compact inputs and the fixed Sobolev estimate for global
inputs. Since $a\leq1$, the former constant bounds both.
It follows that
\[
          \|B_t\phi\|_2\leq Cq_\rho\|\nabla_{\rm ref}\phi\|_2
\]
uniformly in $t$. The reference gap and unitarity prove
\eqref{eq:excision-neumann-gap}. In particular, no small
coefficient estimate for $d\vartheta$ has been used.

Finally choose an end function $h$, linear on the global tails
and zero on the surgery region and compact component, with
zero normal derivative on the boundary. The weak form of
$e^{\omega h}\Delta_t e^{-\omega h}$ is
\[
 b_\omega(u,v)=
  \langle\nabla_tu-\omega\,dh\,u,\,
                         \nabla_tv+\omega\,dh\,v\rangle .
\]
For real adjoint fields its diagonal is
$\|\nabla_tu\|_2^2-\omega^2\|dh\,u\|_2^2$.
The uniform gap gives coercivity for one sufficiently small
$\omega>0$. Lax--Milgram proves the weighted weak inverse.
At each fixed positive scale, smooth-coefficient Neumann
regularity gives the stated $H^2$ solution.
For a negative ordinary one-form $a$ with zero normal trace,
the weak equation for $\phi=\Delta_t^{-1}\nabla_t^*a$ is
$\langle\nabla_t\phi,\nabla_t\psi\rangle
=\langle a,\nabla_t\psi\rangle$.
It gives $\|\nabla_t\phi\|_2\leq\|a\|_2$ and
$\|\phi\|_2\leq\lambda^{-1/2}\|a\|_2$.
The map $a\mapsto a-\nabla_t\phi$ extends to the orthogonal
projection off the closed gradient range on negative $L^2$.
For a general $L^2$ input its boundary condition is understood
in this weak sense. The weighted form gives the same bound
with a uniform constant in place of one.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Coulomb reduction of the orientation parity}

Write the ordinary excision family as
$\mathcal D_t=(L_t,C_t)$, where $L_t$ comprises the geometric
equations and $C_t=-\nabla_t^*$ is its gauge-fixing row.
Let $Z_t$ be the weighted $H^2$ zero-form space with covariant
Neumann condition and no finite critical variables.
The gradient $G_t\phi=(\nabla_t\phi,0)$ maps $Z_t$ into the
ordinary matching domain $X_t$: its normal trace vanishes,
and its surface trace is exact, with zero harmonic part.
The rotation term has no surface component and has zero
normal component at the seam, so this remains true throughout.
At fixed positive scale, Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-neumann-gap}
therefore supplies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-coulomb-projection}
 H_t=(C_tG_t)^{-1}=-\Delta_t^{-1},\qquad
 P_t=I-G_tH_tC_t,\qquad X_t^C=\ker C_t.
\end{equation}
Here $P_t$ is a bounded projection in the ordinary $H^1$ domain.
Fixed-scale elliptic regularity suffices for this assertion;
no uniform bound for this projection in the geometric graph
norms is implied.

\begin{proposition}[removal of the gauge determinant]
\label{prop:excision-coulomb-parity}
With the finite rows of \eqref{eq:excision-index-zero-family},
the path
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-coulomb-augmented-path}
 \mathcal A_t^C=
   (L_t|_{X_t^C},r_t|_{X_t^C}):
            X_t^C\longrightarrow Y_t^{\rm geo}\oplus V
\end{equation}
is Fredholm of index zero, has invertible endpoints, and satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-coulomb-parity}
                 \epsilon_{\rm exc}
                           =\operatorname{par}(\mathcal A_t^C).
\end{equation}
The eliminated Neumann block contributes $+1$ to orientation
transport.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The isomorphism
$(\xi,\phi)\mapsto\xi+G_t\phi$ from $X_t^C\oplus Z_t$ to
$X_t$ has inverse $x\mapsto(P_tx,H_tC_tx)$.
These are continuous at fixed positive scale; the images
of $P_t$ form a continuous Hilbert subbundle.
Replace $r_t$ by $r_tP_t$, using the homotopy
$r_t[(1-s)I+sP_t]$.
The projection is the identity on $\ker\mathcal D_t$.
Thus the endpoint kernel rows stay fixed, the augmented
endpoints remain invertible throughout this homotopy,
and the orientation parity is unchanged.

Set $B_t=L_tG_t$, without assuming it vanishes.
In the displayed source splitting, the augmented operator is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}
       L_t|_{X_t^C}&B_t\\
       0&C_tG_t\\
       r_t|_{X_t^C}&0
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
The target shear
$(f,h,v)\mapsto(f-B_tH_th,h,v)$ is joined to the identity
through invertible shears. It gives the direct sum of
$\mathcal A_t^C$ and the invertible Neumann block $C_tG_t$.
The induced kernel and cokernel identifications yield a
continuous determinant-line isomorphism. The invertible block
has its canonical determinant orientation at every parameter,
so it cancels with parity $+1$. At invertible endpoints all
these kernel and cokernel identifications preserve canonical
orientations. This proves \eqref{eq:excision-coulomb-parity},
as well as the Fredholm and endpoint assertions.
The cut-and-paste maps preserve the corresponding gauge rows
and Neumann gradients, so the reductions concatenate with the
specified identifications in the original excision path.
\end{proof}

The equation-error term has not disappeared from the remaining
geometric problem. If $Q_t$ is a geometric right inverse
in spaces on which the following compositions are bounded, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-equation-error-schur}
 L_tP_tQ_t=I-E_t,\qquad
                     E_t=(L_tG_t)H_tC_tQ_t.
\end{equation}
Only if $\|E_t\|<1$ on that geometric target does the formula
$P_tQ_t(I-E_t)^{-1}$ give a right inverse on the Coulomb slice.
At an exact solution gauge covariance gives $L_tG_t=0$.
At a non-solution it gives the infinitesimal gauge action
on the equation error. The rotation connection itself is flat
on the constant annulus, but the flattened and interpolated
pieces can still have such errors.
We therefore estimate $H_tC_tQ_t$ in the surface-sensitive graph
norms along each part of the path. The annular estimates below,
followed by Propositions~\ref{prop:excision-correction-orientation}
and~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-orientation}, bound the Schur error
and construct continuous frames of the full kernels. These frames
evaluate \eqref{eq:excision-coulomb-parity}.

\subsubsection{Gauge action on the annular equation error}

The smooth flattenings and interpolation to the common constant
annulus have errors supported on a bounded number of annuli
of radius comparable to $r=\rho R_2$.
The nonlinear correction path has an additional distributed
error and is excluded from the following annular assertions.
Put $a=\rho R_1$ and use the fixed compact metric $h$ from
\eqref{eq:excision-rescaled-cap-metric}.
For a compact zero-form write
$\phi_c=\eta_c+\psi_c$, where $\eta_c=m_h\phi_c$ is its
base average in the retained negative bundle frame.

\begin{lemma}[annular error multiplication]
\label{lem:excision-annular-multiplier}
Let $D\times\Sigma$ be a fixed global negative collar containing
the shrinking annuli, and put
\[
 h_\rho=R_2^{-1}+\rho R_2+\frac{\delta}{\rho R_2^2}.
\]
For the global annular geometric error $e_g$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-global-annular-multiplier}
 \|[e_g,\phi_g]\|_{Y_\rho}
 \leq Cr^{-\varepsilon}h_\rho
       \sqrt{1+|\log r|}\,
                        \|\phi_g\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma(D\times\Sigma)}.
\end{equation}
For the compact error in its normalized target $Y_{\rm cap}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-cap-annular-multiplier}
 \|[e_c,\phi_c]\|_{Y_{\rm cap}}
 \leq CR_2^\varepsilon h_\rho
 \left(\|\eta_c\|_{H^k_\Sigma}
       +\sqrt{1+\log(R_1/R_2)}\,
                                \|\psi_c\|_{H^1_hH^k_\Sigma}\right).
\end{equation}
The constants are uniform for $0\leq\varepsilon\leq1/4$.
The compact average is not omitted from this estimate.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a Hilbert-valued $u\in H^1(D)$ and a fixed rescaled annulus
$A_1$, extension to the plane and Fourier splitting at $r^{-1}$
give
\[
 \|u(r\,\cdot)\|_{H^1(A_1)}
                  \leq C\sqrt{1+|\log r|}\,\|u\|_{H^1(D)}.
\]
Indeed the low-frequency supremum is bounded by
$C(\int_{|\xi|\leq r^{-1}}(1+|\xi|^2)^{-1}\,d\xi)^{1/2}\|u\|_{H^1}$.
The high-frequency $L^2$ norm is at most $Cr\|u\|_{H^1}$,
and the base gradient norm is invariant under this rescaling.
This proof uses no point value of an $H^1$ field.
For a coefficient $e_r$ with
$\|e_r(r\,\cdot)\|_{C^1_BH^k_\Sigma}\leq M_r$, the surface
Sobolev algebra and fixed-domain multiplication imply
\[
 \|[e_r,u]\|_{H^\varepsilon_BH^k_\Sigma}
 \leq CM_rr^{1-\varepsilon}\sqrt{1+|\log r|}
                                      \|u\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma}.
\]
A fixed rescaled cutoff supplies the extension outside the annulus
and its fractional cross terms. Reflection handles a half-annulus.

For the actual error put $v=R_2^{-1}$. The interpolated surface
and positive coordinates have size $O(v)$, and the compact
base coefficient has size $O(\delta+\rho^2/v^3)$, with their
fixed rescaled derivatives. The normalized mixed error is
$O(1)$; the normalized scalar error is
$O(\delta v/\rho+\rho/v^2)$. These are the same curvature
cancellations used in the pregluing estimate, also when the
constant configuration is one of the interpolated pairs.
Conversion to physical curvature divides by $\rho f$, where
$f\asymp v^{-2}$. Thus
$r\|e_g(r\,\cdot)\|_{C^1_BH^k_\Sigma}\leq Ch_\rho$.
The global supported source norm is the local mixed Sobolev
norm, proving \eqref{eq:excision-global-annular-multiplier}.

On the compact component, the physical annulus is at radius
$R_2/R_1$ in the $h$ coordinate. Apply the concentration
estimate to $\psi_c$ there and retain the constant $\eta_c$
separately. The normalized compact target has coordinate
radius $v$ and $H^\varepsilon$ scale $v^{1-\varepsilon}$.
The two rescaled coordinates differ by inversion on a fixed
annulus. Its bounded derivatives give
\eqref{eq:excision-cap-annular-multiplier}.
In particular
$r^{-\varepsilon}$ and $R_2^\varepsilon$ are bounded at
$\varepsilon=T^{-1}$, and $h_\rho=O(T^{-40})+O(\rho T^N)$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The compact gauge potential and its base average}

For a compact negative variation $U^-=\rho u+b$ in the original
compact coordinate, define
\[
 X_k(U)=\|u/M_1\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_BH^k_\Sigma}
                         +\|b\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}_BH^k_\Sigma}.
\]
These are terms of the existing raw cap norm.
The next estimate controls the potential rather than the
full differentiated gauge variation.

\begin{proposition}[compact Neumann potential]
\label{prop:excision-compact-potential}
Let $A$ be the exact compact connection or one of its smooth
flattenings toward the attaching constant. If $U^-$ has
zero normal trace, the solution of
$\Delta_A\phi=d_A^*U^-$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-potential}
 \|m_h\phi\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}\leq C_kT^{1/2}X_k(U),
 \qquad
 \|\phi-m_h\phi\|_{H^1_hH^k_\Sigma}
                              \leq C_k\rho T^{N_k}X_k(U).
\end{equation}
Here $k\geq2$ is fixed, $N_k$ is finite, and the average uses
the retained bundle frame. The estimate includes negative rank drop.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First impose ordinary base Coulomb gauge for this estimate.
The small base Neumann construction, normalized to be the identity
at infinity, applies to the flattened base coefficient.
Every fixed compact-coordinate bound for it and its gauge
is $\widehat\delta=\rho^2T^N$ after increasing a fixed $N$.
In the $h$ coordinate,
\[
 d_h^*A_B=0,\quad A_B(n)=0,\quad
 e:=\|A_B\|_{L^\infty_hC^{k+2}_\Sigma}\leq CR_1\widehat\delta,
 \qquad e/a=o(1).
\]
The two-dimensional conformal covariance of base divergence
justifies this gauge condition after dilation.
Writing $B_\Sigma=\beta-\alpha$, the retained decay
$B_\Sigma=O(|\zeta|)+O(\widehat\delta)$ at infinity gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-average-coefficient}
 \int_B\|B_\Sigma\|_{C^{k+2}_\Sigma}\,d\operatorname{vol}_h
                           \leq C(R_1^{-1}+\widehat\delta).
\end{equation}
Indeed $d\operatorname{vol}_h=R_1^{-2}f_{R_1}^2d\operatorname{vol}_B$,
and the integral of $f_{R_1}^2|\zeta|$ is $O(R_1)$.

Normalize the fixed $h$ volume in the notation for averaging.
Let $m$ be averaging, $J$ constant extension, and $Q_b=I-Jm$.
Put
\[
 N=\Delta_A,\quad N_0=a^{-2}\Delta_h+N_\alpha,\quad
 E=N-N_0,\qquad N_\alpha=d_\alpha^*d_\alpha.
\]
On zero-average fields use
\[
 \|z\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}
   =\|z\|_{L^2_hH^{k+1}_\Sigma}
                         +a^{-1}\|z\|_{H^1_hH^k_\Sigma}.
\]
The base Poincar\'e inequality makes its second term equivalent
to the base-gradient term. Product spectral decomposition,
with nonconstant base eigenvalue $\mu$ and surface eigenvalue
$\lambda$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-mean-zero-inverse}
 \|T_0q\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}\leq Ca\|q\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma},
 \qquad T_0=(N_0|_{\ker m})^{-1}.
\end{equation}
The inverse multiplier is $(a^{-2}\mu+\lambda)^{-1}$ and
$\mu$ has a positive lower bound. The same calculation gives
\[
 \|T_0Q_b(a^{-2}d_h^*v+d_\alpha^*b)\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}
                  \leq C(a^{-1}\|v\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}
                                      +\|b\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}).
\]
Flat surface Hodge intertwining proves this estimate without
an additional derivative of $b$.

The surface part $E_\Sigma=N_\beta-N_\alpha$ is first order
with bounded surface coefficients. The base part is
\[
 E_Bz=-2a^{-2}[A_B,d_hz]-a^{-2}[A_B,[A_B,z]].
\]
There is no base divergence term or mixed derivative of $\beta$.
The preceding inverse estimate and
$\|z\|_{H^k_\Sigma}+\|d_hz\|_{H^k_\Sigma}\leq Ca\|z\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}$
show
\[
 \|T_0Q_bEz\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}
                         \leq C(a+e+e^2)\|z\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}.
\]
Thus $T=(Q_bN|_{\ker m})^{-1}$ exists by a Neumann series.
In particular
\[
 \|TQ_bNJ\eta\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}
          \leq C(a+e^2/a)\|\eta\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}
          \leq Ca\|\eta\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}.
\]

The potentially dangerous average of the base derivative vanishes:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-base-average-cancellation}
                 \int_B[A_B,d_hz]\,d\operatorname{vol}_h=0.
\end{equation}
This follows by integration by parts using both base gauge
conditions. For zero-average $z$ it implies
\[
 \|mNz\|_{H^{k-1}_\Sigma}
       \leq C(1+e^2/a^2)\|z\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}.
\]
Consequently the exact surface Schur complement
$S=mN(J-TQ_bNJ)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-surface-schur}
 \|S-N_\alpha\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma\to H^{k-1}_\Sigma}
       \leq C(R_1^{-1}+\widehat\delta+e^2/a^2+a^2)=o(1).
\end{equation}
The irreducible surface inverse therefore makes $S^{-1}$
uniformly bounded.

In the $h$ coordinate write the base input as $v$.
For $f=d_A^*U^-=a^{-2}d_{A_B,h}^*v+d_{\beta,\Sigma}^*b$,
the zero normal trace of $v$ gives
\[
 \|mf\|_{H^{k-1}_\Sigma}
       \leq C(ea^{-2}\|v\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}
                                      +\|b\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}).
\]
The zero-average inverse gives, for $z_0=TQ_bf$,
$\|z_0\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}\leq
C(a^{-1}\|v\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}+\|b\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma})$.
The solution is
$\eta=S^{-1}(mf-mNz_0)$ and $\psi=z_0-TQ_bNJ\eta$.
Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-compact-input-potential}
 \begin{split}
 \|\eta\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}
   &\leq C\bigl((1+e/a^2)\|v\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}
                                     +\|b\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}\bigr),\\
 \|\psi\|_{H^1_hH^k_\Sigma}
   &\leq C\bigl(\|v\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}
          +a\|b\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}+a^2\|\eta\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}\bigr).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Conformal invariance of the base one-form $L^2$ norm gives
$\|v\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}\leq C\rho M_1X_k(U)$.
The fixed-volume norm of $b$ is bounded by its base supremum,
hence by $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}X_k(U)$.
Moreover
$(e/a^2)\rho M_1\leq C\widehat\delta R_1/\rho=\rho T^N$.
This proves \eqref{eq:excision-compact-potential} in the
auxiliary gauge. Changing back has fixed surface norm
$O(\widehat\delta)$ and base $H^1_h$ multiplier error
$O(R_1\widehat\delta)$.
The additional surface derivative of the mean-zero solution
has at worst the polynomial bound in its $\mathcal W_{k,a}$
estimate. All resulting frame errors are absorbed by
$\rho T^{N_k}X_k(U)$, proving the assertion in the retained frame.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The global potential in mixed Sobolev norms}

\begin{proposition}[global Neumann potential near attachment]
\label{prop:excision-global-potential}
For the preglued connection and its smooth unrotated annular
deformations, choose fixed nested negative half-discs
$D_0\Subset D_1\Subset D_2\Subset D_3$ near attachment.
For an input $U$ in the complete gluing domain, the Neumann
solution $\Delta_A\phi=d_A^*U^-$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-global-potential}
 \|\phi\|_{W^{1,4}_BH^k_\Sigma(D_0\times\Sigma)}
   +\|\phi\|_{L^4_BH^{k+1}_\Sigma(D_0\times\Sigma)}
                                      \leq C_k\|U\|_{X_\rho}.
\end{equation}
In particular it is a uniformly bounded multiplier on the local
$H^\varepsilon_BH^k_\Sigma$ source for $0\leq\varepsilon\leq1/4$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $p$ be the finite end parameters and $U_{\rm dec}$ their
subtracted remainder. First use
\[
 \|U\|_{\mathcal U_k}
  =\|U_{\rm dec}^-\|_{L^2_\omega}+|p|
                      +\|U^-\|_{L^4_BH^k_\Sigma(D_3\times\Sigma)}.
\]
The global weak inverse bounds $\phi$ in weighted $H^1$ by
the first two terms. The finite profiles produce fixed smooth
decaying gauge sources of size $C|p|$.
Surface differentiation of the weak equation, with nested
fixed base cutoffs, then gives
\[
 \|\phi\|_{H^1_BH^k_\Sigma(D_2\times\Sigma)}
     +\|\phi\|_{L^2_BH^{k+1}_\Sigma(D_2\times\Sigma)}
                                          \leq C_k\|U\|_{\mathcal U_k}.
\]
The commutators contain surface derivatives of the connection
and lower surface derivatives of $\phi$. Their uniform $L^4$
coefficient bounds and the four-dimensional $H^1$ embedding
bound the weak terms by the preceding surface energy order.
The fixed reference commutators and differentiated cutoffs
have the same lower-order control. No base derivative of
the rapidly varying surface connection is taken.

For the constant product operator
$N^{\rm prod}_\alpha=\Delta_B+N_\alpha$ on the negative
half-plane, the Neumann inverse has the estimate
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-product-lfour-potential}
 \|z\|_{W^{1,4}_BH^k_\Sigma}
       +\|z\|_{L^4_BH^{k+1}_\Sigma}
 \leq C_k\bigl(\|v\|_{L^4_BH^k_\Sigma}
                         +\|f\|_{L^2_BH^k_\Sigma}\bigr),
 \quad N^{\rm prod}_\alpha z=d_\alpha^*v+f.
\end{equation}
Here $d_\alpha$ includes the base derivative.
Reflect the normal input oddly and the other components evenly.
The product heat formula gives operator-valued kernel bounds
$C|z-z'|^{-2}$ and $C|z-z'|^{-3}$ for the twice-differentiated
inverse and its first base derivative. Its $L^2$ bound is the
product spectral bound. The Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition
applied to the Hilbert norm of the input gives weak type $(1,1)$:
use the $L^2$ bound for the good part and kernel cancellation
for the bad parts outside their doubled supports.
Interpolation and the adjoint estimate give the required
$L^4_B$ bound. Flat Hodge intertwining handles surface-form
inputs. The once-differentiated kernels have integrable heat
bounds at zero and exponential decay at infinity by the surface gap.
For the scalar input $f$, the product spectral estimate gives
$H^2_BH^k_\Sigma$ and $H^1_BH^{k+1}_\Sigma$ bounds;
two-dimensional Sobolev embedding completes
\eqref{eq:excision-product-lfour-potential}.

Denote its potential norm by $V_k$. Morrey embedding gives
$V_k\hookrightarrow C^0_BH^k_\Sigma$.
On a sufficiently small fixed $D_3$ the connection difference
from $\alpha$ has small $L^4_BC^{k+2}_\Sigma$ norm $b$:
the residual contributes $O(r_*^{3/2})$, and the primitive
insertion contributes $\rho^{1/2}T^N$.
Extend this difference by a fixed cutoff and write
$d_A=d_\alpha+B$. The weak perturbation is
$d_\alpha^*B+B^*d_\alpha+B^*B$.
Its divergence input $Bz$ has $L^4_BH^k_\Sigma$ bound
$Cb\|z\|_{V_k}$, while the other two terms have scalar
$L^2_BH^k_\Sigma$ bounds $C(b+b^2)\|z\|_{V_k}$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:excision-product-lfour-potential} therefore
gives an inverse by a Neumann series.
The same smallness gives uniqueness in the weak energy class.

Apply that inverse to a cutoff of $\phi$ which equals $\phi$
on $D_0$. Its Laplacian commutator is a divergence of a fixed
cutoff derivative times $\phi$ plus a zeroth-order cutoff term.
The preliminary surface energy estimate bounds the former in
$L^4_BH^k_\Sigma$ and the latter in $L^2_BH^k_\Sigma$.
The localized input is controlled by $\|U\|_{\mathcal U_k}$.
Thus \eqref{eq:excision-global-potential} holds with that
norm on the right.
The physical inner $L^4_BH^k_\Sigma$ bounds have factors
$\rho^{1/2}M_1$ and $\rho^{1/2}M_1^{1/2}$ for base and
surface fields. Both tend to zero. The outer part has its
fixed base $H^1$ bound and retained finite parameters.
Consequently $\|U\|_{\mathcal U_k}\leq C\|U\|_{X_\rho}$.

Finally $W^{1,4}_B$ embeds in $C^{3/8}_B$.
The fractional difference formula shows that multiplication
by this field is bounded on $H^\varepsilon_BH^k_\Sigma$:
the additional radial integral is bounded uniformly because
$3/8-\varepsilon\geq1/8$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Orientation transport on the unrotated annular paths}

\begin{proposition}[the annular configuration paths]
\label{prop:excision-unrotated-annular-orientations}
The compact primitive flattening and the global interpolation
from the prescribed pregluing to its flattened form have
surjective ordinary full-Coulomb operators throughout.
Their kernels have continuous frames whose observations converge
to the corresponding compact deformation coordinates.
On the compact path the resulting orientation is transported
from the complex orientation of $H^1(\End_0E)$.
On the global path it is the orientation specified by $K_E$
and the fixed-attaching observations. Finite rows defined from
these kernel frames make the augmented paths invertible.
The residual flattening remains invertible.
These assertions exclude the coupled rotation and the path
from the pregluing to the actual corrected solution.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the existing geometric norms each annular configuration
displacement is $O(T^{-40})+O(\rho T^N)$.
For the surface and positive fields this is the
$H^{1+\varepsilon}$ norm of an $O(v)$ field on the compact
coordinate disc of radius $v=R_2^{-1}$.
The base change divided by $\rho M_1$ is exponentially small.
The weak exterior surface row has its additional small
$\rho M_1$ factor. Reimposing base Coulomb gauge in the
compact realization changes these bounds only by an
exponentially small polynomial.
The matching-chart transports preserve that base condition
and the compact exact attaching normalization.

The nonlinear constant $CT^4$ therefore bounds the transported
operator change by $CT^{-36}+C\rho T^N$.
When comparing with the actual covariant linearization, retain
the derivative of positive target transport times the equation
error. The strong-times-weak product estimate bounds this
additional term by $C\varepsilon^{-1/2}$ times the error norm,
which is included in the stated bound.
The existing compact and preglued geometric inverses thus
extend by
\[
             Q_t=Q_0\bigl(I+(L_t-L_0)Q_0\bigr)^{-1},
                          \qquad \|Q_t\|\leq CT^2.
\]

Propositions~\ref{prop:excision-compact-potential} and
\ref{prop:excision-global-potential}, together with
Lemma~\ref{lem:excision-annular-multiplier}, give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-annular-schur-bound}
 \|(L_tG_t)H_tC_tU\|_Y
       \leq \mu_\rho\|U\|_X,\qquad
                  \mu_\rho=CT^{-79/2}+C\rho T^N.
\end{equation}
For the global term its multiplier estimate even gives
$CT^{-40}$. For the compact term its average costs $T^{1/2}$,
while the zero-average contribution is exponentially small.
The compact estimate uses the normalized compact target,
as in \eqref{eq:excision-cap-annular-multiplier}.
Consequently
\[
 E_t=(L_tG_t)H_tC_tQ_t,\quad
 \|E_t\|\leq CT^{-75/2}+C\rho T^N=o(1),\quad
               Q_t^C=P_tQ_t(I-E_t)^{-1}
\]
satisfies $C_tQ_t^C=0$ and $L_tQ_t^C=I$.
No bound for $P_t:X\to X$ is needed.
At fixed positive scale $X$ embeds into the ordinary $H^1$
domain, and $P_t$ is bounded there by Neumann regularity.
The ordinary Coulomb-slice operator is Fredholm and its
closed image contains the dense geometric target. It is
therefore onto. Its kernel dimension is four on the global
path and $4+\dim T_pM_g$ on the compact path.

The negative affine-averaged holonomy differentials extend
to $L^2_BH^k_\Sigma$ on the fixed rescaled observation region.
Indeed, for a loop $(w(\tau),y(\tau))$, the map
$(a,b)\mapsto aw(\tau)+b$ has Jacobian
$|\operatorname{Im}w(\tau)|$ bounded below.
Changing variables in its averaging integral, Duhamel's
formula and $H^k(\Sigma)\hookrightarrow C^0(\Sigma)$ prove
the claim. It does not assert boundedness on arbitrary
ordinary four-dimensional $H^1$ fields.
The Neumann potentials used above have their base and surface
derivatives in this local space. In the compact case use
also the finite $\mathcal W_{k,a}$ bound for the extra surface
derivative. Approximation by smooth potentials therefore
extends gauge invariance to
\[
                             \ell_tG_t\phi=0.
\]
The positive attaching evaluation added in the compact case
has the same property. Its graph-norm bound is at most
$CT^{1/2}$.

For the compact problem choose a complex-oriented basis of
the based compact kernel. For the global problem use the
fixed-attaching pregluing derivatives, with norm $O(1)$ and
geometric error $O(T^{-40})$. Transport these finite fields
by the configuration chart and call them $W_t$. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-annular-kernel-frame}
 w_t=(I-Q_tL_t)W_t,\qquad
 J_t=P_tw_t+Q_t^C(L_tG_t)H_tC_tw_t.
\end{equation}
Then $L_tw_t=0$, and both $L_tJ_t$ and $C_tJ_t$ vanish.
The second term of $J_t$ cancels the non-solution error
left by the Coulomb projection.
The geometric operator estimate gives $w_t-W_t=o(1)$.
Their observations converge to the same invertible limiting
matrix $M$. Gauge invariance gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-annular-kernel-observations}
 \ell_tJ_t
 =\ell_tw_t+
       \ell_tQ_t(I-E_t)^{-1}(L_tG_t)H_tC_tw_t=M+o(1).
\end{equation}
The last correction is bounded by
$CT^{1/2}T^2\mu_\rho=O(T^{-37})+O(\rho T^N)$.
Thus $J_t$ is an isomorphism onto the ordinary kernel;
its vectors are smooth by fixed-scale elliptic regularity.
At the exact compact endpoint its value is the ordinary
Coulomb representative of the holomorphic deformation.
It has the complex compact orientation there and transports
it continuously. On the global path the same formula
transports the fixed-attaching observation orientation.
Projecting the ordinary source onto these smooth finite kernels
and composing with $J_t^{-1}$ supplies bounded rows with
invertible augmented operators.

Finally the residual flattening is an ordinary small
perturbation of the fixed invertible residual operator.
Its connection change is $L^4$-small. Its positive principal
coefficient change is $O(\rho R_2)$, and its zeroth-order
coefficients and derivatives of the seam-domain transports
are bounded on a disc of radius $O(\rho R_2)$.
The $H^1_B\to L^4_B$ estimate makes their multiplication
norm to $L^2_B$ of order $(\rho R_2)^{1/2}$.
The surface transports have the same fixed surface operator
bounds. Hence the transported ordinary operator differs
from the residual one by $o(1)$ and remains invertible.
\end{proof}

The preceding kernel frames evaluate the annular configuration
segments. Propositions~\ref{prop:excision-correction-orientation}
and~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-orientation} treat the correction
from the preglued frame to $\mathcal T_\rho$ and the coupled
rotation. The correction path has a distributed nonlinear
equation error, which requires the estimates below.
If $\varepsilon_{\rm rot}$ denotes the comparison between the
flattened endpoint frames through the rotation segment, and
$\varepsilon_{\rm corr}$ the comparison from the preglued frame
to $\mathcal T_\rho$ through the correction segment, concatenation
of determinant transport gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-remaining-two-parities}
             \varepsilon_{\rm exc}
                  =\varepsilon_{\rm rot}\varepsilon_{\rm corr}.
\end{equation}
The correction factor can be evaluated by choosing a path whose
equation error remains on the pregluing annulus.

\subsubsection{Orientation along a path of forced equations}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:excision-correction-orientation}
Under the regular primitive gluing hypotheses, the ordinary
correction transport takes the preglued kernel orientation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-unrotated-annular-orientations}
to the analytic kernel orientation $\mathcal T_\rho$.
In the notation of \eqref{eq:excision-remaining-two-parities},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-correction-sign}
              \varepsilon_{\rm corr}=+1,\qquad
              \varepsilon_{\rm exc}=\varepsilon_{\rm rot}.
\end{equation}
This holds on the same fixed compact regular patches, including
the negative rank-drop locus.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use the augmented equation and observation map
$\mathbb F_\rho$ on the fixed complete subspace $\mathcal B_\rho$
from Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-normalized-correction}.
Write
\[
 \mathbb F_\rho(U)=\mathbf e_\rho+\mathbb A_\rho U
                              +\mathbb N_\rho(U).
\]
Its linear inverse $\mathbb Q_\rho$ has norm at most $CT^3$,
the initial error is at most $CT^{-40}$, and the quadratic
difference constant is at most $CT^5$. For $0\leq s\leq1$
solve, on the same contraction ball,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-forced-correction-path}
 U_s=-\mathbb Q_\rho
             \bigl(s\mathbf e_\rho+\mathbb N_\rho(U_s)\bigr).
\end{equation}
The contraction constant is uniformly smaller than $1/2$.
Thus
\[
 \mathbb F_\rho(U_s)=(1-s)\mathbf e_\rho,\quad
 U_0=0,\quad U_1=U_\rho^{\cO},\quad
 \|U_s\|_{X_\rho}+\|\dot U_s\|_{X_\rho}\leq CT^{-37}.
\]
The derivative follows by differentiating the contraction;
the inverse of $I+\mathbb Q_\rho d\mathbb N_\rho(U_s)$
has norm at most two. The same construction at any prescribed
fixed surface order has identical iterates, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:constructed-higher-surface-orders}.
The bounds are uniform in $s$ at each such order, with a
threshold for $\rho$ allowed to depend on that order.
In particular the endpoint is exactly the observation-normalized
solution used to define $\mathcal T_\rho$.

Put $x_s=(A_s,u_s)=\Phi_\rho(U_s)$. The negative target
trivialization is the fixed bundle frame; only the positive
target is parallel transported. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-retained-forcing}
             \mathcal F^-(x_s)=(1-s)e_{\rm pre}^- .
\end{equation}
The positive equation is $(1-s)$ times its initial error,
transported to $u_s$. Both errors are supported on the original
pregluing annulus. For $G_s\phi=(d_{A_s}\phi,0)$, gauge
covariance therefore gives
\[
 L_sG_s\phi=\bigl([(1-s)e_{\rm pre}^-,\phi],0\bigr),
\]
with the fixed infinitesimal gauge convention.
Although the correction is distributed, its equation error
along this path is annular.

The higher surface bounds just proved and
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-global-potential} control the
Neumann potential in the retained frame. Its local
$C^{3/8}_BH^k_\Sigma$ multiplier bound and
Lemma~\ref{lem:excision-annular-multiplier} imply
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-correction-gauge-coupling}
 \|L_sG_s(C_sG_s)^{-1}C_sV\|_{Y_\rho}
       \leq C(T^{-40}+\rho T^N)\|V\|_{X_\rho},
       \qquad C_s=-d_{A_s}^* .
\end{equation}
The weak estimate extends to the retained coefficients by
approximation in the coefficient norms of that proposition.
No smoothing gauge is used in this uniform bound.

We give the fixed-scale ordinary realization before applying
Fredholm determinant transport. Set
\[
 p=2+\varepsilon/4,\qquad r=p+2,\qquad q=r/2.
\]
Then $r>4$, $2<q<p$, and $r<4p/(4-p)$.
The graph path is continuous in local $W^{1,p}$.
Local relative Coulomb gauges, with normal condition at the
seam and constant gauge freedom fixed, can be chosen
continuously in $W^{2,p}$ on a small parameter interval.
In such a gauge $g_s$ the negative forcing becomes
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-gauged-forcing}
       f_s^-=(1-s)\operatorname{Ad}(g_s^{-1})e_{\rm pre}^- .
\end{equation}
It is continuous in $W^{2,p}$ and hence in $W^{1,r}$.
The positive forcing is a smooth function $f^+(s,z,u_s(z))$.
Neither $g_s$ nor \eqref{eq:excision-gauged-forcing} is
assumed smooth.

The component regularity proof of
\cite[\S3.1]{DaeFukLip21b} applies with these sources as
follows. Add $f_s^-$ to its equation (3.7), and retain its
components in (3.13), (3.20), and (3.23).
The boundary-flatness identity (3.10) is unchanged.
The second entry of (3.23) is now $f^+(s,z,u_s)$.
At an exponent $2<t<4$, that proof estimates the quadratic
source in $W^{1,4t/(8-t)}$ and improves the pair to
$W^{1,2t/(4-t)}$, using its Proposition B.10.
The negative forcing is available in $W^{1,r}$ with $r>4$.
The positive forcing is in $W^{1,t}$ by composition, and
$4t/(8-t)<t$. Thus the same finite iteration reaches $r$,
truncating its last exponent there. The $k=1$ case of
Proposition B.4 and the other component estimates then give
$W^{2,r/2}=W^{2,q}$. At this order the coefficients form
an algebra, since $q>2$. The negative forcing belongs to
$W^{2,q}$ because $q<p$, and the positive forcing has that
regularity by composition. The $k=2$ component estimates
and Proposition B.4 yield
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-forced-ordinary-regularity}
                    (g_s^*A_s,u_s)\in W^{3,q}_{\rm loc},
                              \qquad q>2.
\end{equation}
This finite improvement suffices. Arbitrary smoothness does
not follow by treating \eqref{eq:excision-gauged-forcing}
as a smooth source.

The difference estimates in \cite[\S3.2]{DaeFukLip21b}
and the sequential statements of Propositions B.4 and B.10
give continuity in \eqref{eq:excision-forced-ordinary-regularity}.
At each step the sources converge at the required preceding
order. The perturbed interior argument is unchanged, with
the two derivatives of the compact forcing retained.
On the ends the forcing vanishes, so the ordinary equation,
exact finite critical profiles, and common weight give the
usual weighted regularity and continuity.
Transitions between the local $W^{3,q}$ representatives
satisfy the first-order gauge equation. Starting from
$W^{2,p}$, this equation improves them to $W^{4,q}$.
They therefore define a path in the ordinary Sobolev
configuration quotient, in the original bundle class and
with the prescribed end conventions. Successive local
configuration charts along the interval suffice; no uniform
graph estimate for a global smoothing gauge is required.

We also specify the Fredholm extension at this finite
regularity. The negative coefficients in
\eqref{eq:excision-forced-ordinary-regularity} are
$C^{1,\alpha}$ for some $\alpha>0$, and the positive
map is $C^{2,\alpha}$ after decreasing $\alpha$.
The actual surface Hodge projections and harmonic matching
maps admit local domain identifications bounded on $H^1$
and $L^2$, with their first base derivatives bounded on
$L^2$. To check the coefficient dependence, differentiate
the surface inverse using
$\dot N^{-1}=-N^{-1}\dot N N^{-1}$.
Derivatives of the connection variation in $\dot N$ are
read in divergence form $H^1\to H^{-1}$; the adjacent
inverses supply the derivative. The same identity gives
the required continuity of the projections.
Irreducibility keeps their surface inverse norms bounded.

Freeze the boundary data and positive principal
coefficient on a sufficiently short mixed chart.
The frozen flat connection can be put into a smooth
flat representative by a $C^2$ surface gauge.
The constant-data mixed estimate then applies.
The remaining continuous principal changes are small
and are absorbed; derivatives of the domain transports
are bounded zeroth-order terms. Localization gives the
$H^1$ estimate with compact $L^2$ remainder on the
compact middle. The same argument applies to the
adjoint domain. Together with the fixed end estimates,
including the finite tangent parameters, these estimates
prove Fredholmness. They also prove operator continuity
under $W^{3,q}$ changes and under smooth approximation
in the ordinary configuration charts.
Thus this family has the usual determinant line.
A sufficiently close smooth path in those charts, relative
to the smooth endpoint classes, induces the same transport.
DFL orientability identifies it with the correction
transport in Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision}.

The $W^{2,p}$ gauges act boundedly on ordinary $H^1$
variations, because $dg_s\in L^{4p/(4-p)}$ with exponent
greater than four, and act unitarily on $L^2$ targets.
They intertwine both the actual geometric and Coulomb
rows, also at non-solutions. Hence the retained family
has the same ordinary Fredholm index, equal to four.

It remains to check onto operators and their finite frames.
Include the configuration derivative and target transport
when writing $L_s$ in the fixed geometric spaces.
If $S_s$ is the target transport, the actual operator is
\[
 S_sL_sD\Phi_\rho(U_s)
     =dF_\rho(U_s)-(d_US_s)\mathcal F(x_s).
\]
The last term is retained. The nonlinear constant $CT^4$
and $\|U_s\|\leq CT^{-37}$ give an operator change
$O(T^{-33})+\rho T^N$ from pregluing.
The pregluing inverse of norm $CT^2$ therefore gives
actual right inverses $Q_s$ of norm at most $CT^2$.
Set
\[
 B_s=L_sG_s,\quad H_s=(C_sG_s)^{-1},\quad
 P_s=I-G_sH_sC_s,\quad E_s=B_sH_sC_sQ_s .
\]
By \eqref{eq:excision-correction-gauge-coupling},
$\|E_s\|\leq CT^{-38}+\rho T^N=o(1)$.
The formula
\[
                        Q_s^C=P_sQ_s(I-E_s)^{-1}
\]
satisfies $L_sQ_s^C=I$ and $C_sQ_s^C=0$ on the dense
geometric target.

At fixed scale the retained connection is $H^{1+\varepsilon}$.
In the scalar Neumann expansion, its first-order coefficients
are in $L^{4+\delta}$ and their derivatives in
$L^{2+\delta'}$, for positive $\delta,\delta'$.
Their actions $H^2\to L^2$ are relatively compact by
subcritical Sobolev embedding. The normal coefficient is
fixed. Elliptic regularity and the weak gap give an $H^2$
potential, with scale-dependent constants allowed.
Consequently $P_s$ maps ordinary $H^1$ to itself.
The ordinary Coulomb-slice operator is Fredholm by
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-coulomb-parity}; its closed
range contains the dense geometric target and hence is
the whole ordinary target. The full operator is onto
and its kernel has dimension four.

Transport the finite pregluing fields to $W_s$ and put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-correction-kernel-frame}
 w_s=(I-Q_sL_s)W_s,\qquad
 J_s=P_sw_s+Q_s^CB_sH_sC_sw_s .
\end{equation}
Both ordinary rows annihilate $J_s$.
The averaged observations annihilate the gauge gradients
in the local regularity class supplied by
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-global-potential}.
The same argument as for
\eqref{eq:excision-annular-kernel-observations} gives
$\ell_sJ_s=M_E+o(1)$ uniformly.
The geometric frame change costs at most
$\sqrt T\,T^2T^{-33}$ in observations; the last term
in \eqref{eq:excision-correction-kernel-frame} costs at
most $\sqrt T\,T^2T^{-40}$. Both tend to zero.
Thus $J_s$ is a continuous basis of the entire ordinary
kernel. Projection onto that kernel followed by $J_s^{-1}$
supplies bounded rows with invertible augmented operators
throughout the path.

At $s=0$ this is the prescribed preglued frame.
At $s=1$ the equation error vanishes and the frame is
the analytic map $\mathcal T_\rho$, with the compatible
inverse just constructed. Other inverses with the same
limiting observations give the same orientation.
The finite-row transport therefore has sign $+1$,
proving \eqref{eq:excision-correction-sign}.
\end{proof}

We next compare the coupled rotation with these flattened endpoint
frames. The coherent DFL comparison follows the rotation argument.

\subsubsection{The scalar estimate and attaching map during rotation}

For the split rotation domain $D_0^f\oplus C_E^f$, give
an input $U$ the norm $X_{\rm split}$ obtained from the
global complete cap/residual norm with constant inner cap,
together with the compact primitive raw-cap norm.
Use the corresponding sum of the global and native compact
geometric target norms, denoted by $Y_{\rm split}$.
The actual normal and matching conditions are retained.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:excision-rotation-potential}
The compact flattening in
Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision} can be chosen
so that, along its rotation path $D_t=(L_t,C_t)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-gauge-coupling}
 \|L_tG_t(C_tG_t)^{-1}C_tU\|_{Y_{\rm split}}
       \leq C(T^{-79/2}+\rho T^N)\|U\|_{X_{\rm split}},
                    \qquad 0\leq t\leq1 .
\end{equation}
Here $G_t$ is the covariant gradient of the negative
zero-form connection of the rotation path. The constant
and the finite exponent $N$ are uniform on the fixed
compact regular patch.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The exact compact base coefficient is in ordinary base
Coulomb gauge, with zero normal trace. On the negative
hemisphere it has a stream function
$A_{E,B}=*_B d_B\Psi$, $\Psi|_{\partial B}=0$.
For the primitive flattening cutoff $\tau$, replace its
base coefficient by
\[
                       A_{E,B}^f=*_B d_B(\tau\Psi).
\]
This preserves zero divergence and normal trace and makes
the coefficient exactly zero on the constant annulus and
outer cap. Its change from affine flattening is bounded,
at every required finite order, by $\rho^2$ times a
polynomial in $T$. Near the attaching point
$\Psi=O(\delta|\zeta|)$, so differentiated cutoffs satisfy
the same bounds. Proposition~
\ref{prop:excision-unrotated-annular-orientations} applies
to this choice and its interpolation with the former one.
It does not change the excision transport.

Write
$\mathsf J=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1\\1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$.
On the identified negative annulus,
\[
             \nabla^t=\nabla^{\rm split}-t\mathsf J\,d\vartheta,
                  \qquad C_tG_t=-(\nabla^t)^*\nabla^t .
\]
The connection on the annulus is exactly
$\operatorname{diag}(\alpha,\alpha)$, which commutes
with $\mathsf J$. Since $d(d\vartheta)=0$ and
$d\vartheta\wedge d\vartheta=0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-curvature}
                 F_{\nabla^t}=F_{\nabla^{\rm split}} .
\end{equation}
This is a curvature identity for an orthogonal connection
on the paired vector bundles; no Lie-algebra automorphism
property of the rotation is assumed.
On perturbation supports $G_t$ is the original gauge
gradient. Gauge covariance there, together with
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-curvature}, gives
\[
 L_tG_t(\phi_0,\phi_E)
       =\bigl([e_0^-,\phi_0],[e_E^-,\phi_E],0\bigr).
\]
The errors are supported on their original flattening
annuli. No new equation-error term occurs where
$d\vartheta\ne0$.

Conjugate zero-forms by $V_t=R(t\vartheta)$, extended over
the paired negative inner discs as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-neumann-gap}.
Put $a=\rho R_1$, $E=N_E^f-N_\alpha^c$, and
$N_\alpha^c=a^{-2}\Delta_h+N_\alpha$.
The coefficients of $E$ are supported where $V_t$ is
the constant rotation $R(t\pi/2)$ and the two metrics
agree. Thus the conjugated scalar operator is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-scalar-blocks}
 \widetilde N_t=
 \begin{pmatrix}
    N_0^f+s^2E&scE\\
    scE&N_\alpha^c+c^2E
 \end{pmatrix},
 \qquad s=\sin(t\pi/2),\quad c=\cos(t\pi/2).
\end{equation}
Its source is $\widetilde\nabla_t^*U'$,
where $U'=V_t^{-1}U$; its off-diagonal zero-order
source terms are included below. Set $X=\|U\|_{X_{\rm split}}$.
Value rotation, conformal invariance of the base
one-form $L^2$ norm and the raw-cap evaluation estimate give
\[
 \|U'_g\|_{\mathcal U_k}\leq CX,\qquad
 \|v'_c\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}\leq C\rho M_1X,\qquad
 \|b'_c\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}\leq C\sqrt T\,X .
\]
For example, the compact input entering the global
component has physical $L^4_BH^k_\Sigma$ norm at most
$\rho^{1/2}$ times a fixed polynomial in $T$ times $X$.
The global input entering the compact component is
measured in its existing raw-cap coordinates on the
same inner disc. No derivative of $V_t$ is used in
these input estimates, and the end parameters are unchanged.

Write $z=V_t^{-1}\phi=(z_g,\eta+\psi)$ with
$\eta=m_hz_c$ and $m_h\psi=0$.
Let $G$ be the sum of the global weak energy norm of
$z_g$ and its local norms in
\eqref{eq:excision-global-potential}, let
$H=\|\eta\|_{H^{k+1}_\Sigma}$, and let
$W=\|\psi\|_{\mathcal W_{k,a}}$, where
$\mathcal W_{k,a}$ is the mean-zero norm in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-compact-potential}.

The compact diagonal in
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-scalar-blocks} has the same
base spectral inverse and surface Schur complement as
that proposition. Multiplication of its perturbation by
$c^2$ changes none of the bounds.
Its first-order base term still has zero average.
The global diagonal has the estimate of
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-global-potential}.
Indeed on the inner region it is the convex combination
of the scalar Laplacians at $\alpha$ and $A_E^f$.
It equals the Laplacian of the interpolated connection
plus the nonnegative variance term
$s^2(1-s^2)B^*B$, $B=A_E^f-\alpha$.
The latter is small in the global $L^2$ coefficient norm.
Weak commutation estimates use $L^2\times L^4\times L^4$,
and the local base estimate bounds it by
$\|B\|_{L^4_BC^{k+2}_\Sigma}^2
 \|z_g\|_{C^0_BH^k_\Sigma}$.
Thus the same absorption argument applies.

We record the off-diagonal estimates to retain their
scale factors. The surface coefficient $B_\Sigma$ is
uniformly bounded, has physical $L^4$ norm
$O(\rho^{1/2}T^N)$, physical $L^2$ norm $O(\rho T^N)$,
and normalized compact average $O(R_1^{-1}+\delta)$,
also at the fixed surface derivative orders used here.
The physical base coefficient has $L^\infty$ norm at
most $C\delta\rho^{-1}T^N$ and $L^4$ norm at most
$C\delta\rho^{-1/2}T^N$, where $\delta=\rho^2T^N$.
Also
\[
 \|\psi\|_{H^1_hH^k_\Sigma}\leq aW,\quad
 \|\psi\|_{L^4_{\rm phys}H^k_\Sigma}\leq Ca^{3/2}W,\quad
 \|\psi\|_{L^2_{\rm phys}H^{k+1}_\Sigma}\leq aW .
\]
Writing $Ez_c$ as a divergence source and a scalar
source in the global equation now gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-global-inequality}
                G\leq CX+C\rho^{1/2}T^NH+C\rho T^NW .
\end{equation}
For instance $B_\Sigma\eta$ has $L^4$ norm at most
$C\rho^{1/2}T^NH$, while
$B_\Sigma\nabla_\Sigma\psi$ has $L^2$ norm at most
$CaW$. The base derivative term costs at most
$C\delta R_1T^NW$. The off-diagonal coefficient
terms in $\widetilde\nabla_t^*U'$ have the same bounds
on the input and are absorbed into $CX$.

For the compact mean-zero equation its inverse costs
$a$ on an $L^2_hH^k_\Sigma$ source. Hence
\[
 a\|Ez_g\|_{L^2_hH^k_\Sigma}\leq C\rho^{1/2}T^NG.
\]
In the surface derivative term the conversion cancels
$a$, leaving
$\|B_\Sigma\nabla_\Sigma z_g\|_{L^2_{\rm phys}H^k_\Sigma}$,
bounded by the two physical $L^4$ factors.
The base derivative term is bounded by
$\|A_{E,B}^f\nabla_Bz_g\|_{L^2_{\rm phys}H^k_\Sigma}
 \leq C\delta\rho^{-1/2}T^NG$.
Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-zero-mean-inequality}
                   W\leq CT^NX+CaH+C\rho^{1/2}T^NG .
\end{equation}

For the compact average the divergence-free choice
is essential:
\[
                 \int_B[A_{E,B}^f,d_hz_g]\,d{\rm vol}_h=0.
\]
There is no normal boundary term, and the coefficient
vanishes near the artificial boundary of the paired
inner region. In the first-order surface term the
$H^{k-1}_\Sigma$ target needs only the
$C^0_BH^k_\Sigma$ bound for $z_g$.
Its coefficient average is $O(R_1^{-1}+\delta)$.
The base square term has coefficient at most
$C\delta^2\rho^{-2}T^N$.
The compact Schur complement therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-mean-inequality}
                   H\leq C\sqrt T\,X
                              +C(R_1^{-1}+\rho T^N)G .
\end{equation}
The ordinary divergence of the compact input has zero
average. Its off-diagonal base coefficient term costs
at most $(e/a^2)\rho M_1X=\rho T^NX$, with
$e\leq R_1\delta T^N$; the surface input costs
$C\sqrt T\,X$. The mean-zero inverse contribution
has the extra factor $a$, as in the compact proof.

Substitution of
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-zero-mean-inequality} and
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-mean-inequality} into
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-global-inequality}
leaves only $\rho^{1/2}$ times fixed polynomials
multiplying $G$. Absorption gives
\[
 G\leq CX,\qquad H\leq C\sqrt T\,X,\qquad
 W\leq CT^NX,\qquad
 \|\psi\|_{H^1_hH^k_\Sigma}\leq C\rho T^NX .
\]
The weak inverse already exists by
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-neumann-gap}; density
extends these estimates from smooth inputs.

On the residual error annulus $V_t=I$.
On the primitive error annulus it is a constant rotation,
so the original compact potential is a combination
of $z_c$ and $z_g$. The former has the compact
mean/mean-zero bounds just proved; the latter has
the global $C^{3/8}_BH^k_\Sigma$ multiplier bound.
The two native annular estimates of
Lemma~\ref{lem:excision-annular-multiplier}, applied
to the exact errors in
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-curvature}, prove
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-gauge-coupling}.
\end{proof}

The constant harmonic sector of the same rotation has
an explicit matching map. Put $V=T_pM_g$.
If $h_0$ is the attaching value supplied by the residual
inverse and $a\in V$ is the free value of the outer
constant cap, its inner values obey
\[
                 (b_0,b_E)=R(t\pi/2)(h_0,a).
\]
This follows by applying the actual annular operator
to $R(t\vartheta)h$: both derivatives of the rotation
cancel and the normal and matching conditions are
preserved. For $\theta=t\pi/2$, the two matching
rows and the row prescribing $a$ have matrix
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-finite-matrix}
 \begin{pmatrix}
 I&0&\sin\theta\,I\\
 0&I&-\cos\theta\,I\\
 0&0&I
 \end{pmatrix}
 \quad\hbox{on }(b_0,b_E,a)\in V^3 .
\end{equation}
Its real determinant is one, and its inverse is
uniformly bounded. The zero-attaching parameter
$k\in K_E$ and its row add an identity block.
At the second endpoint $(b_0,b_E)=(-a,0)$ for
homogeneous sources; undoing the signed interchange
sends the constant inner value to $+a$ and retains
the zero-attaching parameter. This finite map has positive orientation.
Lemma~\ref{lem:excision-outer-constant-cap} supplies the
outer inverse used in the geometric construction.
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-orientation}
then constructs the full inverse in
$X_{\rm split},Y_{\rm split}$, identifies its kernel
with \eqref{eq:excision-rotation-finite-matrix},
and evaluates $\varepsilon_{\rm rot}$.

\subsubsection{Localization of the outer constant cap}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:excision-outer-constant-cap}
The constant compact operator supplies an outer geometric
inverse normalized at its inner puncture, followed by a
based local gauge and stream cutoff, with norm at most
$CT^3$ in the native compact norms. If its source vanishes
on $|w|<cR_0$, its stream commutator at radius comparable
to $R_-$ has target norm at most
$CT^2(R_-/R_0)$ times the source norm.
Its exact and harmonic attaching profiles have size at most
$CT^2$ times that norm. The profile commutator is retained
separately from the stream error.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First note that for the constant cap the field
\[
 \mathcal P_\alpha(e,h)
       =(0,e+h,\kappa_\alpha^{-1}h)
\]
is a geometric kernel field whenever $e$ is exact and
$h$ harmonic on $\Sigma$. Its raw-cap norm is bounded
by $C(\|e\|_{H^k}+|h|)$: the weak derivative is
$d_\alpha(e+h)=0$, not a full derivative of $e$.
The constant compact kernel after exact normalization
is the space of constant harmonic fields.
The constant-cap specialization of the cap inverse,
with zero total attaching value at infinity, has norm
at most $CT$. Subtracting
$\mathcal P_\alpha(E_\alpha b(z_*),H_\alpha b(z_*))$
from its output moves the total attaching normalization
to any other fixed seam point $z_*$.
Evaluation costs $C\sqrt T$, so the new inverse has
norm at most $CT^{3/2}$. It is unique: a homogeneous
field in base Coulomb gauge is a constant profile,
and a profile with zero value at $z_*$ vanishes.

Use $x=w/R_1$ and $a=\rho R_1$.
The metric is $a^2h+g_\Sigma$ on the fixed hemisphere,
Euclidean near $x=0$. Write $U^-=av+b$ and use
\[
 \|U\|_{X_h}=
 \|(v,b,\nu)\|_{H_h^{1+\varepsilon}H^k_\Sigma}
 +a\|d_\alpha v\|_{H_h^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}
 +a\|d_\alpha b\|_{H_h^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma}.
\]
Its normalized target is $Y_h=H_h^\varepsilon H^k_\Sigma$,
with the positive finite factor. Coordinate conversion gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-fixed-hemisphere-conversion}
 \|F_h\|_{Y_h}\leq CR_1^{C\varepsilon}\|F\|_{Y_{\rm cap}},
 \qquad
 \|U\|_{X_{\rm cap}^{\rm raw}}
 \leq C(1+\log R_1)^{1/2}R_1^{C\varepsilon}\|U_h\|_{X_h}.
\end{equation}
For the target, one-form pullback and the scalar conformal
weight preserve $L^2$ up to fixed metric constants.
Their $H^1$ bounds are fixed powers of $R_1$;
interpolation proves the fractional estimate.
For scalar domain fields the Dirichlet integral is
conformally invariant. The pulled volume has density
at most $CR_1^2/(1+R_1^2r^2)^2$ near its concentrating
point. Integrating the annular $H^1$ restriction
bound gives the displayed square-root logarithm.
An $H^2$ chain-rule bound and interpolation give
the strong fractional estimate.
The original normalized base one-form is $R_1/M_1$
times pullback of $v$. One-form $L^2$ is conformally
invariant; its exterior and co-exterior derivative
norms, after this normalization, are bounded because
the conformal dilation is at most $CR_1$ and
$R_1/M_1=O(R_1^{-1})$.
The normal-trace Gaffney estimate and interpolation
give its strong bound. The weak rows have the
same conformal target weights. In particular
\eqref{eq:excision-fixed-hemisphere-conversion}
contains no negative power of $\rho$.

Apply the constant-cap inverse at $x=0$ in this
fixed geometry, with parameter $a$. Its output
has $X_h$ norm at most $CT^{3/2}\|F_h\|$ and
base divergence zero. To impose full Coulomb gauge
solve
\[
       (a^{-2}\Delta_h+N_\alpha)\phi=d_\alpha^*b,
                         \qquad \partial_n\phi=0,
\]
and subtract $(a^{-1}d_h\phi,d_\alpha\phi,0)$.
This projection is uniformly bounded in $X_h$.
For a Neumann base eigenvalue $\mu$ and surface
frequency $\lambda$, its two gradient multipliers are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-constant-coulomb-multipliers}
 \frac{a^2\lambda^2}{\mu+a^2\lambda^2}\leq1,\qquad
 \frac{a\sqrt\mu\,\lambda}{\mu+a^2\lambda^2}\leq\frac12 .
\end{equation}
Multiplying the second by $a\lambda$ gives at most
$\sqrt\mu$, controlled by the strong base norm of
$b$. The added surface field is exact, so its
exterior derivative vanishes.
These estimates hold in the stated fractional norms:
on the fixed doubled base, scalar Neumann fields
extend evenly and their gradients have odd normal
and even tangential components. At
$1+\varepsilon\leq5/4$ no normal derivative trace
is required of the scalar input. The doubled
Hodge Laplacian intertwines the scalar gradient.
This identifies the spectral and Sobolev norms
uniformly at the orders used here.
This bounded graph projection uses the constant
connection and is not asserted for the primitive.

The source-free radius in $h$ coordinates is
$L=cR_0/R_1$, with physical radius $aL=c\rho R_0$.
Evaluation and the preceding inverse give the
rescaled size bound $B=CT^2\|F_h\|$.
The homogeneous full-Coulomb estimate and based
local gauge construction used in the residual
inverse apply here with surface mass $aL\lambda$.
Their constants are uniform in that mass.
The local gauge variation in $h$ coordinates is
$(d_h\zeta,a\,d_\alpha\zeta,0)$.
Its strong and weak cap rows cost at most
$CL^{-\varepsilon}B$; for the weak base row use
$a d_\alpha d_h\zeta=d_h(a d_\alpha\zeta)$.
It restores base divergence zero locally and makes
the base value zero at $x=0$, while preserving
the surface and positive attaching values.
These values have size at most $CB$ by the
rescaled estimate, without another evaluation loss.

Subtract their constant profile $P$ locally and
write $U^0=U^G-P$. Every component vanishes at
zero. Its base stream satisfies $\psi(LZ)=L\Psi(Z)$
with $\Psi(0)=d\Psi(0)=0$. At
$r=c'R_-/R_1$, the Taylor and stream estimates give
\[
 \|U^0(r\,\cdot)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}H^k}
       +aL\|U^0(r\,\cdot)\|_{H^{1+\varepsilon}H^{k+1}}
          \leq C(r/L)B,\qquad
 \|\Psi((r/L)\,\cdot)\|_{H^{2+\varepsilon}H^k}
          \leq C(r/L)^2B .
\]
The extra surface derivative is weighted by the
physical radius $aL$.
For the outer stream cutoff put
$V=\mathcal C_-U^0+\chi P$, where $\chi$ vanishes
inside $r$ and equals one outside $2r$.
Since $P$ is a geometric kernel field and
$\chi F_h=F_h$, direct expansion gives
\[
 C_\alpha^{\rm geo}V-F_h
        =E_-+[C_\alpha^{\rm geo},\chi]P,\qquad
 E_-=C_\alpha^{\rm geo}\mathcal C_-U^0
                         -\chi C_\alpha^{\rm geo}U^0 .
\]
The displayed local bounds give
$\|E_-\|_{Y_h}\leq Cr^{-\varepsilon}(r/L)B$.
All fractional radius factors are bounded, so
this is $CT^2(R_-/R_0)\|F_h\|$.
The cutoff field has $X_h$ norm at most $CB$.
Equation~\eqref{eq:excision-fixed-hemisphere-conversion}
then gives the coarse native bound $CT^3$.
The profile commutator remains for cancellation
with the inserted inner profile.

A two-component phase on the disjoint rotation
annulus obeys the same bounds. Its target
multiplier has norm $CR_2^{C\varepsilon}$.
The term with its domain derivative is estimated
by the rescaled value bound $B$ on that annulus,
before the coordinate conversion. The phase is
constant on the stream cutoff annulus.
Thus it adds no larger power of $T$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The rotation inverse and its orientation}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:excision-rotation-orientation}
On the fixed compact regular primitive patches the
rotation path admits geometric right inverses satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-geometric-inverse}
                  L_tQ_t=I,\qquad
        \|Q_t\|_{Y_{\rm split}\to X_{\rm split}}\leq CT^3 .
\end{equation}
Its ordinary operators are onto, and their determinant
transport agrees with the compact complex and flattened
analytic kernel orientations. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-evaluated-parities}
                    \varepsilon_{\rm rot}=+1,\qquad
                    \varepsilon_{\rm exc}=+1 .
\end{equation}
The coherent DFL comparison is a further assertion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Use four reference pieces: the inner constant and
exact primitive caps with zero total attaching
value, the fixed residual with its complete
source and finite end variables, and the outer
constant cap of Lemma~\ref{lem:excision-outer-constant-cap}.
The inner blocks use the cap inverses and stream
localization in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-geometric-inverse}.
The fourth block includes the full-Coulomb and
based local gauge steps just proved.

On $|w|\geq R_-$ use the paired matching-domain
and target identifications with the common
$\alpha$ model. Denote these by $P^X,P^Y$ for
each background. In physical coordinates the
outer transport is
\[
 \mathcal A_t^X=P_{\rm actual}^X R(t\vartheta)
                            (P_{\rm outer}^X)^{-1},\qquad
 \mathcal A_t^Y=P_{\rm actual}^Y R(t\vartheta)
                            (P_{\rm outer}^Y)^{-1}.
\]
It is used only outside the inner cutoff.
For the inner cap pair use the phase
$R(t(\vartheta-\pi/2))$, which is the identity
on the deep inner region and extends there
on the actual inner bundles.
No identification with a constant mixed
domain is made over a nonconstant core.
