On the support of $d\vartheta$ the actual
background is exactly constant and $P_{\rm actual}=I$.
The derivative of either phase cancels precisely
the $tK$ term of $L_t$. All other derivatives
are those of the paired background identifications.
At both stream cutoff annuli the phase is constant.

Partition every source as
$F_{\rm in}=\theta_0F$ and
$F_{\rm out}=(1-\theta_0)F$ at radius $\rho R_0$.
The native cutoff/source conversions are
bounded on the complete target.
Convert $F_{\rm in}$ to the two inner cap
targets, apply their zero-attaching inverses
and the stream cutoff at affine radius $R_1/4$,
then apply the inner transport. Call this
map $P_{\rm in}$.
For the outer pair use the source
\[
                     G_{\rm out}
                       =(\mathcal A_t^Y)^{-1}F_{\rm out},
\]
extended by zero on the smaller source-free
discs, with all native row conversions included.
Its norm is at most $C\|F\|_{Y_{\rm split}}$.
Apply the fixed residual inverse with zero
Coulomb source, retaining its exact end
parameters, and the outer constant-cap
construction. Put the two outputs in their
specified based local gauges. Their attaching
data satisfy
\[
 \|e_0\|_{H^k}+|h_0|+\|e_c\|_{H^k}+|h_c|
                   \leq CT^2\|F\|_{Y_{\rm split}}=:B .
\]
The residual alone has the smaller bound
$C\sqrt T\|F\|$. No second evaluation loss is used.

Let $P=(P_0,P_c)$ be the reference attaching
profiles. The harmonic part of $P_0$ can
have nonzero geometric source; $P_c$ is a
constant geometric kernel field.
Rotate their attaching data by $R(t\pi/2)$
and lift them to the inner constant and
primitive cap profiles, denoting the result
by $B_t$. These lifts use only bounded
sines and cosines. They are exact geometric
kernel fields on the region where inserted.
For the cutoff $\chi$ at $R_-$ define
\[
 V_j=\mathcal C_-(U_j^G-P_j)+\chi P_j,\qquad
                 P_{\rm out}F=\mathcal A_t^XV+(1-\chi)B_t .
\]
Both terms satisfy the actual matching and
normal conditions. The outer finite end
parameters are unchanged.

Set
$E_{-,j}=L_j\mathcal C_-(U_j^G-P_j)
                    -\chi L_j(U_j^G-P_j)$.
Since $\chi G_j=G_j$, the exact identity is
$L_jV_j=G_j+E_{-,j}+[L_j,\chi]P_j$.
The terms $\chi L_jP_j$ cancel, including
the nonzero residual harmonic-profile source.
With
$K_t=L_t\mathcal A_t^X-\mathcal A_t^YL_{\rm out}$,
principal base-symbol intertwining gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-full-source-identity}
 L_tP_{\rm out}F-F_{\rm out}
      =\mathcal A_t^YE_-+K_tV
                    +[L_t,\chi](\mathcal A_t^XP-B_t).
\end{equation}
The last inner source vanishes because $B_t$
is inserted entirely in the exact inner region.
This identity applies to every source in the
same completed target.

We retain the quantitative error counts.
The two inner errors are bounded by
\[
 C\varepsilon^{-3}R_0^8
       (R_1^{-1+2\varepsilon}+R_2^{-2+C\varepsilon})\|F\|
                                      +C\rho T^N\|F\|.
\]
These are the stream and paired inner
background estimates in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:gluing-geometric-inverse}.
The phase derivative has canceled before
these estimates are taken; target rotation
has bounded fractional norm.
For \eqref{eq:excision-rotation-full-source-identity},
the stream term is at most $C(R_-/R_0)B$,
by the residual estimate and
Lemma~\ref{lem:excision-outer-constant-cap}.
Every physical outer coefficient triple
has rescaled $H^1_BH^k_\Sigma$ norm at most
$CB$ on the relevant annuli.
For the constant cap its physical base
coefficient is controlled by the fixed-$h$
construction; the old $a/M_1$ bound is not
substituted for this fact.
The paired dyadic estimate therefore gives
$\|K_tV\|\leq CR_-^{-1}B+C\rho T^NB$.
The profile difference at $R_-$ has zero
attaching value. Its surface and positive
terms are $O(R_-^{-1}+\rho R_-)$, and its
physical base term is
$O(\rho R_-+(\delta/\rho)R_-^{-2})$, times $B$.
The commutator calculation gives
\[
 \|[L_t,\chi](\mathcal A_t^XP-B_t)\|
       \leq C(R_-^{-1}+\rho R_-+(\delta/\rho)R_-^{-2})B .
\]
Only the $H^k$ norm of each exact attaching
datum is used: its gauge potential gains
one surface derivative, and the base
dependence of its profile has a $C^0H^k$
bound without another evaluation factor.

For the chosen radii the inner errors are
$O(T^{-13})\|F\|$ and the three outer errors
are $O(T^{-2})\|F\|$, apart from exponentially
small polynomial terms. Thus, for
$P_t=P_{\rm in}+P_{\rm out}$,
\[
 \|L_tP_t-I\|\leq CT^{-2}+C\rho T^N=o(1),
                         \qquad \|P_t\|\leq CT^3 .
\]
The domain bound includes the local gauges,
profiles and the phase's rescaled value
bound, before coordinate conversion.
All error sources belong to $Y_{\rm split}$.
The Neumann series $Q_t=P_t(L_tP_t)^{-1}$
proves \eqref{eq:excision-rotation-geometric-inverse}.
These maps are continuous in $t$ at fixed scale.

By Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-potential},
\[
 \|L_tG_t(C_tG_t)^{-1}C_tQ_t\|
                       \leq CT^{-73/2}+C\rho T^N=o(1).
\]
Write $P_t^C=I-G_t(C_tG_t)^{-1}C_t$, to
distinguish it from the geometric parametrix.
Then
\[
 Q_t^C=P_t^CQ_t
          (I-L_tG_t(C_tG_t)^{-1}C_tQ_t)^{-1}
\]
solves both ordinary rows on the dense
geometric source class. At fixed scale
$P_t^C$ maps ordinary $H^1$ to itself.
The ordinary path is Fredholm by the
compact-commutator construction, and removing
the invertible Neumann block preserves
Fredholm closed range. Hence $D_t$ is onto.
Its kernel has dimension $m=4+\dim_{\mathbb R}V$.

To orient that kernel use parameters
$(k,a)\in K_E\oplus V$.
For $a$, take outer profiles $(0,\mathcal P_\alpha(0,a))$
and inner harmonic values
$(-\sin\theta\,a,\cos\theta\,a)$,
where $\theta=t\pi/2$.
Join them by the preceding construction,
using for these finite fields the radius
$R_b=T^{20}$ in place of $R_-$.
It lies between $R_0$ and $R_2$.
For $k$ use the finite zero-attaching cap
derivatives and their inner localization.
Their sum $W_t(k,a)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-rotation-finite-frame-error}
                    \|W_t\|\leq C,\qquad
               \|L_tW_t\|\leq CT^{-20}+C\rho T^N .
\end{equation}
The $k$ part has the stronger $T^{-40}$
error. These are fixed finite smooth
profiles, so no growing-source inverse
factor is used in their norm.

Let $\ell_E$ be the four averaged negative
holonomy differentials on the fixed inner
$E$ region, and $\ell_\infty$ positive
evaluation at the outer compact attaching
point. Write the limiting compact
observation matrix as
$\ell_E(k+j_Eh)=M_Ek+N_Eh$ and set
\[
       \ell_t=(\ell_E-\cos\theta\,N_E\ell_\infty,\ell_\infty).
\]
It has graph norm at most $C\sqrt T$, and
$\ell_tW_t=\operatorname{diag}(M_E,I_V)+o(1)$.
It annihilates $G_t\phi$ in the potential
regularity class just proved: the negative
observation lies where the gradient is the
ordinary split gauge gradient, and the
positive observation has zero gauge variation.
The averaged-observation approximation
argument applies as before.

Put
\[
 w_t=(I-Q_tL_t)W_t,\qquad
 J_t=P_t^Cw_t+
        Q_t^CL_tG_t(C_tG_t)^{-1}C_tw_t .
\]
Both ordinary rows annihilate $J_t$.
The geometric correction in its observations
costs at most $\sqrt T\,T^3T^{-20}=T^{-33/2}$,
and the last term costs at most
$\sqrt T\,T^3T^{-79/2}=T^{-36}$.
Thus $\ell_tJ_t=\operatorname{diag}(M_E,I_V)+o(1)$.
Kernel dimension makes $J_t$ a continuous
basis of the whole ordinary kernel.
Projection onto that kernel followed by
$J_t^{-1}$ supplies bounded finite rows
with invertible augmented operators.

At $t=0$ this is the compact complex
splitting $K_E\oplus V$, with the invertible
residual factor. At $t=1$ the signed
interchange sends the constant inner value
$-a$ to $+a$ on the original constant cap
and sends $k$ to the glued primitive.
The latter has the same limiting
observations as the flattened analytic
frame. Its transition is orientation
preserving. The attaching map is precisely
\eqref{eq:excision-rotation-finite-matrix};
the even real indices add no factor
permutation sign. Hence
$\varepsilon_{\rm rot}=+1$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-correction-orientation}
then gives $\varepsilon_{\rm exc}=+1$.
\end{proof}

The actual excision transport now agrees with the
analytic kernel map and the gluing derivative in
the stated regular sector. The remaining local
sign comparison is with DFL's coherent prescription.
In particular the physical signs still satisfy
$\eta_g=\sigma_g\varepsilon_{\rm coh}$;
we next evaluate the genus-two factor in the selected
sphere normalization.

\subsection{The genus-two coherent sign}

The comparison in this subsection takes place at a fixed
strictly positive metric scale. Its sphere lies a fixed
positive distance from the seam.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:compact-interior-sphere-orientation}
Fix a very stable genus-two attaching value and one of
its four sublines. The complex orientation of the compact
primitive determinant agrees, by a path of actual matching
configurations, with the canonical orientation of a positive
generator sphere inserted into the constant mixed cap.
The same assertion holds after removing the complex
attaching factor $T_pM_2$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write the extension line as $F:\mathbb P^1\to M_2$,
with coordinate $\xi$ and $F(0)=p$. Its tangent bundle
splits as $\mathcal O(2)\oplus\mathcal O\oplus\mathcal O$
by Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-sphere-limit}.
Fix $a$ strictly inside the positive disc and put
\[
                     f_b(z)=F\bigl(b/(z-a)\bigr),\qquad b\ne0.
\]
All surface fibers of its extension bundle $E_b$ are stable.
For $\pi:\mathbb P^1\times\Sigma\to\mathbb P^1$, stability
gives $R^0\pi_*\operatorname{End}_0E_b=0$ and
$R^1\pi_*\operatorname{End}_0E_b=f_b^*TM_2$.
There are no higher fiber cohomology groups. Hence Leray gives
the complex-linear identification
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fully-stable-leray-orientation}
 H^1(\mathbb P^1\times\Sigma,\operatorname{End}_0E_b)
                         =H^0(\mathbb P^1,f_b^*TM_2).
\end{equation}
The kernel map of Proposition~\ref{prop:fredholm-dolbeault}
followed by this identification is the actual map
variation on the positive disc.

Let $C_\alpha^\perp$ be the nonharmonic part of the
constant compact mixed operator. It is an isomorphism
in the ordinary spaces by the constant calculation in
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-mixed-index}. Choose global
holomorphic sections $\sigma_1,\sigma_2,\sigma_3$ of
$F^*TM_2$: take $\sigma_1=\partial_\xi$ on $\mathcal O(2)$
and frames of the two trivial summands. They form a
frame near $p$; $\sigma_1$ has a double zero at $\xi=\infty$.
Their pullbacks give a holomorphic map
$S_b:\underline{\mathbb C}^3\to f_b^*TM_2$.
It is a uniformly bounded invertible frame outside any
fixed disc about $a$ when $b$ is small.

Use the reference operator
\[
             D_b^*=C_\alpha^\perp
                                  \oplus\bar\partial_{f_b^*TM_2}.
\]
On the negative disc express its harmonic part in the
holomorphic frame $S_b$ and match its trace to the
positive part. This gives a well-defined ordinary
mixed reference domain. The operator is onto and has
real kernel dimension ten.

Here only a localized inverse estimate is needed.
Fix a positive disc about $a$ disjoint from the seam,
and let $\Omega$ contain its exterior and the whole
negative domain. Allow sources in a fixed closed
subset of $\Omega$, denoting their target by $Y_\Omega$.
There is an inverse on these sources with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sphere-localized-source-inverse}
 D_b^*Q_\Omega F=F,\qquad
 \|Q_\Omega F\|_{H^1(\Omega)}\le C\|F\|_{L^2},
                                      \quad F\in Y_\Omega,
\end{equation}
uniformly as $b\to0$. On nonharmonic sources use
$(C_\alpha^\perp)^{-1}$. On harmonic sources use
\[
                     Q_\Omega F=S_bQ_0(S_b^{-1}F),
\]
where the coefficient source is extended by zero across
the disc and $Q_0$ is a fixed right inverse for
$\bar\partial$ on the trivial rank-three sphere bundle.
Such an inverse exists since $H^{0,1}(\mathbb P^1)=0$.
Holomorphicity of $S_b$ proves the equation exactly.
Its coefficients are uniformly controlled on $\Omega$.
Inside a smaller source-free disc the scalar solution
has a bounded $C^1$ norm by the interior holomorphic
estimate. The sections $S_b$ have uniformly bounded
values and Dirichlet energy, being pullbacks by a
degree-one conformal map. Thus the product is an
ordinary $H^1$ section globally. This also bounds
its values at any fixed finite set of points away
from the source support and from $a$.

Choose $\chi=0$ on the sphere core and $\chi=1$
near the seam and on the negative hemisphere.
On the positive side take the actual configuration path
\[
                   u_{s,b}(z)
              =F\bigl((1-s\chi(z))b/(z-a)\bigr),
                              \qquad 0\le s\le1.
\]
On the negative side use Dirichlet reconstruction
for the holomorphic family with residue $(1-s)b$.
Its boundary agrees with $u_{s,b}$, including at $s=1$,
when the negative connection is $\alpha$.
At the fixed metric scale, Dirichlet invertibility
at the constant pair and smooth dependence give
$A_{s,b}-\alpha=O(|b|)$ in every fixed $C^k$ norm.
The positive map equals $f_b$ on the whole core;
outside it all its derivatives differ from the
constant map by $O(|b|)$.

Use the smooth exact/harmonic seam transports and
complex-linear positive bundle identifications,
equal to the identity on the core, to identify
the actual domains with the reference domain.
On the exterior these maps send
$\sigma_j(f_b)$ to $\sigma_j(u_{s,b})$ and differ
from their constant values by $O(|b|)$.
The normal condition is retained, and Coulomb
gauge remains an output row. The full operators become
\begin{equation}\label{eq:interior-sphere-supported-perturbation}
 D_{s,b}=D_b^*+K_{s,b},\qquad
 \operatorname{supp}K_{s,b}\subset\Omega,\qquad
 \|K_{s,b}U\|_{L^2}\le C|b|\|U\|_{H^1(\Omega)}.
\end{equation}
This includes all negative surface derivatives and
the first-order terms from the transports. It is a
fixed-scale estimate; its constant need not be uniform
in the physical gluing scale.

Equations~\eqref{eq:sphere-localized-source-inverse}
and~\eqref{eq:interior-sphere-supported-perturbation}
give $\|K_{s,b}Q_\Omega\|\le C|b|$ on $Y_\Omega$.
For any fixed-$b$ right inverse $Q_b^*$ of $D_b^*$,
\[
 Q_{s,b}=Q_b^*-Q_\Omega
       (I+K_{s,b}Q_\Omega)^{-1}K_{s,b}Q_b^*
\]
is a right inverse of $D_{s,b}$. No uniform bound
for $Q_b^*$ on arbitrary sources is being assumed.

A complex basis of the reference kernel is
\[
 S_be_1,\quad \frac{S_be_1}{z-a},\quad
 \frac{S_be_1}{(z-a)^2},\quad S_be_2,\quad S_be_3.
\]
The double zero of $\sigma_1$ cancels these apparent
poles. This basis is uniformly bounded on $\Omega$,
though its global norms may grow.
Observe its tangent component at three distinct
fixed positive points in the unchanged core
neighborhood, away from $a$, and its two normal
components at one of them. In the frame $S_b$ the
matrix is a fixed invertible matrix in the powers
of $(z-a)^{-1}$, together with $I_2$.
The localized inverse has bounded values at these
points by the preceding interior estimate.

For the real frame $W_b$ underlying this complex
basis, the corrected frame
\[
 J_{s,b}=W_b-Q_\Omega
          (I+K_{s,b}Q_\Omega)^{-1}K_{s,b}W_b
\]
is annihilated by $D_{s,b}$. Its observed matrix
differs by $O(|b|)$ from the reference matrix.
Surjectivity and index therefore make it a frame
of the full ordinary kernel throughout the path.
At $s=0$, \eqref{eq:fully-stable-leray-orientation}
and the positive observations show that this is
the compact complex orientation.

At $s=1$ the negative connection and positive
seam collar are constant. The operator splits
exactly into $C_\alpha^\perp$ and a complex
Cauchy--Riemann operator on the closed harmonic
sphere. For the smooth map obtained by extending
$u_{1,b}$ constantly over the negative disc,
a K\"ahler connection gives such a complex-linear
operator; other linearization conventions give
its canonical orientation by lower-order homotopy.
In the reference identifications, $K_{1,b}$ has
no nonharmonic coupling and is complex-linear
on the harmonic part. The corrected frame is
therefore complex-linear at this endpoint.
It has the canonical sphere orientation.

Removing the same complex attaching factor
$T_pM_2$ at both ends preserves the comparison.
The full-rank parameter set is connected; the
complex compact orientation varies continuously
on it. Negative rank drop is also regular and
is obtained by continuity. This extends the
comparison from the small-$b$ family to the
stated branch. No extension through positive
rank drop or uniform nodal collar is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:genus-two-coherent-sign}
Choose the marked genus-two component for the sphere
normalization of \cite[Remark~4.76]{DaeFukLip21a}.
For every regular residual and each of the four
very-stable branches in
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:genus-two-evaluated-physical-signs}
             \varepsilon_{{\rm coh},2,j}=+1,\qquad
                              \eta_{2,j}=+1.
\end{equation}
The corresponding actual finite-center artificial
boundary count is $4n(x_0)$.
This is a local count in the selected normalization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We first record the needed naturality of determinant
transport. The construction in
Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision}
works in compact families of smooth configurations
at positive scales, using a common finite target
stabilization. For two surgeries on disjoint constant
collars, retain all pieces in one labelled direct
sum and use the signed interior interchanges.
After relabelling, the two commutator defects
$K_1,K_2$ have disjoint supports on a common
ordinary domain. The family
\[
                         D_{r,t}=D_{0,0}+rK_1+tK_2,
                                  \qquad (r,t)\in[0,1]^2,
\]
is Fredholm, since both defects are compact.
Its edges give the two surgery orders. A common
finite stabilization over the square shows that
the two orientation transports agree. Inserting
an intermediate constant collar gives the same
statement for nested cuts. The kernel-first
determinant exact sequence cancels intermediate
factors associatively. The sphere and attaching
factors have even real dimensions.

Consequently, when a mixed cap is constant on
its negative side and near its seam, excising it
agrees with first removing its constant part and
then inserting its positive sphere. The constant
nonharmonic block is invertible; its harmonic
block and the sphere block carry their complex
orientations. The scalar rotations and harmonic
matching sequence are complex-linear, so they
add no sign.

DFL's selected sphere compatibility applies to
an arbitrary mixed residual as follows. Flatten
a small matching neighborhood and insert the
sphere strictly inside its positive half.
Use \cite[Lemma~4.64]{DaeFukLip21a} to deform
the residual to a symplectically constant pair.
Carry the insertion during this deformation:
over the compact path choose a common small
matching disc, flatten it in the actual charts,
and transport the sphere by a target isotopy
along its attaching value. This uses smooth
configurations and the sphere's homotopy class;
it requires no family of holomorphic spheres.
The flattening is trivial at a symplectically
constant endpoint.

At that endpoint the residual is a gauge
cylinder followed by a constant mixed pair.
Move the sphere through the constant positive
region into the outgoing strip. Cylinder gluing,
mixed-to-strip gluing and sphere insertion have
disjoint collars, so the preceding determinant
square identifies their compositions.
Section~4.7 of \cite{DaeFukLip21a} defines the
strip orientation by gluing to this constant
mixed pair, whose operator is invertible.
It defines the mixed orientation by the gauge
cylinder, compatibly with cylinder composition.
Remarks~2.39 and~4.76 of that source make the
selected positive generator sphere insertion
orientation preserving for these strip
orientations. It is therefore orientation
preserving for the mixed pair. Transporting
back along the simultaneous configuration
homotopy gives the residual DFL orientation
times the canonical sphere factor. This uses
the coherent prescription, not orientability alone.

Now move a full-rank primitive in the chosen
branch to the family in
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-interior-sphere-orientation}.
That lemma identifies its compact complex
orientation with the pure positive-sphere cap
orientation. Family naturality of excision
commutes with this cap path relative to the
flattened residual. Thus its coherent factor
is $+1$. This calculation can be made at any
fixed positive scale, then transported in the
smooth configuration family. It makes no
simultaneous limiting assertion in the sphere
residue and physical gluing scale. At the
original glued solution,
Proposition~\ref{prop:excision-rotation-orientation}
and the correction comparison identify this
configuration-level transport with the actual
gluing orientation.

Negative rank drop follows by continuity in
the regular compact line. Each of the four
extension lines is a positive generator in
the same selected component, so the argument
applies to all four using one coherent
normalization, independently of the residual.
Finally $\sigma_2=+1$ and
$\varepsilon_{\rm exc}=+1$ give
$\eta_{2,j}=+1$ in
\eqref{eq:primitive-orientation-three-factors}.
Each branch has seam degree one, so
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation}
gives $4n(x_0)$.
\end{proof}

Reversing the single index-four normalization
reverses all these relative signs, as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:primitive-orientation-normalization}.
The torus comparison is proved in
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-integral-geometric-comparison};
simultaneous genus-two positivity is proved in
Proposition~\ref{prop:simultaneous-integral-local-signs},
including rationally acyclic cases. The global
boundary identity is proved below in
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}.

\subsection{Interior excision and the closed relative orientation}
\label{sec:closed-relative-orientation}

The following two lemmas isolate an ordinary closed
orientation calculation relevant to the remaining torus
comparison. They do not evaluate the mixed coherent sign.
All scales in the excision construction are positive.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}
Let $X=\PP^1\times\Sigma_g$, let $\Lambda$ have degree one,
and let $E_0$ be the pullback of a stable rank-two bundle
with determinant $\Lambda$. Let $E$ be a primitive extension
\[
 0\longrightarrow\cO_{\PP^1}(1)\boxtimes L
 \longrightarrow E
 \longrightarrow\cO_{\PP^1}\boxtimes(\Lambda L^{-1})
 \longrightarrow0,\qquad \deg L=0.
\]
On the relative determinant line of the closed ASD
operators $Q_E,Q_0$, Donaldson's orientations for these
integral lifts and the K\"ahler homology orientation satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:closed-primitive-orientation}
 o_E^{\rm D}\otimes(o_0^{\rm D})^*
       =(-1)^g o_E^{\CC}\otimes(o_0^{\CC})^*.
\end{equation}
The right-hand orientations are induced by the closed
Dolbeault operators and transported through the space of
connections. The relative sign is unchanged by line
twists of either rank-two lift.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $h,f$ for the positive generators pulled back
from $\PP^1,\Sigma_g$. Then $h^2=f^2=0$,
$\langle hf,[X]\rangle=1$, and
$K_X=-2h+(2g-2)f$. The constant and primitive bundles have
first Chern classes $f$ and $h+f$, and second Chern
classes $0$ and $hf$, respectively.

The closed K\"ahler orientation comparison is
\[
 o_c^{\rm D}
   =(-1)^{\langle c^2+K_Xc,[X]\rangle/2}o_c^{\CC};
\]
see \cite[Proposition~3.25 and its proof,
pp.~418--419]{Donaldson87}. Its exponent is the parity
$\chi(\mathcal L_c^{-1})-\chi(\cO_X)$ in the reduction
calculation, evaluated by Riemann--Roch. The K\"ahler
operator identification and its compatibility with
ordinary instanton addition are given in Section~3(c)
and Proposition~3.20 of that source. This orientation
is defined for the determinant operator without requiring
an ASD solution at the chosen connection.

The two exponents are
\[
 \frac12\langle f^2+K_Xf,[X]\rangle=-1,\qquad
 \frac12\langle(h+f)^2+K_X(h+f),[X]\rangle=g-1.
\]
Taking their difference proves
\eqref{eq:closed-primitive-orientation}. In particular,
the constant reference contributes a minus sign.
For a replacement $c\mapsto c+2a$, the exponent changes
by $\langle2ca+2a^2+K_Xa,[X]\rangle$, which is even
since $K_X$ is even. This proves the lift assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:interior-oriented-excision}
Let $C_E,C_0$ be the compact primitive and constant
mixed operators, with their retained clutching
identification. The contraction and interior cut in
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-mixed-index} determine a
continuous isomorphism of orientation double covers
\begin{equation}\label{eq:interior-oriented-excision}
 \operatorname{Or}(\det C_E\otimes\det Q_0)
 \cong
 \operatorname{Or}(\det C_0\otimes\det Q_E).
\end{equation}
It is compatible with homotopies of those choices
on a fixed small parameter chart. Combined with
Proposition~\ref{prop:actual-determinant-excision},
it gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:global-to-closed-excision}
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_{\rm gl}\otimes\det Q_0)
 \cong
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_{\rm res}\otimes\det Q_E).
\end{equation}
The construction retains the original framing;
independence from another clutching component is not
asserted.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Transport the compact determinant along the
configuration path which makes the positive map
constant and the negative collar equal to the
attaching flat connection. The boundary identification
is transported, and is not extended over the negative
hemisphere. The closed primitive Pontryagin number
therefore remains $-2$.

Cut on a circle strictly inside this negative collar,
and cap both negative interiors with the same ordinary
cap. The signed two-by-two rotation in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-mixed-index} interchanges these
interiors in the sum of one mixed and one closed
operator. It is the identity near the complete mixed
seam and on the positive component. Consequently it
preserves the actual mixed Sobolev domain. After the
source and target identifications, the two operators
differ by a smooth zero-order commutator $K$, compact
from $H^1$ to $L^2$.

Use the Fredholm path $D_t=D_{\rm start}+tK$.
A finite-dimensional target supplement $F$ makes
$(D_t,\iota_F)$ onto for every $t$: take a finite
sum of local cokernel supplements over the compact
interval. Its kernels form a vector bundle, and
the exact sequences give
\[
 \det D_t\cong
 \det\ker(D_t,\iota_F)\otimes(\det F)^*.
\]
Orientation transport in this bundle gives
\eqref{eq:interior-oriented-excision}, including
any cokernel crossings. Enlarging $F$ cancels the
additional factor in the exact sequence. The same
construction over a square proves invariance under
the stated homotopies. It requires no regularity
along the flattening or the compact-perturbation path.

The actual mixed excision gives
$\det D_{\rm gl}\otimes\det C_0
\cong\det D_{\rm res}\otimes\det C_E$
on orientation double covers. Tensor with $\det Q_0$,
apply \eqref{eq:interior-oriented-excision}, and
cancel $\det C_0$. This proves
\eqref{eq:global-to-closed-excision}. The compact
and closed indices are $6g-6$ and $6g-2$, all even,
so these cancellations introduce no odd interchange.
For two compatible surgery orders, retain all
pieces in one labelled sum and use the Fredholm
square $D_{0,0}+rK_1+tK_2$ on disjoint collars.
Its stabilized determinant has trivial boundary
monodromy. Nested cuts are separated by an
intermediate constant collar, whose determinant
then cancels. This proves the required composition
compatibility.
\end{proof}

To specify the remaining comparison, put
\[
 L_{\rm mix}=\det C_E\otimes(\det C_0)^*,\quad
 L_{\rm cl}=\det Q_E\otimes(\det Q_0)^*,\quad
 L_{\rm gl}=\det D_{\rm gl}\otimes(\det D_{\rm res})^*.
\]
Let $J:L_{\rm mix}\to L_{\rm cl}$ and
$G:L_{\rm gl}\to L_{\rm mix}$ be the two orientation
transports just used. Give $L_{\rm mix}$ its
regular-kernel complex orientation and $L_{\rm cl}$
the closed complex orientation. Define the signs
$\delta_{\rm cap},\delta_{\rm ref}$ by
\[
 J(o_{\rm mix}^{\CC})=\delta_{\rm cap}o_{\rm cl}^{\CC},
 \qquad
 (JG)(o_{\rm gl}^{\rm DFL})
                     =\delta_{\rm ref}o_{\rm cl}^{\rm D},
\]
where the DFL orientation system is held fixed.
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:coherent-closed-two-signs}
 \varepsilon_{{\rm coh},g}
          =(-1)^g\delta_{\rm cap}\delta_{\rm ref}.
\end{equation}
Since $\sigma_1=-1$ and $\varepsilon_{\rm exc}=+1$,
the desired torus boundary sign is
$\eta_1=\delta_{\rm cap}\delta_{\rm ref}$.
Neither new sign is evaluated by these lemmas.
They are locally constant on the specified regular
framed families; changing $J$ by a sign changes both
and leaves their product unchanged.

In particular, the smooth kernel identification in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fredholm-dolbeault} does not
compute $\delta_{\rm cap}$ along a flattening path
which need not consist of solutions. The mixed
boundary domain is not complex-linear, even though
the interior rotation is scalar. A determinant
comparison compatible with the compact Dolbeault
orientation is needed; the next subsection constructs
one in genus one. Likewise,
$\delta_{\rm ref}$ compares
\eqref{eq:global-to-closed-excision} with DFL's
reference-cylinder prescription in the chosen
ordinary gauge normalization. The closed parity
calculation does not perform that comparison.

\subsection{The compact complex orientation in genus one}
\label{sec:torus-compact-complex-orientation}

The first sign in \eqref{eq:coherent-closed-two-signs}
can be evaluated in genus one. We use an integrable
flattening and rotation, with a coupled boundary problem
for the gauge potential. The connections in the
flattening need not be ASD.
For an integrable connection family on the excision
sum, write $F_r$ for the holomorphic coefficient
bundle on $\PP^1\times T$ obtained by adjoining
the omitted constant positive adjoint summand,
where $T=\Sigma_1$. It has complex rank six.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:torus-integrable-kernel}
Let $T=\Sigma_1$ and let $B$ be the stable attaching bundle.
Replace the compact reconstructed connection by an
integrable unitary connection on the same holomorphic
$\End_0E$, with the same flat surface restriction at the
seam. Its compact mixed operator satisfies
\[
 \ker C_A\cong H^1(\PP^1\times T,\End_0E),
                    \qquad\operatorname{coker}C_A=0.
\]
The identification is continuous along smooth families
of such connections at positive scale. The same assertion
holds along the integrable interior-rotation family of
$C_E\oplus Q_0$, with its holomorphically capped rank-six
bundle $F_r$ in place of $\End_0E$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Put $F_\alpha=\End_0B$. Stability and Serre duality
give $H^0(T,F_\alpha)=H^1(T,F_\alpha)=0$.
The flat real adjoint cohomology in degrees zero
and one also vanishes. Thus the mixed conditions are
\[
              a_{\mathbf n}=0,\qquad a_\Sigma=d_\alpha\xi
\]
for a real adjoint zero-form $\xi$, with no positive
map variation.

For the direct sum, retain both adjoint summands
on the negative hemisphere and the second on the
positive hemisphere. Omit the first positive
summand and impose the preceding mixed condition
on its boundary. The second summand has ordinary
transmission across the seam. The connection
preserves this splitting near the seam but may
mix the summands strictly inside the negative
hemisphere. Adjoining the omitted holomorphic
cap gives $F_r$. Its surface fibers in a fixed
collar are $F_\alpha\oplus F_\alpha$.

For either capped bundle $F$, a global holomorphic
section vanishes on that collar. The same holds
for $F^*\otimes K_X$, since $K_T$ is trivial.
Therefore $H^0(X,F)=H^2(X,F)=0$.
The real index is four for the primitive and
its direct sum with $Q_0$, and zero for the
constant operator. For the sum it is unchanged
by interior zero-order perturbations. In each
case it equals $-2\chi(F)=2h^1(X,F)$.

Write a complex zero-form as $\phi=\chi+i s$
in the unitary real form. On the omitted
summand impose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:torus-coupled-potential-boundary}
 s|_\Gamma=0,\qquad \nabla_{\mathbf n}\chi|_\Gamma=0.
\end{equation}
Use ordinary transmission of the value and
covariant normal derivative on the retained
summand. The real operator
$L=\bar\partial^*\bar\partial$ has scalar
Laplace principal symbol. These are elliptic
Dirichlet and covariant Neumann conditions
on its imaginary and real parts. All curvature
terms are retained; the potentials are not
decoupled.

The omitted-summand Green boundary term,
up to a fixed positive normalization, is
\[
 \operatorname{Re}\int_{\Gamma\times T}
 \left\langle\phi,\nabla_{\mathbf n}\psi
                         +i\nabla_{J\mathbf n}\psi\right\rangle.
\]
The trace of $\phi$ is real. The normal
derivative of $\psi$ is purely imaginary,
and its tangential derivative has real
trace. Thus both displayed terms have zero
real part. Transmission terms cancel.
Consequently $L$ is self-adjoint as a real
elliptic operator and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:torus-coupled-potential-energy}
 \operatorname{Re}(L\phi,\phi)=\|\bar\partial\phi\|^2.
\end{equation}
A kernel element is holomorphic. Its omitted
seam trace is a holomorphic section of
$F_\alpha$, hence zero. Extending it by
zero on the omitted cap gives a global
holomorphic section of $F$, which vanishes.
Thus $L$ is invertible. Mixed-boundary
regularity gives smooth solutions and
a continuous inverse in the fixed-scale
smooth families.

The K\"ahler identities identify the real
operator on one-forms with
$\bar\partial^*\oplus\bar\partial$ on
$a^{0,1}$, in the fixed target convention;
see \cite[Section~3(c), pp.~412--413]{Donaldson87}.
Integrability makes this a Dolbeault
complex; $\Lambda F_A=0$ is unnecessary.
The identity also holds for the real
orthogonal connection on the sum.

Double a smooth kernel element by subtracting
$\bar\partial$ of an extension of $\xi$
on the omitted side and setting the corrected
form to zero on the positive cap. The
vertical traces agree, so there is no
distributional Dolbeault seam term, as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:fredholm-dolbeault}.
The retained summand continues across
the seam. This defines a class in $H^1(X,F)$.

If the class is zero, its potential vanishes
on the omitted positive cap. Undoing the
$\xi$ correction gives a potential
$\phi=\chi+i s$ with real seam trace.
The real one-form associated to
$\bar\partial\phi$ is $d_A\chi+d_A^c s$,
where $d_A^c=i(\bar\partial_A-\partial_A)$.
Its second term has normal component
equal, up to sign, to a tangential
derivative of the zero trace of $s$.
The zero normal condition therefore gives
\eqref{eq:torus-coupled-potential-boundary}.
Co-closedness gives $L\phi=0$, and the
kernel element vanishes. The same mixed
elliptic problem regularizes weak potentials.

Conversely, a closed Dolbeault representative
$v$ is exact on the omitted cap and a collar:
solve $\bar\partial_\Sigma\zeta=v_\Sigma$
using $H^0=H^1=0$ on each surface fiber.
The cocycle equation makes
$v_B-\bar\partial_B\zeta$ a vertical
holomorphic zero-form, hence zero.
Subtract a cutoff extension of $\zeta$
so that the omitted component of $v$
vanishes on the positive cap and near
the seam. Solve $L\phi=\bar\partial^*v$
with \eqref{eq:torus-coupled-potential-boundary}.
Then $v-\bar\partial\phi$ is closed
and co-closed, and its real one-form
satisfies the mixed and transmission
conditions. Doubling returns the
original cohomology class. This proves
the kernel isomorphism, and the index
calculation proves surjectivity.

The vertical inverses, the inverse of
$L$, and the closed Hodge projections
vary continuously in these families.
The capped cohomology dimensions are
constant by the preceding vanishing.
This proves the family assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:torus-cap-complex-orientation}
In genus one, the framed interior excision of
Lemma~\ref{lem:interior-oriented-excision} can
be chosen with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:torus-cap-sign}
                           \delta_{\rm cap}=+1.
\end{equation}
For this comparison,
$\varepsilon_{{\rm coh},1}=-\delta_{\rm ref}$
and $\eta_1=\delta_{\rm ref}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The stable complex slice has dimension
zero in genus one, and its trace-free
base coefficient vanishes. The
holomorphic adjoint bundle on a stable
seam collar is therefore the pullback
of $F_\alpha$. The local projective
identifications form a covering with
discrete automorphism group. The
given seam identification is a section
over the circle, so extends over its
collar; local uniqueness makes this
extension holomorphic. This retains
the original framing without extending
it over the unstable negative fiber.

In this identification, the reconstructed
and product adjoint metrics agree at
the seam by uniqueness of the NS
metric. Interpolate Hermitian metrics
relative to that boundary value to
make the metric product on a smaller
collar. Their Chern connections are
integrable and retain the flat surface
boundary value. The positive map is
already constant.
Lemma~\ref{lem:torus-integrable-kernel}
transports the compact complex
orientation through this flattening.
For the capped cohomology one may
extend each negative metric smoothly;
its positive extension need not be
product during the interpolation.

On the interior cut annulus both
connections are the product $\alpha$.
Write the rotation as $R=e^{\theta S}$,
where
\[
 S=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\1&0\end{pmatrix}
                              \otimes\id_{F_\alpha},
\]
and $\theta$ depends only on the base.
After the bundle identification,
the connection difference is
$S\,d\theta$, up to its fixed
conjugation sign. Interpolating
this term realizes the first-order
commutator path of the excision.
It remains integrable because
\[
 d(S\,d\theta)=0,\qquad
 (S\,d\theta)\wedge(S\,d\theta)=0,\qquad
                         [\alpha,S]=0.
\]
It is a real orthogonal connection
on the sum. A Lie algebra structure
on the mixed interior summands
is not required, and their seam
splitting is unchanged.

Apply the partially capped case
of Lemma~\ref{lem:torus-integrable-kernel}.
Every kernel is identified
continuously with the complex
$H^1(X,F_r)$ and every cokernel
vanishes. At the bundle
identification, $R$ intertwines
the conjugated Dolbeault
operators complex-linearly.
It is the identity near the
omitted seam, so it also
intertwines the doubling maps.
The determinant transport sends
\[
 o_{C_E}^{\CC}\otimes o_{Q_0}^{\CC}
                \quad\hbox{to}\quad
 o_{C_0}^{\CC}\otimes o_{Q_E}^{\CC}.
\]
All real factor indices are even,
so interchange and cancellation
introduce no odd permutation.
This proves \eqref{eq:torus-cap-sign}.
The remaining assertions follow
from \eqref{eq:coherent-closed-two-signs}.
\end{proof}

The compact complex-orientation comparison is
therefore proved on the specified positive-scale
framed torus patches. Agreement with DFL's
fixed ordinary gauge reference, namely
$\delta_{\rm ref}=+1$, is proved in
Section~\ref{sec:torus-reference-orientation}.

\section{Compactification of the point cut and its boundary}
\label{sec:cut-compactification}

We exclude energy loss down the mixed ends and ordinary positive
bubbles using the chosen incidence conditions. Every remaining
bubbling end then lies in a regular primitive gluing neighborhood.
Finite regularization of the compact complement gives
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}, with the coherent
orientation signs retained.

\subsection{Exclusion of energy loss on the mixed ends}
\label{sec:mixed-end-exclusion}

Fix regular residual moduli spaces through index three, with
no negative-index solutions, and regular ordinary Floer data.
Use the compact secondary perturbations and dependency convention
of Section~\ref{sec:evaluation-transversality}.
The union $\mathcal C$ of the index-zero residual moduli spaces
for the finite sets of ordinary generators is finite by
the index-zero compactness argument of
\cite[Section~5.4]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Choose the point sections as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-end-exclusion}: they are independent
on every completed residual insertion path and at its ordinary
critical limits. The negative sections use finite compact
holonomy dependencies, and the seam sections use the actual
universal identification and normal parallel transport.
These independence conditions can be imposed in every genus:
their parameter spaces have dimension at most one and the
rank-one stratum has real codimension four.
In genus two retain also the line-avoidance conditions of that
proposition.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:mixed-end-exclusion}
For this choice of sections, every sequence in the index-four
point cut has a subsequence convergent on both mixed ends in
the weighted topology of \cite[equation~(5.36)]{DaeFukLip21a},
including its exact critical representatives.
In particular, a concentration center cannot escape to infinity
along the matching line in the point cut.

If such a sequence has a finite matching atom, the atom has
charge one on exactly one surface component, exhausts the
global defect, and has an index-zero residual with the original
ordinary generators. There are no other atoms, ordinary bubbles
or nonconstant ordinary Floer levels. The physical outer and
end hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:physical-sector-coverage} then hold.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply local mixed compactness and ordinary Floer chain
compactness, before imposing an index-based exclusion.
The global defect is \eqref{eq:global-defect}. On either
mixed end, Proposition~5.29 and equations~(5.36)--(5.37) of
\cite{DaeFukLip21a} give a subsequential alternative:
weighted convergence, including the critical representative,
or loss of a strictly positive amount $\epsilon$ of
topological energy. Only positivity of $\epsilon$ is needed.

Suppose the latter alternative occurs on at least one mixed
end. Then $\Delta>0$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:index-exhaustion} gives
$\Delta=\frac12$, an index-zero mixed residual $x_0\in\mathcal C$,
and no nonconstant ordinary levels. Thus $x_0$ has the
original ordinary generators.
By Theorem~\ref{thm:local-charge}, any finite matching atom
costs at least $\frac12$. An ordinary sphere or disc costs
at least $\frac12$, and a gauge bubble costs at least one;
the same bounds hold on the translated ordinary ends.
These nonnegative losses add on disjoint regions in the
energy comparison. Any one of them together with the
positive mixed-end loss would exceed $\Delta=\frac12$.
There are consequently no finite atoms and no
ordinary-end bubbles.

Local compactness now gives smooth convergence on every
compact gauge dependency region and positive domain region,
including the seam crossing. The ordinary ends have neither
bubbles nor nonconstant levels. Ordinary Floer chain
compactness gives energy tightness there: otherwise a
translated nonconstant level or a bubble would be extracted.
The small-energy entry and exit argument and the critical
spectral gap, as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-end-convergence}, give uniform
ordinary-end convergence. This use of the ordinary ends
does not presume convergence on the mixed ends.

Pass to a subsequence in the insertion parameter.
If $t_\nu$ has a finite limit, the evaluated sections converge
to the independent residual pair. For a negative insertion,
convergence holds on the full compact holonomy dependency;
at the crossing it respects the actual seam identification
and normal transport. If $t_\nu\to-\infty$, the prescribed
gauge-end pullback tends to the independent pair at the
incoming critical point. If $t_\nu\to+\infty$, the positive
evaluation tends to the independent pair at the outgoing
critical point. Each case contradicts the point incidence.
Hence both mixed ends satisfy the convergence alternative.
When $\Delta=0$, that alternative follows immediately from
the strict-loss inequality.

Finally, if a finite matching atom occurs, its local half-unit
lower bound and index exhaustion force it to be the unique
charge-one atom on one component and to consume the whole
defect. The other losses vanish. The small-tail argument of
Proposition~\ref{prop:automatic-end-convergence} supplies
convergence on all ends, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:physical-sector-coverage} supplies the outer
data under its stated algebraic hypotheses.
\end{proof}

The compactness inequalities used here occur before the
final index-at-most-one contradiction in
\cite[Section~5.4, pp.~71--74]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The proof replaces that contradiction by
Proposition~\ref{prop:index-exhaustion}; it does not extend
the stated regularity range of DFL Proposition~3.23.
It proves no classification of positive bubbles when there
is no mixed-end energy loss. For a finite matching atom,
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-center-low-genus-coverage} now applies
with its remaining low-genus and section hypotheses, without
separately assuming the global defect or ordinary-end
convergence of the incoming sequence.

\subsection{Interior evaluation and ordinary sphere attachments}
\label{sec:interior-sphere-evaluation}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:positive-interior-evaluation}
At a smooth regular index-zero residual $x=(A,u)$, evaluation
at finitely many distinct positive interior points is jointly
submersive under the compact negative holonomy variations of
Section~\ref{sec:evaluation-transversality}.
These evaluations may be combined with finitely many seam
evaluations. The almost complex structure may remain fixed.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For one interior point $z$, write $K_z\xi=\xi_u(z)$.
It is bounded on the $H^2$ mixed domain and onto on
smooth compact positive configuration variations.
Suppose $(\lambda,\nu)$ annihilates
$(D_2\xi+P_x\dot\eta,K_z\xi)$.
For a compact domain-preserving cutoff $\chi$ zero near $z$,
the compatible lower inverse gives
\[
 (\chi\lambda)(f)
 =-\lambda([D,\chi]D_2^{-1}f),\qquad
 |(\chi\lambda)(f)|
 \le C_\chi\|\lambda\|_{(\mathcal Y^1)^*}
                       \|f\|_{\mathcal Y^0}.
\]
The differential and nonlocal commutators have the bounds
proved in Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation}.
Thus $\lambda$ is locally $L^2$ off $z$, including every
seam point.
The same Coulomb splitting, holonomy separation and interior
unique continuation remove its scalar and self-dual
components. The weak-adjoint matching theorem gives zero
positive adjoint trace on a seam arc. Zero extension and
unique continuation remove that adjoint on the connected
positive domain punctured at $z$. Local $L^2$ representation
excludes residual boundary functionals.

The remaining functional is supported at $z$. In a local
positive coordinate choose $\chi_\epsilon$ equal to one for
$r\le\epsilon^2$, zero for $r\ge\epsilon$, and logarithmic
between them, with a smooth approximation. Then
\[
 \|\chi_\epsilon\|_2=O(\epsilon),\qquad
 \|d\chi_\epsilon\|_2^2=O(|\log\epsilon|^{-1}).
\]
For smooth compact $f$, support at $z$ gives
$\lambda(f)=\lambda(\chi_\epsilon f)\to0$.
Thus $\lambda=0$, and onto configuration evaluation gives
$\nu=0$. Subtracting the graph of the invertible $D_2$
reduces the range question to a finite-dimensional target,
so this proves surjectivity.

For several evaluations, use cutoffs near their finite
union, the same interior point argument, and the seam
capacity argument of
Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation}.
The positive domain minus finitely many points remains
connected. Disjoint compact configuration variations make
the joint evaluation onto.
\end{proof}

The compactness and finite-direction argument of
Proposition~\ref{lem:seam-evaluation} now makes residual
evaluations on a compact positive insertion interval avoid
the genus-two exceptional set $B$ and the higher-genus
images $i_g(N_g)$, simultaneously in all factors.
The seam endpoint uses the seam version of the lemma.
Small variations preserve previously achieved avoidance
on the completed seam. This imposes no avoidance condition
on the outgoing ordinary critical points.

Let $z_t=(t,0)$ lie where compact variations of the positive
almost complex structure are allowed. For a simple
$J(z_t)$-holomorphic sphere $F$ with nodal point $a$ and
$F(a)=u(z_t)$, the universal coupled derivative is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:coupled-sphere-attachment-derivative}
 (\xi,\zeta,\dot\eta,Y)\longmapsto
 \left(
 D_x\xi+P_x\dot\eta+R_xY,\
 D_F\zeta+\tfrac12Y(z_t,F)dFj,\
 \zeta(a)-K_{z_t}\xi
 \right).
\end{equation}
This operator is onto. Indeed, simple-sphere universal
transversality solves the second row using $(\zeta,Y_0)$;
one may also prescribe $\zeta(a)$
\cite[Theorem~6.1]{OhZhu09}.
Extend $Y_0$ by a compact domain cutoff equal to one at
$z_t$. Lemma~\ref{lem:positive-interior-evaluation} solves
the first and third rows, including the full residual
source $-R_xY$, using $(\xi,\dot\eta)$.
The holonomy variation has no sphere component.
Quotienting by sphere reparametrization preserves
surjectivity.
For $c_1(F)=2$, the resulting one-nodal-mark family,
including the real parameter $t$, has dimension
\[
             0+(2n-6+4+2)-2n+1=1,
             \qquad 2n=\dim_\mathbb R M.
\]
One further point moving on the sphere gives parameter
dimension three. We next verify the compactness and end
conditions needed to use this family for incidence exclusion.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:ordinary-positive-exclusion}
For a fixed insertion point $q$, the low-index regular
equation data and the sections in
Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-end-exclusion} can be chosen so
that no index-four point-cut sequence has an ordinary
positive sphere or outer-boundary disc bubble.
This includes bubbles escaping down the outgoing ordinary
end. The fixed standard complex matching collar and
completed-seam residual avoidance are retained.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write $2n=\dim_\mathbb R M$ for the full product target.
Every nonconstant compatible holomorphic sphere has
normalized area at least $\frac12$. A sphere of area
$\frac12$ has $c_1=2$ and is simple: a nontrivial
multiple cover would have a nonconstant underlying
sphere of smaller positive area.
Simple spheres are somewhere injective
\cite[Proposition~4.11]{Zinger17}.
Gromov compactness for varying target structures
\cite[Theorem~1.1]{IvashkovichShevchishin99}
shows that a sequence of one-marked minimal spheres has
a stable limit with exactly one nonconstant component.
There is no stable constant tree with only one external
mark and one nonconstant component. Thus the
one-marked moduli space is proper over compact parameter
families, including a prescribed continuous nodal value.
For two marks, their collision is recorded by allowing
the second point to equal the node on the nonconstant
sphere. Its evaluation remains continuous.

There are only finitely many possible homology classes
under this energy bound in a compact family of compatible
structures. Indeed, integration of any fixed closed
two-form is bounded by a constant times the energy.
A finite integral cohomology basis bounds the
nontorsion class in a lattice, and the torsion subgroup
is finite. No product condition on a varying $J$ is used.

First choose the ordinary family $J_\theta$ generically
also for simple-sphere nodal evaluation at each of the
finitely many outgoing critical points $\beta$, for
$J_0$. Theorem~6.1 of \cite{OhZhu09} supplies this
generic condition. A variation of $J_0$ extends to
$J_\theta$ by a cutoff equal to one at $\theta=0$,
so it can be imposed together with ordinary Floer
regularity as in \cite[Lemma~2.35]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The constrained one-marked sphere spaces have dimension
$(2n-6+4+2)-2n=0$. They are compact and hence finite.
Their constrained operators modulo reparametrization
are isomorphisms. The implicit function theorem
continues their elements uniquely to nearby nodal
values and structures, and properness excludes
additional nearby spheres.

Make this choice small, interpolate it into the positive
equation away from the fixed matching collar, and keep
the far outgoing end translation invariant.
The ordinary generators remain fixed and their
spectral gaps remain bounded below. The regular
index-zero residual set $\mathcal C$ continues under
these small data changes. To see exhaustion, a sequence
of additional index-zero solutions with data tending
to the original data would have a residual for the
original data. Positive loss would force index at
most $-4$, and a nonconstant ordinary level would
force negative residual index. Both are excluded.
The sequence must therefore converge to an original
regular index-zero solution, where the implicit
function theorem applies.

On the compact part $0\le t\le a$ of the insertion path
in the fixed $J_*$ collar, use
Lemma~\ref{lem:positive-interior-evaluation} and its seam
version to avoid the exceptional sets in every target
factor. Genus-one factors have no spheres. In higher
genus, minimal standard spheres attach through
$i_g(N_g)$ and so cannot occur at these values.
In genus two, the four regular extension lines have,
including the constant target factors,
\[
 F^*TM=\mathcal O(2)\oplus\mathcal O^{n-1},\qquad
 H^1\bigl(F^*TM(-a_{\rm node})\bigr)=0.
\]
Thus the sphere equation with nodal evaluation is
onto at fixed $J_*$. Modulo automorphisms preserving
the node it is an isomorphism. Compactness gives
a buffer beyond the fixed collar on which the matched
sphere problem remains regular. Small changes retain
the completed-seam avoidance already established.

Consider the matched one-marked family
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-matched-sphere-family}
 \mathcal S=\left\{
 (x,t,[F,a_{\rm node}]):
 \begin{array}{l}
 x\in\mathcal C,\ t\ge0,\quad
 \bar\partial_{J(t,0)}F=0,\\
 \mathcal E(F)=\frac12,\quad
 F(a_{\rm node})=u_x(t,0)
 \end{array}\right\}.
\end{equation}
Simplicity makes the reparametrization action free;
local slices can be chosen at three distinct
injective points. This family is regular in the
fixed collar. It is regular for all sufficiently
large $t$ by the finite continuation branches at
$\beta$, uniform end convergence of $\mathcal C$,
and $J(t,0)=J_0$ on the far end.
On the remaining compact interval, the universal
operator \eqref{eq:coupled-sphere-attachment-derivative}
is onto. Properness and finiteness of $\mathcal C$
give a compact set of background matched spheres.
A finite cover supplies one finite set of
compact positive $J$ variations and compact negative
holonomy variations making the universal problem
regular. Enlarge the permitted compact positive
support if necessary. Properness excludes extra
solutions outside these neighborhoods for small
parameters. Sard's theorem now makes
\eqref{eq:ordinary-matched-sphere-family} regular
on the middle interval as well, while preserving
the regular buffers.

The resulting $\mathcal S$ is a smooth
one-dimensional manifold with boundary at $t=0$,
proper over $[0,\infty)$. Its projection to $t$
may have critical points. On a sufficiently long
tail it consists of finitely many graphs continued
from the matched spheres at the ordinary critical
points. Adding those limiting spheres gives a
compact completion.
The universal sphere $\mathcal W\to\mathcal S$,
with an additional point $b$ allowed to equal
$a_{\rm node}$, has dimension three and evaluation
\[
             (x,t,[F,a_{\rm node}],b)\longmapsto(F(b),q).
\]
Its completion adds two-dimensional end spheres,
and the evaluation image is compact. Evaluation
continuity includes colliding marks
\cite[Section~1.3]{Zinger17}.
No smooth coordinate at $t=\infty$ is required.

Choose finite global section variations spanning the
complex rank-three evaluated bundle. Two independent
coefficient rows span the pair fiber everywhere.
Parameterized Sard on $\mathcal W$, its boundary,
and its two-dimensional end strata gives a pair
$\sigma^S$ independent on all their images, since
the rank-one stratum has real codimension four.
Use a countable coordinate cover of the noncompact
smooth $\mathcal W$. Impose simultaneously
independence on the completed residual insertion
paths and critical limits. Their union with the
sphere images is compact, so the wedge has a
positive minimum.
At $t=0$ the sphere family includes the genus-two
line families required by
Proposition~\ref{prop:positive-end-exclusion}.
The gauge and compact middle sections are then
chosen by that proposition with the same holonomy
dependencies and seam identification.

Suppose now that an actual point-cut sequence
has an ordinary positive bubble.
Index exhaustion gives a residual in $\mathcal C$,
the original ordinary generators, no nonconstant
ordinary levels, and total defect $\frac12$.
The positive bubble consumes this defect, leaving
no gauge or matching atom and no second
nonconstant bubble. The negative compact
dependency and incoming ordinary end converge
to the residual, so every negative, seam, or
incoming-end insertion limit sees an independent
pair.

A bounded positive insertion away from the bubble
sees the independent residual pair. If it enters
an interior sphere scale, the node lies on the
insertion path and marked Gromov convergence
places its limiting value on that minimal
sphere, including its nodal value. These are
precisely the evaluations of $\mathcal W$.
The actual positive universal bundle and sections
are pullbacks by target evaluation, so the
limiting pair is again independent.
The insertion path is locally separated from
the outer Lagrangian boundary; it cannot enter
a boundary-disc scale or a sphere attached
at that boundary.

If $t_\nu\to+\infty$, translate by the insertion
parameter and use ordinary chain compactness.
There are no nonconstant strip levels, so the
residual strip is the constant $\beta$.
The insertion value tends either to $\beta$
or to a point on a minimal $J_0$ sphere
attached at $\beta$. Both are in the avoided
sets. A boundary disc remains separated from
$\theta=0$.
Every case contradicts the point incidence.
\end{proof}

This is a choice for fixed $q$, or for a prescribed
finite set of insertion points. Parameter families
varying $q$ require their own argument.
The proof establishes neither index-four
regularity of the full mixed moduli space nor
an ambient nodal gluing theorem.

\subsection{Exhaustion of the bubbling ends}
\label{sec:all-bubbling-cut-ends}

The point bundle for $q\in\Sigma_q$ is the pullback of
the universal adjoint point bundle from the marked
target factor $M_{g(q)}$. Choose the positive sections
in Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-positive-exclusion}
from this factor. Their coefficient variations still
span every pair fiber on the sphere evaluation family,
so its avoidance proof is unchanged.
Make the same choice for every seam-dependent section
and model refinement in
Section~\ref{sec:local-seam-incidence}.
Its equivariant extension uses the marked surface
connection and the actual normal transport.
The compact interior holonomy dependencies remain
at positive distance from the matching line.
In genus one the positive factor is a point and its
pair is a fixed independent pair.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:all-bubbling-cut-ends}
For these fixed equation and point-section choices,
the only positive-defect limits of the index-four
cut are the covered charge-one primitive ends on
the marked component, at the insertion crossing,
in genus one or two. There are none when the marked
component has genus at least three.
After removing the finite collection of small-scale
neighborhoods of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation},
every sequence in the remainder has zero energy
defect after ordinary chain extraction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
First suppose a finite matching atom lies on
a component different from $\Sigma_q$.
By index exhaustion and local charge it is the
unique charge-one atom and consumes the entire
half-unit defect. The residual has index zero,
the original ordinary generators and full end
convergence. The local energy measure on
$\Sigma_q$ has no defect. Restricting the mixed
equation to that factor in the fixed product
collar, local compactness and regularity give
smooth convergence of the marked surface
connection and map through the apparent
full-target concentration point.
All compact interior holonomy dependencies and
normal segments at $q$ are disjoint from the atom.
The positive sections depend only on the marked
map component, and the seam-dependent negative
sections depend only on the marked connection
and normal transport. Every bounded insertion
therefore sees the independent residual pair.
Full end convergence treats both insertion
infinities. This contradicts incidence.

We also verify higher-genus attachment support
without a regular normalized primitive limit.
Let $p$ be the residual value at a finite
matching atom on a component of genus $h$.
The finite doubled families and coherent
charge of Section~\ref{sec:charge-splitting},
with the small caps used in the physical
converse, have alternatives
$(d_\nu,\ell_\nu)=(0,1)$ or $(1,0)$.
In the first, the stable replacement has
vanishing area and boundary values tending
to $p$ on a shrinking retained disc.
Holomorphic monotonicity makes its entire
image tend to $p$. At the modification
point its stable fiber contains the
degree-zero quotient line from the Langton
step. Its class belongs to $i_h(N_h)$.
This image is closed, since $N_h$ is
compact, so $p\in i_h(N_h)$.

In the second alternative the stable
classifying maps are holomorphic on the
fixed doubled disc and converge to the
residual map on punctured compact annuli.
Their capped degree and cap convergence
give concentrating area exactly $\frac12$.
Ordinary holomorphic-map compactness
extracts a sphere tree through $p$.
Minimal spherical area leaves exactly
one nonconstant sphere. Mu\~noz's
extension-line classification puts its
image in $i_h(N_h)$, so again
$p\in i_h(N_h)$.
This uses the cap only for the degree
and area comparison; it does not assert
that the cap is holomorphic.
For $h\ge3$ the completed residual seam
avoids $i_h(N_h)$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:global-residual-avoidance}.
Such an atom is impossible.

Now take any positive-defect cut sequence.
Index exhaustion gives defect $\frac12$,
an index-zero residual and no nonconstant
ordinary levels.
Propositions~\ref{prop:mixed-end-exclusion}
and~\ref{prop:ordinary-positive-exclusion}
exclude mixed-end loss and ordinary
positive bubbles; the energy bound
excludes gauge bubbles. Comparing energies
on compact truncations and then using
the tight ends leaves a finite matching
atom. Local charge makes it unique.
The preceding arguments put it on the
marked component in genus one or two.

Proposition~\ref{prop:mixed-end-exclusion}
supplies the physical defect and end
hypotheses. Global residual avoidance
and the line-independent positive pair
supply the remaining genus-two conditions
of Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-center-low-genus-coverage}.
In genus one that theorem applies
before incidence. The section limits
of Section~\ref{sec:local-seam-incidence}
then put the center at the crossing,
bound the normalized center ratio,
and place the insertion in a compact
negative middle interval. The normalized
limit is a zero of the compact relative
model. Its transverse refinement is
still available with the factor-dependent
sections, since their coefficient
variations span every pair fiber.

There are finitely many residuals and
model zeros. The neighborhoods in
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation}
therefore exhaust every such sequence,
with its scale tending to zero.
A sequence remaining after all regions
$0<\rho<\epsilon$ have been removed
cannot have positive defect.
\end{proof}

The artificial boundary counts remain
$8n(x_0)\eta_1(x_0)$ and
$n(x_0)\sum_j\eta_{2,j}(x_0)$.
For the selected genus-two normalization
the latter is $4n(x_0)$.
A zero-defect remainder may still have
ordinary breaking or an escaping insertion;
its global boundary formula does not
follow from the proposition alone.

\subsection{The regular cut and its global boundary}
\label{sec:fixed-point-global-boundary}

For an ordinary Floer trajectory of index $r$, let
$Q_r^G$ and $Q_r^S$ denote the point cuts at the
zero slice. These use parameterized trajectories
and have dimension $r-4$ when transverse.
Write $H_r$ for the mixed point cut with its
insertion coordinate, of expected dimension $r-3$.
Thus $H_3=\cL_0^q$ and $H_4=\cL_1^q$.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}
For fixed $q$, the equation and section choices
of Proposition~\ref{prop:all-bubbling-cut-ends}
can be made so that the index-four point cut
is regular. Its truncation by the finite
primitive neighborhoods compactifies to an
oriented one-manifold whose ordinary boundary
consists of
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fixed-point-ordinary-boundary-sets}
 \begin{aligned}
 &H_3(\alpha,\gamma)\times\breve{\cM}^{S}_1(\gamma,\beta),
 &&\breve{\cM}^{G}_1(\alpha,\gamma)\times H_3(\gamma,\beta),\\
 &\cM_0(\alpha,\gamma)\times Q_4^S(\gamma,\beta),
 &&Q_4^G(\alpha,\gamma)\times\cM_0(\gamma,\beta).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Their product orientation signs are respectively
$+,+,+,-$. The other boundary points are the
artificial primitive scale slices.
Consequently the actual chain counts satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:proved-fixed-point-boundary}
 Nb_q^G-b_q^SN
       =d_SK_q^{p_1}+K_q^{p_1}d_G+B_q,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:proved-fixed-point-B}
 B_q(\alpha)=
 \sum_\beta\sum_{x_0\in\cM_0(\alpha,\beta)}
 n(x_0)
 \begin{cases}
 8\eta_1(x_0),&g(q)=1,\\
 \sum_{j=1}^4\eta_{2,j}(x_0),&g(q)=2,\\
 0,&g(q)\ge3
 \end{cases}\beta.
\end{equation}
Regularity is asserted at the index-four cut
points, not at every uncut index-four solution.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First make the lower mixed cuts, their $t=0$
restrictions, and the ordinary point cuts through
index four transverse. The positive and seam
coefficient variations on the marked factor
span every pair fiber. On the incoming end
use finite holonomy frames on the compact set
of relevant slice evaluations. Ordinary gauge
bubbling costs eight index units and cannot
occur here; chain compactness makes this set
compact, with irreducible limits.
Irreducibility of trajectory slices and of
the sufficiently negative mixed slices is
proved in \cite[Section~7.2, pp.~89--90]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The compact middle restrictions through mixed
index three are likewise compact after ordinary
breaking, since no bubbling occurs.
They admit finitely many interior holonomy
frames. Together with the seam and end
coefficients these span the pair fiber along
the full interpolation.

Parameterized Sard on countably many smooth
coordinate charts makes all the indicated
cuts transverse, including the regular
positive-scale primitive charts. Smallness
retains the independent sphere and residual
images and all model side boundaries.
Rank zero is absent by its real codimension
twelve. The dimension count gives
\[
 H_r=\varnothing\quad(r\le2),\qquad
 Q_r^G=Q_r^S=\varnothing\quad(r\le3).
\]
The zero-manifolds $H_3,Q_4^G,Q_4^S$ are compact.
For $H_3$, a broken limit with finite insertion
would lie on an $H_r$ with $r\le2$;
an escaping insertion would lie on an ordinary
$Q_r$ with $r\le3$ or at an independent
critical pair. Bubbling is excluded in index
three. For $Q_4^G$, a nontrivial broken
limit puts the insertion on a lower-index
ordinary level or a critical point.
There is no gauge bubble.
For $Q_4^S$ the only further possibility
is a minimal positive bubble with constant
index-zero residual strip. Its visible
sphere is attached at a critical point
for $J_0$, and is excluded by the end-sphere
choice in
Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-positive-exclusion}.
A boundary disc is separated from the insertion.

Remove the small primitive neighborhoods.
Proposition~\ref{prop:all-bubbling-cut-ends}
gives zero defect for every remaining
sequence. If $r_0$ is its mixed residual
index and $r_i\ge1$ are its nonconstant
ordinary-level indices, then
\[
                         r_0+\sum_i r_i=4.
\]
A bounded insertion in a broken limit
forces $r_0\ge3$, hence $r_0=3$ and
one ordinary level of index one.
An escaping insertion sees either a
critical pair or an ordinary level
after translation. Critical pairs are
avoided. A level carrying the point
incidence has index at least four,
so it is the sole ordinary level and
the mixed residual has index zero.
These are exactly
\eqref{eq:fixed-point-ordinary-boundary-sets},
and all four sets are finite.

Their ordinary gluing collars are regular.
The factors are onto and the intermediate
critical point has a fixed gap.
The ordinary Floer inverse on kernel
complements and the long-neck correction
give onto glued operators.
This construction uses regular factors,
not a numerical index bound
\cite[Section~5.4 and Proposition~7.12]{DaeFukLip21a}.
For the first two boundary sets, the full
compact incidence dependency stays on
the mixed factor. For the last two,
translate the insertion with the
ordinary level and use the prescribed
end pullback. Gluing gives convergence
of these sections and their first
kernel-parameter derivatives on the
whole dependency. Transversality of
the zero-dimensional factor cut and
the implicit function theorem therefore
give one half-interval at each boundary
point. Ordinary converse gluing covers
every sequence with that limit.

After removing smaller ordinary collars
as well, the remaining set is compact,
without assuming index-four regularity
there. Indeed, every sequence has zero
defect. A broken or insertion-escaping
limit is one of the finite boundary
points just listed and forces the
sequence into its removed collar.
Otherwise weighted compactness gives
an unbroken cut solution with finite
insertion in the remaining closed set.

At every point of this compact set,
the local cokernel separation in
\cite[Section~6.2, pp.~82--83]{DaeFukLip21a}
supplies finitely many compact holonomy
and positive $J$ directions making
the universal equation onto. That
local argument is independent of
the index. Independent section
coefficients then solve its normal
incidence row. A finite cover gives
one finite-dimensional universal
family for the compact set, also
treating $t=0$ separately.

We give the uniformity needed to
exclude new solutions outside this
cover. Keep the ordinary equation
data fixed and let $a$ range over
a small closed ball in this finite
family. Index-zero compactness and
the implicit function theorem give
all residuals as finitely many
smooth branches $x_j(a)$.
Their completed seam avoidance and
end gaps persist.
The sphere independence margin is
open: a violating sequence for
$a_\nu\to0$ would have a marked
minimal-sphere limit in the original
compact avoided image, including
its outgoing-end strata.
The same statement holds for the
residual and model side-boundary
independence conditions.

On the selected finite regular
primitive patches all gluing and
converse estimates are uniform in
$a$. The local equation and cutoff
profiles are unchanged. The compact
surface and primitive inverses,
their finite observations, and
the ordinary residual inverse
vary continuously on compact
regular parameter sets.
Their gaps are positive and norms
bounded. Outer coefficients,
holonomy bounds and decay estimates
are uniform in each fixed order.
The existing quantitative Neumann
and contraction estimates therefore
hold with common constants and
the same logarithmic scale powers.
At positive scale the parameter
maps are smooth by the ordinary
implicit function theorem.

For converse sequences with
$a_\nu\to0$, use the corresponding
$x_j(a_\nu)$, including its exact
end profiles, as reference.
The diagonal outer radii are chosen
for convergence to these references;
no fixed difference from $x_j(0)$
is divided by a shrinking scale.
The finite-family extraction and
the uniform observation-correction
estimates give the same converse
coverage. Thus every positive-defect
sequence for nearby data enters
the continued primitive neighborhoods.
The artificial scale slices are
at fixed positive regular scale
and stay transverse by smooth
dependence. Ordinary gluing gives
the analogous uniform statement
at the finite ordinary boundary.
Any new cut outside these
neighborhoods for $a_\nu\to0$
would therefore converge to
the old compact interior set.
The finite universal cover is
consequently exhaustive for small
parameters.

Sard's theorem now makes the
fixed-parameter equation and
incidence derivative onto at
every index-four cut point.
Its first row is the mixed
operator, which is therefore
onto at these points.
Rank zero is absent.
At $t=0$ the fixed-insertion
equation and cut have index zero
and are onto. On each side the
cut is locally a graph $x=x(t)$.
Their values and their section
derivatives in the $x$ directions
agree at zero by the actual seam
identification. They join to an
interval with coordinate $t$,
with agreeing induced orientations.
No smoothness of the sections
across the seam beyond these
properties is required.

This produces the compact oriented
one-manifold in the statement.
The ordinary gluing determinant
maps are DFL's. In Proposition~7.12
of \cite{DaeFukLip21a}, replacing
the surface insertion by a point
and increasing the trajectory
index by two preserves all
relevant parities. The incidence
normal has dimension four, so
commuting it with the boundary
normal introduces no sign.
Finite-insertion index-one
breaking gives the two positive
differential terms.
For insertion escape, write
$t=+T+\tau$ on the outgoing
end and $t=-T+\tau$ on the
incoming end, with $T$ increasing
neck length and $\tau$ the
ordinary trajectory coordinate.
Their Jacobian signs are $+1$
and $-1$, giving the last two
signs in the statement.

The artificial primitive boundary
counts are those of
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-center-truncation},
with outward normal $-\partial_\rho$.
The signed boundary count of the
compact one-manifold is zero,
which is
\eqref{eq:proved-fixed-point-boundary}
with \eqref{eq:proved-fixed-point-B}.
\end{proof}

For the genus-two component selected
in DFL's sphere normalization,
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-coherent-sign}
gives $B_q=4N$. For $g(q)\ge3$,
$B_q=0$. Thus the rational
fixed-point comparison is proved
with correction $1$ in the selected
genus-two case and $0$ in higher
genus. Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-integral-geometric-comparison}
below identifies the torus factors in
\eqref{eq:proved-fixed-point-B}, giving $B_q=8N$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:simultaneous-integral-local-signs}
gives simultaneous genus-two normalization as well.
No all-uncut
index-four regularity or ambient
positive nodal collar is used.

\section{Comparison of the reference orientations}
\label{sec:reference-comparison}

The signed boundary identity reduces the point comparison to
the torus and genus-two orientation factors. The ordinary torus
tube and closed reference determine the torus factor.
Homologous matching surfaces have the same closed reference;
the local sphere comparison and a stabilized torus example
then determine both genus-two factors in one coherent system.
Theorem~\ref{thm:integral} follows from these local signs and
the geometric boundary identity.

\subsection{Component signs and simultaneous point choices}
\label{sec:component-signs-and-finite-points}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:genus-two-component-sign}
Fix one coherent DFL orientation system $\mathcal O$.
For every genus-two component $\Sigma_j$, there is a sign
$\chi_j(\mathcal O)\in\{+1,-1\}$ such that
\[
 \eta_{2,k}(x_0)=\chi_j(\mathcal O)
       \quad(1\le k\le4),\qquad B_{q_j}=4\chi_j(\mathcal O)N
\]
for every regular residual and every point $q_j\in\Sigma_j$.
The sign is also the compatibility sign for insertion of a
positive generator sphere in this component.
Reversing the reference index-four gauge-path orientation
reverses all $\chi_j$ simultaneously; their pairwise products
are unchanged.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Keep the reference generator, reference paths, their orientations,
and the standard $S^4$ orientation fixed. Let $\mathcal O_j$
be the coherent system obtained by selecting $\Sigma_j$ in
\cite[Remark~4.76]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The corresponding gauge path represents the index-four
class from the reference connection to its image under
the distinguished involution. If the original reference
has index $8k+4$, compare it using the prescribed $S^4$
stabilization. The orientation recipe on pp.~21--22
of \cite{DaeFukLip21a}, using cylinder composition
and $S^4$ gluing, shows that $\mathcal O_j$ and
$\mathcal O$ differ only in the orientation of this
one torsor.

In $\mathcal O_j$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-coherent-sign} gives
$\eta_{2,k}(x_0)=+1$ for every branch and residual.
The normalization law in
Proposition~\ref{prop:primitive-orientation-normalization}
reverses every residual-relative primitive sign when
that torsor is reversed. It accounts for the change
of the residual orientation as well. This proves
the asserted uniform sign and the formula for $B_{q_j}$
by Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}.
The same law applied to the index-four sphere insertion
identifies its compatibility sign. Reference-generator
orientation changes cancel in these ratios.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:simultaneous-finite-point-cuts}
For any fixed finite list of points on $\Sigma$, the
identities in Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}
can be realized with one equation, one pair of ordinary
Floer complexes, and one comparison map $N$.
Each point has its own section pair and cut.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Impose residual attaching-value avoidance on all components
in the list. The matched minimal-sphere families depend on
the equation; each point has a separate evaluated adjoint
bundle and section pair. The independent section choices
of Proposition~\ref{prop:ordinary-positive-exclusion}
can therefore be made for all these finitely many bundles.
Include every prescribed ordinary insertion coordinate
in the finite end-regularity requirements.
Choose the negative and model pairs separately for each
point, retaining its marked-factor dependence.

Take the union of the finite holonomy and coefficient
directions for the lower cuts. Sard's theorem makes
each cut transverse while preserving all independence
margins. After the finite primitive and ordinary collars
are removed, the remaining sets in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary} have
compact union. A finite cover supplies common equation
directions and separate section parameters. At a
configuration belonging to several cuts, the enlarged
family is universally transverse to each cut separately.
No transversality of their intersection is needed.

For this specified finite family, the nearby-data
exhaustion in that theorem applies to each cut.
Intersect its finitely many parameter neighborhoods
and use common scale and neck thresholds. Sard for
the finitely many projections now gives one final
parameter for all cuts. DFL's surface and loop cuts
for a finite generating set can be made transverse
for the same data by their index-at-most-three
construction in Section~7.2 of \cite{DaeFukLip21a}.
This argument does not concern a continuum of
moving point insertions.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The torus-only splitting}
\label{sec:torus-only-comparison}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:torus-only-comparison}
Suppose both matching components have genus one.
There is one coherent gauge normalization in which
the integral point comparison has correction $+8N$
on both components. The rational correction is $+2N$.
If the complexes are nonzero, they have one generator,
$N=\id$, the point operations are
$b_{q_j}^G=8\id$ and $b_{q_j}^S=0$, and every
surface and loop operation vanishes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The target $M_1\times M_1$ is a point. An embedded
