three-manifold Lagrangian is empty or a single point.
Here embeddedness includes injectivity of the
restriction map, as required by DFL; it does not
discard repeated preimages. If either Lagrangian
is empty the assertion is vacuous. Otherwise
$C_G=C_S=\mathbb Z\alpha$, with zero differential,
concentrated in one relative $\mathbb Z/4$ degree.
DFL Proposition~3.23(iii) gives one index-zero residual.
It is the continuation of the constant pair, whose
operator is invertible by their Proposition~3.19,
and its orientation is $+1$. Thus $N=\id$, as also
follows from the diagonal calculation in their
Theorem~3.29. The final small perturbations preserve
this unique residual and its sign.

Choose points on both components using
Lemma~\ref{lem:simultaneous-finite-point-cuts}.
The symplectic point operations vanish because
the target is a point. The point homotopies
vanish by their odd relative degree.
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary} gives
\[
 b_{q_j}^G=8\eta_j\id,\qquad \eta_j\in\{+1,-1\}.
\]
The two gauge point operations agree. Indeed,
join the points by a smooth path in the connected
three-manifold $Y_\#$. Interpolate the universal
section pairs along this path, independently
at the critical connection. Such an independent
interpolation exists because a bundle over an
interval is trivial and the space of independent
complex two-frames in $\mathbb C^3$ is connected.
Extend it to a neighborhood of the critical
connection and make the remaining ordinary cuts
transverse.

The parameterized index-four gauge trajectories,
the path parameter, and the point incidence
give a one-dimensional cut. Gauge bubbling
would cost eight index units and cannot occur.
Broken insertion limits are excluded by the
lower cuts or the independent critical pair.
Ordinary gauge regularity through this index
is provided by DFL Lemma~2.56. The oriented
boundary gives
\[
             b_{q_1}^G-b_{q_0}^G=d_GL+Ld_G=0.
\]
Thus $\eta_0=\eta_1$, with equality deduced
over $\mathbb Z$ before any base change.
If the common sign is negative, reverse the
single reference index-four gauge-path orientation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:primitive-orientation-normalization}
reverses both signs and leaves the unique
index-zero residual orientation unchanged.
This proves the result in one coherent system.
The surface and loop operations have relative
degrees two and three, so vanish on these
one-generator complexes.
\end{proof}

The magnitude eight in this argument comes from
the clutching calculation and the global boundary
theorem. The torus cylinder has the same positive
normalization, but its ring formula is not used
to transfer a sign between different three-manifolds.
When a torus is paired with a higher-genus component,
the one-generator argument does not apply.


\subsection{The ordinary torus tube and its orientation}
\label{sec:ordinary-torus-tube}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:torus-tube-point-operation}
Suppose an oriented torus $T\subset Y_\#$ has odd pairing
with $w_2(E_\#)$. Let $\widetilde I(Y_\#;\mathbb Q)$ be
the ordinary relatively $\mathbb Z/8$-graded instanton
homology, before the distinguished involution is divided
out. There is one coherent lift $\tau_T$ of that
involution for which the positive integral point
operation satisfies
\[
 b^G=8\tau_T\quad\text{on }\widetilde I(Y_\#;\mathbb Q),
 \qquad b^G=8\id\quad\text{on }I(Y_\#;\mathbb Q).
\]
The second group uses the quotient $\tau_T=1$.
For a splitting with $T$ as a matching component,
this is a choice of DFL's one index-four gauge-path
orientation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Remove an open tube $D^2\times T$ about
$\{1/2\}\times T$ from $[0,1]\times Y_\#$.
The ordinary instanton cobordism map is
\[
 \Psi:\widetilde I(S^1\times T)\otimes
       \widetilde I(Y_\#)\longrightarrow\widetilde I(Y_\#).
\]
If $e$ is the relative invariant of the filling
$D^2\times T$, coherent gluing with the product
cobordism gives $\Psi(e\otimes v)=v$.
This is the tube and filling construction in
\cite[proof of Corollary~7.2,
arXiv version, pp.~53--54]{KronheimerMrowka10}.
We use the unreduced theory at this step.
The flat filling with trivial meridional holonomy
gives the nonzero vector $e$; its adjoint deformation
problem is acyclic. Moving the point insertion
through the connected cobordism gives
\[
             b^G\Psi(a\otimes v)=\Psi(b_Ta\otimes v).
\]

The unreduced torus group has two generators of
degrees differing by four. In the positive torus
normalization its involution $\tau_0$ satisfies
$b_Te=8\tau_0e$. The unreduced matrix is recorded
in \cite[Appendix~A, arXiv version, p.~26]{ChenScaduto18};
their $u$ is $\beta=-4\mu(\mathrm{point})$,
the positive $p_1$ generator. Its quotient is the
relation $\beta=8$ in \cite[Lemma~11]{Munoz99a}.

To compare the involutions, continue $T$ from
one radial ray of the removed tube to the outgoing
boundary. This gives a properly embedded
three-manifold $P$ in the tube complement, with
boundary $T$ on outgoing $Y_\#$ and
$\{\theta_0\}\times T$ on $S^1\times T$,
and with no incoming $Y_\#$ boundary.
Its mod-two Poincar\'e dual defines a real line
bundle $\xi$. On the three ends its first
Stiefel--Whitney classes are respectively
$0$, $\operatorname{PD}(\{\theta_0\}\times T)$,
and $\operatorname{PD}(T)$.
Tensoring a $U(2)$ lift by $\xi$ therefore induces
the torus involution and the distinguished outgoing
involution, and is the identity on incoming $Y_\#$.
The determinant of the lift is unchanged, since
$\xi^{\otimes2}$ has its canonical trivialization,
and the adjoint differential expressions are
naturally identified.

Ordinary determinant gluing commutes with this
tensoring: restriction, cutoff multiplication,
and the finite kernel/cokernel matching sequence
commute under the adjoint identification.
Fix the tube-end reference orientation as $\tau_0$,
and choose the outgoing index-four reference-path
orientation to make this determinant square
orientation preserving. This is one choice
for the whole map. Gluing reference cylinders
propagates it to every pair of generators.
Changing the relative instanton number adds the
standard $S^4$ factor, on whose ball $\xi$ is
trivial. Thus the same square commutes with that
factor. These are exactly the reference-cylinder
and $S^4$ extensions of DFL's gauge orientation
recipe on pp.~21--22 of \cite{DaeFukLip21a}.
They give a coherent lift $\tau_T$ with
\[
                  \Psi(\tau_0a\otimes v)=
                                  \tau_T\Psi(a\otimes v).
\]
Both involutions have even degree; no odd
factor has been interchanged.

Combining these identities yields
\[
 b^Gv=\Psi(b_Te\otimes v)
      =8\Psi(\tau_0e\otimes v)=8\tau_Tv.
\]
The quotient by $\tau_T=1$ proves the second
assertion. Its nice homology class is the one
represented by $T$, as in
\cite[Section~7.1]{KronheimerMrowka10}
and DFL's construction.
The cited complex-coefficient tube and torus
identities are rational identities by faithful
extension of scalars from $\mathbb Q$ to
$\mathbb C$. Integral descent through the
finite gauge quotient is not asserted here.
\end{proof}

This proposition concerns the operation on homology.
It does not identify every mixed primitive sign
or prescribe an integral mixed point-cut homotopy.

\subsection{The torus reference orientation and the geometric homotopy}
\label{sec:torus-reference-orientation}

We compare the ordinary reference in the preceding proposition
with the relative closed determinant in
\eqref{eq:global-to-closed-excision}. The comparison is made
on determinant lines before passing to Floer homology.

Let $T$ be a matching torus and let $B$ represent a
nondegenerate ordinary generator. On a normal collar of $T$,
$B$ is pulled back from the odd flat projective connection
$\alpha$. A smooth degree-one rank-two lift on $T$ has a
smooth splitting $L\oplus(\Lambda L^{-1})$ with $\deg L=0$.
Rotate the first summand once in the clutching around an
oriented disc in the time/normal plane. Fix the direction
of the clutching by requiring that its closure be the
bundle of Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-clutching}
 c_1(E)=h+f,\qquad c_2(E)=hf,\qquad
 p_1(\operatorname{ad}E)=-2hf
       \quad\text{on }\PP^1\times T.
\end{equation}
The splitting used here is smooth; the stable attaching
bundle is not asserted to split holomorphically.

The clutching is projectively a loop. Its determinant-one
lift begins at $I$ and ends at $-I$. After the disc is
inserted in $\mathbb R\times Y_\#$, the outgoing framing
therefore differs by a projective gauge transformation
supported on the normal collar of $T$. Its determinant-one
lifts change sign across $T$, so its lifting obstruction
is $\operatorname{PD}(T)$. This is the distinguished
involution $\iota_T$. The absolute cylinder bundle is
unchanged; the compactly supported relative class becomes
zero when the end framings are forgotten. The framed
replacement defines an ordinary path $p_T(B)$ from $B$
to $\iota_T B$ of index four, with the sign of its
relative charge fixed by
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-clutching}.

This model represents every retained primitive
framing in the torus comparison. On
$(D^2\times T,S^1\times T)$ the relative
determinant class lies in $H^2=\mathbb Z$.
With that class fixed, the relative second
Chern class in $H^4=\mathbb Z$ classifies
the rank-two lift: the determinant fiber
$BSU(2)$ is three-connected with $\pi_4=\mathbb Z$.
Here the displayed cohomology groups are
relative groups, and the relative $H^1$ is
zero, so no loop of determinant identifications
changes the classification. Capping reads
the two classes as
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-clutching}.
There is therefore a bundle isomorphism
relative to the retained boundary framing.
Affine connection transport on this bundle
commutes with the same excision square.
The closed Donaldson orientation is retained;
changing the isomorphism has positive action
by \cite[Corollary~3.27]{Donaldson87}.

For an ordinary cylinder connection $A$ and its replacement
$A^E$, the interior rotation of
Lemma~\ref{lem:interior-oriented-excision} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-excision}
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_{A^E}\otimes\det Q_0)
       \longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_A\otimes\det Q_E).
\end{equation}
Use the ordinary Donaldson orientations of $Q_E,Q_0$,
with the product homology orientation and the lifts
already specified. At a constant cylinder $B$, $D_B$
is invertible. Define $\lambda_T^{\rm cl}(B)$ to be
the orientation of $p_T(B)$ for which
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-excision} becomes
positive after cancellation of $\det D_B$ and the
constant closed factor. Thus the relative closed line
$L_{\rm cl}$ determines the reference orientation.
Place all operators in the same target convention
as in Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}.
Changing to DFL's $(d^*,d^+)$ convention uses a fixed
target isomorphism on every family and every gluing
map; no untransported identification with $-D$ is used.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:closed-torus-reference-coherence}
The orientations $\lambda_T^{\rm cl}(B)$ arise from
one choice of DFL's index-four reference orientation,
with the reference-generator paths and the standard
$S^4$ orientations held fixed. Denote this coherent
system by $\mathcal O_T^{\rm cl}$.
In this system the comparison in
\eqref{eq:coherent-closed-two-signs} satisfies
$\delta_{\rm ref}=+1$ at every torus residual.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We first record the determinant transport for moving the
insertion. In a product band of tori choose a normal
rectangle, an oriented smaller disc, and the based
clutching of
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-clutching}.
Translate the disc through the rectangle. Its isotopy
identifies the relatively framed modified bundles.
Connections on the rectangle can be interpolated
relative to its outer boundary, since their space
is affine; metrics can be interpolated through
positive metrics. These are configuration families,
not families of ASD solutions.

Combine the disc motion with the signed rotation in
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-excision}.
After the stated identifications this is a Fredholm
family on a square, with fixed invertible end
operators. A common finite-dimensional target
supplement makes the whole family surjective.
Its kernel determinant, with the supplement
determinant cancelled, shows that the two
boundary transports agree. This includes
cokernel crossings. Cylinder gluing on a
disjoint collar commutes with this square.
For nested collars insert and cancel a
constant product collar, as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:interior-oriented-excision}.

Apply this construction along a reference cylinder
$q$ from $B$ to $B'$. The two end decompositions
are $p_T(B)$ followed by the $\iota_T$ transform
of $q$, and $q$ followed by $p_T(B')$.
Use the natural adjoint identification on the
transformed cylinder. The determinant square
equates these two gluing maps with their
relative closed factors. The orientation
of $q$ cancels, so
$\lambda_T^{\rm cl}(B')$ is determined by
$\lambda_T^{\rm cl}(B)$.

Endpoint product framings can be extended over
this band. Indeed, the odd flat torus connection
has holonomies given by half-turns about two
orthogonal axes. Its four parallel projective
automorphisms realize all four lifting
obstructions in $H^1(T;\mathbb Z/2)$: this
follows by taking commutators of their
$SU(2)$ lifts with the two holonomy lifts.
These are all components of the surface
gauge group, because $\pi_1(SO(3))=\mathbb Z/2$
and $\pi_2(SO(3))=0$.
An endpoint parallel automorphism therefore
removes the obstruction to extending a framing.
Such changes preserve the closed orientation
by \cite[Corollary~3.27]{Donaldson87}.
Line twists of the lifts preserve the relative
orientation by
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}.
The meridional clutching is retained; no
framing is extended across the modified disc.

The doubled reference agrees with the standard
index-eight orientation. Two copies of the
clutching close to a bundle $E_2$ with
\[
 c_1(E_2)=2h+f,\qquad c_2(E_2)=2hf,\qquad
                      p_1(\operatorname{ad}E_2)=-4hf.
\]
Close the insertions by the common product
caps. The disjoint rotation square compares
their composite with
\[
 \det Q_{E_1}\otimes\det Q_{E_1}
                \longrightarrow\det Q_{E_2}\otimes\det Q_0.
\]
This closed excision preserves complex
orientations. On the Kähler product, the
ordinary first-order operator in the fixed
target identification is the complex-linear
operator $\bar\partial^*\oplus\bar\partial$
on $(0,1)$-forms. This first-order identity
does not require $\bar\partial^2=0$.
The scalar rotation intertwines the complex
structures, and its commutator path remains
complex-linear. Its determinant transport
is therefore positive through any cokernel
crossings. The Donaldson/complex exponents
for $E_0,E_1,E_2$ are $-1,0,1$, since
for $c=kh+f$ one has
$\langle c^2+Kc,[\PP^1\times T]\rangle/2=k-1$.
The product signs on both sides are $+1$.
The map consequently preserves the specified
Donaldson orientations as well.

Tensoring $E_2$ by $\mathcal O(-1)$ gives
$c_1=f$, $c_2=hf$, with no orientation sign
by Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}.
It differs from $E_0$ by one instanton with
the first Chern class fixed. Its relative
orientation is therefore the standard
instanton-addition orientation used to define
the Donaldson orientations
\cite[Proposition~3.20]{Donaldson87}.
This verifies the squared-reference
compatibility with the standard $S^4$ factor.
Further $S^4$ factors can be placed in
disjoint balls and commute with the same
rotation square. All inserted factors
have even index. DFL's path classification
and orientation recipe in
\cite[Section~2.4, pp.~20--22]{DaeFukLip21a}
therefore give the asserted coherent system.

For the mixed comparison, first use the smooth
configuration transports in the construction of
\eqref{eq:global-to-closed-excision}. They
replace the glued solution and residual by
representatives differing by the ordinary
framed insertion in a strictly negative
disc times $T$. The positive torus map
is constant. Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-cap-complex-orientation}
supplies the chosen integrable flattening
with its product collar. The other matching
component and all global ends remain in
the common exterior. Naturality for nested
excision identifies this relative comparison
with the specified composite $JG$.

Prepare the two configurations simultaneously
for DFL Lemma~4.62, with preparation away
from the insertion. They have the same
positive map and may use the same positive
extension $A_u$ in DFL's formula (4.63).
In their $\Phi_-$ coordinates the
translation $A_\tau(s,y)=A(s+\tau,y)$
moves the insertion to the left.
For a finite sufficiently large shift
both pairs are symplectically constant
and the entire insertion lies in the
ordinary incoming gauge cylinder.
The original disc framing and the
orientation of its normal plane move
by this same translation.

The shifted pairs are ordinary cylinders,
with or without $p_T$, glued to constant
mixed pairs. If the joining representatives
differ by $\iota_T$, use the correspondingly
transformed constant pair. Its operator
and the original constant operator are
invertible by DFL Proposition~3.19;
their determinant units agree under the
adjoint identification. The framing change
remains in the $p_T$ factor.

DFL Section~4.7 defines the mixed orientation
by this cylinder gluing, with determinant
isomorphism canonical up to positive scalar.
The family shifting lemma, DFL Lemma~4.64,
and the finite-supplement square commute
this gluing with the interior excision.
After the constant mixed factors cancel,
$JG$ is
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-excision}
for the shifted residual cylinder.
Its reference factor is positive by the
coherence just proved. This gives
$\delta_{\rm ref}=+1$ in
$\mathcal O_T^{\rm cl}$ for every residual.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:torus-reference-tube-transport}
The system $\mathcal O_T^{\rm cl}$ agrees with
the tube normalization in
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-tube-point-operation}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $W^\circ$ be the tube complement and
$P\cong[0,1]\times T$ the three-manifold used
in that proposition. Its trivial normal line,
together with the interval direction, gives
a band of discs crossed with $T$.
Orient the disc plane by the orientations
of $W^\circ$ and $T$. At the incoming
tube boundary take $r$ increasing from the
removed disc into $W^\circ$ and $\theta$
its positive meridian. The plane has
orientation $dr\wedge d\theta$, with the
incoming cylinder direction first.
It also agrees with
the outgoing time/normal orientation.
Consequently sliding a disc through the
band retains the clutching in
\eqref{eq:ordinary-torus-reference-clutching}.

The odd bundle near $P$ is pulled back
from $T$. Extend compatible endpoint
framings as in the preceding lemma,
and flatten a cobordism connection $A$
to the product $\alpha$ on a smaller
band, relative to its ends and exterior.
Slide the framed insertion from the
outgoing collar to the tube collar.
The two descriptions of the resulting
connection are
\[
 A\text{ followed by }p_T(B_{\rm out}),
 \qquad
 p_{T^3}(B_{\rm tube})\text{ followed by }A^\xi,
\]
where $\xi$ is the real line with
$w_1(\xi)=\operatorname{PD}(P)$.
To see this last identification, cut
along $P$. The determinant-one lifts
of the rotating-summand clutching
differ by $-I$ across this cut and
agree elsewhere. These are exactly
the central transition functions of
$\xi$. They are trivial on incoming
$Y_\#$ and induce the distinguished
involutions on the tube and outgoing
$Y_\#$. The same based clutching has
been moved by isotopy, so no additional
relative instanton class is introduced.

The disc-motion and rotation square
identifies the determinant maps for
these two factorizations. In both,
the reference determinant is oriented
by the same closed relative line.
The natural adjoint identification
of $A$ with $A^\xi$ also identifies
the end matching factors.
After cancellation the square is
positive. A reference factor has
index four, so passing it through
a cobordism factor of arbitrary
index produces no odd sign.
The overall cobordism orientation
cancels. Reference cylinders and
disjoint $S^4$ factors give the
same extension to all configuration
components as in the preceding lemma.
Thus, at the level of determinant
orientations, the tube satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:closed-torus-tube-square}
 \Psi(\tau_0^{\rm cl}a\otimes v)
                       =\tau_T^{\rm cl}\Psi(a\otimes v).
\end{equation}
Positivity here is proved by the
determinant square, independently
of whether $\Psi$ or the ambient
Floer groups are nonzero.

It remains to identify the tube-end
reference. Use the actual torus-only
DFL splitting of $T^3$ into two
product cylinders. The preceding
lemma gives $\delta_{\rm ref}=+1$
in $\mathcal O_0^{\rm cl}$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-cap-complex-orientation}
gives $\delta_{\rm cap}=+1$.
Since $\sigma_1=-1$ and the closed
relative sign is $-1$, we obtain
$\eta_1=+1$. The single-generator
calculation in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-only-comparison}
and Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}
then give $b_T=8\id$ on the quotient
in this already fixed orientation.

The unreduced torus complex has two
generators of degrees differing by
four, zero differential, and homology
$\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z$
\cite[Section~7.1, pp.~52--53]{KronheimerMrowka10}.
The filling vector $e$ is one
generator up to sign. Its image
in the quotient by $\tau_0^{\rm cl}=1$
generates a free copy of $\mathbb Z$.
The degree and the quotient formula
therefore give
\[
                         b_Te=8\tau_0^{\rm cl}e.
\]
This identifies $\tau_0^{\rm cl}$
with the positive $\tau_0$ used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-tube-point-operation}.
Equation~\eqref{eq:closed-torus-tube-square}
then identifies the outgoing
reference with that proposition's
reference orientation.
The nonzero torus group calibrates
the tube end only; no nonvanishing
assumption on $I(Y_\#)$ has entered
the determinant transport.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:torus-integral-geometric-comparison}
For a point $q$ on a matching torus, use the
coherent tube normalization of
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-tube-point-operation}
and the regular point-cut data of
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}.
Every primitive torus contribution has
$\eta_1=+1$ relative to its residual.
The original integral geometric homotopy satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:torus-integral-geometric-comparison}
 N b_q^G-b_q^S N
    =d_S K_q^{p_1}+K_q^{p_1}d_G+8N.
\end{equation}
The assertion holds for simultaneous finite
point cuts and after any extension of
coefficients from $\mathbb Z$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Lemmas~\ref{lem:closed-torus-reference-coherence}
and \ref{lem:torus-reference-tube-transport}
give $\delta_{\rm ref}=+1$ in the prescribed
system. Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-cap-complex-orientation}
and \eqref{eq:coherent-closed-two-signs}
then give $\eta_1=+1$.
The actual gluing and finite parameter
orientations have already been compared,
so the multiplicity of every torus
contribution is $+8$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}
therefore has $B_q=8N$, proving
\eqref{eq:torus-integral-geometric-comparison}.
Lemma~\ref{lem:simultaneous-finite-point-cuts}
gives the finite simultaneous version.
If both components are tori, their
normalizations agree by the integral
path-of-points argument in
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-only-comparison}.
\end{proof}

This identifies the prescribed geometric homotopy on a
torus component without a rational chain correction.
Simultaneous agreement with a genus-two sphere normalization
is proved in Section~\ref{sec:homologous-surface-reference}.

\subsection{The sphere character and the genus-two relation}
\label{sec:sphere-character-quantum-relation}

For any component of genus at least two, comparison
with the system which selects that component in
DFL Remark~4.76 gives a sphere compatibility sign
$\chi_j$. The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-component-sign}
applies to the sphere insertion itself in every
such genus: its relative index is four, and the
two systems differ only in the orientation of
$p_0$. Thus $\chi_j$ is independent of the strip
class and its generators. Disjoint sphere gluing
extends these signs to the character
\[
 \chi:\pi_2(M)=\bigoplus_{g_j\ge2}\mathbb Z A_j
      \longrightarrow\{+1,-1\},\qquad
 \chi\!\left(\sum_j d_j A_j\right)=\prod_j\chi_j^{d_j}.
\]
Associativity of determinant gluing proves
multiplicativity; the sphere factors have
even index and inverse classes have dual
orientation. DFL Remark~2.39 makes the
comparison depend only on homotopy class.

In the quantum-module convention for this
coherent strip orientation, a closed sphere
term of class $A$ carries the weight $\chi(A)$.
Indeed, the ordinary strip-breaking terms
in the oriented module boundary argument
use the coherent strip gluing, while the
closed-sphere terms use its comparison with
the complex sphere orientation. Their
discrepancy is precisely $\chi(A)$.
Retaining formal sphere variables and then
specializing the variable of $A_j$ to
$\chi_j$ gives this product. The character
law preserves its associativity, unit,
and graded commutativity. We use this
convention for the rational module structure
recalled on p.~5 of \cite{DaeFukLip21a};
an all-positive specialization on unselected
components is not assumed.

More explicitly, choose auxiliary spin structures on
$L,L'$ and let $\lambda_p^{\mathrm{sp}}$ be their
strip orientations. Existence of the spin structures
is stated in DFL Remark~2.39. Write
$\lambda_p^{\mathcal O}=\epsilon(p)\lambda_p^{\mathrm{sp}}$.
Comparison of the strip and sphere gluing maps gives
\[
 \epsilon(p\#p')=\epsilon(p)\epsilon(p'),\qquad
 \epsilon(p\#A)=\epsilon(p)\chi(A).
\]
Marked strips inherit their determinant orientations
from the unmarked problem. A broken strip in total
class $p=p_1\#p_2$ therefore changes orientation by
$\epsilon(p_1)\epsilon(p_2)=\epsilon(p)$.
A boundary with a closed sphere $A$ and remaining
strip $p'$ changes by $\epsilon(p')\chi(A)$,
again equal to $\epsilon(p)$.
Applying this comparison to the reference oriented
module identity gives exactly the sphere weight
above. Any disc terms in its compactification are
retained with their total relative-class signs.
The pair is monotone with minimal Maslov number
four by DFL Proposition~2.27, so changing signs
introduces no Maslov-two obstruction.
This is a classwise comparison of oriented identities;
it does not assume that $\epsilon(p)$ depends only
on the endpoints, or that the chosen spin structures
induce the DFL orientations.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:signed-genus-two-quantum-relation}
For a genus-two component put
$a_j^S=-\frac12 b_{\Sigma_j}^S$, and retain
the geometric point operation $b_j^S$.
On rational symplectic instanton homology,
\[
                    (a_j^S)^2+b_j^S=4\chi_j\id.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In \cite[Example~24]{Munoz99b}, the geometric
generators satisfy $\widehat\beta=\beta+4$ and
$\alpha^2+\widehat\beta-8=0$ on both summands
of the displayed quantum presentation.
Here $\alpha=-\frac12p_1/[\Sigma_j]$ and
$\beta=p_1/[q_j]$. Thus the relation is
$\alpha*\alpha+\beta=4$ in the complex sphere
orientation. Before specialization the right
side is $4q_j$. The minimal Chern number is
two, so $q_j$ has degree four; a degree-four
relation has only its classical term and
this one-sphere constant term.

The coefficient can also be checked geometrically.
In the intersection of two quadrics,
$\alpha=h_2$, $\beta=-4h_4$, and
$\alpha\smile\alpha=4h_4$.
A minimal sphere is a line of hyperplane degree one.
The degree-four attaching map of
Proposition~\ref{prop:degree-four} counts four lines
through a generic point. The divisor equation
then gives
$\operatorname{GW}_{0,3,A}(\alpha,\alpha,[\mathrm{point}])=4$,
which is the stated constant term.

The pulled-back relation in the product
target is the factor relation. With product
data the other factor contributes its
degree-zero unit, so no other sphere variable
occurs. Specializing $q_j$ to $\chi_j$ in
the module convention just described gives
the assertion. This uses the geometric
$\beta$, not the corrected
$\widehat\beta$, and no quantum-to-Floer
phase change.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Homologous surfaces and the genus-two reference}
\label{sec:homologous-surface-reference}

For an odd connected surface $S\subset Y_\#$, define
$\lambda_S^{\rm cl}(B)$ at a nondegenerate generator $B$
by the ordinary excision
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-surface-reference}
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_B^{\,S}\otimes\det Q_{S,0})
      \longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Or}(\det D_B\otimes\det Q_{S,1}).
\end{equation}
Here $D_B^{\,S}$ is the degree-one framed local replacement,
and $Q_{S,0},Q_{S,1}$ are the closed product pair of
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}, with determinant
degree normalized to one. Require positivity for their
relative Donaldson orientation, cancelling the invertible
constant cylinder. This extends the definition in
Section~\ref{sec:torus-reference-orientation}; the constant
closed determinant is retained in every genus.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:homologous-surface-reference}
If two odd connected surfaces in $Y_\#$ bound an
intervening three-manifold, their closed references agree
in the same ordinary index-four path torsor. The comparison
is independent of a reversal of either surface orientation
and commutes with reference cylinders and the standard
$S^4$ orientation.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
At the closed product pair, deform the connections to
preserve the smooth splittings
\[
 E_0=\mathbb C\oplus\Lambda,\qquad
 E_1=\mathcal O(1)\oplus\Lambda.
\]
The adjoint coefficient then splits as
$\mathbb R\oplus\operatorname{Hom}(\Lambda,\mathcal O(k))$,
$k=0,1$. The second term is a complex line regarded
as a real plane; its ASD operator is complex-linear
with respect to the coefficient complex structure,
independently of a Kähler structure on the base.

These reductions give the specified Donaldson
orientations. Indeed,
$E_k=(\mathbb C\oplus\mathcal O(k)\Lambda^{-1})\otimes\Lambda$.
The reduction comparison with the same determinant lift
and trivial second line has sign
$(-1)^{\langle c_1(\Lambda)^2,[\PP^1\times S]\rangle}=+1$
by \cite[Proposition~3.25]{Donaldson87}.
Thus the relative closed orientation is the
coefficient-complex orientation of
\begin{equation}\label{eq:surface-reference-complex-line}
 \det_{\mathbb R}D_{\mathcal O(1)\Lambda^{-1}}
                      \otimes
                  (\det_{\mathbb R}D_{\Lambda^{-1}})^*,
\end{equation}
after cancellation of the identical real coefficient
operators. Kernels and cokernels are both retained.
The complex relative index is two. The real coefficient
operator on $\PP^1\times S$ has even index $2g(S)-2$,
so these cancellations have no odd sign.

First orient $S_0,S_1$ to be homologous. An odd connected
surface is nonseparating and primitive in integral homology.
Tensoring a rank-two lift by an appropriate line therefore
normalizes its determinant degree to one on both surfaces.
This leaves the adjoint unchanged; the closed lift
independence was checked in
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}.
The normalized lift on $Y_\#$ has a smooth nowhere-zero
section, since its real rank is four and the base has
dimension three. Write it as $\mathbb C\oplus\Lambda$.

In a bounded time interval deform a cylinder connection
to preserve this splitting, using a larger interval
to interpolate to the original connection. Turn off
the lower-order perturbations in the smaller interval
with a common cutoff for the two operators being
compared. The asymptotic operators stay fixed and
invertible. This gives Fredholm deformations even
when the intermediate slice connections are reducible.

Close a copy of the smaller interval to
$Z=S^1\times Y_\#$. A line bundle $K_i$ trivialized
outside a disc times $S_i$, with relative first
Chern class the Thom class of $\{t_0\}\times S_i$,
describes the local replacement as
\[
             \mathbb C\oplus\Lambda
                         \quad\longmapsto\quad K_i\oplus\Lambda.
\]
Writing $z$ for the positive class of $H^1(S^1)$ gives
$c_1(K_i)=z\smile\operatorname{PD}(S_i)$.
Its square is zero and
$\langle c_1(K_i)c_1(\Lambda),[Z]\rangle=1$,
so the relative charge is the prescribed one.

Choose a common bounded neighborhood of the intervening
three-manifold and both collars. The two Thom classes
agree in its relative $H^2$, since their difference
is dual to the boundary of that three-manifold.
Classification of line bundles with exterior
trivialization gives $K_0\cong K_1$ relative to the
exterior. Thus the framed modifications are
identified, in addition to having equal index.

The interior excision and the cylinder-closing
excision commute by the stabilized square in
Lemma~\ref{lem:interior-oriented-excision}.
On the closed reducible operators they preserve
the real and complex coefficient splitting.
The unchanged real block contributes the unit:
its rotation extends to a homotopy on the globally
unchanged real coefficient, and conjugation
followed by the commutator path is a contractible
loop of compact perturbations. The complex block
preserves its coefficient-complex orientation.
The real index on $S^1\times Y_\#$ is zero,
and on $\PP^1\times S_i$ it is $2g(S_i)-2$.
Both are even.

Consequently \eqref{eq:ordinary-surface-reference}
is computed on $Z$ by the relative complex
determinants for $K_i\oplus\Lambda$ and
$\mathbb C\oplus\Lambda$. The unitary line
isomorphism $K_0\cong K_1$ and an affine
path of line connections identify these
orientations positively. The same relative
bundle identification gives the comparison
path for the global cylinders. Naturality
proves equality of their reference orientations.
For a reference cylinder between different
generators the identical core reduction
retains that cylinder as a common exterior
factor, whose orientation cancels. Disjoint
$S^4$ insertions commute with the same square;
their orientations are the fixed instanton-addition
orientations. The doubled clutching is normalized
to a fixed lift as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-torus-reference-coherence},
using the reduction comparison above.

Finally reverse a surface orientation, keeping
the ambient orientation fixed. The normal
plane reverses too, and exchanging the two
line summands supplies the degree-one
normalization. The two descriptions are
$K\oplus\Lambda$ and
$\mathbb C\oplus\Lambda K^{-1}$, identified
projectively by tensoring the second with $K$.
Exchanging the summands reverses the real
adjoint axis and conjugates the complex
relative determinant. The real change
cancels between the modified and constant
operators. The complex relative index is
two, so conjugation contributes $(-1)^2=+1$.
The line twist has $c_1(K)^2=0$ and
introduces no Donaldson orientation sign.
The reference is therefore unchanged.
\end{proof}

Let $\lambda_S^{\rm sph}$ be the ordinary reference
orientation defined by a positive generator sphere in
DFL Remark~4.76, with reference-generator paths and
$S^4$ orientations fixed.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:genus-two-reference-locality}
There is a sign $\delta_{{\rm sph},2}\in\{+1,-1\}$, independent
of the ambient splitting and reference generator, such that
\[
          \lambda_S^{\rm sph}=\delta_{{\rm sph},2}\lambda_S^{\rm cl}
                         \qquad\text{when }g(S)=2.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix an attaching flat connection $\alpha$, put
$V=H^1(S;\operatorname{ad}\alpha)=T_{[\alpha]}M(S)$,
and choose a based smooth positive generator sphere $s$,
constant near the boundary of its representing disc.
Insert it strictly in the positive interior of the
compact constant mixed cap $C_\alpha$, giving $C_s$.
The positive-interior excision in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-coherent-sign}
orients the relative line
$\det C_s\otimes(\det C_\alpha)^*$ by
\[
                       \det D_s\otimes(\det V)^*.
\]
Here $D_s$ is the closed parametrized sphere
Cauchy--Riemann operator. The node value is removed
once; no sphere automorphism quotient is introduced.
The harmonic space $V$ has complex dimension three,
and the relative real index is four.
The nonharmonic constant block is invertible.
The harmonic matching sequence and sphere determinant
have their canonical complex orientations, including
when $s$ is not holomorphic or $D_s$ has cokernel.

Insert this cap into a global constant mixed pair
with attaching value $\alpha$. The full uncut constant
residual operator is invertible by DFL Proposition~3.19,
so its determinant has the canonical unit orientation.
In the relative excision square we retain both the cut
exterior determinant and the constant-cap harmonic
factor $\det V$ until the matching factors cancel.
The disjoint cylinder,
mixed-to-strip and sphere gluing squares in
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-coherent-sign}
identify the displayed orientation with exactly
the sphere orientation used in DFL Remark~4.76.

Now perform DFL's shifting simultaneously on
the sphere insertion and the constant pair.
The only varying positive target factor is $M(S)$,
and the sphere is constant near the upper and
lower strip edges. Choose its surface lift
on a supporting rectangle, fixed on three edges.
The fourth edge retains the nonextendible
surface gauge loop described in
\cite[Section~2.2, p.~8]{DaeFukLip21a}.
That loop has half-unit charge. In the ordinary
normal collar it produces the distinguished
outgoing involution. No lift fixed on all
four edges is assumed.

For two ambient extensions of the same local data,
use the same surface lifts and fourth-edge transition
in the positive extension of DFL (4.63).
The ambient piece connections are constant outside
the supporting collar. The outgoing constant
mixed pair is involution-transformed if required
by the retained transition; its determinant unit
is unchanged. A finite shift puts the entire
support in the ordinary negative cylinder.
Any coordinate preparation and exterior deformation
is performed on both members of each relative pair.

This cancellation is an identity of determinant
maps. Choose a common outer constant collar
over the compact shifting interval. The compact
mixed domains keep the harmonic modes, gauge
block and normal condition; their smooth family
remains elliptic Fredholm. Combine shifting
with the signed excision rotation. A common
finite target supplement over the parameter
square gives equality of the two boundary
transports through all cokernel crossings.
Nested cuts are separated by a constant collar
before the factors are cancelled.

At the shifted endpoint the ordinary insertion
has the retained nontrivial meridional class
and relative $p_1=-2$. After the base line
normalization its closed lift has $c_1=h+f$,
$c_2=hf$. The relative bundle classification
used in Section~\ref{sec:torus-reference-orientation}
identifies it with the model defining
$\lambda_S^{\rm cl}$. Thus the ratio of
the sphere reference to the closed reference
is the sign of one local compact determinant
transport, independent of the ambient exterior.

This sign is continuous in the local data.
The surface moduli space is connected and simply
connected, with $\pi_2=\mathbb Z$
\cite[Section~2.2, p.~8]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Move $\alpha$ along a path and carry the based
positive sphere with it. Representatives of
the same based class are homotopic; compatible
almost complex structures and the surface
metrics vary in connected families.
The stabilized determinant comparison includes
nonregular configurations along these paths.

Gauge changes preserve the canonical sphere
and harmonic orientations and the closed
Donaldson orientation
\cite[Corollary~3.27]{Donaldson87}.
Lift changes preserve the latter relative
orientation by
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-primitive-orientation}.
Orientation-preserving identifications of
genus-two surfaces carry the odd bundle
and positive generator to the corresponding
data. Hence the sign is the single $\delta_{{\rm sph},2}$
in the statement. No torus acyclicity is
used for the genus-two harmonic block.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:genus-two-reference-calibration}
The sign in Lemma~\ref{lem:genus-two-reference-locality}
is $\delta_{{\rm sph},2}=+1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use $T^3=S^1\times T$ with the odd bundle pulled
back from $T$. Cut at a product torus $T_0$ and
stabilize the product relative Heegaard splitting
of $T\times I$ once in a three-ball. The new
surface $S$ has genus two. Both pieces are
compression bodies from $T$ to $S$, obtained
by adding one one-handle to $T\times I$.
Rejoining their negative torus boundaries
gives the original $T^3$ split along $T_0\sqcup S$.
These surfaces are homologous up to boundary
orientation and remain odd.

We check embeddedness. A compression body $C$
retracts to $T$ with one additional one-cell.
Every flat adjoint local system restricts to
the acyclic odd torus system. The relative
cellular complex gives
\[
 H^0(C;\operatorname{ad}B)=H^2(C;\operatorname{ad}B)=0,
 \qquad \dim H^1(C;\operatorname{ad}B)=3.
\]
Poincaré--Lefschetz duality gives
$H^1(C,\partial C;\operatorname{ad}B)=0$.
The flat moduli space is therefore regular,
compact and three-dimensional, with a
Lagrangian immersion into the six-dimensional
$M(S)$; see also DFL Propositions~2.10 and~2.21.
Restriction is injective because
$\pi_1(S)\to\pi_1(C)$ is surjective.
For the liftable gauge quotient, the
obstruction to lifting an equivalence
which lifts on $S$ vanishes, since
$H^1(C;\mathbb Z/2)\to H^1(S;\mathbb Z/2)$
is injective. A parallel boundary
automorphism extends by the same
$\pi_1$ surjectivity, so an equivalence
can be chosen with the prescribed
liftable boundary restriction.
The compact injective immersion is
an embedding, on both sides.

The adjoint cohomology of $T^3$ vanishes
by its torus factor. The intersection
is consequently transverse by DFL
Lemma~2.47. Small regularizing data
preserve these compact embeddings and
the isolated intersection. The unreduced
torus group is $\mathbb Z\oplus\mathbb Z$;
the quotient by the nice involution is
$\mathbb Z$
\cite[Section~7.1]{KronheimerMrowka10}.
In particular this example has nonzero
rational Floer homology.

Use the fixed torus normalization. Let
$\chi$ be the genus-two sphere compatibility
sign in this system. The torus operation
and the signed global genus-two boundary give
\[
 b^G=8\id,\qquad
 N_Hb^G=(b_S^S+4\chi\id)N_H.
\]
The torus surface operation is zero.
Homology of the two matching surfaces
and DFL's surface comparison imply
$a_S^S=0$. The signed genus-two quantum
relation in
Section~\ref{sec:sphere-character-quantum-relation}
therefore gives $b_S^S=4\chi\id$.
Since $N_H$ is an isomorphism and this
homology is nonzero, $8\id=8\chi\id$
implies $\chi=+1$.

Lemma~\ref{lem:torus-reference-tube-transport}
identifies this normalization with the
closed torus reference.
Lemma~\ref{lem:homologous-surface-reference}
identifies the closed references of $T_0$
and $S$. Thus the positive genus-two
sphere reference is its closed reference,
and $\delta_{{\rm sph},2}=+1$ by locality.
Only the explicit $T^3$ example is
required to have nonzero homology.
No invariance of a mixed point count
under Heegaard stabilization is assumed.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:simultaneous-integral-local-signs}
Choose the torus normalization when a torus is present;
otherwise select a genus-two component when there is one.
Keep that coherent system fixed for both components.
Every torus primitive and each of the four genus-two
branches has positive orientation relative to its residual.
For the simultaneous regular point cuts,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:simultaneous-integral-local-signs}
 B_{q_j}=c_{g_j}N,\qquad
               c_1=8,\quad c_2=4,\quad c_{g\ge3}=0.
\end{equation}
No Floer homology nonvanishing assumption is required.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The preceding two lemmas identify each genus-two
positive-sphere reference with its closed reference.
Lemma~\ref{lem:homologous-surface-reference} identifies
the two matching closed references. They agree with
the torus normalization when a torus is present.
Thus each genus-two component has $\chi_j=+1$
in the chosen single system.
Proposition~\ref{prop:genus-two-component-sign}
gives positivity of every branch at every residual.
The torus assertion is
Proposition~\ref{prop:torus-integral-geometric-comparison},
and the higher-genus contribution already vanishes.
Substitute these signs into
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}, using
Lemma~\ref{lem:simultaneous-finite-point-cuts}.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Evaluation of the local coefficient}\label{sec:coefficients}

For genus at least three, attachment support and residual
avoidance in Proposition~\ref{prop:all-bubbling-cut-ends}
exclude the primitive contribution, so
\begin{equation}\label{eq:higher-coefficient}
                           c_g^{\rm loc}=0,\qquad g\geq3.
\end{equation}
Propositions~\ref{prop:torus-seam-obstruction}
and~\ref{prop:genus-two-seam-obstruction}
compute the actual relative seam-incidence magnitudes
eight and four. The latter is one on each of the four
attaching branches.
Proposition~\ref{prop:simultaneous-integral-local-signs}
compares these local orientations with DFL's coherent
orientation at every residual in one system. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:integral-coefficients}
 c_g^{\rm loc}=
 \begin{cases}
 8,&g=1,\\
 4,&g=2,\\
 0,&g\geq3.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
The comparison is on determinant lines and applies
also when rational Floer homology vanishes. Substitution
in the global boundary formula gives the point theorem.

\subsection{The oriented boundary count}\label{sec:oriented-boundary}

\begin{theorem}[integral geometric point comparison]\label{thm:integral}
In the embedded DFL setting, use the single coherent normalization
of Proposition~\ref{prop:simultaneous-integral-local-signs}.
For simultaneous regular point-cut data, before division by four,
the operations represented by the positive integral degeneracy
cycle and the original geometric counts satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:integral-main}
 N b_q^G-b_q^S N
 =d_SK_q^{p_1}+K_q^{p_1}d_G+c_{g_j}N,
 \qquad
 c_g=
 \begin{cases}
 8,&g=1,\\
 4,&g=2,\\
 0,&g\geq3.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
The same equation data and comparison map work for both
matching components. The identity extends from $\mathbb Z$
to any commutative coefficient ring.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-point-global-boundary}
proves the integral identity with $B_q$ in place of
$c_{g_j}N$. Proposition~\ref{prop:simultaneous-integral-local-signs}
identifies these maps in one coherent system, without
a homology nonvanishing assumption.
Lemma~\ref{lem:simultaneous-finite-point-cuts}
gives the simultaneous data. All coefficients in
the oriented boundary count are integral, so
extension of coefficients preserves the identity.
\end{proof}

\section{Coefficient changes and observable operations}
\label{sec:integral}

\begin{theorem}[rational point-class comparison]\label{thm:main}
Let $(Y_\#,E_\#)$ have an admissible splitting
$Y_\#=Y\cup_\Sigma(-Y')$ with two matching components
in the embedded-Lagrangian DFL setting. Assume
the flat connections are irreducible and use compatible standard
perturbations that vanish near the matching line.  Let
$q\in\Sigma_j$ and write $g_j$ for the genus of that component.  Over
$\QQ$, there is one coherent normalization and a
rational chain homotopy for which the comparison map
and the positive quarter-Pontryagin point operations satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main}
 N m_q^G-m_q^S N
 =d_SK_q+K_qd_G+\kappa_{g_j}N,
 \qquad
 \kappa_g=
 \begin{cases}
 2,&g=1,\\
 1,&g=2,\\
 0,&g\geq3.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
The choices can be made for both components with the same
equation and comparison map. The homotopy can be chosen
as the original integral point-cut count divided by four.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Base-change Theorem~\ref{thm:integral} to $\QQ$ and divide
its identity by four. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:final-rational-homotopy}
                            K_q=\frac14K_q^{p_1}.
\end{equation}
Since $c_g=4\kappa_g$, this gives \eqref{eq:main}
with the stated geometric homotopy.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:corrected-operator}
Define
\[
                  \widehat m_q^S=m_q^S+\kappa_{g_j}\id.
\]
Then $N$ intertwines $m_q^G$ and $\widehat m_q^S$ up to chain homotopy:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:corrected-intertwining}
             Nm_q^G-\widehat m_q^S N=d_SK_q+K_qd_G.
\end{equation}
In particular, the induced Atiyah--Floer isomorphism intertwines these
corrected point operations on homology.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Move $\kappa_{g_j}N$ to the left-hand side of
\eqref{eq:main} and use the definition of $\widehat m_q^S$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The full corrected operation algebra}
\label{subsec:full-operation-algebra}

We now prove the operation relations over arbitrary coefficients.
The only two-insertion moduli spaces required are ordinary
instanton trajectory spaces of index at most seven.

We use the cohomological indexing introduced in
Section~\ref{sec:intro}. An insertion of codimension $p$
has degree $p$, and its comparison homotopy has degree $p-1$.
Give $H_k(\Sigma;\ZZ)$ degree $4-k$. For a commutative ring $R$, put
$V_R=H_*(\Sigma;\ZZ)\otimes_{\ZZ}R$ and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:integral-observable-algebra}
 \mathbb A_{p_1}(\Sigma;R)
 =T_R(V_R)\big/
   \left\langle x\otimes y-(-1)^{|x||y|}y\otimes x\right\rangle
 =:\operatorname{Sym}^{\rm gr}_R(V_R).
\end{equation}
The relations are imposed on homogeneous generators. In particular,
they impose $2x^2=0$, but not $x^2=0$, for odd $x$.
If two is invertible in $R$, this is the familiar presentation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:usual-observable-algebra}
 \mathbb A_{p_1}(\Sigma;R)
 \cong
 \operatorname{Sym}_R(H_0(\Sigma;R)\oplus H_2(\Sigma;R))
 \otimes_R\Lambda_R H_1(\Sigma;R).
\end{equation}

For $\sigma\in H_k(\Sigma;\ZZ)$ let $b_\sigma^G,b_\sigma^S$
be the operations associated with the positive integral cycle
$p_1/[\sigma]$. Choose oriented representatives of an integral
basis of $H_*(\Sigma;\ZZ)$ and extend the constructions linearly.
For $q\in\Sigma_j$, set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:integral-corrected-point}
 \widehat b_q^S=b_q^S+c_{g_j}\id,\qquad
 c_g=
 \begin{cases}
 8,&g=1,\\
 4,&g=2,\\
 0,&g\geq3.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
Write $A_x=b_x^G$ and let $B_x$ be the corrected symplectic
operator; thus $B_x=b_x^S$ for a surface or loop.
The degree of each operation is $|x|=4-\dim x$.
The point correction has the same degree in the relative
$\ZZ/4$ grading.
We use the graded chain convention
\[
 d_GA_x=(-1)^{|x|}A_xd_G,\qquad
 d_SB_x=(-1)^{|x|}B_xd_S.
\]
For the point operation, the latter identity also follows
from Theorem~\ref{thm:integral} and the fact that $N$ is a
chain isomorphism.

\begin{lemma}[integral generator comparisons]
\label{lem:integral-generator-comparisons}
The data of Theorem~\ref{thm:integral} can be chosen so that,
for every generator $x$, there is an integer map $K_x$ of
degree $|x|-1$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:generator-graded-homotopy}
 NA_x-B_xN=d_SK_x+(-1)^{|x|}K_xd_G.
\end{equation}
All identities use the same integral chain isomorphism $N$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:integral} proves the point assertion with
the geometric homotopy. The surface construction in
\cite[Section~7.2, pp.~88--92, Proposition~7.12]{DaeFukLip21a}
uses the integral two-section dependence cycle with its
$p_1$ coorientation, and its counts are integers.
There is no division by four in those cutdowns.
The final paragraph on p.~92 constructs the loop comparison
by increasing the indices by one. The homotopies have
degrees one and two for surfaces and loops, respectively.
With the graded chain convention, moving the insertion
past the odd differential gives the sign $(-1)^{|x|}$
in the displayed boundary identity. In particular it is
negative for a loop.

Lemma~\ref{lem:simultaneous-finite-point-cuts} permits
these finitely many section choices for the same equation
and $N$. Finally \cite[Theorem~3.29]{DaeFukLip21a} is an
integral chain isomorphism theorem: the matrix of $N$
is triangular in the energy ordering, with diagonal
entries $\pm1$. Its inverse is an integer chain map.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[integral operation relations]
\label{lem:integral-operation-relations}
On gauge and symplectic Floer homology with coefficients
in any commutative ring $R$, the integral operations satisfy
\[
                       b_xb_y=(-1)^{|x||y|}b_yb_x
\]
for homogeneous $x,y\in V_R$. The corrected symplectic
operations satisfy the same relations. All these
commutators are induced by integer chain homotopies.
For an odd loop class the assertion is $2b_x^2=0$;
no odd-square vanishing in characteristic two is used.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Work first over $\ZZ$ and on the ordinary gauge side.
Let $X,Y$ represent generators of dimensions $k,l$,
and write $p=4-k$, $q=4-l$. Suppose first that $p+q\leq7$.
For an ordinary parametrized trajectory $A$ and $T\in\RR$,
place the first insertion at time zero along $X$ and
the second at time $T$ along $Y$. At actual integer
Fredholm index $r$ the uncut space is
\[
 \cM_G(\alpha,\beta)_r\times\RR_T\times X\times Y.
\]
The two labeled section-dependence conditions each
have codimension four. Hence the cut dimension is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-two-insertion-dimension}
                         r+1-p-q.
\end{equation}
Use $r=p+q-1$ for the count $H_{xy}$ and $r=p+q$
for its one-dimensional boundary.

We describe the relative section choices. At a broken
end where the marks lie on different nonconstant levels,
prescribe the original single-insertion pairs on those
levels. Where they lie on the same level, prescribe
the lower-index two-insertion data there. Induction
on index and iterated products give compatible data
at multiple-breaking corners. Extend from these
ordinary gluing neighborhoods and make the two
pulled-back section pairs independently transverse
on the remaining parts. On a bounded $T$ interval
the pairs may depend on $T$. Even if $x=y$ and
$T=0$, their variations remain independent.
Thus coincident labeled marks form an interior
parameter value and create no diagonal boundary.

At every critical connection keep each pair
independent on its entire cycle, uniformly in $T$.
The complexified adjoint bundle has $c_1=0$ and
is trivial over the three-manifold, so the necessary
frames exist. The original critical frames can be
kept fixed throughout this parameter family, with
independence on their critical neighborhoods.
No interpolation of unrelated global framings is
required. These choices exclude a cut in a long
constant critical segment. The rank-zero stratum
of either pair has codimension twelve, whereas
the maximal uncut dimension above is nine.
Relative generic choices therefore avoid it on
all the relevant strata.

All ordinary trajectories of index at most seven
are regular by \cite[Lemma~2.56]{DaeFukLip21a}.
The ordinary index-energy formula bounds their
energy. On a small ball the bundle lifts to $SU(2)$,
so a local Uhlenbeck bubble removes eight index
units; the distinguished global index-four
involution is not a local bubble. Bubbling
would therefore leave a negative-index solution,
which regularity excludes. Admissibility excludes
reducible ends. Consequently the only limits
are ordinary broken trajectories with the two
marks retained on nonconstant levels.

For a broken configuration with $n$ nonconstant
levels and marks on different levels, the cut
dimension is
\[
                         r-(p+q)-(n-2).
\]
If the marks lie on the same level with finite
separation, the dimension is
\[
                         r+1-(p+q)-(n-1).
\]
These formulas subtract the translation on every
unmarked level. A level carrying a single insertion
has index at least $p$ or $q$; a level carrying
both insertions and their separation parameter
has index at least $p+q-1$.
At $r=p+q$ the only boundary possibilities are
two marked levels of indices $p,q$ with
$T\to+\infty$ or $T\to-\infty$, or a two-marked
level of index $p+q-1$ and an unmarked index-one
level. They give the two composites and the
differential terms, respectively. Additional
breakings have negative cut dimension.
At $r=p+q-1$ there are no broken ends.

Ordinary instanton gluing supplies a collar at each listed
boundary point; the compactness, gluing and orientation
conventions are those of
\cite[Theorem~4.17, Proposition~5.11 and
Sections~5.1, 5.4--5.6]{Donaldson02}
and the admissible setup in \cite[Section~2.4]{DaeFukLip21a}.
Iterated gluing gives the compatible product neighborhoods
used for the relative section choices above.
The section pairs are prescribed
to converge with their derivatives to the product
pairs there. Transversality and the implicit
function theorem identify each collar with
the indicated product cut. Compactness after
removing these ends and relative section
transversality give a compact oriented
one-manifold with exactly this boundary.
No change to the equation or to $N$ is needed.

For the sign, let $\nu_x$ be the oriented rank-four
normal bundle to the first dependence stratum.
The determinant of its effective normal condition
after integration along $X$ is
$\det\nu_x\otimes(\det TX)^*$, of parity $p$.
The second effective condition has parity $q$.
Interchanging their labels has sign $(-1)^{pq}$.
Equivalently the sign is $(-1)^{kl}$, since
the rank-four factors are even. The two separation
ends have opposite outward normals. With the
ordinary coherent gluing orientations and the
corresponding orientation of $H_{xy}$, the
boundary identity is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-integral-commutator}
 A_xA_y-(-1)^{pq}A_yA_x
     =d_GH_{xy}+(-1)^{p+q}H_{xy}d_G.
\end{equation}
The differential sign is that of the graded
Hom differential on a map of degree $p+q-1$.
These are integer counts, including when $x=y$.

Only a pair of points has $p+q>7$. Join $q_0,q_1$
by a path in the connected $Y_\#$, and evaluate
the universal bundle at slice zero along that
path. An interpolating pair can be kept independent
at each critical connection, because the space
of independent complex two-frames in $\CC^3$
is connected. Keep the given endpoint pairs,
extend over critical neighborhoods, and choose
the remaining interpolation generically.
Parameterized index-three cuts define a
degree-three map $L$. The boundary of the
parameterized index-four cut consists of
the two endpoint point cuts and ordinary
index-one breakings. The same bubbling and
constant-level exclusions apply. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-point-path-homotopy}
                    A_{q_1}-A_{q_0}=d_GL+Ld_G.
\end{equation}
Writing $P=A_{q_0}$, $Q=A_{q_1}$, direct
substitution gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-point-pair-commutator}
 PQ-QP=d_GJ+Jd_G,\qquad J=PL-LP.
\end{equation}
Indeed $P$ is an even chain map. A repeated
point generator commutes with itself identically.
No index-eight compactification has been used.

For completeness the symplectic commutator
homotopy can be written explicitly. Put
\[
 F_{xy}=K_xA_y+(-1)^pB_xK_y,\qquad s=(-1)^{pq}.
\]
The generator identities and graded Leibniz rule give
\[
 d_SF_{xy}+(-1)^{p+q}F_{xy}d_G
                          =NA_xA_y-B_xB_yN.
\]
Let $H$ be the gauge homotopy just constructed,
including $J$ in the two-point case. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:transferred-integral-commutator}
 H^S_{xy}=(NH-F_{xy}+sF_{yx})N^{-1}
\end{equation}
satisfies
\[
 B_xB_y-sB_yB_x
       =d_SH^S_{xy}+(-1)^{p+q}H^S_{xy}d_S.
\]
The point corrections are even central scalars,
so the same equation gives the raw symplectic
commutator. All maps, including $N^{-1}$,
are integral. Tensoring these actual chain
identities with $R$ proves the assertion for
every $R$, without a flatness assumption or
an identification of $H(C_R)$ with $H(C_{\ZZ})\otimes R$.
\end{proof}

Denote the resulting homology actions by
$\rho_{G,p_1}^R$ and $\widehat\rho_{S,p_1}^R$,
and write $N_R$ for the base change of $N$.

\begin{corollary}[all-coefficient operation algebra]
\label{thm:full-operation-algebra}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:integral},
for every commutative ring $R$ the comparison
isomorphism is an isomorphism of
$\mathbb A_{p_1}(\Sigma;R)$-modules:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-integral-action}
 N_R\rho_{G,p_1}^R(a)
   =\widehat\rho_{S,p_1}^R(a)N_R,
 \qquad a\in\mathbb A_{p_1}(\Sigma;R).
\end{equation}
For each chosen word representing $a$, the equality
is induced by an explicit chain homotopy.
No strict chain-level graded-commutativity or
homotopy-coherence assertion is included.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:integral-generator-comparisons}
gives the integer homotopy for every generator.
If it holds for homogeneous words $u,v$, set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:product-homotopy}
                 K_{uv}=K_uA_v+(-1)^{|u|}B_uK_v.
\end{equation}
Then the graded Leibniz rule gives
\begin{align*}
 NA_uA_v-B_uB_vN
 &=(NA_u-B_uN)A_v+B_u(NA_v-B_vN)\\
 &=d_SK_{uv}+(-1)^{|u|+|v|}K_{uv}d_G.
\end{align*}
Induction and linearity prove the identity for
each ordered polynomial expression.
Lemma~\ref{lem:integral-operation-relations}
makes the homology actions factor through
\eqref{eq:integral-observable-algebra}.
The homotopy may depend on the representing
word. Tensor the maps, homotopies, $N$ and
its integer inverse with $R$ to obtain
\eqref{eq:full-integral-action}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[change of normalization]
\label{cor:full-quarter-operation-algebra}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:integral},
suppose four is invertible in $R$ and put
$m_\sigma=\frac14b_\sigma$. The comparison
intertwines the normalized observable actions,
with corrected point operator
\begin{equation}\label{eq:quarter-corrected-point}
 \widehat m_q^S=m_q^S+\kappa_{g_j}\id,\qquad
       (\kappa_1,\kappa_2,\kappa_{g\geq3})=(2,1,0).
\end{equation}
The observable algebra has the presentation
\eqref{eq:usual-observable-algebra}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Divide each integral generator identity and its
homotopy by four. The coefficients $8,4,0$
become $2,1,0$. Since two is invertible as well,
\eqref{eq:usual-observable-algebra} applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:full-rational-observable-action}
With the normalization of Theorem~\ref{thm:main},
the DFL isomorphism intertwines the full rational
observable algebra
\[
 \mathbb A(\Sigma)=
 \operatorname{Sym}\bigl(H_0(\Sigma;\mathbb Q)
                 \oplus H_2(\Sigma;\mathbb Q)\bigr)
                         \otimes\Lambda H_1(\Sigma;\mathbb Q)
\]
after correcting each symplectic point generator
by $\kappa_{g_j}\id$. The action kernels agree,
their image algebras are conjugate by $N_H$,
and each chosen word has a chain comparison homotopy.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Corollary~\ref{cor:full-quarter-operation-algebra}
with $R=\QQ$. Its generator homotopies use the
same geometric counts as \eqref{eq:final-rational-homotopy}.
Invertibility of $N_H$ identifies the kernels
and conjugates the image operation algebras.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[operation images and kernels]
\label{cor:operation-images-kernels}
For every coefficient ring covered by
Corollary~\ref{thm:full-operation-algebra}, conjugation by $N_R$ identifies the
two image operation algebras.  Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:operation-kernels}
 \ker\rho_{G,p_1}^R=\ker\widehat\rho_{S,p_1}^R,
\end{equation}
so the identity on $\mathbb A_{p_1}(\Sigma;R)$ induces an isomorphism of the
two operation quotients.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Equation~\eqref{eq:full-integral-action} and invertibility of $N_R$ give
\eqref{eq:operation-kernels}; the same conjugacy identifies the images.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:operation-algebra-boundary}
Corollary~\ref{thm:full-operation-algebra} concerns the action of the full
observable algebra.  It does not say that the mixed comparison map preserves
a gauge-theoretic pair-of-pants product, and the wordwise homotopies
\eqref{eq:product-homotopy} do not constitute an $A_\infty$ module morphism.
An integral operation representing $\frac14p_1/[\sigma]$ still requires the
divisibility data discussed in Remark~\ref{rem:coefficients}; the
all-coefficient statement instead uses the integral $p_1$ cycle.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:uncorrected-false}
The correction in genera one and two cannot be removed by changing the
compactification.  Proposition~\ref{prop:cylinder-identities} detects it
algebraically on $\Sigma_g\times S^1$.  It is therefore part of the point
operation itself under the DFL comparison, rather than an artifact of the
proof.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:coefficients}
The quarter-Pontryagin statement is proved over $\QQ$. An integral
operation representing $\frac14p_1/[q]$ requires a divisibility choice for
the universal bundle that is not used here. The
integral geometric comparison is Theorem~\ref{thm:integral},
formulated directly with the degeneracy cycle $p_1/[q]$.
Its original geometric homotopy follows from the
simultaneous local sign comparison.
\end{remark}

\clearpage
